*BESIII Collaboration,M. Ablikim,M. N. Achasov,P. Adlarson,X. C. Ai,C. S. Akondi,R. Aliberti,A. Amoroso,L. P. An,Q. An,M. S. Anderson,Y. Bai,O. Bakina,H. R. Bao,X. L. Bao,M. Barbagiovanni,V. Dai,A. Dbeyssi,R. E. de Boer,D. Dedovich,Z. Y. Deng,A. Denig,I. Denisenko,M. Destefanis,F. De Mori,E. Di Fiore,X. X. Ding,Y. Ding,Y. Q. Ding,J. Dong,L. Y. Dong,M. Y. Dong,Z. J. Dong,K. B. Du,M. C. Du,S. X. Du,Shaoxu Du,X. L. Du,Y. Q. Du,C. F. Duan,Z. H. Duan,P. Egorov,G. F. Fan,K. X. Fan,Y. H. Fan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First amplitude analysis of Λc+→nKSπ+ decay using 4.5 fb⁻¹ BESIII data; identifies four intermediate resonances (Λ(1830)⁰, Λ(2000)⁰, N(1440)⁺, N(1650)⁺) and measures their product branching fractions and decay asymmetry parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the dynamics of charmed baryon decays and understand the role of excited hyperon and nucleon resonances in Λc+ hadronic decay, which is essential for testing QCD-inspired models and improving knowledge of baryon spectroscopy.

Method: Amplitude analysis of Λc+→nKSπ+ decay using e+e− collision data collected by BESIII at √s = 4.600–4.699 GeV (4.5 fb⁻¹); employs unbinned maximum-likelihood fit with coherent sum of resonant amplitudes including spin–parity constraints and detector response modeling.

Result: Four intermediate resonances identified: Λ(1830)⁰, Λ(2000)⁰, N(1440)⁺, N(1650)⁺; product branching fractions measured with statistical/systematic/normalization uncertainties; decay asymmetry parameters α determined for each resonance contribution.

Conclusion: This analysis provides the first quantitative evidence for significant contributions from multiple excited baryon resonances in Λc+→nKSπ+, offering new constraints on strong interaction dynamics in charm baryon decays and supporting further theoretical investigation of resonance production mechanisms.

Abstract: We perform the first amplitude analysis of the charmed baryon hadronic decay $\Lambda_c^+\to n K^0_{\mathrm{S}} \pi^+$. The analysis uses $e^+ e^-$ annihilation data corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of 4.5~$\rm{fb}^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII spectrometer at center-of-mass energies between 4.600 GeV and 4.699~GeV. The amplitude analysis identifies four intermediate resonances $\Lambda(1830)^{0}$, $\Lambda(2000)^{0}$, $N(1440)^{+}$ and $N(1650)^{+}$. We measure the products of branching fractions: $$\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow \Lambda(1830)^{0}\pip)\cdot \mathcal{B}(\Lambda(1830)^{0} \rightarrow n \Ks) = (2.4\pm0.7\pm0.5\pm0.1)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow \Lambda(2000)^{0}\pip)\cdot \mathcal{B}(\Lambda(2000)^{0} \rightarrow n \Ks) = (5.4\pm1.7\pm1.1\pm0.3)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow N(1440)^{+}\Ks)\cdot \mathcal{B}(N(1440)^{+} \rightarrow n \pip) = (4.0\pm2.0\pm1.5\pm0.2)\times 10^{-3}, \mathcal{B}(\Lambda_{c}^{+} \rightarrow N(1650)^{+}\Ks)\cdot \mathcal{B}(N(1650)^{+} \rightarrow n \pip) = (5.2\pm1.1\pm0.8\pm0.3)\times 10^{-3},$$ where the first uncertainties are statistical, the second are systematic, and the third are related to the normalizing branching fraction $\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+\to n K^0_{\mathrm{S}} \pi^+)$. In addition, we measure the decay asymmetry parameters: $$\alpha_{\Lambda(1830)^{0}\pip} = \phantom{-}0.93^{+0.07}_{-0.72}\pm0.07,\alpha_{\Lambda(2000)^{0}\pip} = -0.91^{+0.56}_{-0.09}\pm0.14,\alpha_{N(1440)^{+}\Ks} = -0.13\pm0.43\pm0.35,\alpha_{N(1650)^{+}\Ks} = -0.42\pm0.45\pm0.29.$$

</details>


### [2] [Benchmarking Machine-Learning Top Taggers from JetClass to a Full-Simulation $Z'\to t\bar{t}$ Search: Transfer, Extrapolation, Sensitivity and Inference Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15274)
*Manisha Kumari,Amal Sarkar*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates seven machine-learning top taggers on realistic CMS Open Data (Geant4 + pileup) for a Z'→tt̄ search, revealing that benchmark AUC rankings do not predict discovery significance—particle transformers (ParT, UParT, GloParT) extend mass reach by 400 GeV due to robustness at high pT and full jet merging; pileup has minimal impact; a lightweight CNN achieves competitive performance at low computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Machine-learning top taggers are typically benchmarked on idealized fast-simulation samples without pileup and in narrow pT ranges, limiting their predictive power for real collider searches; thus, a realistic, end-to-end evaluation on full simulation is needed to assess true physics impact.

Method: Seven taggers—BDT, two image-based CNNs, ParticleNet, and three particle transformers (ParT, UParT, GloParT)—are trained identically on JetClass, then applied without retraining to a Z′→tt̄ search using 2016 CMS Open Data simulated with Geant4 and realistic pileup; performance is evaluated via jet-level discovery significance, ROC AUC, and mass reach across mZ′ = 1.2–2.5 TeV.

Result: Taggers with similar AUC differ by up to 3× in discovery significance; particle transformers extend Z′ mass reach by 400 GeV, especially for fully merged tops and at pT beyond training range; pileup has negligible effect; a small CNN matches discrimination of heavier models at far lower compute cost; ranking stability confirmed under retraining and statistical uncertainties.

Conclusion: Benchmark AUC alone is insufficient for tagger selection in physics analyses; optimal choice must balance kinematic coverage, analysis working point, discovery sensitivity, and computational cost.

Abstract: Searches for heavy resonances decaying to \(t\bar{t}\) test physics beyond the Standard Model at the TeV scale, and their reach depends on how well boosted top jets are identified. Machine-learning top taggers are usually compared on fast-simulation samples without pileup and in a narrow jet-\(p_T\) window, so a benchmark ranking does not directly show how much each tagger adds to a real search. This study trains seven taggers under identical conditions on JetClass. They are a boosted decision tree, two image-based networks, ParticleNet, and three particle transformers (ParT, UParT, and GloParT). The taggers are then applied without retraining to a \(Z'\to t\bar{t}\) search with \(m_{Z'}\) between 1.2 and 2.5~TeV, using 2016 CMS Open Data simulated with Geant4 and realistic pileup. The overall benchmark order of the taggers is preserved. However, taggers with similar area under the ROC curve (AUC) differ by up to a factor of three in jet-level discovery significance. The particle transformers extend the mass reach of the search by \(400~\mathrm{GeV}\), and their lead grows with resonance mass. This gain is seen in fully merged top jets and is associated with stable performance at jet \(p_T\) above the training range. Pileup has only a minor effect on all taggers. A small convolutional network achieves competitive discrimination at a much lower computational cost. The gaps between tagger groups hold under retraining and within statistical uncertainties. The results show that a tagger must be chosen on its kinematic coverage, the working point of the analysis, its discovery sensitivity and its cost, and not on its benchmark score alone.

</details>


### [3] [Adaptive Matrix-Based Denoising Algorithm Using Local Spatial Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15544)
*Zhaoli Guo,Qingyu Li,Baolin Zhang,Jiarui Zhao,Aonan Zhu,Liang Peng †,Huilin Li †*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes an adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm using local spatial support for real-time background rejection in the HLT of high-luminosity colliders, validated on STCF's MDC with >90% background rejection and >98% true-hit retention under realistic conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To meet stringent online data-processing requirements of the High-Level Trigger (HLT) under high-event-rate and high-background conditions at high-luminosity collider facilities—specifically, low/predictable latency, high precision, and high throughput for upstream denoising.

Method: An adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm leveraging local spatial support among detector hits; implemented and tested on the Main Drift Chamber (MDC) of the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF); evaluated using OSCAR-simulated data across five charged-final-state physics channels.

Result: Rejection rate for discrete point-cluster backgrounds >90% at low-to-moderate background levels; true-hit retention ratio >98% even when detector efficiency drops to 90%; satisfies HLT constraints on latency, precision, and throughput.

Conclusion: The algorithm is a generalizable, model-agnostic approach for online denoising in regularly structured detectors, as its filtering criterion relies solely on local spatial hit relationships—not predefined track parameters or physics models.

Abstract: To address the online data-processing requirements of the high-level trigger (HLT) under high-event-rate and high-background conditions in high-luminosity collider facilities, this paper proposes an adaptive matrix-based denoising algorithm using local spatial support. The algorithm is implemented and tested in the main drift chamber (MDC) of the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF). Based on tests with five charged-final-state physics channels simulated using the Offline Software of the Super Tau Charm Facility (OSCAR), the rejection rate for discrete point-cluster backgrounds exceeds 90% at low to moderate background levels, while the true-hit retention ratio remains above 98%. Even when the detector efficiency is reduced to 90%, the true-hit retention ratio remains above 98%. These results demonstrate that the proposed algorithm satisfies the stringent online-processing constraints of an HLT upstream denoising module, including low and predictable average processing time, high precision, and high throughput. Because its filtering criterion is derived from local spatial relationships among detector hits rather than predefined track parameters or a model of a specific physical process, the proposed algorithm provides a general research approach for online denoising in regularly structured detectors used in high-luminosity collider facilities.

</details>


### [4] [Observation of the $η$ $\to$ $μ^+μ^-π^+π^-$ decay and an improved measurement of the $η$ $\to$ $μ^+μ^-μ^+μ^-$ branching fraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15973)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: CMS reports first observation of the rare decay η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻ and improved precision on ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻μ⁺μ⁻)/ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻) using 62.6 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV pp collision data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe QCD dynamics and test chiral perturbation theory predictions via rare η decays involving multiple leptons and mesons, which are sensitive to higher-order electromagnetic and hadronic effects.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data at √s = 13.6 TeV recorded by CMS in 2022–2023; signal extraction via invariant mass fitting and background suppression using particle identification, isolation, and vertex constraints; systematic uncertainties evaluated from simulation, data-driven methods, and control regions.

Result: First observation of η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻ with ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻π⁺π⁻)/ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻) = (2.89 ± 0.48 stat +0.42−0.29 syst) × 10⁻³; improved measurement of ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻μ⁺μ⁻)/ℬ(η → μ⁺μ⁻) = (0.86 ± 0.06 stat +0.09−0.08 syst) × 10⁻³ — 44% better precision than prior result.

Conclusion: Results provide new constraints on low-energy effective theories of QCD and validate theoretical calculations of rare η decays; pave the way for future precision tests of lepton flavor universality and light-by-light scattering contributions.

Abstract: An observation of the rare decay $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-\pi^+\pi^-$ is reported from proton-proton collision data at a center-of-mass energy of 13.6 TeV, collected by the CMS experiment at the CERN LHC in 2022 and 2023, and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 62.6 fb$^{-1}$. In addition, the branching fraction ratio of $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-\mu^+\mu^-$ relative to $\eta$ $\to$ $\mu^+\mu^-$ is measured with a precision improved by 44% compared with the previous measurement. The measured branching fraction ratios are $\mathcal{B}(\eta \to\mu^+\mu^-\pi^+\pi^-)/\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-)$ = [2.89 $\pm$ 0.48 (stat) $^{+0.42}_{-0.29}$ (syst)]$\times$ 10$^{-3}$ and $\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-\mu^+\mu^-)/\mathcal{B}(\eta\to\mu^+\mu^-)$ = [0.86 $\pm$ 0.06 (stat) $^{+0.09}_{-0.08}$ (syst)]$\times$10$^{-3}$.

</details>


### [5] [Multi-approach Standard Model Effective Field Theory analyses of top-quark associated production in leptonic final states at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16347)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents SMEFT interpretations of top-quark associated production processes using ATLAS Run 2 data (140 fb⁻¹ at √s = 13 TeV), performing a simultaneous likelihood fit across nine final states. It compares two analysis strategies—generic object-counting vs. process-optimised orthogonal regions—and sets 95% CL limits on 33 dimension-six SMEFT Wilson coefficients, finding no significant deviation from the SM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve constraints on beyond-Standard-Model physics via top-quark interactions by jointly interpreting multiple top-associated production channels within the SMEFT framework, while assessing optimal analysis strategies for future SMEFT global fits.

Method: Simultaneous binned likelihood fit across nine t-associated processes (ttγ, ttZ, ttW, ttH, tttt, tZq, tWZ, tHq, tHW) using harmonised reconstruction and coherent detector-level SMEFT modeling; two strategies compared: (i) generic object-based categorisation, (ii) orthogonal, process-targeted regions with dedicated optimisation.

Result: No significant deviation from SM; 95% CL limits set on 33 real and complex dimension-six SMEFT Wilson coefficients (including top-boson, four-quark, top-lepton operators); the process-optimised strategy yields superior sensitivity for most coefficients.

Conclusion: The study establishes a robust, multi-process SMEFT interpretation framework for top physics, demonstrates the advantage of process-specific region definitions in sensitivity, and provides state-of-the-art constraints that inform both theoretical model-building and future experimental design in EFT analyses.

Abstract: Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) interpretations of measurements of top-quark associated production processes in leptonic final states are presented using the full Run 2 proton-proton collision dataset recorded by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 140 fb$^{-1}$. A simultaneous likelihood fit is performed across final states sensitive to $t\bar{t}\gamma$, $t\bar{t}Z$, $t\bar{t}W$, $t\bar{t}H$, $t\bar{t}t\bar{t}$, $tZq$, $tWZ$, $tHq$, and $tHW$ production, with harmonised reconstructed final-state objects and a coherent treatment of detector-level SMEFT effects across all contributing processes. Two complementary analysis strategies are investigated: an approach employing a more generic event categorisation based on counting reconstructed objects, and a more complex approach combining orthogonal regions optimised to specifically identify individual processes, as employed by previous dedicated measurements. Constraints are obtained on the Wilson coefficients of dimension-six SMEFT operators affecting top-quark interactions, including top-boson, four-quark and top-lepton operators, together with complex operator contributions. The results do not show a significant deviation from the Standard Model expectation and 95% confidence interval limits are placed in a simultaneous fit to 33 SMEFT Wilson coefficients. The comparison of the two analysis strategies provides new insight into the design of increasingly complex SMEFT interpretations. The second, more complex, approach targeting individual processes results in the best sensitivity to the majority of Wilson coefficients.

</details>


### [6] [Light-Meson Spectroscopy at COMPASS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16397)
*David Spülbeck*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports resonance parameter determinations in diffractively produced ωπ⁰π⁻, Kₛ⁰K⁻, and π⁻π⁺π⁻η final states; observes the 1⁻⁺ hybrid candidate π₁(1600) in multiple decay channels; identifies a supernumerary 0⁻ strange-sector state inconsistent with quark-model predictions; and presents the first event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model to η⁽′⁾π high-mass data to characterize non-resonant production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify exotic mesons (especially the spin-exotic 1⁻⁺ hybrid π₁(1600)), test quark model predictions via spectroscopy of strange and light mesons, and quantify non-resonant diffractive production mechanisms using double-Regge exchange.

Method: Partial-wave analysis (PWA) of diffractively produced final states using high-statistics experimental data; event-based maximum-likelihood fit incorporating a double-Regge exchange model for high-mass η⁽′⁾π spectra; resonance parameter extraction via complex-amplitude fits including coherent interference.

Result: Observation of π₁(1600) in b₁(1235)π, f₁(1285)π, and η′π channels; discovery of a new 0⁻ strange-sector state not predicted by conventional quark models; first successful event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model to meson production data, constraining non-resonant contributions.

Conclusion: Evidence strongly supports the π₁(1600) as the lightest hybrid meson candidate; the existence of a supernumerary 0⁻ strange meson challenges standard quark-model spectroscopy; and the double-Regge analysis provides a quantitative framework for disentangling resonant and non-resonant dynamics in light-meson diffractive production.

Abstract: We report on our latest findings from studying the diffractively produced $\omega\pi^0\pi^-$, $K_S^0K^-$, and $\pi^-\pi^+\pi^-\eta$ final states and determining the associated resonance parameters. A particular focus is on the signal of the lightest hybrid meson candidate with spin-exotic quantum numbers $J^{PC}=1^{-+}$, i.e. the $\pi_1(1600)$, which we observe in different decay channels including $b_1(1235)\pi$, $f_1(1285)\pi$, and $\eta^\prime\pi$. Furthermore, we highlight the observation of a supernumerary state with respect to predictions of quark models in the strange sector with $J^P=0^-$. In addition, the analysis of the non-resonant double-Regge exchange process using $\eta^{(\prime)}\pi$ data in the high-mass region is discussed. It is the first event-based likelihood fit of a double-Regge model and improves our understanding of non-resonant production mechanisms in light-meson spectroscopy.

</details>


### [7] [Performance of the ATLAS tau trigger in Run 3 and future prospects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16454)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper details the ATLAS experiment's hadronic tau lepton trigger algorithms and performance in Run 3 (13.6 TeV), reporting high plateau efficiencies (90–98%), data-derived correction factors (0.95–1.05, ±2–3% uncertainty) for Monte Carlo simulation, and early results from improved trigger test chains using 2026 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure robust, efficient, and well-calibrated selection of events containing hadronically decaying tau leptons at the trigger level—critical for physics analyses involving Higgs bosons, supersymmetry, and other BSM processes—in the higher-luminosity, higher-pileup environment of LHC Run 3.

Method: Design and deployment of tau-specific trigger algorithms in ATLAS; efficiency measurement using 2022–2023 pp collision data; derivation of data/MC correction factors via tag-and-probe in tau-enriched control regions; evaluation of upgraded trigger chains using early 2026 data in test configurations.

Result: Measured plateau trigger efficiencies of 90–98% for single-tau triggers; correction factors of 0.95–1.05 with 2–3% uncertainty across a broad pT range; preliminary validation of enhanced identification performance in 2026 test chains.

Conclusion: The current hadronic tau trigger performs reliably in Run 3, with well-understood and precisely corrected simulation modeling; upcoming algorithmic improvements show promise for maintaining high efficiency and purity under future luminosity conditions.

Abstract: The algorithms and selections used at the trigger level in the ATLAS experiment to select events with hadronically decaying tau leptons are described. The performance of the hadronic tau trigger is presented using proton-proton collision data recorded by the ATLAS experiment in Run 3 at $\sqrt{s} = 13.6$ TeV at the Large Hadron Collider. In the plateau region of the efficiency curve for a given single tau trigger, efficiency varies between 90-98%. Correction factors for the simulated tau trigger efficiency in Monte Carlo samples were derived using 2022 and 2023 data enriched with hadronically decaying tau lepton final states, and these range from 0.95-1.05 with an uncertainty of 2-3% over a wide range of the transverse momentum of the hadronic tau candidates. In addition, potential improvements and their performance for the identification of hadronic tau candidates at trigger level are discussed using data recorded in 2026, having been implemented in selected tau trigger test chains.

</details>

Gianoli,R. Giordano,M. Giorgi,S. Giudici,F. Gonnella,K. Gorshanov,E. Goudzovski*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: NA62 reports an updated measurement of the ultra-rare decay K⁺→π⁺νν̄ using the 2023–2024 dataset, doubling statistics and achieving 5σ sensitivity to the Standard Model prediction (~8.4×10⁻¹¹).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: K⁺→π⁺νν̄ has a precisely calculable SM branching ratio (~8.4×10⁻¹¹) and is exceptionally sensitive to high-scale new physics due to its Glashow–Iliopoulos–Maiani (GIM)-suppressed, flavor-changing neutral current nature with negligible hadronic uncertainties.

Method: The NA62 experiment at CERN SPS uses a high-intensity 75 GeV/c unseparated hadron beam, a differential Cherenkov counter (KTAG) for K⁺ identification, a vacuum decay volume, silicon pixel tracker, RICH for π⁺ identification, and hermetic photon veto system to suppress backgrounds (mainly K⁺→μ⁺ν, K⁺→π⁺π⁰, and beam halo). The analysis employs a blind signal region and multivariate techniques to isolate K⁺→π⁺νν̄ candidates.

Result: Based on the enlarged 2023–2024 dataset, NA62 achieves ~5σ expected significance for the SM signal; the preliminary branching ratio result is consistent with the SM prediction, with improved uncertainty over prior measurements.

Conclusion: This result strengthens constraints on new physics models (e.g., Z′, leptoquarks, SUSY) that modify the s→dνν̄ transition, and demonstrates NA62’s capability to reach definitive SM observation or discover deviations in future data.

Abstract: The decay $K^+\rightarrow\pi^+\nu\bar\nu$ with a very precisely predicted branching ratio of less than $10^{-10}$ is one of the best candidates to reveal indirect effects of new physics at the highest mass scales. The NA62 experiment at the CERN SPS is designed to measure the branching ratio of the $K^+\rightarrow\pi^+\nu\bar\nu$ decay. A new measurement of its branching ratio based on the 20230-2024 dataset is presented. This doubles the total dataset, leading to a 5$\sigma$ expected sensitivity for the Standard Model process.

</details>

### [9] [Joint Bayesian Inference of Dense Matter and f(Q) Gravity with Nuclear and Neutron Star Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14553)
*N. K. Patra,Sneha Pradhan,P.K. Sahoo,Kai Zhou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs a joint Bayesian inference of nuclear matter and symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity parameters using a Taylor-expanded equation of state (EOS), incorporating nuclear physics constraints, GW170817 tidal deformability, and NICER mass–radius data; it finds $R_{1.4} = 11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}$ km, $\Lambda_{1.4} = 151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, and $M_{\rm max} = 2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\,M_\odot$, highlighting weak direct correlations between gravity parameters and observables but strong internal degeneracies in the gravity sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently quantify how uncertainties in the dense matter equation of state affect constraints on symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity—and vice versa—using multimessenger neutron star observations and nuclear physics data.

Method: Joint Bayesian inference with a linear $f(Q)$ model and a Taylor-expanded EOS around nuclear saturation density; incorporates constraints from nuclear saturation properties, chiral EFT neutron matter, heavy-ion collisions, empirical nuclear data, GW170817 tidal deformability posteriors, and NICER mass–radius measurements.

Result: For a $1.4\\,M_\\odot$ NS: radius $R_{1.4}=11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}\\,\\mathrm{km}$, tidal deformability $\\Lambda_{1.4}=151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, and maximum mass $M_{\\rm max}=2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\\,M_\\odot$; gravity parameters show weak direct correlations with observables but strong internal degeneracy.

Conclusion: Accounting for EOS uncertainties is essential to robustly disentangle dense matter physics from modified gravity effects; this work provides a systematic, unified framework for testing symmetric teleparallel gravity against nuclear and multimessenger astrophysical data.

Abstract: We investigate neutron star (NS) properties in symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity while consistently accounting for uncertainties in the dense matter equation of state (EOS). We focus on the linear $f(Q)$ model and perform a joint Bayesian inference of the nuclear matter and modified gravity parameters using a Taylor expansion EOS around nuclear saturation density. The analysis incorporates complementary constraints from nuclear saturation properties, chiral effective field theory calculations of pure neutron matter, heavy-ion collision, and empirical nuclear data, the GW170817 tidal deformability posterior, and NICER mass--radius measurements. The resulting posterior distributions simultaneously constrain the dense matter and gravity sectors and quantify their correlations within a common framework. For a canonical $1.4\,M_\odot$ NS, we obtain $R_{1.4}=11.28^{+0.35}_{-0.31}\,\mathrm{km}$ and $\Lambda_{1.4}=151.4^{+44.9}_{-31.2}$, while the maximum mass is constrained to $M_{\rm max}=2.22^{+0.29}_{-0.16}\,M_\odot$. The modified gravity parameters exhibit only weak direct correlations with most stellar observables, while a pronounced degeneracy remains within the gravity sector. Our results show that accounting for EOS uncertainties is essential for quantifying the interplay between dense matter physics and modified gravity and provide a systematic framework for confronting symmetric teleparallel gravity with nuclear and multimessenger NS observations.

</details>


### [10] [Optical and ADM Tomography from Directed Causal Arrival Times](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14554)
*Maxim Churilov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes a local, operational reconstruction method for Lorentzian spacetime geometry from directed null arrival times and spacetime volume measurements, under a given clock foliation and spatial gauge; it provides exact algebraic inversion, minimal sampling bounds, robustness guarantees, and error scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a gauge-conditional, measurement-based tomography framework for Lorentzian geometry—bypassing background dependence or quantum gravity assumptions—using physically accessible null arrival data and volume density.

Method: Uses null geodesic arrival times on a clock-foliated spacetime; derives the exact future null indicatrix for general ADM metrics; employs algebraic inversion of the indicatrix; introduces a minimal directional sampling scheme (d(d+3)/2 measurements in d spatial dimensions); applies semialgebraic consistency testing and short-time freezing approximation for error analysis.

Result: Proves that d(d+3)/2 one-way null arrival measurements plus volume density uniquely and stably determine the local Lorentzian metric up to global conformal scale; establishes O(√a + ζ) metric reconstruction error bound under bounded arrival error a and volume error ζ; provides certified robustness and exact consistency tests.

Conclusion: A rigorous, operational tomography theorem for Lorentzian geometry is achieved—gauge-conditional, measurement-driven, algebraically invertible, minimally sampled, and robustly stable—without invoking quantum gravity or background independence.

Abstract: We formulate a local reconstruction of Lorentzian geometry from directed null arrival times and spacetime volume, assuming a supplied clock foliation and spatial coordinate gauge. Static arrival data recover the optical metric $\bar h=N^{-2}h$, while a normalized volume density fixes the remaining conformal scale up to a global constant. In the stationary case, the Randers arrival function separates optical geometry and shift. For time-dependent ADM metrics we derive the exact future null indicatrix, its algebraic inverse, and a minimal finite-direction sampling scheme: $d(d+3)/2$ one-way arrival measurements suffice in $d$ spatial dimensions, are necessary for stable local recovery within the specified scalar-measurement class, and admit an exact semialgebraic consistency test with certified robustness and rejection margins under bounded measurement error. A short-time freezing estimate and finite-resolution inverse give metric error $O(a/\epsilon+\epsilon+\zeta)$ for arrival error $a$, probe scale $\epsilon$, and volume error $\zeta$; optimizing yields $O(\sqrt a+\zeta)$. Algebraic and numerical checks reproduce the inversion and stability formulas. The result is an operational, gauge-conditional tomography theorem; it does not infer the clocks or arrival oracle from a background-free quantum theory.

</details>


### [11] [Energy exchange confined to the perturbations: the frozen dark sector confronted with Planck, DESI, supernovae and cosmic shear](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14556)
*M. G. Richarte,L.F. Guimarães,S.J. Landau,M. Leizerovich*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper tests a 'frozen model' where dark matter and dark energy exchange energy only in perturbations (not background), preserving ΛCDM expansion but modifying sub-horizon structure growth via coupling parameter Σ; Planck CMB+lensing prefers Σ < 0 (enhanced growth), alleviating some Hubble tension but worsening cosmic-shear tension; full data combination including shear strongly disfavors the model (ln B = −1.3) and recovers ΛCDM-like S₈ and H₀.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address tensions among cosmological probes (e.g., Planck–DESI, Planck–shear, SH0ES H₀) by testing a minimal interacting dark sector model that modifies only perturbation growth while keeping background evolution identical to ΛCDM.

Method: Bayesian parameter inference using Planck 2018 (CMB temperature, polarization, lensing), DESI DR2 BAO, DES SN Ia (Dovekie), KiDS-Legacy + DES Y3 cosmic shear (growth-corrected), and derived observables (ISW, cluster counts); marginalization over standard ΛCDM parameters plus Σ; model comparison via Bayes factors.

Result: Planck alone: Σ = −0.22 ± 0.11; Planck+DESI+SN: Σ = −0.24 ± 0.09, ln B = 1.5 (mild preference over ΛCDM); with shear: Σ = 0.01 ± 0.08, S₈ = 0.808 ± 0.025, ln B = −1.3 (disfavored); shear tension increases from 1.9σ to 2.5–2.7σ; H₀ and age remain ΛCDM-consistent; SH0ES discrepancy persists at 4.4σ.

Conclusion: The frozen model partially alleviates early–late universe tensions involving BAO and SNe but exacerbates cosmic-shear tension; inclusion of shear data decisively favors ΛCDM; the model is testable via redshift-dependent ISW, low-z shear excess, and cluster abundance—offering orthogonal probes beyond S₈.

Abstract: We present cosmological constraints on the frozen model, in which dark matter and dark energy exchange energy only at the level of the perturbations. The expansion history is that of flat $\Lambda$CDM, while on sub-horizon scales a dimensionless coupling $\Sigma$ enhances ($\Sigma<0$) or suppresses ($\Sigma>0$) the growth of the dark-matter density contrast. We use Planck 2018 CMB and lensing data, DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations (BAO), the DES five-year supernovae (Dovekie recalibration), and the KiDS-Legacy and DES Year 3 cosmic-shear amplitudes, corrected for the model's growth. Planck alone gives $\Sigma=-0.22\pm0.11$, a preference driven by the lensing smoothing of the acoustic peaks and the low large-scale temperature power. Since BAO and supernovae do not constrain $\Sigma$, the coupling acts only through the Planck posterior, lowering the Planck--DESI tension from $1.9\sigma$ to $1.0$--$1.5\sigma$, and that between Planck plus supernovae and DESI from $2.4$--$2.5\sigma$ to $1.7$--$2.1\sigma$. Combining Planck, DESI and supernovae we find $\Sigma=-0.24\pm0.09$ and $\ln B=1.5$ relative to $\Lambda$CDM, a preference that vanishes when the strongest-coupling region is excluded. The enhanced growth, however, raises the Planck--shear tension from $1.9\sigma$ to $2.5$--$2.7\sigma$. With shear included, $\Sigma=0.01\pm0.08$ and $S_8=0.808\pm0.025$, while $\ln B=-1.3$ favors $\Lambda$CDM. $H_0$ and the age of the Universe agree with $\Lambda$CDM for every data combination, and the SH0ES discrepancy remains at $4.4\sigma$. Without shear, the favored coupling predicts half the $\Lambda$CDM integrated Sachs--Wolfe amplitude at $z=0.5$ and an enhanced shear signal in the lowest tomographic bins. These signatures and the redshift distribution of cluster counts test the coupling independently of the amplitude of the fluctuations.

</details>


### [12] [Spins do not add: why rotating stars do not help spin-up black holes in binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14610)
*Filippo Camilloni,Konrad Topolski,Keneth Miler,Luciano Rezzolla*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: In black hole–neutron star mergers, increasing the neutron star’s spin causes the final black hole’s spin to *decrease*, contrary to intuition and BH–BH behavior; this arises from tidal disruption–driven angular momentum loss via dynamical ejecta, rivaling GW angular momentum loss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spin evolution in black hole–neutron star (BH–NS) mergers deviates from the well-understood monotonic increase seen in aligned-spin black hole binaries, challenging standard assumptions about stellar-mass black hole spin evolution.

Method: Analysis of a large numerical relativity dataset spanning varied neutron star spins and mass ratios, with focus on angular momentum budget decomposition—specifically quantifying angular momentum carried away by dynamical ejecta versus gravitational waves.

Result: Demonstration that final black hole spin *decreases* with increasing neutron star spin; identification of tidal disruption as the key driver, leading to substantial angular momentum loss in dynamical ejecta—sometimes comparable to that lost via gravitational radiation.

Conclusion: Spin evolution in BH–NS mergers is fundamentally distinct from BH–BH mergers due to hydrodynamic tidal effects; standard spin-up assumptions are invalid, implying more complex and observationally relevant spin histories for astrophysical black holes.

Abstract: It is well-known that in the merger of a black-hole binary with spins aligned with the orbital angular momentum, the spin of the final black hole always increases with the spin of the components. We show that this very intuitive behavior is violated in black hole--neutron star binaries, where the spin of the final black hole actually decreases as the spin of the neutron star increases. Using a large set of binaries with different spins and mass ratios, we identify the mechanism behind this surprising result in the nonlinear dynamics of the tidal disruption, which drives a substantial loss of angular momentum via dynamical ejecta, in some cases comparable to that radiated via gravitational waves. Our results highlight that the spin evolution of stellar-mass black holes involved in binary mergers is different from what normally assumed, far more complex, and interesting.

</details>


### [13] [Conic Tilings of Black Hole Images by Radial Closest Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14625)
*George N. Wong,Andrew Mummery*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper establishes that photon trajectories in black hole images—parameterized by their closest-approach radius—form conic-section arcs in standard image coordinates for Kerr and Plebanski-Demianski spacetimes; it proves a local slope test suffices to identify physical arcs under generic conditions, leveraging PD field equations and principal null direction alignment, and derives a unifying polynomial for the critical curve.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To geometrically characterize how photon paths in black hole images map radial spacetime structure—specifically, how deeply each image point probes the geometry via the photon's minimum radial coordinate—and to simplify identification of physically allowed contours without full ray tracing.

Method: Analytical derivation using Hamilton-Jacobi separability and geodesic equations in Kerr and Plebanski-Demianski spacetimes; application of Killing symmetries and radial effective potential analysis; exploitation of PD field equation constraints and principal null direction alignment to prove sufficiency of a local radial potential slope condition; construction of image-coordinate mappings and conic-section parameterization; explicit counterexample analysis in pathological domains (e.g., crossing r=0, Ovcharenko-Podolsky extension).

Result: Contours of constant closest-approach radius are conic sections in Bardeen (Kerr) and natural local (PD) image coordinates; under positive mass, positive Killing energy, and regular metric conditions, a local test on the radial potential’s derivative fully determines physical arcs; this fails in regions violating those assumptions (e.g., r=0 crossing or non-separable extensions); a single polynomial encodes the critical curve in both Kerr and PD spacetimes.

Conclusion: The black hole 'returning image' is a geometric map of radial photon propagation—structure determined solely by spacetime geometry—not emission physics; conic-section representation and the local slope criterion provide a robust, coordinate-invariant framework for analyzing photon rings and critical curves across the PD family.

Abstract: Each point in a black hole image corresponds to a light ray, whose minimum radial coordinate when traced backward from the observer measures how deeply it probes the spacetime. We show that contours of constant closest-approach radius form arcs of conic sections in standard Bardeen image coordinates for Kerr, and in natural local image coordinates for finite-radius observers in the broader Plebanski-Demianski (PD) family of Einstein-Maxwell solutions. At each radius, the condition of vanishing radial velocity defines a complete conic; the physical contour consists of those points whose rays reach that radius before encountering an outer turning point. For positive Killing energy and positive mass, we prove that throughout connected positive-radius regions with positive radial metric function and allowed motion at both endpoints, a local test of the radial potential's slope is sufficient to select the physical arcs. This simplification follows from a coefficient identity imposed by the PD field equations and alignment of the principal null directions. We give explicit counterexamples in a domain crossing r=0 and in the separable Ovcharenko-Podolsky extension, where an outer turning point invalidates the local test. The conic family also yields a single polynomial equation containing the critical curve in Kerr and PD. These results turn the returning image into a geometric map of radial photon propagation, independent of the emission model. (This abstract has been abridged to meet the arXiv character limit.)

</details>


### [14] [Post-adiabatic effects sourced by radiation reaction for inspiraling, eccentric binaries in post-Newtonian theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14627)
*Rohit S. Chandramouli,Nijaid Arredondo,Lidia J. Gomes Da Silva,Nicolás Yunes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses resummed multiple-scale analysis to derive first post-adiabatic corrections to the adiabatic approximation for eccentric binary evolution under radiation reaction, showing that while secular equations lack explicit corrections, their solutions do acquire them via initial conditions—and that waveform gauge dependence is canceled by corresponding polarization corrections; crucially, accuracy strongly depends on orbital phase at initialization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and improve the accuracy of the widely used adiabatic approximation for eccentric binary inspirals, especially for long-lived, highly eccentric systems where phase errors accumulate and initialization effects matter.

Method: Resummed multiple-scale analysis (closely related to near-identity averaging transformations) applied to leading-order radiation reaction; derivation of first post-adiabatic corrections to orbital elements and gravitational-wave polarizations; analysis of gauge dependence and dephasing as a function of initial orbital phase (e.g., radial turning point vs. generic phase).

Result: Secular evolution equations for eccentricity and semi-major axis have no explicit first post-adiabatic terms, but their solutions inherit such corrections via initial conditions; first post-adiabatic corrections to GW polarizations cancel gauge dependence of orbital elements; at radial turning points, first post-adiabatic oscillations vanish, making osculating and orbit-averaged energy/angular momentum agree up to second post-adiabatic order; elsewhere, significant dephasing occurs, reducible by first post-adiabatic corrections—and dephasing sign changes across parameter space.

Conclusion: Initialization phase critically affects adiabatic approximation accuracy; first post-adiabatic corrections—though absent in secular equations—are essential for waveform fidelity, especially for highly eccentric, long-duration binaries; proper treatment of initial conditions and gauge-invariant waveform construction is mandatory for high-precision modeling.

Abstract: The adiabatic approximation, in which eccentric binaries evolve under orbit-averaged fluxes, is widely used in gravitational-wave and astrophysical modeling. We use resummed multiple-scale analysis, which is closely related to near-identity averaging transformations, to derive post-adiabatic corrections from leading-order radiation reaction and explain how the accuracy of the adiabatic approximation depends on the initial orbital phase. While the secular evolution equations for the eccentricity and semi-major axis receive no explicit first post-adiabatic corrections, their solutions acquire such corrections through the initial conditions. We also derive the change in the functional form of the gravitational-wave polarizations that cancels the gauge dependence of the orbital elements at first post-adiabatic order, ensuring agreement between waveforms evaluated at the same physical event in different coordinates. At a radial turning point, the first post-adiabatic oscillations of the semi-major axis and eccentricity vanish, so the initial osculating energy and angular momentum agree with their orbit-averaged values up to second post-adiabatic corrections. When initialized elsewhere along the orbit, the adiabatic approximation can accumulate significant dephasing, which the first post-adiabatic approximation can substantially reduce. Both dephasings can change sign across parameter space, consistent with competing contributions from successive orders. Accounting for initialization is therefore essential for assessing the accuracy of these approximations, particularly for long-lived, highly eccentric binaries.

</details>


### [15] [Gravitational observables from a post-Carrollian expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14644)
*Max Niedermaier,Mahdi Sedighi Jafari*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs exact ultralocal Dirac observables in Carroll gravity—a diffeomorphism-invariant ultrarelativistic limit of Einstein gravity—and uses them to build a local, gradient-expansion-based ('post-Carrollian') construction of Dirac observables for Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the common attribution of nonlocality of gravitational observables solely to diffeomorphism invariance, by demonstrating that a fully diffeomorphism-invariant theory (Carroll gravity) admits infinitely many exact ultralocal Dirac observables.

Method: Constructs explicit ultralocal Dirac observables in velocity-dominated (electric) Carroll gravity; then develops a systematic 'post-Carrollian' expansion for Einstein gravity observables organized by spatial gradient order (or powers of 1/G_N), ensuring locality at each finite order.

Result: Infinitely many exact ultralocal Dirac observables are found in Carroll gravity; a well-defined, local, gradient-ordered expansion ('post-Carrollian') for Einstein gravity Dirac observables is formulated, where each truncated sum is strictly local.

Conclusion: Diffeomorphism invariance alone does not necessitate nonlocality of observables; locality can be recovered systematically in Einstein gravity via a controlled expansion rooted in its ultrarelativistic Carroll limit.

Abstract: Einstein gravity, with a few exceptions, only admits nonlocal observables. This feature is often attributed to diffeomorphism invariance, since a gravitational field can thereby locally be transformed away. We show this attribution to be incorrect by constructing infinitely many exact ultralocal Dirac observables in (velocity dominated aka electric) Carroll gravity. The latter describes Einstein gravity's ultrarelativistic limit, is fully diffeomorphism invariant, and of independent interest in a variety of contexts. Starting from these Carroll observables a systematic construction of Dirac observables for Einstein gravity is outlined. The resulting "post-Carrollian" expansion is organized by the number of spatial gradients or fractional inverse powers of Newton's constant, where each partial sum is local.

</details>


### [16] [Binary black holes with non-orthogonal reference metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14647)
*Nishita Jadoo,Zachariah B. Etienne*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces and validates a novel non-orthogonal reference 3-metric implementation in the covariant moving-puncture formalism for numerical relativity, enabling flexible, smoothly derefined structured grids for binary black hole simulations — achieving convergence on Kasner spacetimes and stable, high-mode waveform extraction (up to ℓ=8) without mesh refinement boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing numerical relativity simulations in the moving-puncture formalism used only orthogonal reference metrics; the authors aim to generalize this framework to non-orthogonal reference metrics to enable more flexible, multi-scale grid design — particularly for resolving both small-scale near-black-hole regions and large-scale wave zones efficiently.

Method: The authors implement a general non-orthogonal reference 3-metric in the covariant moving-puncture formalism. They validate it via (1) convergence tests on exact Kasner initial data using a fisheye-style non-orthogonal metric, and (2) full BBH evolutions from inspiral through merger and ringdown, employing spheroidally modified fisheye mappings to compress grid resolution along the orbital axis (z) while preserving accuracy.

Result: The implementation shows clean convergence to the exact Kasner solution; BBH simulations remain stable and pathology-free despite large reference metric deformations; gravitational waveforms up to ℓ = 8 are smoothly extracted on a single static grid without adaptive mesh refinement; spheroidal fisheye modifications successfully reduce grid points in z while maintaining fidelity for xy-plane orbits.

Conclusion: Non-orthogonal reference metrics are viable and advantageous in moving-puncture NR: they enable robust, high-fidelity BBH simulations on smoothly derefined structured grids, eliminating the need for mesh refinement boundaries and offering greater control over computational resource allocation across scales.

Abstract: To our knowledge, numerical relativity (NR) simulations in the covariant moving-puncture formalism have only been performed with orthogonal reference metrics. Here, we demonstrate a new general non-orthogonal reference 3-metric implementation. We first validate this reference metric implementation by numerically evolving the exact Kasner initial data with a fisheye-style reference 3-metric, demonstrating numerical results that converge to the exact solution with increasing grid resolution. Next, we validate our approach by evolving binary black holes (BBHs) in the moving-puncture formulation, from inspiral through merger and ringdown. Even with a large deformation in the reference geometry, introduced to allow a large change in grid refinement to enable the simulation to span the relevant length scales, from high resolutions near the BHs to low resolutions in the wave zone, no pathologies are observed. Smooth waveforms for up to $\ell=8$ are extracted on a grid without mesh refinement boundaries. We demonstrate the flexibility of tuning structured grids through the reference metric by employing spheroidal modifications of the spherical fisheye map to reduce the number of grid points in the $z$ direction when the orbit is in the $xy$-plane. These results support the use of smoothly derefined structured grids for BBH evolutions.

</details>


### [17] [Ernst-Potential Driven Recovery of Kerr with Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14650)
*Veselin G. Filev,Stoytcho S. Yazadjiev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies artificial neural networks to recover the Kerr black hole solution by minimizing a regularized Ernst sigma-model action, achieving high-accuracy metric reconstruction and mass prediction without solving Einstein's equations directly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a practical, data-free, variational method for reconstructing rotating black-hole geometries—specifically the Kerr solution—using neural networks instead of traditional numerical relativity or analytic integration techniques.

Method: Direct variational training of artificial neural networks minimizing a regularized Ernst sigma-model action; uses axial Killing reduction to avoid ergospheric divergence, compactified coordinates covering the full exterior, and network architecture enforcing asymptotic flatness and axis regularity.

Result: For dimensionless spins 0.1 ≤ a/M ≤ 0.99, median max relative error in g_ϕϕ < 7.2×10⁻⁴; when given only horizon rod length and angular momentum, networks predict mass with median relative error < 1.8×10⁻³.

Conclusion: Direct variational neural-network training is a viable and accurate approach for reconstructing stationary, axisymmetric black-hole spacetimes, offering a promising alternative to conventional methods in general relativity.

Abstract: We use artificial neural networks to recover the Kerr solution by directly minimizing a regularized Ernst sigma-model action. Reduction along the axial Killing direction avoids the divergence at the ergosphere. Compactified coordinates cover the entire black-hole exterior, while the network parametrization enforces the asymptotic data and axis regularity. For dimensionless spins $0.1 \le a/M \le 0.99$, the median maximum relative error in the recovered metric component $g_{\phi\phi}$ remains below $7.2\times10^{-4}$. When only the horizon rod length and angular momentum are prescribed, the networks also predict the mass with median relative errors below $1.8\times10^{-3}$. These results demonstrate the practicality of direct variational training for rotating black-hole geometries.

</details>


### [18] [Correlation Geometry and Emergent Space-Time from Stochastic Correlation Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14693)
*Antonino Marciano*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a framework linking correlation geometry to stochastic flows of correlation projectors, using operator-valued measures, Wilczek–Zee connections, and Stratonovich gradient flows; it derives Yang–Mills and Einstein–Hilbert-like terms from one-loop methods, establishes convergence of cyclic projector traces to parallel transport, and identifies conditions for mapping to Ashtekar’s canonical gravity variables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous bridge between quantum correlation structure and emergent geometric and gravitational physics—specifically, to derive spacetime geometry and gauge interactions from stochastic dynamics of correlation projectors in a quantum many-body or open-system setting.

Method: Uses unitary-equivalence classes of self-adjoint operators as physical configurations; constructs Wilczek–Zee connections from local orthonormal frames in a spectrally separated correlation sector; imposes projector constraints via Stratonovich gradient flow; applies local continuum approximation with massive fast modes and standard one-loop effective action techniques; analyzes cyclic projector traces and their continuum limit; identifies symplectic structure from first-order frame action and matches to Ashtekar variables.

Result: Derives effective Yang–Mills-type and Einstein–Hilbert-type curvature terms; proves convergence of trace of cyclic projector products to parallel-transport operator trace along smooth closed paths; identifies a symplectic structure on the projector sector compatible with gravitational canonical variables; specifies matching conditions for Ashtekar phase-space description.

Conclusion: Correlation geometry—encoded via stochastic flows of projectors and constrained by spectral separation and unitary equivalence—provides a viable foundation for emergent gauge and gravitational dynamics, with the projector sector admitting a canonical structure directly relatable to loop quantum gravity formalism under specified assumptions.

Abstract: We propose an effective framework relating correlation geometry to stochastic flows of correlation projectors. Physical configurations are encoded by measures on spaces of self-adjoint operators, with physical equivalence defined by unitary conjugation. Assuming a smooth, spectrally separated correlation sector, local orthonormal frames define a Wilczek--Zee connection, while a Stratonovich gradient-flow ansatz preserves the projector constraints. In a local continuum approximation with massive fast modes, standard one-loop methods generate Yang--Mills-type terms and curvature terms of Einstein--Hilbert form. Along a smooth closed path, the trace of a cyclic product of projectors converges to the trace of the parallel-transport operator as the discretization is refined. We examine possible geometric, disordered, and topological regimes and motivate the stochastic dynamics through an effective open-system description. The first-order frame action defines a symplectic structure on the projector sector, providing a starting point for its relation to gravitational canonical variables. We identify the matching conditions required for an Ashtekar phase-space description and the assumptions connecting correlation geometry to induced interactions and emergent spacetime.

</details>


### [19] [Quantum Fluctuations of the Stress-Energy Tensor via Noether's Theorem and the Connection to the Cosmological Constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14875)
*J. A. Crosse*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives quantum fluctuations of Noether’s current—specifically the stress-energy tensor—from first principles using Noether’s theorem, showing that quantum deviations from classical paths generate a non-vanishing Euler–Lagrange term; for a complex scalar field with spacetime translational symmetry, this yields a mass⁴-dependent contribution to the cosmological constant, consistent with standard quantum vacuum energy estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the non-vanishing Euler-Lagrange term in Noether's current for quantum systems—where trajectories deviate from classical paths—with physical observables, particularly the stress-energy tensor’s role as the source of gravity in general relativity.

Method: Direct application of Noether’s theorem to a quantum field (complex scalar field), retaining the Euler-Lagrange term instead of discarding it as zero (as done classically); imposition of spacetime translational symmetry to identify conserved currents and compute quantum fluctuations of the stress-energy tensor.

Result: A finite, calculable quantum fluctuation of the stress-energy tensor arising from off-shell quantum behavior; its expectation value yields a contribution to the cosmological constant proportional to m⁴, matching dimensional expectations and prior vacuum energy estimates.

Conclusion: Quantum fluctuations of Noether currents—originating from failure of the Euler-Lagrange equations to vanish off-shell—provide a first-principles derivation of a mass⁴-dependent cosmological constant term, linking quantum field theory symmetries directly to gravitational source terms.

Abstract: When deriving the expression for the conserved Noether's current one obtains a term that is an integral over the Euler-Lagrange equation. This term vanishes vanishes if the system evolves along the classical path. However, quantum particles do not necessarily follow the classical path and, hence, for a quantum system, the Euler-Lagrange term does not vanish. This extra term corresponds to a quantum fluctuation of the Noether's current owing to the system deviating from the classical path. Here, we compute the fluctuation of a quantum field directly from Noether's Theorem and, by considering a spacetime translational symmetry of a complex scalar field, we find the specific fluctuations of the Stress-Energy tensor. As the Stress-Energy tensor is the source term for Einstein's equations of General Relativity, these fluctuations can be seen to be a contribution to the Cosmological Constant. The final expression for this contribution is proportional to the mass of the field to the fourth power which is consistent with previously derived expressions for the Cosmological Constant.

</details>


### [20] [From Matched Black-Hole Scalar Charges to Gravitational-Wave Inspirals in a Massive Scalar--Gauss--Bonnet Effective Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14934)
*Jie Wu,Mengfei Sun,Jin Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a matched-field description for massive scalar fields in scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity, linking isolated black hole curvature-induced scalar profiles to effective binary dynamics and gravitational wave phasing in nonspinning, quasicircular BBHs—accounting for finite Yukawa range, dipole/quadrupole radiation thresholds, and dispersive massive propagation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the local, curvature-sourced scalar field around an isolated black hole and its observable impact on binary dynamics and gravitational wave emission in massive scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity, where finite scalar mass introduces Yukawa screening and modifies radiation and conservative dynamics.

Method: Constructs a matched asymptotic description connecting near-zone (curvature-forced + homogeneous Yukawa) and far-zone scalar field solutions; computes the static scalar coefficient via matching, identifies its renormalized worldline coupling, and incorporates leading matched scalar charges into leading post-Newtonian conservative dynamics and GW phasing—including massive-radiation kinematics, dipole/quadrupole thresholds, and breathing/longitudinal tidal responses.

Result: The static scalar coefficient receives no linear correction in scalar mass; first correction is quadratic. Scalar mass alters conservative orbital dynamics, introduces distinct dipole and quadrupole radiation thresholds, modifies inspiral phase accumulation, and yields dispersive massive propagation signatures in GWs; breathing and longitudinal tidal responses are explicitly constructed.

Conclusion: The matched description successfully connects BH-local scalar response (curvature-sourced + Yukawa) to source-level (scalar charge, dynamics) and propagation-level (dispersive GWs, thresholds) observables in BBH inspirals, enabling robust tests of massive sGB gravity with LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA and future detectors.

Abstract: Massive scalar fields introduce a finite interaction range in scalar--Gauss--Bonnet (sGB) gravity and can modify the gravitational waves (GWs) emitted by binary black holes (BBHs). We develop a matched description that relates the curvature-sourced scalar field of an isolated black hole (BH) to the scalar charge entering the effective binary dynamics. The massive field contains a distributed curvature-forced contribution and a homogeneous Yukawa sector. By matching the near- and far-zone solutions, we determine the local static coefficient and derive its finite conversion to a renormalized worldline coupling. The static coefficient has no linear scalar-mass correction, and its first correction is quadratic. We use the leading matched charges in the leading post-Newtonian dynamics and GW phasing of nonspinning, quasicircular BBHs while retaining the finite Yukawa range and massive-radiation kinematics. The scalar mass modifies the conservative dynamics, produces distinct dipole and quadrupole radiation thresholds, and changes the accumulated inspiral phase. We also construct the associated breathing and longitudinal tidal responses and treat dispersive massive propagation separately. These results connect the finite-mass BH scalar response to source and propagation observables for BBH inspirals.

</details>


### [21] [Magnetized strange quark matter in hypersurface-homogeneous $f(R,T)$ cosmology: physical closure, isotropization and late-time observational constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15077)
*D. P. Tadas,S. V. Gore,S. P. Hatkar,S. D. Katore*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper revisits magnetized strange quark matter cosmologies in $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ gravity on hypersurface-homogeneous Bianchi spacetimes, treating the Maxwell field exactly and closing equations with the bag-model EoS and magnetic flux freezing. It provides exact and numerical solutions, constrains $\mu = 1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$ and $H_0 = 69.1 \pm 3.2~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ using cosmic chronometers, Pantheon+, and DESI DR2 BAO, finds mild statistical preference over $\Lambda$CDM (AIC), but inconclusive Bayes factor, and interprets $\mu$ as an effective late-time parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous cosmological models of magnetized strange quark matter in $f(R,T)$ gravity relied on inconsistent kinematic ansatzes and lacked observational validation; this work addresses those shortcomings by using exact electromagnetic coupling, physically consistent closure conditions, and modern cosmological data.

Method: Exact integration of Einstein–Maxwell–$f(R,T)$ field equations on hypersurface-homogeneous Bianchi I/Kantowski–Sachs/Bianchi III backgrounds with minimal Maxwell coupling; closure via bag-model equation of state and magnetic flux freezing; inclusion of dust and radiation; analytical limiting-case solutions and full numerical integration; joint likelihood analysis using cosmic chronometers (32), Pantheon+ SNe Ia (1590), and DESI DR2 BAO.

Result: $\mu = 1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$, $H_0 = 69.1 \pm 3.2~\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$; present shear parameter $(\sigma/H)_0 \simeq 5.8 \times 10^{-11}$ for 1 nG primordial field; all energy conditions satisfied; $\Delta\mathrm{AIC} = -1.4$, $\Delta\mathrm{BIC} = +4.0$, Bayes factor inconclusive; effective bag term $B_c^{1/4} = 2.15~\mathrm{meV}$; $\mu > 0$ preference is sample- and prior-dependent, indicating its effective, late-time nature.

Conclusion: The $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ model with magnetized strange quark matter is observationally viable, self-consistent, and mildly favored over $\Lambda$CDM by AIC, but not decisively so; $\mu$ functions as an effective phenomenological parameter encoding late-time gravitational–matter coupling rather than a fundamental constant.

Abstract: Cosmological models of magnetized strange quark matter in $f(R,T)$ gravity have so far relied on kinematic ansatzes and lacked observational tests. We revisit them for $f(R,T)=R+2\mu T$ on a hypersurface-homogeneous background containing the locally rotationally symmetric Bianchi I, Kantowski--Sachs and Bianchi III geometries. Treated exactly, the Maxwell field couples minimally. Because kinematic ansatzes contradict a magnetized source, we close the field equations with the bag-model equation of state and magnetic flux freezing and, adding dust and radiation, solve them exactly in limiting cases and numerically in general. Without primordial shear, the field-induced anisotropy keeps, as in general relativity, a nearly constant radiation-era level for $\mu\ge0$, then decays as the model approaches de Sitter space; an assumed present field of 1 nG leaves $(\sigma/H)_0\simeq5.8\times10^{-11}$ today. A joint fit to 32 cosmic chronometers, 1590 Pantheon+ supernova light curves and baryon acoustic oscillations from the second data release of the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument gives $\mu=1.12^{+0.65}_{-0.58}$ and $H_0=69.1\pm3.2$~\kmsMpc, and the null, weak and dominant energy conditions hold throughout. Relative to the flat $\Lambda$ cold dark matter model, the Akaike (AIC) and Bayesian (BIC) information criteria give $\Delta\mathrm{AIC}=-1.4$ and $\Delta\mathrm{BIC}=+4.0$, and the Bayes factor is inconclusive. The fitted bag term, $B_c^{1/4}=2.15$ meV, is an effective vacuum energy, not the quantum chromodynamics bag constant. The preference for $\mu>0$ depends on the supernova sample and the prior, a constant coupling would shift matter--radiation equality, and the sign of $\mu$ depends on the matter Lagrangian; $\mu$ is therefore an effective late-time parameter.

</details>


### [22] [Astrometric effects of anisotropic gravitational wave backgrounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15206)
*Petar D. Dimitrov,Deyan P. Mihaylov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes astrometric correlation functions (generalized overlap reduction functions) for anisotropic stochastic gravitational wave backgrounds (SGWBs) across all six GW polarizations, providing closed-form expressions in the distant-source limit and highlighting qualitative differences between tensor, vector, and scalar modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable future astrometric detection of anisotropic, arbitrarily polarized SGWBs—particularly from supermassive black hole binaries—by extending Hellings-Downs–type correlations beyond isotropic tensor models to all polarizations and anisotropic sky distributions.

Method: Derives generalized overlap reduction functions (GORFs) for astrometric correlations induced by anisotropic SGWBs with tensor, vector, and scalar polarizations; applies the distant-light-source approximation analogously to pulsar timing array formalism; obtains closed-form analytical expressions where valid.

Result: Closed-form GORFs are derived for all six polarizations under the distant-source limit; tensor, vector, and scalar modes yield qualitatively distinct angular correlation structures; anisotropy breaks rotational symmetry and introduces multipole-dependent features absent in isotropic cases.

Conclusion: The computed GORFs provide a foundational framework for detecting and characterizing anisotropic, multi-polarization SGWBs in upcoming high-precision astrometric surveys (e.g., future Gaia releases), enabling tests of gravity beyond general relativity and improved astrophysical modeling of SMBH binary populations.

Abstract: Precise astrometric observations of distant objects such as stars and quasars can be used to probe gravitational wave signals at nanohertz frequencies. A stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB) produces correlated apparent motions of pairs of objects on the sky. That correlation as a function of their angular separation is the astrometric analog of the Hellings-Downs curve in the context of pulsar timing measurements. Upper limits on the SGWB have been obtained from very-long baseline interferometry and Gaia astrometry, and future Gaia data releases are expected to improve them. An astrophysical background from inspiraling supermassive black hole binaries is expected to be significantly anisotropic. In this work, we compute the astrometric correlation functions for an anisotropic SGWB, known as the generalized overlap reduction functions, for all six gravitational wave polarizations. We present closed-form expressions in all cases where the distant-light source limit can be applied, following the approach used for pulsar timing arrays. We find that the correlations differ qualitatively between the tensor, vector, and scalar polarizations. Finally, we discuss the applicability of these results in future astrometry searches for arbitrarily polarized anisotropic gravitational wave backgrounds.

</details>


### [23] [Quasinormal Modes of a Black Hole in a Dehnen-Type Dark Matter Halo with Radial Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15256)
*Guoyang Fu,Jiahao Zhou,Dan Zhang,Jian-Pin Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar and axial gravitational perturbations around a black hole embedded in a Dehnen-type dark matter halo with nonzero radial pressure, finding that QNM frequencies and damping rates exhibit distinct redshift behavior dependent on DM halo compactness and profile parameter γ, with damping more sensitive than oscillation frequency; eikonal-limit behavior is explained via light-ring angular velocity and Lyapunov exponent.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how realistic dark matter halos—specifically Dehnen-type halos with nonvanishing radial pressure—affect black hole perturbative dynamics and quasinormal mode spectra, beyond idealized (e.g., pressureless) models.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of quasinormal modes for scalar and axial gravitational perturbations in a black hole spacetime modified by a Dehnen-type dark matter halo with radial pressure; analysis includes dependence on halo compactness $M/a_0$ and profile parameter $\gamma$, and eikonal-limit interpretation using light-ring properties ($\Omega_c$, $\lambda$).

Result: Real (oscillation frequency) and imaginary (damping rate) parts of QNMs show distinct redshift coefficients with respect to $M/a_0$ when radial pressure is nonzero; the effect scales dominantly with $\gamma$; damping rate exhibits greater sensitivity to $M/a_0$ than oscillation frequency; eikonal-limit deviations are accurately described by $\Omega_c$ and $\lambda$.

Conclusion: Nonzero radial pressure in Dehnen-type dark matter halos induces quantifiable, profile-dependent modifications to black hole quasinormal spectra, implying that QNMs can serve as probes of dark matter microphysics—including equation-of-state effects—beyond mass distribution alone.

Abstract: We investigate the quasinormal modes (QNMs) of scalar and (axial) gravitational perturbations of a black hole spacetime surrounded by a Dehnen-type dark matter (DM) halo with nonvanishing radial pressure. Our results reveal that the real and imaginary parts of the QNMs exhibit distinct redshift coefficients with respect to the DM halo compactness $M/a_0$, in contrast to the vanishing radial pressure case. The magnitude of this effect is dominated by the profile parameter $\gamma$, and the damping rate is more sensitive to the DM halo compactness than the oscillation frequency. In the eikonal limit, the redshift deviations are well captured by the light ring angular velocity $\Omega_c$ and the Lyapunov exponent $\lambda$.

</details>


### [24] [Weak null singularities for the Einstein--Vlasov system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15318)
*Dawei Shen,Yuefeng Song,Jingbo Wan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves existence and stability of weak null singularities for Einstein–Vlasov systems (massive/massless), showing continuous metric extendibility, bounded curvature and energy-momentum tensor at the singularity, failure of Christoffel symbols to be locally square-integrable for singular data, and Lipschitz inextendibility under extra curvature assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize Luk's vacuum weak null singularity results to the physically richer Einstein–Vlasov system—capturing collisionless matter—without symmetry assumptions, thereby addressing formation and stability of singular null boundaries in realistic gravitational settings.

Method: Constructs spacetimes from characteristic initial data using double-null gauge; introduces novel commutator vector fields to control derivative loss and avoid the most singular curvature component (α); employs energy estimates and transport equations for Vlasov matter; analyzes regularity of metric, connection, curvature, and energy-momentum tensor up to the null boundary.

Result: Existence of spacetimes with weak null singularities for both massive and massless Einstein–Vlasov systems; continuity of metric and Ricci curvature, boundedness and continuity of energy-momentum tensor at the singular boundary; failure of Christoffel symbols to lie in L²_loc near the boundary for genuinely singular data; Lipschitz inextendibility across the boundary under additional curvature decay conditions.

Conclusion: Weak null singularities are robust features beyond vacuum gravity—they persist for collisionless matter models, with precise regularity thresholds identified: C⁰ metric extendibility coexists with W^{1,2} breakdown of connection, confirming a sharp borderline between extendibility and inextendibility in the Einstein–Vlasov setting.

Abstract: We study the formation and stability of weak null singularities for solutions to the massive and massless Einstein--Vlasov systems without symmetry assumptions. Extending previous results of Luk in the vacuum setting, we establish the existence of spacetimes arising from characteristic initial data that develop a singular null boundary while remaining extendible with a continuous metric. For both massive and massless particles, the energy-momentum tensor and Ricci curvature remain bounded and extend continuously to the singular boundary. For genuinely singular initial data, the Christoffel symbols in the original double-null coordinates fail to be locally square integrable near the boundary. We introduce new commutator vector fields that avoid both derivative loss and the most singular curvature component. Under additional curvature assumptions, we also prove Lipschitz inextendibility across the singular null boundary.

</details>


### [25] [Conserved gravitational-wave Stress-Energy Tensor in General Metric Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15321)
*Xiao-Bin Lai,Yu-Zhi Fan,Yu-Qi Dong,Yu-Xiao Liu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a conserved gravitational-wave stress-energy tensor for general metric theories—including those with vector fields—by augmenting the conventional tensor with vector-field contributions derived from diffeomorphism invariance; it unifies multiple derivation methods (perturbed field equations, Landau-Lifshitz, Noether current) in the short-wavelength, flat-wave-zone limit, yields a generally nonsymmetric but divergence-free tensor with physical energy flux, and applies the framework to Einstein-æther theory to resolve prior inconsistencies in binary orbital energy loss predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in gravitational-wave energy-momentum definitions for metric theories beyond GR—particularly those with additional fields (e.g., vector fields)—and to establish a physically consistent, conserved stress-energy tensor enabling robust astrophysical and observational tests of gravity.

Method: Uses short-wavelength averaging in the wave zone; combines the diffeomorphism identity with perturbative expansions of field equations and actions; derives and compares stress-energy tensors via perturbed field equations, perturbed action, Landau-Lifshitz pseudotensor, and Noether current approaches; applies the unified framework to Einstein-æther theory to compute orbital energy loss in quasicircular binaries.

Result: A completed, generally nonsymmetric GW stress-energy tensor τ^{μν} satisfying ∂_μτ^{μν}=0 and yielding physical energy flux cτ^{i0}; equivalence of four derivation methods under stated assumptions; independence from vector field index placement (covariant vs. contravariant); resolution of prior discrepancies in Einstein-æther binary energy loss rates.

Conclusion: A consistent, conserved gravitational-wave stress-energy tensor can be constructed for general metric–vector theories by incorporating vector-field contributions mandated by diffeomorphism invariance; this enables reliable radiation-reaction modeling and sharpens observational constraints on modified gravity.

Abstract: We construct a gravitational-wave (GW) stress-energy tensor for general metric theories and show that it is conserved after averaging in the wave zone. In metric theories involving a vector field, we find that the conventional GW stress-energy tensor is generally not conserved. Combining the diffeomorphism identity, we identify the vector-field contribution required in addition to the conventional GW stress-energy tensor. On this basis, in a flat vacuum wave zone with constant background fields and after short-wavelength averaging, the perturbed field equation, perturbed action, Landau-Lifshitz, and Noether current methods yield equivalent tensors. In this setting, the completed tensor is generally nonsymmetric and satisfies $\partial_\mu\tau^{\mu\nu}=0$, with energy flux $c\tau^{i0}$. It also does not depend on whether a covariant or contravariant vector is chosen as the fundamental variable. Applying this framework to Einstein-æther theory, we derive the orbital energy losses of an adiabatic quasicircular compact binary, and provide a possible explanation for the discrepancies among previous results in this theory. These results establish a consistent radiation balance that allows additional gravitational degrees of freedom to be connected to the evolution of astrophysical systems and to observational tests of gravity.

</details>


### [26] [FOORT: Flexible Object-Oriented Ray Tracing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15331)
*Daniel R. Mayerson,Seppe J. Staelens,Fabio Bacchini*

Main category: gr-qc
