Result: No informative constraints on scalar-field mass μ; marginalized 90% credible upper limits on coupling length scale: ℓ < 60.4 km (dCS) and ℓ < 59.8 km (sGB).

Conclusion: Current ringdown observations lack sensitivity to scalar mass but provide the first direct observational bounds on the coupling scales of dCS and sGB gravity from ringdown spectroscopy.

Abstract: We search for massive scalar fields with non-minimal curvature couplings using the loudest ringdown signals observed by LIGO, Virgo, and KAGRA. We find that current ringdown observations do not provide informative constraints on the scalar-field mass. We nevertheless constrain the coupling length scale for scalar fields satisfying $M\mu<1$, where $\mu$ is the scalar-field mass, yielding marginalized 90$\%$ credible upper limits of 60.4 km and 59.8 km for dynamical Chern-Simons and scalar Gauss-Bonnet couplings, respectively.

</details>


### [39] [Degeneracies in the quasinormal mode spectrum of the Kerr-Sen black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15932)
*Sérgio V. M. C. B. Xavier,João Paulo Cavalcante,Enzo Boschilia Vita,Maurício Richartz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates exceptional points (EPs) in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum of scalar perturbations on Kerr-Sen and Kerr-Newman black holes, locates EPs via double-root conditions on the QNM secular equation, and demonstrates hysteresis—mode exchange upon encircling an EP in parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-Hermiticity in black hole perturbation theory gives rise to exceptional points in the QNM spectrum, and how such EPs—especially in string-theoretic (Kerr-Sen) vs. GR (Kerr-Newman) black holes—affect ringdown signatures and spectral degeneracies.

Method: Analytical and numerical identification of exceptional points by enforcing double-root conditions on the quasinormal mode secular equation for scalar fields; tracing exceptional lines in the 3D parameter space (spin, charge, field mass); analyzing hysteresis via adiabatic parameter loops around EPs.

Result: Exceptional lines are explicitly located in Kerr-Sen and Kerr-Newman parameter spaces; EPs persist away from the Kerr limit when charge and field mass are tuned; hysteresis—i.e., permutation of QNMs—is confirmed upon encircling an EP.

Conclusion: The exceptional structure of QNMs is robust across different gravitational theories (string-inspired vs. GR), and EPs provide a geometrically and physically meaningful organizing principle for black hole spectroscopy, with observable implications for gravitational-wave ringdown analysis.

Abstract: The non-Hermitian nature of the eigenvalue problem underlying black hole perturbations can induce the presence of exceptional points (EPs) in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum. EPs are points in parameter space where QNMs become degenerate. Near an EP, QNMs are resonantly excited, leaving imprints on the ringdown signal. In this work, we investigate the exceptional structure of scalar perturbations of the Kerr-Sen black hole, a rotating, charged solution of the low-energy limit of heterotic string theory, and compare it with the EP structure of the Kerr-Newman black hole in General Relativity. Starting from the EP associated with a massive scalar field in the Kerr limit, we trace exceptional lines in the parameter space spanned by the black hole spin, charge, and field mass. We locate these degeneracies directly by imposing double-root conditions on the QNM secular equation, thus avoiding the need for parameter space scans. We also investigate the hysteresis phenomenon, showing that encircling an EP in parameter space leads to an exchange of the associated QNMs.

</details>


### [40] [Machine learning and high accuracy: Neural preconditioners for computational gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16106)
*Thomas Osburn,Nils Vu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a hybrid machine learning method using a graph neural network (GNN) as a fast, generalizable preconditioner for solving Helmholtz-type sparse linear systems arising in gravitational and elastic wave modeling—achieving 2–15× fewer iterations than traditional preconditioners while maintaining high accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational wave modeling demands high numerical accuracy, making pure ML approaches unsuitable due to inherent noise floors; however, hybrid methods that embed ML into traditional numerical schemes can accelerate convergence without sacrificing precision.

Method: The authors train a graph neural network (GNN) to act as a preconditioner for iterative solvers of Helmholtz-type sparse linear systems. The GNN is trained directly on the sparse matrix structure using a novel loss function based on the true residual of the underlying iterative algorithm—not proxy losses like reconstruction or label prediction.

Result: For challenging parameter regimes in asymmetric-mass black hole binary and elastic wave simulations, the GNN preconditioner reduces iteration counts by 2–15× versus state-of-the-art traditional preconditioners (e.g., multigrid or ILU). It generalizes across problem instances with minimal retraining due to its graph-based, physics-agnostic architecture operating natively on sparse matrices.

Conclusion: Hybrid ML—specifically GNN-based preconditioning—is a viable, accurate, and scalable strategy for high-fidelity PDE solving in gravitational wave astrophysics and other wave-dominated physical domains, bridging the gap between speed and precision.

Abstract: Machine learning (ML) developments have empowered valuable calculations in many areas of theoretical physics. Certain topics involving moderate-to-low accuracy requirements have been particularly fortunate because many ML techniques, such as neural networks, introduce a noise floor that would be difficult to penetrate with stricter tolerances. For this reason, topics requiring high accuracy can be hesitant to pursue ML. One prominent example is gravitational wave modeling, which relies on traditional methods that accommodate the high sensitivities of detectors such as LISA. However, recent progress in applied mathematics has identified hybrid methods where fast-but-noisy ML mechanisms are inserted into otherwise traditional numerical schemes to accelerate convergence in a way that ultimately meets or exceeds the accuracy of standard methods. We develop an optimized hybrid ML method to tackle a class of challenging PDEs; specifically, we demonstrate ML implementations for gravitational waves from asymmetric-mass black hole binaries and elastic waves. These require solving sparse linear algebra systems with Helmholtz features that are especially difficult for traditional iterative methods. Our new approach is to train a graph neural network (GNN) as a significantly faster preconditioner using a novel loss function based on the true residual of the iterative algorithm. For the most difficult regions of parameter space, our neural preconditioner decreases iteration counts by 2-15x compared to traditional preconditioners. Because the GNN operates directly on the sparse matrix and needs only modest physical context, it generalizes beyond its training domain and can be adapted to new problems across physics by retraining rather than redesign. Hybrid ML methods such as this offer a new and compelling avenue to handle high accuracy and speed requirements in gravitational wave modeling and beyond.

</details>


### [41] [Separation of Variables and Spectroscopy of Massive Spin-3/2 Fields on a Kerr Background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16120)
*Ziqian Tang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper separates the neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger equation on a Kerr black hole background, deriving decoupled radial and angular equations, obtaining quasibound states, greybody factors, and helicity-resolved Hawking radiation; it identifies an anomalous rotational splitting sign relative to Dirac fields and traces its origin to angular momentum coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study quantum field behavior of spin-3/2 fermions in strong gravity—specifically, to understand quasibound states, scattering properties (greybody factors), and Hawking emission for neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger fields on rotating Kerr black holes, and to compare their rotational fine structure with that of spin-1/2 Dirac fields.

Method: Matrix separation of variables applied to the neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger equation on Kerr spacetime; construction of two commuting matrix angular operators and a physical first integral; reduction to a second-order radial ODE; reversible reconstruction of the full field from separated solutions; spectral analysis for quasibound modes and computation of greybody/Hawking quantities.

Result: Obtained fully separated radial and angular equations; derived quasibound spectra, frequency-dependent greybody factors, and helicity-resolved Hawking emission rates; discovered that the leading rotational splitting for a weakly bound level has opposite sign compared to Dirac fields; computed the precise coefficient of this splitting and identified its origin in the total angular momentum quantum number j = l ± 1/2 coupling to rotation.

Conclusion: The neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger field exhibits distinct spectral and radiative features on Kerr backgrounds due to its higher spin structure; the sign reversal in rotational splitting is a genuine spin-3/2 effect rooted in angular momentum composition, offering a potential observational discriminant between fundamental fermionic fields of different spin.

Abstract: We separate the neutral massive Rarita-Schwinger equation on a Kerr background at finite rotation. Two commuting matrix angular operators and a physical first integral yield a second-order radial equation and reversible field reconstruction. The resulting equations give quasibound spectra, greybody factors, and helicity-resolved Hawking emission. For a matched weakly bound level, the leading rotational splitting has the opposite sign to that of a Dirac field. We derive its coefficient and identify its angular momentum origin.

</details>


### [42] [Double null coordinates for Kerr-Vaidya spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16141)
*Xiao-Kan Guo,Xiankai Pang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs double-null coordinates for the Kerr-Vaidya spacetime—a nonstationary, rotating black hole with time-dependent mass—by introducing two independent Carter-type angular corrections to the null coordinates; it derives and solves master and integrating-factor equations, shows failure of closure of canonical null one-forms under mass variation, and constructs a regular Kruskal-type extension with time-dependent surface gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize null-coordinate constructions (e.g., Eddington-Finkelstein and Carter coordinates) to nonstationary rotating black holes where mass varies with retarded/advanced time, overcoming the breakdown of nullness and coordinate regularity at the horizon in standard formulations.

Method: Solves the eikonal equation to obtain canonical null one-forms; analyzes their closure conditions, leading to two distinct master equations and associated integrating-factor equations; solves them numerically; constructs outgoing/ingoing null coordinates with Carter-type angular corrections; builds Kruskal-type extension using time-dependent surface gravity.

Result: Explicit double-null form of the Kerr-Vaidya metric; identification of two independent Carter-type functions required for outgoing/ingoing congruences; demonstration that canonical null one-forms are non-closed under mass variation; derivation of horizon-regular Kruskal-type coordinates with time-dependent surface gravity.

Conclusion: The Kerr-Vaidya spacetime admits a well-defined double-null structure only when two distinct angular correction functions are employed; its horizon singularities in standard null coordinates are resolved via a time-dependent Kruskal extension, enabling consistent analysis of radiation and horizon dynamics in nonstationary rotating black holes.

Abstract: We construct a pair of null coordinates for the Kerr-Vaidya spacetime, a nonstationary rotating black-hole geometry whose mass varies with the retarded or advanced time. The metric is naturally written in Eddington-Finkelstein-type coordinates, but the corresponding time coordinate fails to be null when the spin is nonzero, so each null coordinate must acquire a correction of the Carter type that depends on the polar angle. We show that the outgoing and ingoing null congruences require two independent Carter-type functions. The canonical null one-forms obtained from the eikonal equation are not closed when the mass varies, and their closure conditions are different equations. For constant mass both closure equations reduce to the equation found by Argañaraz and Moreschi. We derive the two master equations together with the two integrating-factor equations, solve both sectors numerically, and obtain the Kerr-Vaidya metric in double-null form. We also show that the unextended null coordinates are singular at the horizon, where the advanced coordinate diverges logarithmically and the metric determinant vanishes. We construct the corresponding Kruskal-type extension with a time-dependent surface gravity and show that it is regular at the horizon.

</details>


### [43] [A Universal Law for Horizon Formation in Black Hole Mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16168)
*Yu-Cun Xie,Vaishak Prasad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper identifies a universal local behavior during black hole merger horizon formation: the outer and inner horizons separate as (t-t_*)^{1/2} along a dominant positive mode, with linear drift; confirmed numerically to ~0.5% precision across diverse mergers, and the reconstructed formation surface is marginally stable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the universal local geometry and dynamics of the common horizon formation and bifurcation during black hole mergers, independent of symmetry assumptions.

Method: Analytical derivation of the universal local near-critical behavior of horizon bifurcation, combined with high-precision numerical relativity simulations—including non-symmetric, eccentric, and precessing binary black hole mergers—to test the predicted scaling exponent and stability.

Result: The outer and inner horizons separate as (t - t_*)^{1/2}; the separation is dominated by a single everywhere-positive mode; a linear drift term is present; numerical tests confirm the 1/2 exponent to within 0.5%; the reconstructed critical surface at t = t_* is marginally stable (zero-mode eigenvalue).

Conclusion: The bifurcation of the common horizon in black hole mergers exhibits a universal, symmetry-independent critical behavior governed by a square-root scaling law and marginal stability—establishing a robust local universality class for dynamical horizons in general relativity.

Abstract: When two black holes merge, a common horizon appears around them as a single surface, which immediately splits into an outer and an inner one. We show that this event has a universal local form. Without any symmetry assumption, the two surfaces separate as $(t-t_*)^{1/2}$ along a single, everywhere positive mode and share a drift linear in $t-t_*$. Three numerical simulations, including an eccentric, precessing one, confirm the exponent to half a percent, and the reconstructed formation surface is marginally stable, as predicted.

</details>


### [44] [Exceptional point excitation by a plunging particle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16390)
*Xia-Yuan Liu,Liang-Bi Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that a plunging particle excites a second-order exceptional point (EP) in the quasinormal mode spectrum of a black hole, and that conventional QNM templates fail to accurately recover the EP frequency—even when Bayesian evidence appears to favor them—highlighting a fundamental limitation in ringdown modeling when EPs are present.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-Hermitian spectral features—specifically second-order exceptional points (EPs) in black hole quasinormal mode spectra—are physically excited and whether standard QNM-based waveform models can reliably infer their complex frequencies from gravitational wave signals.

Method: Derives the master equation for odd-parity perturbations from a gravitational action with a Gaussian-bump-modified potential; numerically computes the ringdown waveform for (ℓ,m)=(2,1); analytically isolates the EP contribution from the particle source; performs waveform fitting and Bayesian model comparison between ordinary QNM and EP-inclusive templates.

Result: The plunging particle excites the EP response, which—along with its mirror—dominates the intermediate ringdown; EP template yields more accurate ω recovery in fitting; at SNR=100, the ordinary template’s 90% posterior excludes the true EP frequency while the EP template’s includes it; Bayes factor favors ordinary template at low SNR but EP template at high SNR.

Conclusion: Conventional QNM templates can produce misleadingly narrow posteriors and favorable Bayesian evidence even when they omit essential EP physics—thus failing to ensure accurate frequency inference—revealing a critical caveat in gravitational-wave ringdown analysis when exceptional points are present.

Abstract: We investigate the excitation of a second-order exceptional point (EP) in the quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum by a plunging particle. Starting from a gravitational action, we derive the master equation with a Gaussian bump modified potential. For the odd-parity mode $(\ell,m)=(2,1)$, we compute the waveform numerically and derive an analytic expression for the EP contribution to the ringdown waveform directly from the particle source. Comparison with the numerical waveform shows that the particle excites the EP response, which, together with its mirror contribution, dominates the intermediate ringdown stage. We use waveform fitting and Bayesian analysis to assess the limitations of conventional QNM templates for EP ringdown. In the fitting, the EP template recovers the complex frequency $\omega$ more accurately than the ordinary template. In Bayesian analysis, at a signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of $\rho=100$, the ordinary template yields a narrow frequency posterior whose $90\%$ credible region excludes the true EP frequency, whereas the broader EP-template region contains it. The Bayes factor nevertheless favors the ordinary template at lower SNRs and the EP template only at higher SNRs. These results show that in the EP case a narrow posterior and favorable Bayesian evidence do not guarantee accurate frequency inference when a conventional template omits the characteristic EP ringdown structure.

</details>


### [45] [Tricritical Dynamics Reveal Black Hole Interior Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16564)
*Fu-Guo Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper links exterior perturbation dynamics (radial growth rate Ω_u) to interior Kasner exponents in scalarized black holes, using entropy-potential curvature and horizon scalar amplitude scaling near a tricritical point; it achieves ~0.5% agreement between reconstructed and numerically integrated angular Kasner exponents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge exterior observable dynamics (perturbation growth) with interior geometric structure (Kasner exponents) in scalarized black-hole equilibria, particularly near the tricritical endpoint of the Reissner–Nordström–scalarized coexistence line.

Method: Analyzes linear radial perturbations on asymptotically flat and AdS black-hole backgrounds; relates the radial growth rate Ω_u to entropy-potential curvature; exploits scaling laws of curvature (∝ amplitude⁴) and interior scalar gradient (∝ 1/amplitude) near the tricritical point; eliminates amplitude to derive p ∼ 2√(Ω_u/C); fixes C via static source expansion and RN zero-mode analysis; validates against inward numerical integration.

Result: Derives analytic relation p ∼ 2√(Ω_u/C) connecting exterior perturbation growth to interior angular Kasner exponent; computes Ω_u for near-tricritical equilibria; achieves ~0.5% agreement between reconstructed p and independently integrated interior geometry.

Conclusion: Exterior linear perturbation dynamics encode interior Kasner geometry in scalarized black holes near tricriticality; the mapping is robust and quantitatively precise, enabling indirect inference of interior structure from horizon-scale observables.

Abstract: We reconstruct the interior Kasner exponents of a scalarized black-hole equilibrium from the growth rate of a radial perturbation outside the horizon. The equal-entropy coexistence line of Reissner--Nordström and scalarized phases ends at a tricritical point. We follow the intermediate equilibrium separating the two phases along the coexistence line. The radial growth rate $\Omega_u$ is proportional to the magnitude of the entropy-potential curvature. As the tricritical point is approached, the curvature vanishes as the fourth power of the horizon scalar amplitude. The interior logarithmic scalar gradient grows inversely with the horizon amplitude. Eliminating the amplitude connects exterior dynamics to interior geometry, giving the angular Kasner exponent $p\sim2\sqrt{\Omega_u/C}$. The static source expansion and the critical Reissner--Nordström zero mode fix $C$ before the scalarized interior is integrated. We compute the growth rate from linear radial perturbations on asymptotically flat and anti-de Sitter black-hole backgrounds. At the sampled equilibria closest to tricriticality, the reconstructed angular exponent agrees with the result of independent inward integration at about the $0.5\%$ level.

</details>


### [46] [The uniqueness of smooth stationary black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16853)
*Yishen Huang,Jiongyue Li,Junbin Li,Xi-Ping Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that a smooth, asymptotically flat, stationary vacuum black hole spacetime with a single non-degenerate rotating horizon must be isometric to a subextremal Kerr solution—without requiring analyticity or smallness assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the rigidity conjecture for stationary black holes in vacuum general relativity by removing restrictive assumptions (e.g., analyticity, perturbative closeness) previously used in uniqueness proofs.

Method: Applies the Harrison transform to generate a family of Einstein–Maxwell solutions; uses null area comparison and the area-charge inequality to constrain geometric quantities on the bifurcate sphere in the limiting transformed geometry; normalizes ADM mass to M=1 and derives constant flux densities and unit area radius, satisfying the technical conditions of Ionescu–Klainerman.

Result: The domain of outer communications is shown to be isometric to that of a subextremal Kerr spacetime under physically reasonable geometric assumptions (smoothness, asymptotic flatness, stationarity, vacuum, single non-degenerate rotating horizon).

Conclusion: The Kerr metric uniquely describes such black hole exteriors—establishing strong rigidity without analyticity or small-data hypotheses.

Abstract: We show that the domain of outer communications of a smooth, asymptotically flat, stationary vacuum spacetime describing a single rotating non-degenerate black hole is isometric to the domain of outer communications of a subextremal Kerr solution under reasonable geometric and physical assumptions, without analyticity or any smallness assumptions. The method is to apply Harrison transform to construct a family of solutions of Einstein--Maxwell equations. Combining null area comparison with the area-charge inequality, we show that, in the limiting transformed geometry, the bifurcate sphere has area radius one and constant electric and magnetic flux densities, with the original ADM mass normalized to $M=1$. These identities imply the technical assumptions of Ionescu--Klainerman, yielding the identification with Kerr.

</details>


### [47] [Relational Cosmology: Covariant and Canonical Dressings, and the Hamiltonian of Internal Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16864)
*Francesco Sartini*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes relational dressings (covariant vs. canonical) and gauge-fixing reductions in flat FLRW minisuperspace with a massless scalar, clarifying how clock choice breaks relational-time reparametrization covariance, enabling a nontrivial relational Hamiltonian; it establishes conditions for dynamical equivalence and relates relational generators to complete observables and reduced Hamiltonians, while noting quantum equivalence remains open.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve conceptual and technical ambiguities in relational approaches to time and observables in constrained Hamiltonian systems—particularly in cosmological minisuperspace models—by distinguishing covariance of dressing maps from symmetries of the resulting relational theory, and by clarifying the origin and interpretation of relational Hamiltonians.

Method: Analytical comparison of covariant and canonical relational dressings with gauge-fixing reductions in the flat FLRW minisuperspace model coupled to a homogeneous massless scalar field; employs constraint analysis, Dirac quantization framework, Legendre transformation, and relational evaluation on solution space; distinguishes background structure emergence under clock fixation.

Result: Clock fixation converts frame fields into background structures, breaking relational-time reparametrization covariance and permitting a nonvanishing relational Hamiltonian distinct from the vanishing Hamiltonian constraint; identifies precise conditions under which dressed dynamics reproduce original constrained evolution; shows the relational generator induces a well-defined Hamiltonian flow and coincides with the canonical complete observable and reduced Hamiltonian via Legendre map and relational evaluation in each clock frame.

Conclusion: Covariant and canonical relational approaches yield classically compatible descriptions with identical physical predictions but differ in variational principles and intermediate phase spaces; the loss of diffeomorphism covariance upon clock choice is essential for defining dynamics, yet quantum equivalence—especially regarding Hilbert space structure and operator ordering—remains unresolved.

Abstract: We compare covariant and canonical relational dressings with gauge-fixing reductions for the flat FLRW minisuperspace with a homogeneous massless scalar. We distinguish covariance of the dressing map from symmetries of the relational theory: fixing a clock makes its frame fields background structures, so its reorientations act as vector fields on relational field space rather than as spacetime diffeomorphisms. This loss of relational-time reparametrization covariance permits a nontrivial relational Hamiltonian, distinct from the undressed and dressed Hamiltonian constraints, which vanish on the constraint surface. We identify the conditions under which the dressed equations reproduce the original constrained dynamics. The relational generator defines a Hamiltonian flow even when relational-time translations are not symmetries of the dressed action. In each clock frame, it corresponds to the canonical complete observable and reduced Hamiltonian on the appropriate solution space, through the Legendre map and relational evaluation. The classical descriptions are compatible but retain distinct variational principles and intermediate phase spaces; their quantum equivalence remains an open question.

</details>


### [48] [Lurkers at the Threshold: GWTC-4.0's near-threshold events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16985)
*Daniel Williams,Michael Williams*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs parameter estimation on 42 marginal gravitational-wave candidates from GWTC-4.0 (p_astro ≥ 0.5 but FAR ≥ 1 yr⁻¹), using public data and a reproducible, LVK-independent workflow; 29 pass validation, revealing several astrophysically interesting events—including a potential NSBH merger and two with non-zero effective spin—though spin constraints remain weak per KL divergence analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether scientifically valuable parameter constraints can be extracted from gravitational-wave candidates excluded from the official GWTC-4.0 catalogue due to marginal significance (FAR ≥ 1 yr⁻¹), despite their high p_astro, and to develop an open, reproducible analysis framework independent of LVK collaboration resources.

Method: Applied a catalogue-style Bayesian parameter estimation pipeline to 42 sub-threshold GW candidates using publicly available LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA strain data and open-source software (e.g., bilby, gwpy); incorporated automated posterior validation (e.g., convergence diagnostics, prior-posterior consistency, noise subtraction checks); classified results into validated, noise/artefact-contaminated, or uninterpretable categories.

Result: 29/42 candidates yielded validated posteriors; 7 were consistent with noise or instrumental artefacts; 6 were uninterpretable. Notable candidates include GW230920_064709 (possible NSBH), GW230902_172430 and GW230823_142524 (non-zero χ_eff), with the latter showing mild precession evidence. KL divergence quantification confirmed weak spin constraints overall.

Conclusion: Marginal-significance GW candidates can yield astrophysically informative parameter estimates when rigorously validated, supporting broader inclusion of such events in population studies—provided robust, automated validation is integral to the analysis, and limitations (e.g., weak spin constraints, artefact sensitivity) are transparently reported.

Abstract: We present the first systematic parameter-estimation analysis of 42 candidate gravitational-wave signals identified in GWTC-4.0 with a probability of astrophysical origin $p_\mathrm{astro} \geq 0.5$, but a false-alarm rate of at least $1\,\mathrm{yr}^{-1}$, which were therefore not selected for parameter estimation in the catalogue. We use a catalogue-style analysis workflow and publicly available strain data to obtain posterior samples for these candidates, of which 29 pass our validation criteria. Of the remaining 13 candidates, seven are consistent with noise or impacted by instrumental artefacts, while six yield results that cannot be reliably interpreted. We identify several interesting candidates: a possible neutron star-black hole merger, GW230920_064709, and two candidates with evidence for non-zero effective spin: GW230902_172430 and GW230823_142524. The latter also shows mild evidence of precession, although further analysis is needed to establish the robustness of this result. We quantify the information gained from these observations using the Kullback-Leibler divergence, finding that the inferred spin parameters are generally only weakly constrained. We present a reproducible analysis workflow that uses publicly available data and software, without access to LVK collaboration resources, and incorporates automated posterior-validation tools. We also provide a data release containing results in a format compatible with those from GWTC-4.0. Our results demonstrate that useful parameter constraints can be obtained for many candidates below the catalogue's parameter-estimation significance threshold, while highlighting the challenges of analysing marginal signals and the importance of careful validation.

</details>


### [49] [Spin to win: enhancing the quantum Otto cycle with the circular Unruh effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17027)
*Leo J. A. Parry,Dimitris Moustos,Benito A. Juárez-Aubry,Obinna Abah,Jorma Louko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a quantum Otto cycle using a qubit Unruh-DeWitt detector in uniform circular motion in Minkowski spacetime, interacting with a thermal scalar field; it derives motion-induced effective temperatures that deviate from the bath temperature, enabling Carnot-bound violations—while respecting generalized Carnot limits—and shows circular motion enables such violations at lower speeds than inertial motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how relativistic motion (specifically uniform circular motion) of a quantum detector modifies its thermodynamic interaction with a quantum field, and whether such motion can enable quantum thermal machines to surpass standard Carnot bounds—challenging conventional thermodynamic expectations in relativistic quantum settings.

Method: Analytical computation of the transition rate of a uniformly rotating Unruh-DeWitt qubit coupled to a massless scalar field in a thermal state in (3+1)D Minkowski spacetime; derivation of an energy-gap-dependent effective temperature from detailed balance; thermodynamic analysis of a quantum Otto cycle (isochoric heating/cooling, adiabatic strokes) using this effective temperature to compute efficiency (heat engine) and coefficient of performance (refrigerator).

Result: The detector exhibits an effective temperature that exceeds the bath temperature for small energy gaps and falls below it at high bath temperatures; the quantum Otto engine’s efficiency and refrigerator’s COP exceed standard Carnot bounds based on rest-frame bath temperatures, but obey generalized Carnot bounds defined by the detector’s effective temperatures; circular motion enables bound violation at lower velocities than inertial motion in the high-temperature limit.

Conclusion: Relativistic non-inertial motion induces detector-specific effective temperatures that fundamentally reshape quantum thermodynamic performance, permitting apparent violations of standard Carnot limits—without violating generalized second-law constraints—and highlighting the operational relevance of local, motion-dependent thermality in relativistic quantum thermodynamics.

Abstract: We study a quantum Otto cycle whose working medium is a qubit Unruh-DeWitt detector undergoing uniform circular motion in $(3+1)$-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, interacting with a massless scalar field in a thermal state during the isochoric strokes. We compute the detector's transition rate and determine its energy gap-dependent effective temperature, showing that the effective temperature can be higher than the ambient bath temperature in the small energy gap limit and lower in the high bath temperature limit. We then investigate how these motion-induced effective temperatures affect the operating conditions and performance of the quantum Otto cycle as a heat engine or a refrigerator. We show that the engine efficiency and refrigerator performance coefficient can exceed their standard Carnot bounds based on the bath's rest-frame temperatures, while remaining bounded by generalised Carnot limits expressed in terms of the effective temperatures experienced by the detector. In the high-temperature limit, the effective temperature coincides with that of an inertial detector moving through a thermal bath, and a comparison with the small-gap limit reveals that circular motion allows the standard Carnot bounds to be exceeded at lower speeds than in the inertial case.

</details>


### [50] [Rapid Multiparameter Gravitational-Wave Tests of General Relativity with Neural Posterior Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17038)
*Hong Su,Tao Yang,He Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: DINGO-TGR is a fast neural posterior estimation framework for multiparameter gravitational-wave tests of general relativity, using importance sampling and PCA to efficiently infer binary-source parameters and six post-Newtonian phase deformations; it achieves ~4–6× speedup over conventional Bayesian sampling and finds no statistically significant deviation from GR in GW150914 or GW250114.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multiparameter tests of general relativity with gravitational waves are hindered by strong parameter correlations among post-Newtonian phase deformations and the high computational cost of traditional Bayesian inference.

Method: DINGO-TGR employs neural posterior estimation (normalizing flows) to jointly infer binary-source parameters and six PN deformations under the TIGER parametrization, using IMRPhenomXPHM waveforms with spin precession and higher-order modes; importance sampling corrects the neural proposal; PCA identifies two dominant, well-constrained linear combinations of deformations; significance is assessed via 1000 GR injection-based p-value estimation.

Result: Validated on zero-noise GR and beyond-GR injections; recovers GR-consistent posteriors and injected deformation shifts; for GW150914 and GW250114, results agree with bilby-tgr/dynesty; inference time reduced from ~177–238 hours to 38 min–4.4 hrs; GW150914 shows a mild offset in the leading PCA mode, but empirical p-value = 11.5% → not significant at 5% level; GW250114 fully consistent with GR.

Conclusion: DINGO-TGR enables efficient, high-dimensional GR tests and large-scale significance assessments, demonstrating that neural inference with importance sampling and PCA is a viable and scalable alternative to conventional sampling for multiparameter consistency tests of general relativity.

Abstract: Multiparameter gravitational-wave tests of general relativity (GR) are limited by strong correlations among post-Newtonian (PN) phase deformations and the computational cost of Bayesian inference. We present DINGO-TGR, a neural posterior estimation framework that jointly infers binary-source parameters and six PN phase deformations within the TIGER parametrization, using IMRPhenomXPHM waveforms with spin precession and higher-order modes. Importance sampling reweights the neural proposal samples, while principal component analysis (PCA) identifies two well-constrained combinations of PN deformations. We validate the framework using 16 zero-noise GR injections and 12 beyond-GR injections, recovering GR-consistent posteriors in the former and leading-mode shifts consistent with the injected deformations in the latter. For GW150914 and GW250114, our results agree with independent \texttt{bilby-tgr} analyses using \texttt{dynesty}. After training, inference including importance sampling takes approximately 38 minutes and 4.4 hours, respectively, compared with 177 and 238 hours for conventional sampling. GW150914 exhibits a mild offset from GR in the leading PCA mode, whereas GW250114 is consistent with GR. To assess the significance of the GW150914 offset, we analyze 1000 GW150914-like GR injections with independent Gaussian-noise realizations and fixed detector power spectral densities. The resulting empirical upper-tail $p$-value is $11.5\%$, indicating that the observed offset does not reject the adopted GR null model at the 5\% significance level. Our results demonstrate the potential of importance-sampling-corrected neural inference for efficient multiparameter GR tests and large-scale injection-based significance assessments.

</details>


### [51] [Gauge aspects of teleparallel gravity and Noether's second theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17147)
*Sebastian Brezina,Martin Krššák*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the gauge structure of TEGR using Noether's second theorem, identifying diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations as its fundamental gauge symmetries, clarifying their distinct roles, and proving that matter coupling—including for spinors—is uniquely fixed and identical to that in general relativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the gauge-theoretic foundation of TEGR by rigorously identifying its variational gauge symmetries via Noether's second theorem, resolving ambiguities about the role of translations and matter coupling in teleparallel gravity.

Method: Application of Noether's second theorem to the TEGR action to classify variational symmetries yielding Noether identities; analysis of the geometric and algebraic roles of diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations; examination of matter coupling consistency, especially for spinor fields, under these symmetries.

Result: Diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations are the only gauge symmetries of TEGR; translations alone do not generate independent gauge identities; matter coupling—including minimal coupling for spinors—is uniquely determined and matches GR's prescription.

Conclusion: TEGR is a genuine gauge theory with two independent gauge symmetries (diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz), not a 'translation gauge theory'; its physical content and matter interaction are fully equivalent to GR at the level of symmetries and coupling.

Abstract: We examine the gauge structure of the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity (TEGR) from the perspective of Noether's second theorem. We show that diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations are the gauge symmetries of TEGR, as these are the only variational symmetries that give rise to Noether identities. We then discuss the distinct roles of these two symmetries and their implications for the gauge-theoretic interpretation of gravity. Finally, we discuss consequences of these observations for the role of translations in gauge theories of gravity and the coupling of matter in TEGR. In particular, we demonstrate that the matter coupling prescription is uniquely determined and coincides with that of general relativity, including in the case of spinor fields.

</details>


### [52] [Effects of Lensing by Sgr A* on Semicoherent Searches for Continuous Gravitational Waves. I. Signal Model and Mismatch Studies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17153)
*Aditya Kumar Sharma,Alicia Calafat,Rodrigo Tenorio,David Keitel,Alicia M. Sintes*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how gravitational lensing by Sgr A* affects the detection of continuous gravitational waves (CWs) from distant neutron stars, showing that lensing-induced interference and time-varying modulations degrade standard semicoherent search performance in a signal-strength- and lensing-parameter-dependent way, necessitating full waveform injection studies—not just magnification rescaling—for accurate reinterpretation of Galactic Center search limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard CW searches assume unlensed waveforms; however, CWs from extragalactic or distant Galactic neutron stars can be strongly lensed by Sgr A*, producing overlapping, interfering images with time-evolving amplitudes and phases due to transverse source motion—effects unmodeled by current templates.

Method: The authors simulate lensed and unlensed CW signals injected into Gaussian noise and evaluate detection performance using a Hough-based semicoherent search, quantifying degradation via semicoherent mismatch. They systematically vary impact parameter, transverse velocity, source-lens distance, and intrinsic signal strength, and conduct a population study sampling astrophysically motivated distributions.

Result: Lensing enhances detectability via magnification but degrades template matching via evolving interference modulations; net effect depends critically on signal strength (loud signals show small fractional losses; weak signals gain substantially), impact parameter (smaller improves recovery unless modulation dominates), transverse velocity (higher worsens mismatch, especially at small impact parameters and short source-lens distances), and source-lens geometry. Population-averaged mismatches are modest but skewed toward larger values for high-velocity, close-distance configurations.

Conclusion: Gravitational lensing by Sgr A* introduces waveform distortions beyond simple amplitude scaling, invalidating sensitivity rescaling by magnification alone. Reinterpretation of existing Galactic Center CW search upper limits under lensing requires full injection campaigns spanning the physical lensing parameter space with accurate lensed waveforms.

Abstract: Continuous gravitational waves (CWs) from neutron stars beyond the Galactic Center can be lensed by the supermassive black hole in Sgr A$^*$. Since the signal lasts far longer than the lensing time delay, the two geometric-optics images overlap and interfere. Transverse source motion during the observation makes their magnifications and relative time delay evolve, producing evolving amplitude and phase modulations absent from standard CW templates. Using lensed and unlensed injections in Gaussian noise, we quantify their effect on a Hough-based semicoherent search through a semicoherent mismatch. Recovery is governed by the competition between lensing amplification and waveform modulation. Smaller impact parameters generally improve recovery through stronger magnification, while higher transverse velocities partially offset this gain through faster modulation, especially at small impact parameters and short source-lens distances, where image interference is appreciable. The response also depends on the intrinsic signal strength. Loud signals exhibit small fractional changes, with net losses in some configurations, whereas weaker signals yield broader mismatch distributions extending to more negative values, indicating greater enhancement. A population study, sampling lensing parameters from astrophysically motivated velocity and source-lens distance distributions, reproduces the strong dependence on signal strength. Differences between populations are modest, but high-velocity, short source-lens distance populations tend towards larger mismatches. Lensing effects therefore cannot be accounted for by rescaling search sensitivity with the magnification alone; reinterpreting directed Galactic Center search sensitivities and upper limits under the lensing hypothesis requires injection studies with the full lensed waveform across the lensing parameter space.

</details>

### [53] [Galilean cosmologies in two dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14636)
*Daniel Grumiller,Kai-Yu Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a general 2D Galilean dilaton gravity theory inspired by Carroll black holes, showing its solutions describe cosmological spacetimes and that the infinite-speed-of-light limit yields Galilean extremal surfaces linking singularities; entropy is given by the dilaton at those surfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the structure and thermodynamics of gravity in the ultra-relativistic (Carrollian) limit by generalizing dilaton gravity to Galilean symmetry in two dimensions.

Method: Constructs the most general action for Galilean dilaton gravity in 2D, analyzes its classical solution space, studies the non-relativistic (infinite-c) limit, identifies Galilean extremal surfaces, and computes entropy via dilaton evaluation at those surfaces.

Result: The theory generically yields cosmological spacetimes; white and black hole singularities are connected through a bifurcation surface that becomes a Galilean extremal surface in the c→∞ limit; entropy equals the dilaton field value at that extremal surface.

Conclusion: Galilean dilaton gravity provides a consistent framework for studying ultra-relativistic limits of black hole physics, with entropy geometrically encoded in a Galilean extremal surface—paralleling Bekenstein–Hawking entropy in relativistic settings.

Abstract: Inspired by Carroll black holes, we consider the most general theory of Galilean dilaton gravity in two dimensions. We find generically that the solution space describes cosmological spacetimes. From a limiting perspective, these Galilean geometries connect the white and black hole singularities through the bifurcation surface. The latter turns into a Galilean extremal surface in the limit of infinite speed of light. We also discuss aspects of Galilean tachydynamics and find that the entropy is given by the dilaton evaluated at the Galilean extremal surface.

</details>


### [54] [Time Advances and Dispersive Constraints in Dressed Gravitational Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14721)
*Ziyu Dong*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes a low-energy time advance in gravitational scattering at leading eikonal order with full soft dependence, using a finite-scale sharp dressing; it argues that analytic crossing is excluded due to dispersive constraints and energy-growing internal scales—linked to string diffusion and nonlinear graviton rescattering—and suggests that joint hard-soft detection could observe the effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile apparent time advances in gravitational scattering with fundamental principles (e.g., causality, analyticity, unitarity) by incorporating full soft radiation and finite-scale dressing, while addressing how quantum gravity effects (e.g., stringy diffusion, nonlinear graviton dynamics) modify high-energy behavior.

Method: Derives the dressed S-matrix for 4D gravitational scattering at leading eikonal order with a sharp, finite-soft-scale dressing profile; applies dispersive matching and positivity of absorption to constrain analytic continuation; analyzes scaling of internal quantum gravity scales with energy.

Result: A finite, calculable low-energy group time advance emerges; analytic crossing of the amplitude is ruled out under standard high-energy boundedness assumptions due to nonnegative absorption constraints; internal scales (e.g., string diffusion length, graviton interaction range) grow with energy, preventing naive Regge or fixed-angle extrapolation.

Conclusion: The observed time advance is physical and compatible with causality when soft clouds are properly included; its experimental signature requires coherent joint detection of hard scattered particles and their associated soft radiation—highlighting the necessity of inclusive, infrared-safe observables in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We derive a low energy group time advance in 4D dressed gravitational scattering at leading eikonal order with full soft dependence for a sharp dressing profile of finite soft scale. Dispersive matching of nonnegative absorption excludes the amplitude's analytic crossing with high energy boundedness. This reflects internal scales growing with energy via string diffusion in quantum gravity and high energy nonlinear graviton rescattering, coupled to soft clouds beyond leading soft order. Interference preserving joint detection of hard particles and coherent soft radiation may reveal the advance.

</details>


### [55] [Self-gravitating spinning mesonic tubes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14772)
*Alex Giacomini,Marcela Lagos,Seung Hun Oh,Aldo Vera*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs new regular, spinning, pulsed gyratonic solutions for the 4D SU(2) Einstein–nonlinear sigma and Einstein–Skyrme models coupled to Maxwell field; solutions are singularity-free, tube-localized, and—uniquely for the Skyrme case—require asymptotically de Sitter spacetime and exhibit nontrivial topological charge density despite vanishing total charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable, regular, self-gravitating, spinning solitonic configurations (mesonic tubes) in four dimensions that avoid curvature singularities and incorporate electromagnetic coupling, while exploring the interplay between topology, gravity, and gauge fields.

Method: Analytic construction of exact or semi-analytic gyratonic spacetime solutions using symmetry reduction (propagation axis + angular momentum), solving coupled Einstein–matter–Maxwell equations for SU(2) nonlinear sigma model and its Skyrme-generalized version; careful analysis of boundary conditions, regularity, asymptotics, and topological charge density.

Result: 1) Regular, asymptotically flat, spinning pulsed gyratonic solutions for the Einstein–nonlinear sigma–Maxwell system; 2) For the Einstein–Skyrme–Maxwell system, only asymptotically de Sitter solutions exist that resolve the matter field equations smoothly; 3) All solutions feature finite-energy, tube-localized energy density with no curvature singularities; 4) Non-zero topological charge density persists despite zero total topological charge.

Conclusion: Gyratonic mesonic tubes provide a consistent framework for regular, rotating, electromagnetic solitons in 4D gravity–matter systems; the necessity of de Sitter asymptotics for the Skyrme case highlights a fundamental tension between higher-derivative stabilization and asymptotic flatness, while localized topology enables physically meaningful solitonic behavior without global topological constraints.

Abstract: In this paper, we construct new self-gravitating solutions for the four-dimensional $SU(2)$ Einstein non-linear sigma model and the Einstein-Skyrme model, both minimally coupled to the Maxwell field, which describes spinning pulsed mesonic tubes. These solutions are gyratonic in the sense that they are characterized by a propagation axis and possess angular momentum. The gyratonic solutions are described by a smooth matter distribution, which is free of curvature singularities. The maximum of the energy density is concentrated on a tube around the propagation axis. We first consider the $SU(2)$ non-linear sigma model minimally coupled to General Relativity and the Maxwell field, constructing asymptotically flat, spinning, pulsed gyratonic solutions. Subsequently, we include the Skyrme term in the analysis. In this case, we show that in order to maintain the resolvability of the matter field equations, the spacetime must be asymptotically de Sitter. It is also shown that, despite the fact that the total topological charge is zero, these solutions possess a non-zero topological charge density.

</details>


### [56] [Spectra and Central Charges of Gauged Curvature Squared Supergravities in Five Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15444)
*Erol Aksoy,Cagatay Okyay,Mehmet Ozkan,Cemal Berfu Senisik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes off-shell gauged 5D N=2 supergravity extended by curvature-squared invariants (Weyl², Ricci², R², and Gauss-Bonnet), determining exact spectra, unitarity conditions, and central charges (a, c, flavor) around AdS₅ and AdS₃×Σ₂ vacua; R² yields a ghost-free long vector multiplet for finite coupling, while Weyl²/Ricci² introduce unavoidable ghosts; Gauss-Bonnet preserves the two-derivative spectrum up to rescaling and satisfies a-maximization and c-extremization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine how higher-derivative curvature invariants affect unitarity, supersymmetric spectra, and holographic central charges in off-shell 5D N=2 gauged supergravity — particularly whether such extensions admit stable, ghost-free supersymmetric vacua compatible with AdS/CFT predictions like a-maximization and c-extremization.

Method: Exact analysis in all couplings using off-shell supergravity formalism with full auxiliary field content; spectral decomposition into supermultiplets around maximally supersymmetric AdS₅ and AdS₃×Σ₂ vacua; quadratic fluctuation analysis to identify ghost modes and mass degeneracies; direct computation of Weyl anomaly coefficients (a, c) and flavor central charge from the on-shell action and kinetic terms; consistency checks via vacuum equations and extremization principles.

Result: R² invariant yields a ghost-free long vector multiplet for finite coupling range; Weyl² and Ricci² always introduce ghosts/instabilities; Gauss-Bonnet adds no new degrees of freedom and rescales the two-derivative action — fixing Ricci² spectrum; vacuum equation is a-maximization; graviton/vector kinetic terms ∝ c/flavor charge; critical points (spin-2/graviton degeneracy) occur at c = 0; c-extremization reproduces 2D central charges on AdS₃×Σ₂ but unitarity generally fails due to unbounded KK towers — though part of parameter space admits positive-norm spin-2 towers with no finite density of states.

Conclusion: Only the Ricci scalar squared deformation admits a consistent, ghost-free, supersymmetric extension of 5D N=2 gauged supergravity within a finite coupling window; the Gauss-Bonnet term is spectrally inert but crucial for satisfying holographic extremization principles; generic curvature-squared extensions violate unitarity, highlighting strong constraints from supersymmetry and locality on higher-derivative completions in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We study the spectra and the central charges of off-shell gauged five-dimensional $\mathcal N=2$ supergravity extended by the Weyl squared, Ricci tensor squared and Ricci scalar squared invariants, and by their Gauss-Bonnet combination. Working exactly in the couplings and keeping all auxiliary fields, we organize the spectra around the maximally supersymmetric AdS$_5$ vacuum into supermultiplets. With the Ricci scalar squared invariant, the spectrum gains a long vector multiplet and is free of ghosts in a finite range of the coupling, while with the Weyl squared or Ricci tensor squared invariant, it gains a long spin-2 multiplet and always contains ghosts or instabilities. The Gauss-Bonnet combination adds no new modes: around its supersymmetric vacuum, the quadratic action is a rescaled copy of that of the two-derivative theory, and this fixes the spectrum of the Ricci tensor squared extension. We also compute the central charges $a$ and $c$ and the flavor central charge exactly in the couplings. The vacuum equation is $a$-maximization, and the kinetic terms of the massless graviton and vector multiplets are proportional to $c$ and to the flavor central charge, so that the critical points, where the massive spin-2 multiplet becomes degenerate with the graviton multiplet, are zeros of $c$. Finally, around supersymmetric AdS$_3\times\Sigma_2$ backgrounds of the Gauss-Bonnet extension, the two-dimensional central charges are reproduced by $c$-extremization, while the spectrum is generically not unitary and contains towers of states whose energies stay bounded at large Kaluza-Klein levels. In part of the parameter space, the tower of spin-2 multiplets has positive norm, so that the spectrum has no finite density of states.

</details>


### [57] [How to find regular black-hole islands in the swamp](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15795)
*Astrid Eichhorn,Pedro G. S. Fernandes,Verena Koeder*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how higher-curvature corrections in effective field theories of gravity can progressively regularize black-hole singularities, imposing monotonicity conditions on metric deviations from Schwarzschild to constrain EFT coefficients; it identifies a consistent 'island' of regularity at curvature order eight and tests whether string theory and asymptotically safe gravity satisfy these constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand how infinite higher-curvature corrections in quantum gravity may resolve black-hole singularities—not abruptly, but gradually across orders of the effective field theory—while respecting a single new-physics scale and maintaining theoretical consistency within the EFT regime.

Method: Imposes monotonicity constraints on metric function deviations from Schwarzschild geometry as a proxy for progressive regularization; derives necessary conditions for 'less singular' behavior on and outside the horizon; applies them to curvature-8 operators in the gravitational EFT; identifies a bounded region ('island') in coefficient space satisfying all five motivated conditions.

Result: Five physically motivated monotonicity-based regularity conditions yield a non-empty, bounded 'island' in the parameter space of dimension-eight curvature invariants; string theory (with known α′ corrections) lies near but outside this island, whereas asymptotically safe gravity exhibits marginal compatibility depending on scheme-dependent beta-function inputs.

Conclusion: Regular black-hole behavior can be encoded in systematic, testable constraints on low-energy EFT coefficients; the existence of a finite 'island' supports the viability of singularity resolution via controlled higher-curvature corrections, but current UV-complete candidates only partially satisfy the derived conditions.

Abstract: An infinite tower of higher-curvature corrections is expected to lead to regular black-hole solutions. We assume that this regular behavior does not abruptly arise, but gradually builds up with each successive order of the effective field theory of gravity. In addition, we assume that the effective field theory comes with a single scale of singularity-resolving new physics. We translate this assumption into a monotonicity requirement for deviations of the metric functions from a Schwarzschild black hole. Under these assumptions, conditions can be formulated which trace the increase of ``less singular'' black-hole behavior on and outside of the black-hole horizon within the regime of validity of the effective field theory. These conditions constrain the effective field theory coefficients. To exemplify this construction, we motivate five specific conditions and find a corresponding island of black hole regularity conditions at order eight in the effective field theory. We also explore whether string theory as well as asymptotically safe gravity lie on this island.

</details>


### [58] [Cosmological correlators and kinematic flow on complex Hartle-Hawking saddles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15868)
*Vikramaditya Mondal,Shinji Mukohyama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes cosmological correlators on complex Lorentzian saddle-point geometries (e.g., Hartle-Hawking) for a conformally coupled scalar with cubic interaction, expressing wave function coefficients as discrete spectral sums over S³ modes; these sums collapse via recursion into rational functions, yielding difference equations (discrete kinematic flow) for tree-level Feynman-Witten diagrams and explicit late-time boundary correlators up to four points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute cosmological correlators on complex, non-real saddle-point geometries (e.g., Hartle-Hawking) without explicit bulk time evolution, and to understand how they transform under shifts of kinematic variables—i.e., define a 'kinematic flow' intrinsic to the geometry.

Method: Analyzes the Lorentzian gravitational path integral over homogeneous isotropic closed universes with Λ > 0; expands a conformally coupled scalar field with cubic interaction around complex saddle points; expresses wave function coefficients as spectral sums over spherical harmonics on S³; identifies summands with Srivastava-Daoust multi-variable hypergeometric functions; derives recursion relations and difference equations for kinematic variables; constructs transformation matrices for discrete flow and computes correlators up to four points.

Result: Wave function coefficients are discrete spectral sums reducible to rational functions via recursions; one- and two-site tree diagrams obey closed difference equations with upper-triangular rational transformation matrices satisfying path-independence; explicit two-, three-, and four-point cosmological correlators are obtained; a late-time difference equation for the four-point function is derived at the conformal boundary.

Conclusion: Cosmological correlators on complex Hartle-Hawking saddles admit a rigorous, discrete kinematic flow formalism rooted in S³ harmonic analysis and hypergeometric recursions—bypassing continuous time evolution—and provide a concrete, computable framework for Lorentzian quantum cosmology beyond semiclassical approximations.

Abstract: The Lorentzian gravitational path integral over homogeneous and isotropic metrics in a closed universe with positive cosmological constant is dominated by complex saddle points, such as the Hartle-Hawking saddles. We ask how to compute cosmological correlators on such complex backgrounds, and how they respond to the shifts of the kinematic variables---the kinematic flow---without tracking the bulk time evolution explicitly. We, specifically, study a conformally coupled scalar test field with a cubic interaction around these saddles. We find that the wave function coefficients can be written as discrete spectral sums with the summands containing the corresponding coefficients in the Einstein static universe, which are obtainable in analogy with the polytopes program for flat spacetime. Whereas the wave function coefficients of the flat FLRW case are given by integrals over shifted kinematics, the discrete spectrum on the three-sphere for Hartle-Hawking saddles produces sums. We identify the coefficients, which depend on the final hypersurface through a phase fixed by its size, with Srivastava-Daoust multi-variable hypergeometric functions. They satisfy recursion relations for any number of sites, under which the sums collapse into rational functions. For the one- and two-site Feynman-Witten tree diagrams, we derive difference equations in the kinematic variables which close on a finite set of sums, giving a discrete kinematic flow. The transformation matrices are upper triangular with rational elements, and satisfy a consistency condition which makes the flow path independent. We then obtain the two-, three- and four-point cosmological correlators. At the late-time conformal boundary, we derive an explicit difference equation for the four-point function.

</details>


### [59] [Cosmological Dressing Rules for Massive Scalars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15870)
*Jonathan Gräfe,Matthias Nowinski,Ivo Sachs,Siva Pawan Venna*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the equivalence of different Schwinger-Keldysh formulations of de Sitter correlation functions as spectral representations of a common Sturm-Liouville problem, and derives explicit dressing rules for scalar fields using Euler-integral auxiliary propagators compatible with flat-space diagrammatics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify seemingly distinct Schwinger-Keldysh formulations (harmonic analysis vs. dressed flat-space correlators) of de Sitter quantum field theory correlation functions under a common mathematical framework.

Method: Identifies the underlying Sturm-Liouville problem governing de Sitter correlators in the Schwinger-Keldysh formalism; derives dressing rules for arbitrary-mass scalars by solving this eigenvalue problem, yielding auxiliary propagators expressed as Euler integrals.

Result: Demonstrates equivalence of harmonic and dressed-flat-space representations as spectral decompositions of the same Sturm-Liouville operator; provides explicit, analytically tractable dressing rules via Euler-integral auxiliary propagators.

Conclusion: The diverse Schwinger-Keldysh representations are unified spectral manifestations of a single Sturm-Liouville structure, enabling systematic application of flat-space diagrammatic techniques to de Sitter QFT through Euler-integral dressing.

Abstract: The Schwinger-Keldysh formulation of de Sitter correlation functions comes in different guises including harmonic analysis and dressed flat space correlators. We observe that these are equivalent spectral representations of the same Sturm-Liouville problem. Starting from the latter we derive dressing rules for scalar fields of arbitrary mass with auxiliary propagators in the form of Euler integrals that are accessible to flat space diagrammatic tools.

</details>


### [60] [Exact Infrared Triangle in Gravity with Long-Range Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15921)
*Sangmin Choi,Ameya Kadhe,Prahar Mitra,Andrea Puhm*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes an exact-in-coupling equivalence between superrotation charge conservation and the massless limit of the classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem in Einstein gravity with massless matter, proposes a tail correction to displacement memory, and computes new higher-order corrections to superrotation charges corresponding to 1-loop exact corrections to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft graviton theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend prior understanding of infrared universality and soft theorems from massive to massless matter, and to uncover exact gravitational coupling structure of long-range dressings and superrotation symmetries.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of asymptotic symmetries, soft limits, and gravitational dressings in asymptotically flat spacetimes; exact treatment of gravitational coupling (i.e., non-perturbative in Newton’s constant G); derivation of conservation laws from boundary symmetry algebras and matching to soft theorems.

Result: 1) Exact equivalence between superrotation charge conservation and massless limit of classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem; 2) A proposed formula for the tail contribution to displacement memory induced by long-range gravitational dressings; 3) New higher-order corrections to superrotation charges, exact in G, corresponding to 1-loop exact corrections to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft graviton theorem.

Conclusion: Long-range gravitational dressings resum exactly in G, revealing deeper infrared structure: superrotation symmetries encode universal soft physics even for massless matter, and their charges govern both memory tails and quantum-corrected soft theorems beyond leading order.

Abstract: Einstein gravity coupled to massless matter exhibits universality in the infrared. In the presence of long-range interactions we show that a novel superrotation charge conservation is equivalent to the massless limit of the classical logarithmic soft graviton theorem, and we propose a formula for the corresponding tail to the displacement memory. These results, which extend earlier work on gravity coupled to massive matter in a qualitatively different way, are exact in the gravitational coupling and reveal that long-range gravitational dressings resum. We also compute higher-order corrections to the known superrotation charge which are exact in the gravitational coupling and would correspond to a 1-loop exact correction to the Cachazo-Strominger subleading soft graviton theorem.

</details>


### [61] [Imaging the two-sided black hole interior with four-point correlators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15933)
*Joydeep Chakravarty*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a smeared boundary four-point function to probe local physics behind the horizon of a two-sided AdS black hole, using high-frequency wavepackets, nontrivial time ordering, and a state-dependent operator; enables imaging of interior features like null shocks and matter shells via signal jumps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an observable that probes local bulk physics behind the event horizon of an AdS black hole—beyond standard boundary correlators—and to image dynamical interior structures such as null shocks and matter shells.

Method: Constructs a boundary four-point function smeared with high-frequency wavepackets; employs geodesic approximation for bulk propagation, nontrivial time ordering of operators, and inserts one state-dependent operator at mid-thermal-circle to access the black hole interior.

Result: The correlator factorizes into a flat-space-like scattering amplitude near a bulk interior point; signal exhibits sharp jumps when the probed bulk point crosses null shocks or matter shells, enabling direct imaging of interior geometry and matter distribution.

Conclusion: This smeared, time-ordered, state-dependent correlator provides a well-defined, local probe of the black hole interior in AdS/CFT, bridging bulk locality, scattering physics, and quantum state dependence.

Abstract: We propose a boundary four-point function smeared with wavepackets, which probes local physics behind the horizon of a two-sided AdS black hole. At high frequencies, boundary wavepackets aimed at an interior bulk point travel along geodesics and scatter locally about the bulk point, factorizing the correlator into a flat-space like scattering amplitude. To obtain this configuration, we utilize a non-trivial time ordering of operators, and also use one state-dependent operator insertion moved halfway across the thermal circle. An interesting property of this observable is that it also probes features of nontrivial states: null shocks and matter shells behind the horizon show up as jumps in the observed signal as the bulk point crosses them, thereby imaging the black hole interior.

</details>


### [62] [Self-Force and Bremsstrahlung in Finite Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15993)
*Kyungsun Lee,Piljin Yi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the self-force and Bremsstrahlung radiation of a point charge in Born–Infeld nonlinear electrodynamics, demonstrating consistency between local self-force and global radiative dissipation, and highlighting how residual nonlinearity modifies effective multipole moments compared to Maxwell theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the classical self-force problem—well-studied in Maxwell electrodynamics—to nonlinear electrodynamics (specifically Born–Infeld), where finite self-energy and field regularity offer new physical insights and resolve divergences present in Maxwell theory.

Method: Analytical computation of both the local self-force (via regularization of the field stress tensor near the worldline) and global radiative energy-momentum loss (via asymptotic flux analysis at null infinity); comparison and consistency check between the two; examination of effective multipole structure induced by nonlinearity.

Result: The local self-force and global radiative dissipation are mutually consistent in Born–Infeld theory; nonlinearity, though asymptotically suppressed in radiation, leaves observable imprints via modified effective multipole moments of the moving charge, leading to measurable deviations from Maxwell predictions.

Conclusion: Born–Infeld electrodynamics provides a physically consistent, finite-energy framework for the self-force problem; the theory reconciles local and global descriptions of radiation reaction, and its nonlinearity induces subtle but robust corrections to electromagnetic response beyond the linear regime.

Abstract: We study the self-force and the accompanying Bremsstrahlung for a moving point charge in nonlinear electrodynamics with finite self-field and self-energy. The self-force in the Maxwell theory has been the subject of many studies, often by renowned physicists. After reviewing Coleman's lucid treatise for Maxwell among them, we wish to explore the same question in the Born--Infeld theory, our prototype. We independently compute the local self-force and the global radiative dissipation, and show how the two are mutually consistent. Despite the nonlinearity turning off for the asymptotic radiative field, its residual effect survives via the effective multipole moments of a moving charge, allowing deviation from Maxwell. We close with a detailed discussion about two different manifestations of the self-force in the literature and how these enter our computation. One of the appendices explores Born--Infeld ModMax as a generalization.

</details>


### [63] [Silent Heavy Fields: An Exclusion Rule for the Cosmological Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16029)
*Shuntaro Aoki,Zhehan Qin,Masahide Yamaguchi,Yuhang Zhu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies an exclusion rule that causes cosmological collider signals to vanish for certain classes of diagrams involving de Sitter invariant heavy sectors coupled covariantly to a single external field; it proves the rule to all loop orders for exchange diagrams and extends it to one-loop polygons, necessitating reevaluation of prior approximate signals, while also computing—for the first time—the leading triangle-loop signal in a chemical-potential-motivated toy model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Heavy fields during inflation may remain 'silent' in primordial correlators despite dynamical evolution ('ticking'), creating blind spots for the cosmological collider probe; the paper aims to systematically identify when such silence occurs.

Method: The authors derive an exclusion rule using spectral decomposition of the heavy-sector two-point function followed by a field redefinition to prove vanishing signals for exchange diagrams to all loop orders; they then extend the result to one-loop polygon diagrams by matching their oscillatory contributions to those of the exchange class.

Result: A rigorous exclusion rule is established under which cosmological collider signals vanish for two diagram classes: (i) exchange diagrams mediated by the heavy sector’s two-point function (to all loops), and (ii) one-loop polygons (via oscillatory matching); previously approximated signals in these classes are invalidated; the leading triangle-loop signal is computed explicitly in a chemical-potential-motivated toy model.

Conclusion: The exclusion rule reveals fundamental limitations of the cosmological collider as a probe of heavy fields—certain couplings and topologies inherently suppress oscillatory signatures—and mandates careful diagrammatic classification before interpreting correlator features; the nonvanishing triangle-loop example demonstrates that detectable signals persist outside the excluded classes.

Abstract: The cosmological collider uses oscillatory signatures in primordial correlators to probe heavy fields during inflation. This probe has blind spots, as heavy fields can keep ticking in time while remaining silent in the correlators. In this letter, we derive an exclusion rule under which cosmological collider signals vanish. The rule covers two classes of diagrams where a de Sitter invariant heavy sector couples covariantly to a single external field at one vertex. We first prove it for exchanges mediated by the two-point function of the heavy sector to all loop orders, using a spectral decomposition followed by a field redefinition. The result then extends to one-loop polygons by matching their oscillatory contributions onto those of the first class. The approximate signals of previously studied processes that fall into these classes must therefore be revisited. As a nonvanishing example, we derive the leading signal of a triangle loop for the first time in a toy model motivated by chemical potentials.

</details>


### [64] [Holographic Counterterms from Restricted Weyl Symmetry in Even-Dimensional Einstein-AdS Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16187)
*Giorgos Anastasiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a symmetry-based renormalization method for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity using asymptotically-preserving infinitesimal bulk Weyl transformations; it constructs a bulk-covariant completion of the Einstein-Hilbert action via Euler density and covariant total derivatives, yielding closed-form, on-shell finite renormalized actions in all even dimensions—including explicit 10D results—and validates agreement with holographic renormalization for black hole geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
