Motivation: To resolve divergences in the gravitational action for even-dimensional asymptotically locally AdS (AlAdS) spacetimes while preserving bulk covariance and conformal symmetry at the boundary, avoiding ad hoc counterterms and extending beyond low-dimensional cases.

Method: Imposes vanishing variation of the bulk action under restricted Weyl transformations (with parameter Σ(X)) modulo finite-boundary terms; solves resulting recursive relations for compensating terms—Euler density and covariant total derivatives—using factorized shifted Laplacian operators; verifies consistency with ambient construction of conformal invariants on Einstein backgrounds.

Result: Derives a closed-form, bulk-covariant renormalized action valid in arbitrary even dimensions; recovers known 4D, 6D, 8D results; provides first explicit 10D completion involving □³; proves on-shell finiteness of Euclidean AlAdS Einstein geometries; confirms finite on-shell actions for 8D and 10D AdS black holes with non-conformally-flat horizons, matching Holographic Renormalization.

Conclusion: The symmetry-driven, Weyl-covariant renormalization scheme is universal for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity, unifying and generalizing prior dimensional-specific approaches, and rigorously establishes on-shell finiteness without reference to boundary counterterms or Fefferman–Graham expansions.

Abstract: We develop a symmetry-based approach to renormalization for even-dimensional Einstein-AdS gravity based on infinitesimal bulk Weyl transformations that preserve its field equations asymptotically. We determine a bulk-covariant completion of the Einstein-Hilbert density by requiring its variation under restricted Weyl transformations with parameter $\Sigma(X)$ to vanish modulo terms whose integrals remain finite as the radial cutoff is removed. This requirement induces two types of compensating terms with fixed couplings: the Euler density and a hierarchy of covariant total derivatives. The resulting recursive relations admit a factorized solution in terms of shifted Laplacians, yielding a closed-form completion in arbitrary even bulk dimensions. On Einstein backgrounds, these expressions agree, after matching normalizations, with those obtained through the ambient construction of conformal invariants. The associated renormalized-volume identity establishes on-shell finiteness for Euclidean AlAdS Einstein geometries in arbitrary even dimensions. The prescription reproduces the known conformally renormalized actions in four, six and eight dimensions and provides an explicit ten-dimensional completion involving powers of the Laplacian up to $\Box^3$. Applications to eight- and ten-dimensional AdS black holes with non-conformally-flat Einstein horizons yield finite on-shell actions in agreement with Holographic Renormalization.

</details>


### [65] [Thermodynamics within and across AdS Gauss--Bonnet holographic CFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16364)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates thermal phase competition in holographic CFTs using 5D AdS Gauss–Bonnet gravity, deriving a boundary first law to identify work conjugate to the dimensionless coupling; it finds first-order transitions between CFTs with different anomaly coefficients, establishes a generalized Clapeyron relation for the coexistence line, identifies critical behavior (entropy gap ∝ ΔT^{1/2}, heat capacity divergence ∝ |T−T_c|^{-2/3}) near vanishing critical charge, and analyzes Hawking–Page and small–large black hole transitions within fixed CFTs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermal stability and phase structure depend on higher-curvature corrections (encoded in the Gauss–Bonnet coupling) and anomaly coefficients across holographically dual CFTs, especially under controlled external sources and unified time parametrization.

Method: Holographic thermodynamics in five-dimensional AdS Gauss–Bonnet gravity; derivation of boundary first law with unit-lapse cylinder clock; analysis of charged spherical gravitational saddles (black holes); identification of coexistence lines via free energy comparison; critical scaling analysis near endpoints and critical points.

Result: A first-order transition between distinct CFTs occurs at fixed radius due to strict concavity; a generalized Clapeyron relation links coexistence line slope to entropy difference; a kink emerges when Einstein gravity endpoint exhibits its own small–large black hole coexistence; critical charge vanishes at finite coupling; entropy gap closes as √|T−T_c| and heat capacity diverges as |T−T_c|^{-2/3} at critical charge; neutral Hawking–Page and charged small–large coexistence surfaces are fully mapped.

Conclusion: Thermal competition in holographic CFTs is governed by anomaly-sensitive, coupling-dependent free energy landscapes, exhibiting rich phase structure—including cross-theory transitions, multi-saddle coexistence, and universal critical exponents—consistent with mean-field scaling modified by gravitational dressing.

Abstract: We study thermal competition within individual holographic conformal field theories (CFTs) and across theories with different anomaly coefficients in five-dimensional AdS Gauss--Bonnet gravity. We derive the work term conjugate to the dimensionless coupling from the boundary first law and use a common boundary clock to compare charged spherical saddles at fixed external source. Varying this source drives a first-order switch between the CFTs at the endpoints of the chosen coupling interval. In the unit-lapse cylinder clock with the zero-type-D vacuum energy, strict concavity at fixed radius reduces the global comparison to these two endpoints. The preferred coupling jumps across the coexistence line, whose slope is related to the entropy difference by a generalized Clapeyron relation. Where the Einstein endpoint itself undergoes small--large coexistence, a third saddle joins the competition and produces a kink in the cross-theory boundary. A compensating change of the external control preserves saddle ordering under finite energy shifts. Within fixed CFTs, we determine the neutral Hawking--Page line and the charged small--large black hole coexistence surface. The critical charge tends to zero at a finite coupling. The entropy gap closes with exponent one half, and the heat capacity diverges with exponent two thirds along a temperature sweep at the critical charge.

</details>


### [66] [Holographic viscous transport in scalar-coupled Gauss-Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16936)
*Tao Ma,Song He,Li Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests horizon-based formulas for shear and bulk viscosities in a 5D Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet holographic model calibrated to lattice QCD. It compares horizon-conserved-current predictions against Kubo formula results from renormalized stress-tensor correlators, finding agreement for η/s minima and validating the unmodified Eling-Oz formula ζ/s = (4π)⁻¹(∂ϕₕ/∂ln s)² even with scalar-dependent coupling and finite Gauss-Bonnet parameter α.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously test the validity of horizon-based prescriptions—particularly the Eling-Oz formula for bulk viscosity and horizon conserved-current methods—for computing transport coefficients in holographic models beyond simple constant-coupling or small-α approximations, using a realistic, UV-complete, lattice-calibrated setup.

Method: Computes renormalized retarded stress-tensor correlators (Kubo) in a 5D Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet black brane background; solves full linearized gravitational/scalar fluctuation equations without horizon conservation assumptions; includes curvature and dilaton counterterms for UV finiteness; compares against horizon conserved-current calculations (including first-order α expansion) and the Eling-Oz formula; uses lattice QCD equation of state to fix model parameters.

Result: Horizon conserved-current method reproduces boundary Kubo η/s exactly for constant coupling; both methods find a minimum in η/s for scalar-dependent coupling; for ζ/s, first-order α truncation underestimates peak height but preserves peak temperature; the unmodified EO formula ζ/s = (4π)⁻¹(∂ϕₕ/∂ln s)² matches Kubo results across couplings, confirming the 1/(4π) prefactor remains valid despite α-induced deviations in η/s.

Conclusion: The horizon-based Eling-Oz formula for bulk viscosity is robust beyond leading-order approximations and holds for scalar-dependent couplings and finite Gauss-Bonnet coupling; this provides strong evidence that horizon prescriptions can be quantitatively reliable when properly benchmarked against boundary correlators in realistic holographic QCD models.

Abstract: We test horizon formulas for the shear and bulk viscosities of a holographic plasma against viscosities extracted directly from retarded stress-tensor correlators. We work in a five-dimensional Einstein-dilaton-Gauss-Bonnet model calibrated to the lattice QCD equation of state at zero baryon density. UV-finite renormalized correlators are obtained by including the required curvature and scalar counterterms, and the fluctuation equations are solved while retaining the full dependence on the Gauss-Bonnet coupling $\alpha$, without using a horizon conservation law or an entropy-production formula. For constant coupling, the horizon conserved-current calculation reproduces the boundary Kubo results in both channels. For the scalar-dependent coupling studied here, both methods find a minimum in the shear-viscosity-to-entropy-density ratio $\eta/s$. In the bulk channel, the conserved-current calculation truncated at first order in $\alpha$ yields a lower peak height in $\zeta/s$, with the peak temperature nearly unchanged relative to the boundary result. For both couplings, the unmodified Eling-Oz (EO) formula, $\zeta/s=(4\pi)^{-1}(\partial\phi_h/\partial\ln s)^2$, reproduces the boundary result for $\zeta/s$, where $\phi_h$ is the horizon value of the scalar. The prefactor $1/(4\pi)$ is thus retained even when the Gauss-Bonnet interaction shifts $\eta/s$ from this value. These results provide a direct boundary benchmark for horizon prescriptions and support the unmodified EO formula beyond constant coupling.

</details>


### [67] [De Sitter Gravity and Emergent Time from Fusion Matrix Product States and SYK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17045)
*Shinsei Ryu,Yu-ki Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a quantum-mechanical model—realized as a matrix-product-state quantum channel related to a chordless limit of the SYK model—that is dual to 2D de Sitter gravity; it exhibits emergent SL(2,R) symmetry matching the future boundary isometry, reproduces the Schwarzian action and JT disk amplitude, and matches Bunch-Davies two-point functions for free scalars, thereby concretely realizing dS/CFT in 2D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a concrete, solvable quantum mechanical model realizing the dS/CFT correspondence for two-dimensional de Sitter gravity—a long-standing conceptual challenge due to the lack of a conventional holographic dual with unitary CFT interpretation.

Method: Constructs a quantum channel via a matrix product state that fuses one spin per step; identifies its low-energy limit as an SL(2,R)-symmetric random walk; maps this walk to the Schwarzian theory; computes the disk partition function and two-point correlators for free massless scalars in dS₂ and compares them with bulk JT gravity predictions.

Result: The model yields the exact disk amplitude of Jackiw-Teitelboim gravity on dS₂; reproduces the Schwarzian effective action for time evolution; matches the Bunch-Davies two-point function; and realizes the future boundary’s SL(2,R) isometry as an emergent symmetry of the quantum channel.

Conclusion: A well-defined, non-perturbative quantum-mechanical dual—based on a chordless SYK-like quantum channel—is established for 2D de Sitter gravity, offering the first explicit realization of dS/CFT with calculable bulk-boundary dictionary and symmetry matching.

Abstract: We propose a quantum-mechanical model dual to two-dimensional de Sitter (dS) gravity. It is a quantum channel, defined by a matrix product state that fuses one spin at each step, and is related to a limit of the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev model without chord crossings. Its simplest part is a random walk with an emergent $SL(2,\mathbb R)$ symmetry, which we identify with the symmetry of the future boundary of dS. It selects a time variable in which the walk obeys the Schwarzian action and yields the disk amplitude of dS Jackiw-Teitelboim gravity. As the simplest case, we consider free massless scalar fields in the fixed background of dS$_2$. We find the agreement of the two-point function in the Bunch-Davies vacuum and the matching of symmetry. Our construction provides a concrete model for the dS/CFT correspondence for two-dimensional de Sitter gravity.

</details>


### [68] [When Nonlocal Total Derivatives Are Not Variationally Trivial](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14612)
*Carlos Heredia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates total time derivatives in infinite-order and nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics, showing that while they remain variationally trivial in the formal infinite-order case, they do not necessarily vanish in the nonlocal Euler–Lagrange expression—even when the Lagrangian is an exact total derivative $L = DW$—and provides a smooth convolution-type counterexample.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the variational role of total time derivatives beyond standard finite-order Lagrangian mechanics, particularly in infinite-order and nonlocal settings where standard variational principles may break down.

Method: Analytical study of variational properties of total time derivatives in formal infinite-order jet bundles and nonlocal functional spaces; construction of a smooth convolution-based nonlocal Lagrangian to demonstrate failure of exactness to imply vanishing Euler–Lagrange expressions.

Result: In infinite-order mechanics, total time derivatives remain variationally trivial; in contrast, for nonlocal functionals, $L = DW$ does not guarantee vanishing of the nonlocal Euler–Lagrange expression—demonstrated via an explicit smooth convolution counterexample.

Conclusion: Exactness ($L = DW$) is insufficient for variational triviality in nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics, revealing a fundamental departure from both classical and infinite-order variational theory.

Abstract: We study total time derivatives in infinite-order and nonlocal Lagrangian mechanics. In the formal infinite-order setting, such terms remain variationally trivial. For nonlocal functionals on trajectories, however, exactness in the form $L=DW$ is not sufficient to ensure a vanishing nonlocal Euler-Lagrange expression. We exhibit a smooth convolution-type counterexample.

</details>


### [69] [A cubic closed string field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14670)
*Harold Erbin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a cubic closed bosonic string field theory using a Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation, introducing an infinite tower of auxiliary k-string fields Σₖ to convert higher-order vertices into cubic couplings and kinetic terms; the resulting action is cubic, gauge-invariant, and recovers the original non-polynomial action at tree level via Gaussian integration, while preserving physical content and satisfying extended IBL∞ relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To circumvent the long-standing no-go theorem (Sonoda & Zwiebach, 1990) that forbids a cubic formulation of closed bosonic string field theory with a single string field, symmetric vertex, and canonical propagator.

Method: Hubbard–Stratonovich transformation introducing auxiliary k-string fields Σₖ (entering at order gₛ^{2k−2}), converting ≥4-point vertices into quadratic kinetic terms and cubic interactions; elimination via Gaussian integrals recovers the original action under invertibility/kernel conditions on auxiliary kinetic operators; worldsheet analysis uses OPE and asymmetric cubic gluing for Σ₂; algebraic structure verified via cyclic L∞ and extended IBL∞ relations.

Result: An explicit, fully consistent cubic closed string field theory at all orders in gₛ; the action is cubic and triangular, preserves the original gauge symmetry without new parameters, introduces no extra physical states (where auxiliary kinetic operators are invertible), and yields entangled multi-string states (e.g., Σ₂ as an entangled two-string state); vertices satisfy extended genus-zero IBL∞ relations iff string products form a cyclic L∞ algebra.

Conclusion: The no-go theorem is evaded not by modifying foundational assumptions but by enlarging the field space with auxiliary fields—yielding a computationally tractable, physically equivalent, and algebraically coherent cubic formulation of closed bosonic SFT.

Abstract: The action of closed bosonic string field theory is non-polynomial, and for more than thirty years a no-go theorem of Sonoda and Zwiebach (1990) has excluded a cubic formulation with a single string field, a symmetric vertex and the canonical propagator. We evade it with a Hubbard--Stratonovich transformation and provide an explicit construction of a cubic closed string field theory at all orders. The transformation introduces an infinite tower of auxiliary $k$-string fields $\Sigma_k$, entering at order $g_s^{2 k - 2}$, which turn the vertices with four or more legs into kinetic terms and cubic couplings. The action is cubic, at most quadratic in each auxiliary field, and triangular: eliminating the auxiliary fields by Gaussian integrals recovers the original action order by order at tree level, under invertibility and kernel conditions on the auxiliary kinetic operators. The original gauge symmetry lifts to the cubic action with no new parameter, and we argue that the auxiliary fields add no physical state at generic momentum, wherever their kinetic operators are invertible. On the worldsheet, $\Sigma_2$ is an entangled two-string state: by an operator product expansion, the quartic form reduces to a gluing of two asymmetric cubic vertices dressed by the motion of the local coordinates and defined on states without level matching. We expect this picture to generalize to higher orders, defining a gluing of patches carrying $k$ punctures each. The string vertices, read as products with several outputs, satisfy an extended form of the genus-zero $\mathrm{IBL}_\infty$ relations, including operations with no input or no output, exactly when the string products form a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra. Finally, we compare our action with the bar--cobar construction of Chiaffrino, Mamade and Zwiebach.

</details>


### [70] [Anatomy of AdS black hole on-shell actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14688)
*Junho Hong,Sourav Roychowdhury*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits regularization of on-shell actions for rotating, charged AdS black holes in gauged supergravity using holographic renormalization and background subtraction, emphasizing the role of the radial cutoff frame. It shows that the asymptotically global AdS frame enables consistent quantum statistical relations (up to a Casimir term in odd dimensions) for conformally flat boundaries (e.g., $S^1 	imes S^{d-2}$), grounding prior holographic comparisons on first principles; background subtraction fails in the same frame due to uncontrolled ambiguities tied to cutoff surface choice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in regularizing on-shell actions of rotating, charged AdS black holes—specifically, to assess the frame-dependence of holographic renormalization versus background subtraction, and to place quantum statistical relations used in holographic thermodynamics on rigorous, first-principles footing.

Method: Systematic comparison of two regularization schemes—holographic renormalization (using counterterms) and background subtraction—applied to rotating, electrically charged AdS$_d$ black holes in gauged supergravity, across dimensions $d=5,6,7$. Focuses on the asymptotically global AdS frame with $S^1 \times S^{d-2}$ conformal boundaries; computes renormalized on-shell actions and checks consistency with the quantum statistical relation.

Result: Holographic renormalization in the asymptotically global AdS frame yields a renormalized on-shell action satisfying the quantum statistical relation up to a dimension-dependent, parameter-independent Casimir term in odd $d$; background subtraction fails to reproduce this result in the same frame—the mismatch is quantified as the difference between the Casimir term and the counterterm contributions, revealing an unresolved ambiguity in cutoff surface definition.

Conclusion: The asymptotically global AdS frame is optimal for holographic renormalization with conformally flat boundaries; background subtraction remains ambiguous without a principled criterion for the cutoff surface; both methods retain ambiguities for non-conformally-flat boundaries.

Abstract: We revisit the regularized on-shell actions of rotating electrically charged AdS black holes of gauged supergravity via holographic renormalization and background subtraction, with a particular emphasis on the frame in which the radial cutoff is placed. For holographic renormalization, the asymptotically global AdS frame, recently employed in seven dimensions, works equally well in five and six dimensions: the renormalized on-shell action of the AdS$_d$ black holes with $S^1\times S^{d-2}$ conformal boundaries satisfies the quantum statistical relation, up to a parameter-independent Casimir term in odd dimensions, and thereby places the holographic comparisons that so far relied on this relation on a first-principles footing. Background subtraction, in contrast, fails to reproduce this result in the same frame; the mismatch, which we identify exactly in terms of the Casimir term and the counterterms, demands a criterion for the cutoff surface yet to be established. For precision holography, our results settle the choice of frame for holographic renormalization with conformally flat boundaries and delineate the ambiguities that remain in background subtraction and in holographic renormalization for boundaries that are not conformally flat.

</details>


### [71] [On Tunnel Fusion and Yangian $R$-matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14793)
*Dmitry Galakhov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores the role of the upper-triangular factor U from the Gauss decomposition of the rational R-matrix in Yangian symmetry, linking algebraic (factorization twist/universalizer), physical (Berry phase in SUSY quiver QFT), and geometric (renormalized stable envelope) interpretations; it derives recursion relations for tunneling amplitudes, reveals channel intertwinement, cancellations, and wall-crossing, and focuses on Y(sl₂) (higher-spin/non-evaluation) and Y(sl_{n+1}) (fundamental).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify algebraic, physical, and geometric perspectives on the Gauss factor U of the rational R-matrix, resolve subtleties in the Yang-Baxter equation arising from tunneling amplitudes in SUSY quiver QFT, and understand amplitude structure—especially intertwinement, cancellation, and parameter-dependent channel decomposition (wall-crossing)—in integrable spin chains with nontrivial Yangian representations.

Method: Uses R-matrix formalism and Gauss decomposition R=LDU; analyzes U as: (i) algebraic factorization twist conjugating pseudo-coproduct ℰ to true coproduct Δ; (ii) Berry phase computed via sum over tunneling amplitudes in 2D N=(2,2) SUSY quiver gauge theory associated with 𝔤’s Dynkin diagram; (iii) renormalized Maulik–Okounkov stable envelope. Derives recursion relations for collective tunneling amplitudes and studies their analytic dependence and channel decomposition.

Result: Establishes equivalence of three distinct realizations of U; shows YBE follows from flatness of Berry connection (guaranteed by SUSY); uncovers nontrivial constraints on tunneling amplitudes imposed by YBE; constructs explicit recursion relations yielding higher amplitudes from elementary ones; demonstrates amplitude intertwinement across channels, leading to enhanced cancellations and wall-crossing behavior—i.e., piecewise-analytic representation of amplitudes depending on moduli/parameters.

Conclusion: The Gauss factor U serves as a unifying bridge between representation theory, quantum field theory, and enumerative geometry; its physical realization as a Berry phase subject to YBE constraints reveals deep connections between integrability, supersymmetry, and wall-crossing phenomena in quiver gauge theories; results are concretely verified for Y(sl₂) (beyond evaluation modules) and Y(sl_{n+1}) (fundamental representation).

Abstract: XXX spin-chain Hamiltonians can be derived within the $R$-matrix formalism. For a simple Lie algebra $\mathfrak{g}$, the rational $R$-matrix $R$ intertwines the coproduct $\Delta$ in the Yangian $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{g})$. $R$ admits a particular Gauss decomposition $R=LDU$, where the upper-triangular matrix $U$ can be obtained from the lower-triangular matrix $L$. We treat $U$ in three ways. Algebraically, $U$ is known as a factorization twist. It plays the role of a ``universalizer'': it conjugates a non-universal pseudo-coproduct $\mathscr{D}$ in the molten crystal chain basis to the true Yangian coproduct $\Delta=U^{-1}\mathscr{D} U$. Physically, $U$ can be obtained as a Berry phase in SUSY quiver QFT for a quiver associated with the Dynkin diagram of $\mathfrak{g}$. In this case, $U$ is calculated as a sum over tunneling amplitudes. Geometrically, $U$ can be interpreted as a renormalized Maulik-Okounkov stable envelope localized at a fixed point. In the physical setting, the Yang-Baxter equation (YBE), which ensures integrability, follows from the flatness of the Berry connection, which in turn follows from supersymmetry. However, the YBE imposes subtle constraints on the tunneling amplitudes, which we examine here. We construct recursion relations that allow higher collective amplitudes to be obtained from more elementary ones. We observe that different tunneling channels are in fact intertwined, leading to finer cancellations among amplitudes and to wall-crossing phenomena: depending on the parameters, the same amplitude is represented as a sum over different channels. We focus on $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ with higher-spin and non-evaluation representations, as well as on the fundamental representation of $\mathsf{Y}(\mathfrak{sl}_{n+1})$.

</details>


### [72] [Casimir Attraction and Repulsion in a Magnetic Domain-Wall Background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14799)
*Inés Cavero-Peláez,Beatriz Gómez-López,Jose M. Muñoz-Castañeda,Gonzalo Sancho-Garrido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the one-loop Casimir interaction between two δ–δ′ plates embedded in a fixed sine-Gordon kink (Pöschl–Teller) background, modeling transverse fluctuations of an S² sigma model / easy-axis antiferromagnet with a domain wall. It finds exact factorization of the background-dressed determinant, orientation-dependent sign of interaction per mode, and non-monotonic position- and separation-dependent attraction/repulsion — with energy and force changing sign at different locations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how topological backgrounds (e.g., kinks) modify quantum vacuum (Casimir) forces between localized boundary conditions (δ–δ′ plates), particularly in models with internal symmetry (S² sigma model) and no classical plate–wall coupling.

Method: One-loop effective action computation using zeta-function regularization; spectral analysis of background-dressed fluctuation operators; exact factorization of the relative functional determinant via Gel’fand–Yaglom method; analysis of sign structure of mode contributions and force/energy dependence on plate positions and couplings (v, w).

Result: The relative determinant factorises into position- and gradient-dependent effective plate strengths; interaction sign per frequency channel is fixed by plate orientation; for identical oriented plates, translation induces switch from attraction (near kink) to repulsion (away); energy and force change sign at distinct positions; for v=1.05, w=0.5, d=1.5, energy zeros at c≈−0.44, 2.63 and force zeros at c≈−0.30, 2.00.

Conclusion: Casimir interactions in topological backgrounds are highly nontrivial: they depend sensitively on background geometry, plate orientation, and coupling strength; strict stability (v > 2|w|) ensures absence of zero modes and consistency of the Gaussian theory; energy sign alone is insufficient to infer attraction/repulsion — the force must be used.

Abstract: We study the one-loop Casimir interaction between two $\delta$--$\delta'$ plates in the fixed one-soliton Pöschl--Teller background of the sine-Gordon kink. The transverse fluctuation problem is the Gaussian truncation of an $S^2$ sigma model, equivalently an easy-axis antiferromagnet with a domain wall, in which the plates exert no classical force on the wall. For $v\ge2|w|$ the fluctuation operators are non-negative, and strictly stable couplings, $v>2|w|$, also exclude zero modes, so the quadratic field theory is consistent at the fixed sine-Gordon mass. The background-dressed relative determinant factorises exactly into effective, gradient-dependent strengths of the two plates, and the sign of each frequency channel of the interaction energy is fixed by their orientation. Two identical, equally oriented, strictly stable plates switch from attraction near the kink to repulsion away from it under a rigid translation at fixed couplings and separation. The interaction energy and the force conjugate to the separation neither share their sign everywhere nor change sign at the same positions: there are regions of negative energy and repulsive force, so attraction and repulsion must be read from the force. For such plates with $w\neq0$ the force is repulsive at short and attractive at large separation, and the interaction energy has a minimum at a separation that depends on the position of the pair. For $v=1.05$, $w=0.5$ and $d=1.5$ the energy changes sign at $c\simeq-0.44$ and $2.63$, and the force at $c\simeq-0.30$ and $2.00$.

</details>


### [73] [Solving spectral flow in AdS$_3$: from covering maps to pencils](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15070)
*Sergio Iguri,Nicolas Kovensky,Julian H. Toro*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper solves the long-standing problem of computing arbitrary $n$-point correlators of spectrally flowed (winding) vertex operators in the SL(2,R) WZW model dual to string theory on AdS₃ with pure NS-NS flux, by introducing a novel 'pencil of rational maps' that universally unwinds insertions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute generic $n$-point correlation functions of spectrally flowed (winding) string vertex operators in the exactly solvable SL(2,R) WZW model — a key setting for AdS₃/CFT₂ duality with pure NS-NS flux — where only up to three-point functions were previously known, and the four-point case remained conjectural.

Method: Introduces and exploits a 'pencil of rational maps' from the worldsheet (Riemann sphere) to the AdS₃ boundary (also a sphere), which generically exists for arbitrary insertion points, simultaneously trivializes all spectral flow charges (i.e., unwinds all operators), and reduces to the standard covering map when the latter is defined; correlators are then expressed as unflowed correlators twisted by this map.

Result: All $n$-point correlators of spectrally flowed operators — for arbitrary $n$, arbitrary individual windings, and generic worldsheet positions — are explicitly constructed in closed form via reduction to unflowed correlators; the Dei–Eberhardt four-point conjecture is rigorously proven as a special case.

Conclusion: The spectral flow symmetry of the SL(2,R) WZW model can be fully leveraged for correlation functions via geometric uniformization using rational maps, resolving the winding string correlator problem completely and establishing a unified framework beyond the covering map paradigm.

Abstract: Strings in AdS$_3$ with pure NS-NS flux are described by the exactly solvable SL(2,$\mathbb{R}$) WZW model, yet correlators of winding strings, given by spectrally flowed operators, were so far known only up to three points. We obtain all of them, for any number of insertions and windings, in terms of unflowed correlators, proving the four-point conjecture of Dei and Eberhardt. The key ingredient is a pencil of rational maps from the worldsheet to the boundary which exists for generic insertion points, unwinds all insertions at once, and collapses to the covering map when the latter exists.

</details>


### [74] [Solitons of the intermediate long wave equation with residue source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15072)
*Yuto Ishii,Atsushi Nakamula,Soma Yoshida*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the intermediate long-wave (ILW) equation using Sato hyperfunctions, introducing a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources arising from zeros of tau-functions inside analytic contours; it classifies soliton sectors via a residue index, constructs exact multi-soliton solutions (including mixed elevation–depression types), proves residue-index additivity and collisional invariance (barring contour-crossing), and links spectral decay rates in Fourier space to distances of complex singularities—revealing vanishing decay at odd-multiple boundaries between residue sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in handling principal-value integrals in the bilinear ILW formulation by leveraging Sato hyperfunction theory, and to systematically incorporate zero-induced residue contributions—thereby generalizing the ILW framework to include self-consistent sources and richer soliton structures beyond the conventional source-free case.

Method: Interprets ILW principal-value integrals via Sato hyperfunctions; defines a residue index counting tau-function zeros within analytic strips; constructs exact tau-functions for one- and multi-solitons (including factorised mixed-type configurations); performs Fourier-space spectral analysis of soliton solutions to relate exponential decay rate to location of nearest complex singularity.

Result: Derives a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources determined by tau-function zeros; classifies solitons into residue-indexed sectors (elevation-solitons: zero index; depression/higher branches: non-zero); obtains exact multisoliton solutions with additive, collision-invariant residue indices; establishes exponential spectral decay ∼exp(−γ|k|) where γ equals the minimal distance from real axis to nearest complex singularity of the tau-function; shows γ → 0 at odd-multiple boundaries between residue sectors.

Conclusion: The residue-index formalism provides a rigorous, classification-ready extension of the ILW hierarchy grounded in hyperfunction theory; soliton dynamics and spectral properties are fully encoded in the analytic structure of tau-functions, with residue index serving as a topological invariant under elastic collisions—offering a unified perspective on elevation/depression duality and sector transitions.

Abstract: We revisit the bilinear formulation of the intermediate long-wave (ILW) equation by interpreting its principal-value integrals in the framework of Sato hyperfunctions. When the tau-functions have no zeros inside the relevant contours, the conventional source-free ILW equation is recovered. When zeros enter the contours, their residue contributions must instead be retained, leading naturally to a residue-extended ILW system with self-consistent sources determined by the tau-functions themselves. We introduce a residue index that counts the zeros enclosed in each analytic strip and use it to classify the resulting soliton sectors. The conventional elevation-soliton belongs to the zero-residue sector, whereas depression-solitons and higher elevation branches generally carry non-zero residue indices. Exact one- and multisoliton tau-functions are constructed, including mixed elevation--depression configurations with factorised two-body interactions. Their residue indices are additive and remain invariant during collisions, provided that no zero crosses a contour boundary. A Fourier-space analysis shows that the soliton spectra decay exponentially at large wavenumbers, with a decay rate determined by the distance of the nearest complex singularity from the real axis. This rate vanishes as an odd-multiple boundary between adjacent residue sectors is approached, providing a direct spectral signature of the transition between them.

</details>


### [75] [$n$-Gon Correlators in $d$-dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15147)
*Yong-Xiang Su,Dongyu Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the analytic structure of scalar one-loop cosmological n-gon correlators using integrand-level reduction and a Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators; it determines normalization factors for local leading singularities, constructs explicit dlog forms, evaluates discontinuities involving elliptic integrals, and relates tube cuts to cosmological dressing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure of cosmological correlators beyond local leading singularities, particularly how integration domains influence physical observables such as discontinuities and transcendental functions in de Sitter space.

Method: Integrand-level reduction and Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators; evaluation of external-energy discontinuities in collinear kinematics; comparison of successive tube-energy discontinuities in d=3 and d=4; mapping to cosmological dressing representation.

Result: At d=n, algebraic normalization factors for local leading singularities are determined and explicit dlog forms constructed for arbitrary n; for the complete box (d=n=4), a nonvanishing complete elliptic integral of the second kind arises from summing time orderings; tube cuts are related to cosmological dressing.

Conclusion: Integration domains play a crucial role in shaping the analytic structure of cosmological correlators—beyond local leading singularities—governing discontinuities, transcendental weight, and connections to cosmological wavefunction representations.

Abstract: We study the analytic structure of scalar one-loop cosmological $n$-gon correlators using an integrand-level reduction and a Baikov representation adapted to linear tube-energy denominators. At spatial dimension $d=n$, we determine the algebraic factors that normalize their local leading singularities to unity and construct explicit $d\log$ forms for arbitrary $n$. For the complete box at $d=n=4$, we evaluate an external-energy discontinuity in a collinear kinematic family and find a nonvanishing complete elliptic integral of the second kind after summing all time orderings. We also compare successive tube-energy discontinuities in $d=3$ and $d=4$, and relate selected tube cuts to the cosmological dressing representation. These results illustrate the importance of integration domains in determining the analytic structure beyond local leading singularities.

</details>


### [76] [Canonical Commutation Relations and Poisson Brackets with Non-locality in Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15173)
*Yasu Gen,Hiroaki Matsunaga,Jojiro Totsuka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a string-inspired nonlocal field theory with time-shifted kinetic terms, constructs a conserved symplectic structure from boundary variations, derives Poisson and quantum commutators via Schwinger–Dyson, and shows that canonical quantization requires taking the local limit σ→0—not λ→0—to recover standard commutation relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand canonical structure and quantization of nonlocal field theories inspired by string theory, where kinetic terms couple fields at finite time separations—challenging standard locality-based canonical formalism.

Method: Variational analysis of the action to extract boundary terms and define a conserved symplectic form; derivation of positive-time Poisson bracket; application of Schwinger–Dyson equations to relate commutators to the Poisson bracket; careful limiting analysis of λ→0 vs σ→0.

Result: The commutator exhibits a discontinuous jump of magnitude iℏ at time separation s = σ, vanishes identically for 0 < s < σ for any λ > 0, and only recovers the standard free-field commutator in the local limit σ → 0 (at fixed λ), not in the weak-coupling limit λ → 0 (at fixed σ).

Conclusion: Locality (σ → 0) is essential for recovering standard canonical quantization; nonlocality introduces an intrinsic time-scale σ that fundamentally alters causal and algebraic structure of observables, rendering λ → 0 insufficient to restore local commutation relations.

Abstract: We study the canonical structure of a string-inspired model in which the kinetic term couples fields at times separated by a finite shift $\sigma$, with a coupling $\lambda$ to a fixed background. From the boundary term in the variation of the action, we construct a conserved symplectic form and derive the positive-time Poisson bracket. Using the Schwinger--Dyson relation, we show that at positive time separation $s$ the commutator equals $i\hbar$ times this bracket. The canonical normalization $\hbar$ appears as a jump of the commutator expectation value at $s=\sigma$, which is equal time for shifted variables with a local kinetic term; for $0<s<\sigma$ the commutator vanishes for every $\lambda>0$. Hence $\lambda\to0$ at fixed $\sigma$ does not reproduce the free commutator on this interval, whereas $\sigma\to0$ at fixed $\lambda$ recovers the local commutator at every fixed $s>0$.

</details>


### [77] [Simplicity of the Three-Loop Six-Point ABJM Amplitude](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15193)
*Hanyu Fang,Chia-Kai Kuo,Kai Yan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper computes the three-loop six-point amplitude in planar ABJM theory using negative geometries, finding it is infrared finite and exactly proportional to the one-loop amplitude (−π² times), implying no three-loop correction to the collinear anomalous dimension; the full amplitude is fully determined by lower-loop data and this coefficient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the structure and simplicity of scattering amplitudes in planar ABJM theory, particularly whether iterative or hidden symmetry-based relations exist between loop orders, and to probe infrared behavior and dual conformal invariance at three loops.

Method: Uses negative geometries to compute the three-loop six-point amplitude; isolates the single contributing bipartite chain in the logarithm; analyzes dual-conformal invariance and local finiteness of the integrand; integrates the chain and restores disconnected lower-loop products to reconstruct the full amplitude.

Result: The integrated bipartite chain is IR finite and equals −π² × one-loop amplitude; the collinear anomalous dimension receives no three-loop correction; the full three-loop amplitude is completely fixed by lower-loop data and the coefficient −π².

Conclusion: The six-point ABJM amplitude exhibits unexpected simplicity at three loops, suggesting an iterative odd-even loop relation and reinforcing connections between geometry, integrability, and infrared structure in ABJM theory.

Abstract: We compute the three-loop six-point amplitude in planar ABJM theory using negative geometries. At this order, only a single bipartite chain contributes to the logarithm of the amplitude, and we find that its dual-conformally invariant integrand is locally finite. The integrated chain is therefore infrared finite and, remarkably, equals exactly $-\pi^2$ times the one-loop amplitude. Hence the collinear anomalous dimension, like the cusp, receives no three-loop correction. Restoring the disconnected products of lower-loop contributions, which carry all the infrared divergences, we obtain the full three-loop amplitude, fixed entirely by lower-loop data and the coefficient $-\pi^2$. These results reveal an unexpected simplicity of the six-point ABJM amplitude and suggest an iterative relation between its odd- and even-loop terms.

</details>


### [78] [D(-1)/D7 bound states in supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15423)
*Gongrui Cheng,Klaas Parmentier,Thomas Van Riet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs supersymmetric D(-1)/D7 bound states in Euclidean type IIB supergravity, resolving prior issues with supersymmetry and smearing by reducing the BPS equations to holomorphic data; it identifies a distinguished solution with constant dilaton interpolating between asymptotic vacuum and a controlled $\mathbb{R} \times S^1 \times \mathbb{T}^8$ scaling limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unresolved issues in prior supergravity constructions of D(-1)/D7 bound states—specifically, ambiguities in ten-dimensional Euclidean supersymmetry and reliance on partial codimension-one smearing—while providing a rigorous, fully localized (codimension-two), BPS-compliant description aligned with the open-string instanton realization in 8D super Yang–Mills by Billò et al.

Method: Systematic analysis of Euclidean type IIB supergravity BPS equations for D(-1)/D7 bound states; reduction of supersymmetry conditions to constraints on two holomorphic functions plus one complex algebraic constraint; classification of local codimension-two solutions; consistency check via D3-brane flux quantization and D7 worldvolume coupling analysis.

Result: Full classification of local codimension-two supersymmetric D(-1)/D7 solutions governed by two holomorphic functions satisfying a single complex constraint; identification of a physically distinguished solution with constant dilaton, asymptoting to flat space at infinity and flowing to a controlled $\mathbb{R} \times S^1 \times \mathbb{T}^8$ geometry in the near-core scaling limit; confirmation that required D3-brane fluxes are consistent with D7-brane worldvolume Chern–Simons and Wess–Zumino couplings.

Conclusion: The construction provides the first complete, fully localized, supersymmetric supergravity dual of eight-dimensional instantons realized as D(-1)/D7 bound states, with the constant-dilaton solution offering a geometrically well-behaved holographic avatar suitable for further study of 8D SYM dynamics and non-perturbative string dualities.

Abstract: We construct D($-$1)/D7 bound states in Euclidean type IIB supergravity motivated by the string-theory setup of Billò et al., whose open-string description realizes instantons in eight-dimensional super Yang--Mills theory. Previous supergravity constructions either left unresolved issues concerning ten-dimensional Euclidean supersymmetry or relied on partial smearing to codimension one. We classify the local codimension-two solutions by showing that the supersymmetric field equations reduce to two holomorphic functions subject to a single complex constraint. The candidate bound-state solutions require complementary D3-brane fluxes, consistent with the D7 worldvolume analysis. A particularly interesting solution has constant dilaton and connects the vacuum at infinity to a controlled scaling limit in which the geometry becomes $\mathbb R\times S^1\times\mathbb T^8$.

</details>


### [79] [A ménage à quatre without strings attached](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15424)
*Iosif Bena,Gongrui Cheng,Klaas Parmentier,Dimitrios Toulikas,Thomas Van Riet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a supersymmetric D0-D8-D4-D4' quadruple bound state in type II supergravity where Hanany-Witten strings are absent due to a quadratic flux constraint; it is realized both as a bulk supergravity solution and as a domain-wall solution in the non-Abelian DBI action of D8-branes, with matching Killing spinors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between individual Hanany-Witten string production by brane crossings and their mutual cancellation in multi-brane configurations—specifically, to find stable, string-free bound states of intersecting D-branes preserving supersymmetry.

Method: Constructs an explicit supergravity background for a D0-D8-D4-D4' system; imposes the no-Hanany-Witten-string condition (vanishing net NSNS B-field sourcing) to derive a quadratic flux constraint; solves the non-Abelian DBI action for a fluxed D8-brane stack as a domain wall interpolating between two asymptotic supersymmetric backgrounds; matches bulk and worldvolume Killing spinors to confirm supersymmetry preservation.

Result: A fully explicit, supersymmetric supergravity solution for a D0-D8-D4-D4' quadruple with vanishing HW strings; derivation of a quadratic relation among RR and NSNS fluxes ensuring no string creation; realization of the system as a BPS domain wall in the D8-brane effective theory with precise charge matching across the wall.

Conclusion: Multiple HW-producing brane intersections can form a stable, supersymmetric, string-free bound state when their fluxes satisfy a specific quadratic constraint; this demonstrates nontrivial consistency between bulk supergravity and non-Abelian brane dynamics, with implications for brane engineering and holographic dualities.

Abstract: When two D-branes with eight mixed Dirichlet-Neumann directions cross, a fundamental string is created. This is the Hanany-Witten (HW) effect. In supergravity, the string shows up as an electric NSNS B-field sourced by the RR fields of the two branes. We show that two brane pairs, each of which would create an HW string on its own, can combine into a quadruple bound state with no HW strings attached. We explicitly construct a background with the fluxes of such a D0-D8-D4-D4' quadruple. Requiring no HW strings imposes a quadratic relation among the fluxes. We also realize this bound state as a supersymmetric solution of the non-Abelian DBI action of the D8-branes, whose Killing spinors match those of the bulk supergravity solution. The fluxed D8 stack acts as a domain wall between two supersymmetric D0-D8-D4-D4' backgrounds. Each background satisfies the no-string constraint, and the jumps in the fluxes give the charges carried by the stack.

</details>


### [80] [Radiation from scattering on multiple shockwaves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15468)
*Tim Adamo,Anton Ilderton,Hannah Kingham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper exactly computes radiation from a charged probe scattering on multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves, recovering the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) suppression for radially symmetric shocks (e.g., ultraboosted Coulomb fields), and revealing stronger spectral suppression for impulsive plane waves in the LPM regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond standard approximations (linearized scatterers, high-energy probes, soft photons) in modeling the LPM effect by constructing an exactly solvable multi-scattering setup.

Method: Exact computation of elastic scattering and single-photon emission amplitudes for a charged particle interacting with multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves; radiation density is doubly-averaged over transverse positions and lightfront time; analysis performed for radially symmetric shock profiles and source-free impulsive plane waves.

Result: Exact recovery of LPM suppression for radially symmetric transverse shock profiles (including ultraboosted Coulomb field) under general kinematics; discovery of significantly enhanced spectral suppression in the LPM regime for impulsive/ultra-short plane wave backgrounds.

Conclusion: The shockwave model provides an exactly solvable framework for multi-scattering radiation that generalizes the LPM effect beyond conventional approximations and reveals stronger interference-driven suppression in idealized plane-wave limits.

Abstract: It is well known that radiation produced by scattering an electron off multiple sources depends on the length of the mean free path between these scatterings. When this is sufficiently small, the spectrum is suppressed due to destructive interference, a famous result known as the Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) effect. This is usually modelled approximately, with scattering centres treated linearly (e.g., as screened Coulomb potentials), high-energy probes and soft emitted photons. In this paper, we consider a scenario where multiple scatterings can be computed exactly: charged probe scattering on multiple null electromagnetic shockwaves. Here, the amplitudes for elastic scattering and single photon emission can be determined exactly, for arbitrary kinematics, in terms of the shock profiles in the transverse plane. We use these amplitudes to compute the radiation density (doubly-averaged over shock locations in the transverse plane and lightfront time) in the regimes of independent and interfering scatterings. For shock profiles which are radially symmetric in the transverse plane (including the ultraboosted Coulomb field), we recover the known LPM suppression, albeit with more general kinematics. In addition, our setup allows us to consider the source-free case of multiple scattering on impulsive/ultra-short plane waves, where we find much stronger suppression of the spectrum in the LPM regime.

</details>


### [81] [Numerical Thermodynamics of AdS Black Holes with Higher-Order Mass Corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15470)
*Saad Eddine Baddis,Adil Belhaj,Hajar Belmahi,Yassine Hassouni,Maryem Jemri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs new asymptotically AdS black hole solutions with a higher-order mass deformation term, and numerically investigates their thermodynamic properties—including stability, P–v criticality, and phase transitions—using CUDA-accelerated simulations for n = 3, 4, 5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend black hole solutions in anti-de Sitter spacetime by incorporating insights from noncommutative geometry and quintessence, enabling exploration of novel thermodynamic behavior via higher-order mass deformations.

Method: Analytical construction of asymptotically AdS black hole metrics with a deformed mass term ∝ aM^{n−1}/r^n; thermodynamic analysis performed via parallelized numerical simulations using custom CUDA codes due to analytical intractability.

Result: Numerical evidence for thermodynamic stability regimes, van der Waals–like P–v critical behavior, and first- and second-order phase transitions depending on n and parameter space (a, M, r).

Conclusion: The introduced mass deformation yields rich thermodynamic structure analogous to fluid systems, suggesting that higher-order geometric corrections can qualitatively alter black hole phase structure—even in asymptotically AdS settings—opening avenues for holographic interpretations of noncommutative or dark energy–inspired gravity models.

Abstract: Inspired by noncommutative geometry and quintessence, we construct a new class of asymptotically anti-de Sitter black hole solutions characterized by a higher-order mass deformation term $\frac{aM^{n-1}}{r^n}$ involving two parameters, $a$ and $n$. Due to analytical difficulties, we approach the underlying thermodynamics through parallel programming simulations using CUDA codes. By considering models with $n=3$, $n=4$, and $n=5$, we numerically study certain thermodynamic properties, including the stability, the $P$-$v$ criticality, and the phase transitions.

</details>


### [82] [BPS invariants from resurgent topological string](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15480)
*Jie Gu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews the conjectural link between resurgence in topological string free energies and BPS invariants in type II string theory on Calabi-Yau threefolds, identifying Borel singularities with D-brane central charges and Stokes constants with generalized DT invariants; it demonstrates the correspondence via trans-series solutions of holomorphic anomaly equations, discusses moduli/polarization dependence, and cites supporting evidence from exact WKB, blowup equations, Stokes automorphisms, and wall-crossing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To elucidate and consolidate the conjectural relationship between the resurgent structure of topological string perturbative expansions and enumerative BPS invariants (generalized Donaldson-Thomas invariants) in type II string compactifications on Calabi-Yau threefolds.

Method: Review and synthesis of recent developments: analysis of Borel–Ecalle resummation of topological string free energies; derivation of non-perturbative amplitudes via trans-series solutions to the holomorphic anomaly equations; examination of Stokes phenomena in flat coordinates and dual partition functions; cross-validation using exact WKB, blowup equations, Kontsevich–Soibelman wall-crossing, and modular/monodromy properties.

Result: Establishes that Borel singularities of topological string free energies occur at integral periods corresponding to D-brane central charges, and that Stokes constants compute BPS indices; demonstrates consistency across local and compact Calabi-Yau geometries, under changes of polarization and moduli; provides multiple independent lines of evidence supporting the resurgence–BPS correspondence.

Conclusion: The resurgence–BPS correspondence is strongly supported across diverse mathematical and physical frameworks, yet lacks a general proof; key open problems include rigorous derivation of non-perturbative completions, physical interpretation of non-perturbative amplitudes, and extension to arbitrary Calabi-Yau backgrounds.

Abstract: We review recent progress on the conjectural relation between the resurgent structure of topological string free energies and BPS invariants of type II string theory compactified on the same Calabi-Yau threefold. In this picture, Borel singularities are located at integral periods associated with central charges of stable D-brane bound states, while Stokes constants encode their BPS indices, or generalized DT invariants. After introducing resurgence and perturbative topological strings, we explain how trans-series solutions of the holomorphic anomaly equations determine non-perturbative amplitudes and illustrate the proposed correspondence in local and compact geometries. We discuss the behavior under changes of polarization and moduli, and review supporting arguments based on exact WKB analysis, blowup equations, the Stokes automorphisms of flat coordinates and dual partition functions, and Kontsevich-Soibelman wall-crossing. We conclude with open questions concerning a general proof, the interpretation of non-perturbative amplitudes, and the construction of non-perturbative completions.

</details>


### [83] [Holographic dual of the 2d Ising model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15484)
*Kazumi Okuyama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes a holographic duality between the 2D Ising model on a torus and a Turaev-Viro topological quantum field theory (TQFT) on a solid torus, where bulk geometry is partitioned into core, sandwich, and bedding surface regions to encode defect states, string-net boundary states, and Ising spin degrees of freedom respectively.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise holographic correspondence for the critical 2D Ising model — a paradigmatic example of a conformal field theory — by realizing it as a topological bulk theory (Turaev-Viro TQFT), thereby bridging statistical mechanics, topological order, and holography.

Method: Constructs a discretized bulk spacetime (solid torus) divided into three geometric regions: (i) a core region preparing a topological defect state |𝒟⟩ on a topological boundary 𝔻; (ii) a sandwich region computing the Turaev-Viro state sum ⟨𝒟|Π|ℱ⟩ between |𝒟⟩ and a physical boundary state |ℱ⟩ encoded via the Aasen-Fendley-Mong string-net construction on boundary 𝔹; and (iii) a bedding surface 𝕊 hosting Ising height variables (spins). The bulk is triangulated by a 2-skeleton P interpolating 𝒟 and ℱ.

Result: A concrete geometric and algebraic realization of the 2D Ising model’s partition function as a Turaev-Viro state sum with defect insertion; identification of the bedding surface 𝕊 as the natural locus for Ising spin degrees of freedom; and interpretation of the 2-skeleton P as a worldsheet for the string-net when defects are absent.

Conclusion: The Ising model on a torus admits a rigorous holographic dual in terms of a defect-enriched Turaev-Viro TQFT on a solid torus, where spatial structure (core/sandwich/bedding), topological data (defects, fusion diagrams), and lattice degrees of freedom (spins on 𝕊) are mutually consistent and geometrically embedded.

Abstract: We propose a holographic dual of the 2d Ising model on a torus as the Turaev-Viro TQFT on a solid torus. The bulk spacetime is divided into the ``core'' region and the ``sandwich'' region. The core region prepares a defect state $|\mathcal{D}\rangle$ on the topological boundary $\mathbb{D}$. The sandwich region computes the Turaev-Viro state sum $\langle\mathcal{D}|\Pi|\mathcal{F}\rangle$ between the state $|\mathcal{D}\rangle$ on $\mathbb{D}$ and the state $|\mathcal{F}\rangle$ on the physical boundary $\mathbb{B}$. The fusion diagram (or string-net) $\mathcal{F}$ on $\mathbb{B}$ is given by the Aasen-Fendley-Mong construction. There is also a ``bedding surface'' $\mathbb{S}$ between $\mathbb{D}$ and $\mathbb{B}$, and the height variables (or Ising spins inside/outside of the domain wall) are naturally living on $\mathbb{S}$. The bulk spacetime is discretized by the 2-skeleton $P$ which interpolates $\mathcal{D}$ and $\mathcal{F}$. When the defect $\mathcal{D}$ is absent, the 2-skeleton $P$ ends on the bedding surface $\mathbb{S}$ and $P$ is interpreted as the bulk worldsheet of the string-net $\mathcal{F}$.

</details>


### [84] [1-loop partition function of 11d supergravity on $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_k$: matching $A(k)$ in ABJM free energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15510)
*Stefan A. Kurlyand,Arkady A. Tseytlin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves a mismatch between the 1-loop supergravity partition function on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ and the exact ABJM free energy A(k) by including previously omitted 3-form fluctuations from harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean AdS₄; the corrected, ℤₖ-projected 1-loop free energy—expressed via superconformal characters and zeta-regularized—exactly reproduces A(k); the same method successfully extends to the ADHM theory dual to AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₚ with localized 7d vector multiplet contributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the discrepancy between the known exact large-N free energy A(k) of ABJM theory (from localization) and the earlier 1-loop supergravity computation on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ, which failed to match due to an incomplete treatment of the 3-form field spectrum.

Method: Identifies missing 1-loop contribution from square-integrable harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean AdS₄ tensored with S⁷ modes—uncaptured by standard KK reduction—and incorporates it alongside the conventional KK tower expressed in superconformal characters; applies ℤₖ (or ℤₚ) orbifold projection and evaluates the total 1-loop free energy using zeta-function regularization; extends the analysis to ADHM theory by including localized KK modes of the 7d SU(p) vector multiplet supported on the fixed-locus singularity AdS₄×S³.

Result: The augmented 1-loop supergravity free energy on AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₖ exactly reproduces the localization result A(k); similarly, for AdS₄×S⁷/ℤₚ, inclusion of localized vector multiplet modes yields agreement with the ADHM-theory counterpart of A(k).

Conclusion: The mismatch stemmed from incompleteness of the standard KK spectrum for the 3-form field; properly accounting for boundary-supported harmonic modes and localized degrees of freedom restores precision matching between holographic 1-loop supergravity and exact field-theoretic results, reinforcing the consistency of AdS₄/CFT₃ duality beyond leading order.

Abstract: The function $A(k)$ appearing in the large $N$ expansion of free energy of ABJM theory on $S^3$ is known exactly from localization. An earlier calculation of the 1-loop partition function of 11d supergravity on $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb Z_k$, based on the standard KK spectrum on $S^7$, did not reproduce it. We identify a missing 1-loop supergravity contribution as that of the 3-form field fluctuations built from square-integrable harmonic 2-forms on Euclidean $\mathrm{AdS}_4$ tensored with $S^7$ modes, which are not captured by the KK spectrum. Adding it to the KK tower contribution expressed in terms of superconformal characters and applying the $\mathbb{Z}_k$ projection, the total expression simplifies, and its evaluation in a zeta-function prescription reproduces the expression for $A(k)$. We also apply a similar approach to the 3d ADHM theory whose M-theory dual is the orbifold $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^7/\mathbb{Z}_p$, which has singularity along the fixed locus $\mathrm{AdS}_4\times S^3$, supporting a 7d $SU(p)$ vector multiplet. Adding the localized KK modes of this multiplet to the $\mathbb{Z}_p$-projected supergravity spectrum gives a 1-loop free energy that reproduces the ADHM localization counterpart of $A(k)$.

</details>


### [85] [Schwinger-Unruh Interplay and Mode-Resolved Entanglement of Charged Scalar Fields in Rindler Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15623)
*Shagun Kaushal,Himanshu Parihar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates entanglement structure of a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime under parallel electric and magnetic fields, focusing on pairwise and cross-horizon correlations; it finds that cross-wedge antiparticle–particle pairs can become separable (despite nonzero mutual information), while intra-wedge pairs remain entangled under Schwinger squeezing, and identifies collective Gaussian modes carrying total right–left wedge entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external electromagnetic fields—particularly parallel E and B fields—affect quantum entanglement structure across Rindler horizons, especially the interplay between Landau quantization, Schwinger pair production, and vacuum-induced correlations in accelerated frames.

Method: Analyzes a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime with parallel electric and magnetic fields; constructs the global vacuum in the negative-frequency sector; derives an outgoing four-mode Gaussian state; computes logarithmic negativity and quantum mutual information for specific mode pairs; applies Gaussian Schmidt decomposition to identify collective entangled modes across the full right–left wedge partition.

Result: Cross-wedge antiparticle–particle pairs are entangled only if q₂ < tanh r_U and may become separable while retaining nonzero mutual information; intra-wedge particle–antiparticle pairs stay entangled for nonzero Schwinger squeezing; total right–left wedge entanglement is carried by exactly two collective Gaussian modes, irrespective of separability of individual mode pairs; results depend on Landau level index n via effective mass Mₙ² = m² + (2n+1)|qB|.

Conclusion: Entanglement in this non-inertial, field-dressed setting is mode- and vacuum-structured: separability of local mode pairs does not imply absence of global bipartite (right–left) entanglement, which resides robustly in collective Gaussian modes—highlighting the necessity of mode-resolved, Gaussian-state analysis beyond single-pair measures.

Abstract: We study pairwise and cross-horizon correlations of a charged scalar field in Rindler spacetime in the presence of parallel electric and magnetic fields. The transverse magnetic field produces Landau levels, while the longitudinal mode problem follows the charged-scalar Rindler construction with the effective mass $M_n^2=m^2+(2n+1)|qB|$. For a specified global vacuum in the negative-frequency sector, we obtain the outgoing four-mode Gaussian state and calculate logarithmic negativity and quantum mutual information for selected mode pairs. We find that the cross-wedge antiparticle-particle pair is entangled only when $q_2<\tanh r_U$. It can therefore become separable although its mutual information remains nonzero. The intra-wedge particle-antiparticle pair remains entangled for nonzero Schwinger squeezing. Using the Gaussian Schmidt decomposition, we show that the entanglement across the complete right--left wedge partition is carried by a pair of collective modes, even when the selected outgoing cross-wedge pair becomes separable. Using the Gaussian Schmidt decomposition, we show that the entanglement across the complete right--left wedge partition is carried by a pair of collective modes, even when the selected outgoing cross-wedge pair becomes separable. We discuss the Landau-level dependence and the limits of these mode-resolved conclusions, without identifying the vacuum-dependent mixing parameter with a detector temperature.

</details>


### [86] [Entropy and charge transport limit Mpemba preparation in holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15654)
*Yu Shi,Sheng Long*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the Mpemba effect—faster relaxation to equilibrium from a more distant initial state—in a holographic superconductor with full gravitational backreaction. It identifies entropy reserves tied to slow scalar field dynamics near criticality, quantifies preparation costs for state initialization, and derives fundamental time and flux bounds rooted in horizon geometry and charge transport.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand non-monotonic relaxation (Mpemba effect) and its thermodynamic cost in strongly coupled quantum systems via holography, particularly how initial-state preparation—including slow scalar modes and exterior charge—affects relaxation speed and entropy evolution.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of holographic superconductors with full metric and gauge field backreaction; analysis of horizon area/entropy responses to scalar perturbations; asymptotic expansion near critical slow branch; derivation of flux-cost bounds using covariant current conservation and horizon regularity constraints.

Result: 1) Boundary drives can invert horizon entropy ordering post-quench at fixed final energy and charge; 2) Critical slow states exhibit O(ε⁴) entropy reserve due to cancellation of quadratic horizon area response; 3) Preparation time for suppressing leading scalar and charge scales as ≥ C ε⁻²; 4) Charge transport enforces a minimum scalar flux cost, tightening the time bound.

Conclusion: The Mpemba relaxation advantage in this holographic setting is tightly constrained by geometric and hydrodynamic constraints at the black hole horizon: entropy reserves are highly suppressed near criticality, and preparation requires diverging time and flux cost as initial scalar amplitude ε → 0—revealing intrinsic trade-offs between non-equilibrium speed and thermodynamic cost in strongly coupled superconductors.

Abstract: We study Mpemba relaxation and its preparation cost in a holographic superconductor with full backreaction. At matched final energy and charge, boundary drives can reverse the ordering of the remaining horizon entropy after source removal. In separate numerical comparisons, some protocols reach a given tolerance earlier even when driving and waiting times are included. To examine the limits of preparation, we consider critical states that already carry a slow scalar field and exterior charge. Along the critical slow branch at fixed energy and charge, horizon displacement cancels the quadratic area response, leaving an entropy reserve of $O(\varepsilon^4)$ for an initial scalar amplitude $\varepsilon$. The same coefficient connects this reserve to the entropy advantage of the nearby superconducting equilibrium and the entropy weight of the slow tail on the normal side. This limited reserve constrains how quickly the existing field can be removed. For releases that suppress the leading horizon field and exterior charge, the preparation time grows at least as $\varepsilon^{-2}$ under horizon regularity and uniform radial control. Charge transport constrains the same covariant scalar flux that produces horizon entropy, raising the minimum flux cost and strengthening the time bound.

</details>


### [87] [Triple-Massive-Exchange Bispectrum of Quasi-Single-Field Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15660)
*Xingang Chen,Zhong-Zhi Xianyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides an exact, non-perturbative analytic solution for the triple-exchange bispectrum in quasi-single-field inflation using Mellin-Barnes representation applied to the mixed propagator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The triple-exchange bispectrum in scalar quasi-single-field inflation had remained analytically unsolved across its full parameter space despite advances in cosmological collider physics.

Method: Direct application of the Mellin-Barnes representation to the mixed propagator between massless and massive fields, yielding a convergent double series solution.

Result: An exact, non-perturbative, and physically transparent analytic expression for the triple-exchange bispectrum, valid for general mass and mixing strength.

Conclusion: The method is generalizable and resolves a long-standing problem in primordial non-Gaussianity computation, enhancing predictive power in cosmological collider physics.

Abstract: The triple-exchange bispectrum of the scalar quasi-single-field inflation model has resisted analytic evaluation across its full parameter space since its advent, despite rapid progress in cosmological collider physics. In this Letter, we solve it for general mass and mixing strength as a convergent double series, by applying the Mellin-Barnes representation directly to the mixed propagator between the massless and massive modes. The result is exact, non-perturbative, and physically transparent; the method is broadly applicable.

</details>


### [88] [Quantum and classical thermal equilibrium for probabilistic classical fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15749)
*C. Wetterich*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explores how classical field probability distributions evolve in time and can be described using quantum formalism; it constructs a 'classical-quantum map' for free fields, distinguishes Rayleigh-Jeans vs. Planck spectra as dynamically selected equilibria under interactions, and uses numerical simulations of interacting probabilistic classical fields to determine which equilibrium emerges asymptotically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how classical field statistics—particularly thermal equilibria (Rayleigh-Jeans vs. Planck)—emerge from underlying probabilistic dynamics, and whether their selection is fundamental or dynamically determined when interactions are present.

Method: Constructs a rigorous classical-quantum map linking classical probability distributions to quantum density matrices for free fields; analyzes momentum dependence of energy and field correlations in classical vs. quantum thermal equilibria; employs numerical simulations of interacting probabilistic classical fields to study asymptotic dynamical selection.

Result: Demonstrates that the Rayleigh-Jeans and Planck spectra correspond to distinct classical probability distributions; shows that their selection is not fundamental but governed by interaction-driven asymptotic dynamics; provides an explicit classical-quantum map for free fields.

Conclusion: Quantum field theory arises as a subsystem of probabilistic classical field dynamics; thermal spectra are dynamically selected under interactions, and the classical-quantum correspondence is exact for free fields via the constructed map.

Abstract: The time evolution of the probability distribution for classical fields can be described by the quantum formalism. For free classical fields the free quantum field theory arises as a subsystem. Different probability distributions for classical fields correspond to the classical thermal equilibrium with the Rayleigh-Jeans divergence or the quantum thermal equilibrium with the Planck spectrum. The selection of the Rayleigh-Jeans or Planck spectrum is not a question of fundamental concepts. It becomes an issue of dynamical selection once interactions are present. We compare the momentum dependence of the classical energy and field correlations for the classical and quantum equilibrium. This permits to decide which equilibrium is selected as a result of asymptotic dynamics by numerical simulations of the probabilistic classical fields including interactions. For free probabilistic classical fields we construct a general ``classical-quantum map'' between the classical probability distribution and the density matrix of the quantum field theory.

</details>


### [89] [Harmonic superspace and Kaluza-Klein reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15782)
*Nikita Zaigraev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends Kaluza-Klein reduction to harmonic superspace to construct off-shell superfield formulations of massive BPS multiplets in 4D, N=2 (and subsequently N=1), preserving manifest supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity via deformed harmonic derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain gauge-invariant, off-shell superfield descriptions of massive higher-spin BPS multiplets in 4D, N=2 and N=1 supersymmetry — a longstanding challenge due to difficulties in maintaining both off-shell closure and gauge invariance for massive theories.

Method: Deforms harmonic derivatives by mass terms within harmonic superspace to implement Kaluza-Klein reduction from 5D, N=1 to 4D, preserving off-shell N=2 supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity; applied to free 5D Maxwell and linearized 5D Poincaré supergravity.

Result: Derives unconstrained off-shell harmonic-superspace actions for the massive BPS 4D, N=2 vector and supergravity multiplets, featuring analytic Stückelberg-type gauge symmetry; shows compatibility with reduction to 4D, N=1 superspace.

Conclusion: The deformed harmonic derivative approach provides a systematic, technically simple route to gauge-invariant off-shell formulations of massive BPS higher-spin multiplets in 4D, N=2 and paves the way for analogous N=1 constructions.

Abstract: We extend the Kaluza-Klein reduction procedure to the framework of harmonic superspace and apply it to the construction of off-shell superfield formulations of supersymmetric gauge multiplets. The reduction is implemented by a deformation of the harmonic derivatives by mass terms, while ensuring that manifest off-shell supersymmetry and Grassmann analyticity are preserved at all stages. As a first application, we reduce the free \(5D,\mathcal{N}=1\) Maxwell theory and obtain an off-shell superfield description of the BPS \(4D,\mathcal{N}=2\) massive vector supermultiplet. The resulting formulation naturally incorporates an analytic Stückelberg-type gauge symmetry.
