We then reduce linearized \(5D,\mathcal{N}=1\) Poincaré supergravity and derive the unconstrained harmonic-superspace action for the BPS \(4D,\mathcal{N}=2\) massive supergravity multiplet. The technical simplicity of the procedure stems from the structure of gauge theories in harmonic superspace, where the gauge symmetry is naturally encoded in analytic prepotentials and harmonic derivatives. The approach presented in this paper opens a direct route to gauge-invariant off-shell superfield formulations of massive higher-spin $4D,\mathcal{N}=2$ BPS multiplets. Furthermore, the resulting theories can be reformulated in $4D,\mathcal{N}=1$ superspace, opening the way to gauge-invariant off-shell descriptions of $4D,\mathcal{N}=1$ massive higher-spin theories.

</details>


### [90] [Swing Surfaces in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15854)
*Jacqueline Caminiti,Sabrina Pasterski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes holographic entanglement entropy in the flat limit of AdS₃/CFT₂, showing the standard Ryu-Takayanagi (RT) formula lacks a well-defined flat-space limit due to issues with the state-operator map; it then constructs an AdS₃ uplift of the 'swing' prescription and provides two information-theoretic interpretations: (i) swing surface length equals mutual information between nearly null boundary intervals in specific pure CFT states, linked to deep-bulk scattering, and (ii) swing surfaces are formally related to black hole inner horizons’ information-theoretic meaning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity in defining holographic entanglement entropy in asymptotically flat 3D gravity via the flat limit of AdS₃/CFT₂, particularly addressing the failure of the Ryu-Takayanagi formula in that limit and seeking a consistent information-theoretic foundation for the recently proposed 'swing surfaces'.

Method: Analytic continuation and limiting procedures applied to AdS₃ geometry and RT surfaces; construction of an explicit AdS₃ uplift of the swing prescription; computation of mutual information for nearly null boundary intervals in pure CFT₂ states using conformal symmetry and bulk reconstruction; conceptual mapping between swing surfaces and black hole inner horizons via geometric and entropic analogies.

Result: The Ryu-Takayanagi formula does not admit a smooth flat-space limit due to breakdown of the state-operator correspondence; the swing surface length in the uplifted AdS₃ setup computes boundary mutual information for specific null-separated intervals; this mutual information is holographically dual to scattering processes localized near the AdS₃ center; swing surfaces are formally equivalent—under certain identifications—to inner horizons of BTZ black holes, suggesting their interpretation may hinge on understanding quantum extremal inner horizons.

Conclusion: Swing surfaces provide a viable, information-theoretically grounded generalization of RT surfaces for flat-space holography, but their physical meaning is intrinsically tied either to fine-grained null-boundary correlations or to unresolved questions about inner horizon entropy and quantum extremality in AdS/CFT.

Abstract: We study holographic entanglement entropy in the flat limit of AdS$_3$/CFT$_2$, and clarify the relation between Ryu-Takayanagi surfaces and the swing surfaces proposed for flat-space holography. We show that the ordinary RT formula does not admit a well-defined flat-space limit and connect this to subtleties of the state-operator map in the flat limit. We then construct an AdS$_3$ uplift of the swing prescription and develop its corresponding information-theoretic interpretation through two complementary approaches. First, we show that the swing surface length computes the mutual information between suitably chosen nearly null boundary intervals in a special class of pure CFT states. The gravity interpretation of this result involves scattering processes which take place deep inside of the AdS$_3$ bulk. Second, we reduce the problem of finding an information-theoretic interpretation for swing surfaces to the more general problem of finding an information-theoretic interpretation of black hole inner horizons in holography.

</details>


### [91] [$α'^2$-corrections to the bosonic string from an action-level double copy map](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15862)
*Eric Lescano,Agustina Pereyra Grau,Jesús A. Rodríguez*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a double-copy description of higher-derivative corrections (Riem² and Riem³) to the bosonic string effective action, showing that matching the cubic Riem³ vertex requires both an F³ gauge-theory operator and a deformed color-to-kinematics map—leading uniquely to a left–right symmetric (YM + F³) ⊗ (YM + F³) structure, which correctly reproduces key string couplings at α′ and α′² orders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically derive bosonic string higher-derivative corrections (Riem², Riem³) from gauge-theory building blocks via the double copy, addressing the challenge that standard color-kinematics duality fails for Riem³ without modification.

Method: Constructs a cubic-order double-copy action by introducing: (i) a higher-derivative gauge-theory Lagrangian containing an F³ operator (up to field redefinitions), and (ii) a deformed color-to-kinematics replacement rule where structure constants are mapped to a three-momentum tensor. Matching is performed in transverse-traceless gauge, modulo field redefinitions.

Result: The Riem³ vertex cannot be obtained with the standard color-kinematics map; inclusion of the F³ operator and the deformed map yields a unique (double-root) solution enforcing left–right symmetry — (YM + F³) ⊗ (YM + F³). This reproduces the cubic Riem H² coupling at O(α′), vanishing six-derivative Kalb–Ramond couplings at O(α′²), and absence of odd-H interactions — all consistent with bosonic string effective action.

Conclusion: The cubic bosonic string effective action (up to α′²) admits a precise double-copy construction only when both a specific higher-derivative gauge-theory operator (F³) and a deformed kinematic algebra are simultaneously introduced — establishing a constrained, symmetry-enforced correspondence between string amplitudes and double-copied gauge-theory data.

Abstract: The low-energy effective action of the bosonic string contains higher-derivative corrections of the form Riem$^2$ and Riem$^3$ to the Einstein--Hilbert action, together with the associated Kalb--Ramond couplings. We construct an action-level double copy description of these corrections up to cubic order in the fields. Two ingredients are required: a higher-derivative gauge-theory Lagrangian, which up to field redefinitions is controlled by a single $F^3$ coupling, and a deformation of the color-to-kinematics replacement of the structure constants by the corresponding three-momentum tensor. We show that no higher-derivative gauge-theory operator can produce the Riem$^3$ vertex with the standard replacement rule, and that the matching conditions for the gauge-theory coupling and the deformation of the map admit a unique solution, a double root, which renders the cubic action left--right symmetric, of the form $(\mathrm{YM}+F^3)\otimes(\mathrm{YM}+F^3)$. Without further input, the resulting map reproduces the cubic $\mathrm{Riem}\,H^2$ coupling of the bosonic string at order $\alpha'$, the absence of six-derivative cubic Kalb--Ramond couplings at order $\alpha'^2$, and the absence of interactions odd in the Kalb--Ramond field. The comparison is performed in transverse-traceless gauge, up to field redefinitions.

</details>


### [92] [Subsystem Flow: Quantum Error Correction, Petz Recovery, and Ergodic Inclusions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15887)
*Yidong Chen,Nima Lashkari,Kwing Lam Leung*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bridge between quantum error correction (QEC) and modular theory in quantum many-body systems and QFT, introducing 'modular autonomy' as a criterion for subsystem preservation under modular flow; it defines the 'subsystem flow' to quantify deviation from autonomy, links it to relative entropy loss and Petz recovery, constructs 'inclusion parent Hamiltonians', proves ergodicity for standard QFT subregion inclusions, connects $L^2$-nuclearity to spectral discreteness and spectral gaps in associated dissipative dynamics, and proposes using eigenstructures of these Hamiltonians as local chaos diagnostics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify quantum error correction with modular theory in local quantum systems by interpreting subsystem preservation under modular flow (i.e., modular autonomy) as a natural QEC condition — where protection of subsystem N against erasure of its complement is equivalent to invariance under the modular dynamics of M in a reference state — and to develop quantitative tools (subsystem flow, history algebra, persistent core) to characterize deviations from this ideal error-correcting regime.

Method: Uses Tomita–Takesaki modular theory for von Neumann algebras; defines subsystem flow via infinitesimal generator of modular automorphism group restricted to a subsystem inclusion N ⊂ M; analyzes analytic continuation to construct Petz recovery maps and inclusion parent Hamiltonians; employs $L^2$-nuclearity to derive spectral properties; studies ergodicity via generation and retention properties of modular orbits; constructs a dissipative semigroup from repeated Petz recovery and analyzes its fixed-point algebra (modular persistent core) and spectral gap.

Result: Modular autonomy is equivalent to QEC of N against complement erasure; subsystem flow quantifies relative-entropy loss away from autonomy; Petz recovery maps arise from analytic continuation of subsystem flow; inclusion parent Hamiltonians generically drive N outside M and can generate the full algebra M ∨ M'; standard QFT subregion inclusions are ergodic; $L^2$-nuclearity implies discrete spectrum for parent Hamiltonians and spectral gap for the associated dissipative semigroup; the fixed-point algebra of the semigroup is the modular persistent core.

Conclusion: Modular dynamics provides an intrinsic, state-dependent framework for quantum error correction in physical systems; the subsystem flow and derived structures (history algebra, persistent core, parent Hamiltonians) constitute a complete diagnostic toolkit for autonomy, recoverability, and chaos; ergodicity and nuclearity conditions yield concrete spectral consequences, supporting a conjecture that eigenvalues/eigenvectors of inclusion parent Hamiltonians serve as local measures of quantum chaos.

Abstract: In quantum error correction (QEC), the goal is to engineer an encoding that protects logical information against the erasure of a physical subsystem. In local many-body quantum systems, by contrast, nature provides a subsystem inclusion $\mN\subset \mM$ in a fixed reference state $\ket{\Omega}$. Asking whether the inclusion admits a reference-preserving code that protects subsystem $\mN$ against erasure of the complement is equivalent to asking whether $\mN$ is preserved under the modular dynamics of $\mM$ in this state (modular autonomy). We define the subsystem flow, which measures the departure from this error correction regime. Away from autonomy, the infinitesimal flow quantifies relative-entropy loss, while the history algebra and persistent core describe which observables the modular orbit generates and retains. We call an inclusion ergodic when modular orbits generate everything and retain nothing. Analytic continuation of subsystem flow yields Petz recovery maps and a family of positive inclusion parent Hamiltonians. We show that, in general, evolving with these parent Hamiltonians allows $\mN$ to leave $\mM$, and in some cases, it can generate the entire $\mM\vee\mM'$. Inclusions with a common cyclic and separating vector generate everything, and standard inclusions of factors, such as those of nested subregions in QFT, are ergodic. Moreover, the $L^2$-nuclearity property implies that the spectrum of parent Hamiltonians is discrete. We define a dissipative semigroup by repeating the Petz recovery map, whose fixed-point algebra is the modular persistent core and whose spectrum is gapped under the assumption of $L^2$-nuclearity. We conjecture that the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of inclusion parent Hamiltonians can be used to define local spectral measures of quantum chaos.

</details>


### [93] [Magnetic-slope crossovers in the QCD bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15929)
*Aalok Misra,Shivam S. Kushwah,Keshav Dasgupta,Charles Gale,Sangyong Jeon*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper predicts a double sign reversal in the magnetic-field dependence of the longitudinal bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio ζ∥/η∥ across the QCD phase transition, using a top-down M-theory model, kinetic theory with quasiparticle truncation, and consistency checks against lattice QCD equation-of-state data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-time transport coefficients—especially magnetic-field-dependent dissipation in quark-gluon matter—are inaccessible to first-principles lattice simulations; hence, a theoretical framework bridging holography, kinetic theory, and lattice constraints is needed.

Method: Combines a top-down MQGP-inspired string-theoretic model of thermal QCD-like plasma, a kinetic-theory identity under single-mode quasiparticle truncation, and compatibility matching to lattice equation-of-state (e.g., speed-of-sound) data; cross-validates with bottom-up VQCD and phase-appropriate carriers (quark-gluon plasma above T₀, chiral pion gas below).

Result: Predicts two sign reversals in ∂B(ζ∥/η∥) vs. T: negative → positive → negative, occurring at T₁ < T₀ < T₂, where T₀ is the pseudo-critical deconfinement temperature; T₁ existence hinges on a pion-gas scattering rate inequality; lattice speed-of-sound data and VQCD support qualitative consistency.

Conclusion: The sign-reversal pattern is robust across complementary approaches (holographic, kinetic, lattice-constrained), suggesting a universal magnetic-response feature tied to the QCD phase structure—potentially testable via heavy-ion collision observables sensitive to longitudinal dissipation.

Abstract: How a magnetic field alters dissipation in quark--gluon matter is almost unconstrained from first principles, since real-time transport is not directly accessible to lattice simulation. Using a top-down MQGP-inspired M/string-theory construction of a QCD-like thermal plasma, together with a kinetic-theory identity within a specified single-mode quasiparticle truncation and a compatibility construction using available lattice equation-of-state data, we propose a normalization-insensitive sign prediction that the field slope of the longitudinal bulk-to-shear viscosity ratio \zeta_\parallel/\eta_\parallel reverses sign twice with temperature---below and above the deconfinement pseudo-critical temperature T_0, at T_1<T_0<T_2 (the lower reversal T_1 conditional on a stated pion-gas rate inequality). A kinetic-theory identity with phase-appropriate carriers, a quark--gluon plasma above T_0 and a chiral pion gas below, reproduces the same pattern, and lattice speed-of-sound data can be shown to be consistent as well. The bottom-up VQCD in the deconfined phase supports a negative slope for large temperatures; the slope crossover is not resolvable from the available \zeta_\parallel/s, \eta_\parallel/s plots.

</details>


### [94] [Extension of Poincare superalgebra via hidden symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15946)
*Ümit Ertem*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the Poincaré superalgebra by incorporating Killing-Yano forms and Killing spinors, constructing a symmetry superalgebra where the odd part includes special odd Killing-Yano and even closed conformal Killing-Yano forms, and the even part comprises Killing spinors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify geometric symmetries—specifically Killing vector fields, their higher-form generalizations (Killing-Yano forms), and spinorial counterparts (Killing spinors)—into a coherent supersymmetric algebraic structure mirroring the Poincaré superalgebra.

Method: The authors define appropriate graded Lie brackets between Killing spinors (even part) and special odd Killing-Yano forms / even closed conformal Killing-Yano forms (odd part), verifying superalgebra axioms including graded Jacobi identity and compatibility with the underlying manifold’s geometry.

Result: A well-defined extension of the Poincaré superalgebra is constructed, where hidden symmetries encoded by Killing-Yano forms and Killing spinors jointly generate a symmetry superalgebra.

Conclusion: Killing spinors and suitably restricted Killing-Yano forms form a consistent superalgebraic structure extending the Poincaré superalgebra, thereby providing a geometric realization of supersymmetry beyond standard spacetime isometries.

Abstract: Isometries of a manifold and special types of spinors called Killing spinors which are related to Killing vector fields constitute a symmetry superalgebra structure corresponding to Poincare superalgebra. The antisymmetric generalization of Killing vector fields to higher degree forms which are called Killing-Yano forms are considered. The odd part of the Poincare superalgebra is extended from the space of Killing vector fields and their rotational representation to the space of special odd Killing-Yano forms and special even closed conformal Killing-Yano forms. The even part is again corresponds to the space of Killing spinors. By defining the relevant brackets, we show that hidden symmetries and Killing spinors together define an extension of the Poincare superalgebra.

</details>


### [95] [Anti-periodic Gravitino and Dirac Effective Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16047)
*Karim Benakli*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an effective superspace operator for Dirac gravitino masses in 5D $N=1$ supergravity compactified on $S^1/\mathbb{Z}_2$, using gauge-invariant gravitino curvature superfields and a nonlocal $\Box^{-1}$ kernel; physical KK Dirac masses emerge as poles in two-point functions of these superfields, and the fermionic propagator matrix matches $N=1$ superspace invariants on a background with nonzero radion auxiliary field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To realize Dirac gravitino masses—enabled by a preserved $U(1)_R$ symmetry that forbids Majorana masses—within a consistent, local, and supersymmetric effective field theory framework, particularly in extra-dimensional supergravity.

Method: Constructs a spin-$3/2$ bilinear effective operator in $N=1$ superspace using gravitino curvature superfields; employs nonlocal kernel $\Box^{-1}$ to maintain gauge invariance; analyzes minimal 5D $N=1$ supergravity on $S^1/\mathbb{Z}_2$ with Scherk-Schwarz-induced anti-periodic boundary conditions; computes two-point functions of curvature superfields with endpoint-localized sources; separates physical and gauge modes to extract a $16\times16$ fermionic propagator kernel; verifies matching with fermionic components of two $N=1$ superspace invariants—including spinor contractions and normalization—on a background with nonzero radion auxiliary field.

Result: The Dirac gravitino mass operator is realized via gauge-invariant curvature superfields coupled by $\Box^{-1}$; physical Kaluza-Klein Dirac masses appear as poles in curvature superfield two-point functions; the quadratic fermionic kernel is explicitly identified as a $16\times16$ matrix whose entries precisely reproduce the fermionic components (including index structure and normalization) of two known $N=1$ superspace invariants.

Conclusion: A consistent, supersymmetric, and geometrically realized effective description of Dirac gravitino masses is achieved in warped/compactified $N=1$ supergravity, linking nonlocal superfield operators, Scherk-Schwarz boundary conditions, and invariant-based fermionic structure—providing a template for R-symmetric supergravity phenomenology beyond the MSSM.

Abstract: Introducing a preserved $U(1)_R$ symmetry forbids gravitini Majorana masses while allowing a Dirac mass. We investigate how a Dirac mass for gravitini can be described by an effective operator in $N=1$ superspace, in analogy with the well-known Dirac gaugino mass. We find that the corresponding spin-$3/2$ bilinear operator can be expressed in terms of gauge-invariant gravitino curvature superfields coupled through a nonlocal kernel involving an inverse d'Alembertian. We study this construction in minimal five-dimensional supergravity compactified on $S^1/\mathbb Z_2$. The gravitino satisfies anti-periodic boundary conditions induced by a Scherk-Schwarz twist. The physical Kaluza-Klein Dirac masses then appear as poles in the two-point functions of the curvature superfields associated with sources localized at the endpoints. Separation of physical and gauge modes relates these propagators to a quadratic fermionic kernel represented by a $16\times16$ matrix. Its elements match the fermionic components of two invariants written in $N=1$ superspace, including their spinor contractions and normalization. This matching is carried out on a fixed background with a nonzero radion auxiliary field.

</details>


### [96] [Holographic Wilsonian RG of Yang-Mills theory in AdS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16074)
*Soner Albayrak*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the stochastic quantization–Wilsonian renormalization duality to non-Abelian gauge theories in AdS space, identifying the radial coordinate as stochastic time; it establishes that the Langevin distribution equals the product of UV/IR radial wavefunctions at cutoff ε = t, interprets the process as a Doob transform, refines the stochastic–holographic dictionary (e.g., radial field ↔ Zwanziger’s gauge-fixing function, radial gauge ↔ Parisi–Wu quantization), and computes finite-cutoff holographic Wilsonian couplings (triple- and quadruple-trace) matching known IR correlators and flat-space YM amplitudes at the energy pole.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the equilibrium-evolution duality between stochastic quantization and Wilsonian RG to strongly coupled non-Abelian gauge theories in curved spacetime—specifically anti-de Sitter space—where holography provides a natural framework for RG flow interpretation via the radial direction.

Method: Analytic derivation using stochastic quantization (Langevin dynamics) applied to bulk non-Abelian gauge fields in AdS; identification of radial evolution with stochastic time; Doob transform analysis to relate Langevin distribution to ground-state wavefunction product; explicit computation of holographic Wilsonian effective action at finite radial cutoff; mapping of bulk gauge choices to stochastic gauge-fixing prescriptions (Zwanziger, Parisi–Wu).

Result: 1) Exact all-order identification of Langevin distribution with UV × IR radial wavefunctions at cutoff ε = t; 2) Radial field component identified as Zwanziger’s stochastic gauge-fixing function; 3) Radial gauge ↔ Parisi–Wu quantization; bulk gauge A_z = α⁻¹∂_i A_i ↔ Zwanziger’s gauge; 4) Closed-form expressions for triple- and quadruple-trace couplings in holographic Wilsonian action; 5) IR limit reproduces gluon n-point functions; energy-pole limit recovers flat-space Yang–Mills scattering amplitudes.

Conclusion: The stochastic–holographic duality is robustly extended to non-Abelian gauge theories in AdS, unifying Wilsonian RG, stochastic quantization, and holography; the radial direction acts as stochastic time, and the Doob-transformed Langevin process encodes the ground-state evolution; the refined dictionary enables precise computation of finite-cutoff holographic actions and connects to physical observables including scattering amplitudes.

Abstract: In this work, we extend the equilibrium-evolution duality between stochastic quantization and Wilsonian renormalization to non-abelian gauge theories in anti-de Sitter space, for which the radial coordinate of AdS plays the role of the stochastic time. We show, to all orders in the fields for bulk theories without explicit radial dependence, that the Langevin distribution at time $t$ is the product of the ultraviolet and infrared radial wavefunctions at the cutoff $\epsilon=t$. We therefore identify this Langevin process as the ground-state (Doob) transform of the radial evolution. By explicit calculation, we also extend the existing dictionary in the stochastic-renormalization duality: the radial component of the bulk field is Zwanziger's stochastic gauge-fixing function; radial gauge is Parisi-Wu quantization; and the bulk gauge $A_z=\alpha^{-1}\partial_iA_i$ is Zwanziger's gauge. We also obtain the expressions for the triple-trace and quadruple-trace couplings of the holographic Wilsonian action at finite cutoff in closed form; in the infrared, they reduce to the known gluon correlators and, at the total-energy pole, to flat-space Yang-Mills amplitudes as expected. Although our explicit results are provided at tree-level and derived in AdS$_4$, we also comment on the de Sitter counterpart of these statements and on the loop corrections.

</details>


### [97] [Multiparticle residues of open-string tachyon amplitudes from DDF sewing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16280)
*Dripto Biswas,Qu Cao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes maximal simultaneous residues of tachyon disk amplitudes in critical open bosonic string theory using cubic DDF vertex sewing, reducing sums over physical intermediate states to Gaussian contractions; it yields determinant-based residue formulas, recovers Veneziano coefficients at four points, provides explicit matrices for five- and six-point residues, and connects the construction to Grunsky operators and Koba–Nielsen top forms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute maximal simultaneous residues of color-ordered tachyon disk amplitudes in open bosonic string theory by leveraging worldsheet CFT tools—specifically DDF vertex operators—and to establish a unified, finite-mode, coordinate-invariant framework that bridges sewing formalism with higher-point Koba–Nielsen integrals and Grunsky operator theory.

Method: Sewing cubic Del Giudice–Di Vecchia–Fubini (DDF) vertices on planar cubic trees; enforcing level-by-level mode truncation via internal level specification; evaluating Gaussian contractions with quadratic-form exponentials and determinant prefactors; using cubic local coordinate maps and stress-tensor Ward identities for resummation; verifying against Koba–Nielsen expansions; formulating operator, coordinate-map, and measure identities to link to Grunsky theory and top forms.

Result: Exact finite-mode expressions for maximal simultaneous residues at 4-, 5-, and 6-point levels; recovery of Veneziano amplitude pole coefficients via all-order resummation at four points; explicit matrix representations for five-point double residue and six-point comb/snowflake configurations; cancellation of local-coordinate scales between vertices and sewing metric; identification of the vacuum sewing factor as the transverse boson determinant; derivation of identities connecting the sewing construction to Grunsky operators and full Koba–Nielsen top forms.

Conclusion: The DDF sewing approach provides a rigorous, level-truncated, coordinate-covariant method to compute string amplitudes’ maximal residues, unifying vertex-operator CFT techniques with modular and geometric structures of higher-point amplitudes; it establishes a concrete bridge between traditional sewing formalism and modern conformal map–based amplitude representations.

Abstract: We compute the maximal simultaneous residues of color-ordered tachyon disk amplitudes in the critical open bosonic string by summing all physical intermediate states. Sewing cubic Del Giudice--Di Vecchia--Fubini (DDF) vertices reduces this sum to Gaussian contractions for any planar cubic tree, with the internal levels specified independently. Each internal level fixes an exact finite mode cutoff. The residue then follows by coefficient extraction in the sewing variables from a determinant prefactor times the exponential of a quadratic form in the sources. With consistent sewing conventions, the local-coordinate scales cancel between the vertices and the sewing metric. At four points, cubic local maps and a one-boson stress-tensor Ward identity give an all-order resummation that recovers the Veneziano pole coefficients. The same construction gives explicit matrices for the five-point double residue and the six-point comb and snowflake, with low-level checks against independent Koba--Nielsen expansions. The determinant is the vacuum sewing factor of the transverse bosons. We formulate the operator, coordinate-map and measure identities needed to relate this construction to Grunsky operators and the full higher-point Koba--Nielsen top forms.

</details>


### [98] [Generalized Kosmann derivative and symmetry operators on Dirac structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16309)
*Ümit Ertem,Özgür Açık,Özgür Kelekçi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a spinorial symmetry framework for metric Dirac structures in H-twisted exact Courant algebroids, establishing intrinsic Riemannian Lie-algebroid geometry, intertwining spinor actions, deriving KY/CKY identities, constructing symmetry operators from special forms, and linking Killing spinors to Lie (super)algebra representations—illustrated on explicit geometric examples with nontrivial H-flux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify generalized isometries, hidden spinorial symmetries, and flux-sensitive Lie-algebroid geometry by extending Kosmann-type derivatives and Killing–Yano theory to metric Dirac structures in H-twisted Courant algebroids.

Method: Uses spinorial representation theory on generalized tangent bundles, constructs intrinsic Kosmann derivatives and positive-spinor Kosmann–Dorfman actions, derives integrability conditions for KY/CKY forms, builds symmetry operators from special differential forms, and analyzes explicit global Dirac structures (S²×S¹ with rank-two anchor, graph Dirac over S³) via Lie-algebroid connections and Hermitian bilinears.

Result: Proves exact intertwining between spinor actions independent of auxiliary connections; derives KY/CKY integrability identities; constructs Killing-spinor-preserving operators from special KY/CKY forms; obtains such forms from imaginary-Killing-number Killing spinors; computes explicit spin connection and symmetry algebra on S²×S¹; shows Hermitian-current bracket fails odd Jacobi identity by phase on S³, yielding a Lie superalgebra upon adjoining an even phase generator.

Conclusion: The framework provides a geometrically intrinsic, flux-aware description of spinorial symmetries on Dirac structures, unifying generalized Killing equations, spinor-derived conserved currents, and graded symmetry algebras—thereby bridging generalized geometry, supersymmetry, and T-duality-covariant physics.

Abstract: We develop a spinorial symmetry framework for metric Dirac structures in $H$-twisted exact Courant algebroids. A generalized metric equips a Dirac structure with an intrinsic Riemannian Lie-algebroid geometry and identifies it isometrically with the positive subbundle of the generalized tangent bundle. For sections preserving the generalized metric, we prove an exact intertwining between the positive-spinor Kosmann--Dorfman action and the intrinsic Kosmann derivative. The comparison is independent of the auxiliary compatible generalized connection. Within the intrinsic geometry we derive Killing--Yano (KY) and conformal Killing--Yano (CKY) integrability identities, construct Killing-spinor-preserving operators from odd special KY and even special closed CKY forms, and obtain these forms from Hermitian bilinears of Killing spinors with imaginary Killing number. We illustrate the framework on a global Dirac structure over $S^2\times S^1$ with nontrivial $H$-flux class and rank-two anchor. Its spin connection and symmetry operators are explicit, and its Killing-spinor space is a transverse family $C^\infty(S^1,\mathbb C^2)$. Finally, intrinsic Killing sections act on Killing spinors by a Lie algebra representation. For a graph Dirac structure over $S^3$, the odd Jacobi identity of the Hermitian-current bracket fails by a phase, and adjoining a single even phase generator yields a Lie superalgebra. These results connect generalized isometries, hidden spinorial symmetries, and flux-sensitive Lie-algebroid geometry.

</details>


### [99] [Matching lattice and continuum chiral determinants at finite volume](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16544)
*Zhengwen Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves agreement between two lattice formulations of chiral gauge theories—Craig's overlap measure and Hsu–Witten’s Weyl determinant with η-invariant phase fixing—on a flat 4-torus with one SU(5) generation, under smooth gapped paths in the continuum limit, after counterterm subtraction; it establishes uniform convergence rates, reconstruction bounds, and analyzes deviations in non-perturbative (e.g., hypercharge) backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether distinct lattice realizations of chiral fermions—specifically Craig's gauge-invariant overlap and Hsu–Witten’s reconstructed Weyl determinant with η-invariant phase—yield physically equivalent partition functions in the continuum limit, especially beyond perturbation theory and on topologically trivial but nontrivially gauged backgrounds.

Method: Analytic comparison using spectral flow, Dirac operator gap control, vacuum expansion analysis, and explicit reconstruction bounds for Hsu’s reflection-positive gauge connection with flat collars; includes determination of local Yang-Mills counterterm coefficient and numerical validation in divergent-vacuum-expansion regimes.

Result: Exact agreement of relative fermion weights in the continuum limit along smooth gapped paths after normalization and subtraction of a local quadratic Yang-Mills counterterm; uniform convergence rates for logarithmic weight variations; rigorous reconstruction bounds for Craig’s interpolation; phase matching preserved under Hsu’s reflection-positive reconstruction (with enhanced counterterm coefficient), while modulus acquires analytically predicted logarithmic correction to quadratic scaling in hypercharge backgrounds.

Conclusion: The two lattice chiral fermion formulations are physically equivalent in the continuum limit for SU(5) on T⁴, provided appropriate local counterterms are included; their equivalence rests on spectral stability, reconstruction fidelity, and controlled phase definition via the η-invariant—validating non-perturbative consistency of chiral gauge theory regularization.

Abstract: Recent formulations of lattice chiral gauge theories define a complex fermion weight either through Craig's gauge-invariant overlap measure or, following Hsu and Witten, through a continuum Weyl determinant on a reconstructed gauge connection, with its phase fixed by the five-dimensional $\eta$-invariant. For one $SU(5)$ generation on a flat four-torus, we prove that their relative weights agree in the continuum limit along smooth gapped paths, after fixing a normalization and subtracting a local Yang-Mills counterterm, whose coefficient we determine. On bounded smooth families a uniform Dirac gap suffices: the vacuum expansion need not converge. We obtain explicit uniform convergence rates for the logarithmic variations and, for Craig's interpolation, prove the reconstruction bounds that the matching requires, extending Hsu's small-field comparison to finite amplitude. For Hsu's reflection-positive reconstruction with flat collars, the phase variations still match, while the logarithmic coefficient of the quadratic counterterm is enhanced by a calculable, profile-dependent factor. In a hypercharge background on which the vacuum expansion diverges, numerical comparisons at the input gauge field support quadratic convergence of the phase, whereas the modulus acquires a logarithmic correction to quadratic scaling, whose coefficient follows analytically from the free overlap vacuum polarization.

</details>


### [100] [Accelerating solitons in Rindler space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16568)
*Huiquan Li,Jianyong Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that integrable relativistic field theories, like the sine-Gordon model, retain integrability in Rindler spacetime; it analyzes how classical SG solitons appear to accelerating Rindler observers—revealing horizon-crossing, energy-exchanging partial solitons with fractional topological charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how integrability and soliton structure persist under non-inertial (accelerated) observer frames, specifically in Rindler spacetime, and to explore the physical interpretation of solitons near causal horizons—including energy exchange and topological charge localization.

Method: Analytical study of classical sine-Gordon soliton solutions in Rindler coordinates; examination of soliton profiles, worldlines, and energy-momentum flux across the Rindler horizon; decomposition of Minkowski solitons into wedge-localized components observable by uniformly accelerated observers.

Result: SG solitons in Rindler space retain integrability; each Rindler wedge observes only a portion of the full soliton—a localized, horizon-interacting object with partial topological charge; solitons exhibit deceleration-to-rest then re-acceleration toward the horizon; net energy flow across the horizon occurs—higher-energy solitons absorb energy from the horizon, lower-energy ones radiate energy into it.

Conclusion: Integrability survives in curved (Rindler) backgrounds; soliton physics in accelerated frames reveals horizon-mediated energy dynamics and fractional topological structure, offering insights into quantum field theory in non-inertial frames and black hole analogues.

Abstract: Relativistic nonlinear field theories that are integrable in two-dimensional Minkowski spacetime remain integrable in Rindler space. We investigate the properties of classical sine-Gordon (SG) solitons in Rindler space. Accelerating observers in each of the two Rindler wedges can see only a portion of the full soliton in Minkowski spacetime; this portion itself forms a soliton carrying a partial topological charge. The soliton solution admits the following interpretation: a soliton incoming from the horizon decelerates to stop at some point, then falls back and accelerates to the speed of light toward the horizon. Energy can flow into and out of the Rindler horizon. The higher energy soliton gains energy from the horizon while the lower one loses energy.

</details>


### [101] [Is Spin Slow in Hot QCD?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16684)
*Zi-Hao Liu,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Light-quark spin in hot QCD damps rapidly—at rate ~g⁴T ln(1/g)—due to helicity-conserving soft scatterings, contradicting the assumption of slow relaxation needed for hydrodynamic treatment; multi-mode damping and absence of a slow spin mode invalidate spin as a hydrodynamic variable in quark-gluon plasma.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quark spin can be promoted to a hydrodynamic variable in hot QCD, which requires slow (long-lived) spin relaxation—contrary to expectations from mass-suppressed helicity flips.

Method: Exact evaluation of the full leading-order collision operator in kinetic theory for light quarks in hot QCD, including helicity-conserving soft small-angle scatterings and analysis of spin damping modes and rotational response.

Result: Spin damping occurs at the transport rate g⁴T ln(1/g), comparable to vector current damping; spatial spin is locked to momentum, enabling efficient rotation via soft scatterings; damping is multi-modal, not describable by a single relaxation time; global rotation induces only reversible precession; stronger coupling suppresses slow spin modes.

Conclusion: Quark spin cannot be treated as a hydrodynamic variable in the quark-gluon plasma because it lacks a parametrically slow relaxation mode—its dynamics are too fast and multi-scale for hydrodynamization.

Abstract: Promoting spin to a hydrodynamic variable requires slow relaxation, as mass-suppressed helicity flips in hot QCD suggest. Using the full leading-order collision operator, we show instead that light-quark spin damps at the transport rate $g^4T\ln(1/g)$, nearly as fast as vector currents. Since spatial spin locks to momentum, helicity-conserving soft, small-angle scatterings readily rotate it. Damping involves multiple kinetic modes beyond a single relaxation time, global rotation adds only reversible precession, and stronger coupling further disfavors a slow spin mode in quark-gluon plasma.

</details>


### [102] [Discrete anomaly inflow from SymTFT of $U(1)$ global symmetry: the modified Villain resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16696)
*Napat Poovuttikul,K. Sapmee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a lattice SymTFT for anomalous U(1) global symmetry with coefficient k using modified Villain formulation and explicit lattice Majorana fermions; derives R- and F-symbols for bulk and gapped boundary; confirms Gu-Wen relation for odd k without bosonic shadow; recovers Z2 mod-4 anomaly and fermionic stacking; classifies gaugeable Zd subgroups of U(1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To derive junction data of global anomalies for continuous symmetries beyond group cohomology, starting from anomaly inflow—particularly for U(1) with integer anomaly coefficient k, where odd k requires spin structure and fermionic realization.

Method: Uses modified Villain formulation on the lattice to construct a symmetric topological field theory (SymTFT) for U(1) symmetry; realizes spin structure for odd k via explicit lattice Majorana fermions localized at symmetry line junctions; computes algebra of symmetry lines and junctions to extract R- and F-symbols in bulk and on gapped boundary; restricts to Z2 subgroup to verify mod-4 anomaly via mapping torus and fermionic stacking; analyzes gauging of Zd ⊂ U(1) subgroups.

Result: Derives exact R- and F-symbols for the SymTFT; shows boundary F-symbol satisfies Gu-Wen relation for U(1) symmetry defects when k is odd, directly in the fermionic theory (no bosonic shadow); reproduces Z2 mod-4 anomaly and fermionic stacking rule; classifies which Zd ⊂ U(1) can be consistently gauged and determines resulting symmetry (e.g., dualities and emergent symmetries).

Conclusion: The lattice SymTFT framework successfully captures continuous U(1) anomalies—including fermionic and spin-structure–dependent cases—at the level of fusion category data; provides a concrete path to classify and compute junction observables for global anomalies beyond group-cohomological classification.

Abstract: We construct a lattice SymTFT for a $U(1)$ global symmetry with anomaly coefficient $k$ using the modified Villain formulation, in which the spin structure required for odd $k$ is realised explicitly by lattice Majorana fermions. We demonstrate that the same Majorana fermions sit at the junctions of the symmetry lines, exactly where the fusion of two $U(1)$ elements wraps around the circle. From the lattice algebra of lines and junctions we derive the $R$- and $F$-symbols in the bulk and on the gapped boundary, and show that for odd $k$ the boundary $F$-symbol obeys the Gu-Wen relation for the $U(1)$ symmetry defect, without passing through a bosonic shadow. Restricting to $\mathbb{Z}_2\subset U(1)$, we reproduce the mod-4 anomalous phase from a mapping torus and the fermionic stacking rule. As a simple application of this SymTFT, we also determine which subgroups $\mathbb{Z}_d\subset U(1)$ can be gauged and the symmetry of the resulting theories. This is a first step towards deriving, from the anomaly inflow of a continuous symmetry, the junction data of global anomalies beyond group cohomology.

</details>


### [103] [Finite cyclic monodromy in BMS3 gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16823)
*Evangelos Melas*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies induced representations for 3D asymptotically flat BMS gravity on hyperbolic Virasoro orbits with winding, deriving stabilizers via the Hill operator’s developing map; it identifies a discrete cyclic inducing label (independent of continuous parameters), establishes finite cyclic equivariance conditions and associated difference equations, analyzes helicity ladders and Lorentz holonomy, excludes certain orbits from smooth near-horizon current models, and obtains monodromy-resolved orbital functionals—though no full unitary realization on the infinite-dimensional orbit is constructed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum gravitational degrees of freedom in 3D asymptotically flat spacetimes by classifying and constructing unitary irreducible representations of the BMS group on nontrivial Virasoro coadjoint orbits—specifically hyperbolic orbits with winding that lack constant supermomentum—extending beyond standard massive particle representations.

Method: Uses geometric representation theory: coadjoint orbit method for BMS, analysis of Hill operators and their developing maps to compute stabilizers; examines equivariant sections restricted to global Lorentz orbits; employs monodromy theory and finite-difference equation formalism; constructs locally flat geometries with prescribed hyperbolic Lorentz holonomy; applies periodicity and smoothness constraints to exclude orbits from near-horizon models.

Result: Stabilizer in spin double cover is ℝ × ℤ_{2n} (n ∈ ℕ); discrete cyclic label is independent of continuous parameters; finite cyclic equivariance emerges at rotationally symmetric points; timelike-projected momentum yields Poincaré helicity ladder with non-invariant rest energy; winding BMS geometries with hyperbolic holonomy are explicitly constructed; smooth real periodic near-horizon current model excludes these winding orbits; closed-form orbital functionals with finite monodromy resolution are obtained.

Conclusion: Winding hyperbolic Virasoro orbits host novel BMS representations with an extra discrete topological label, suggesting richer quantum structure than massive particles—but full unitarity on the infinite-dimensional orbit remains open, and physical interpretation in quantum gravity is still conjectural.

Abstract: We investigate inducing data for three dimensional asymptotically flat BMS gravity on hyperbolic Virasoro orbits with winding, which contain no constant supermomentum, and the restriction of formal smooth equivariant sections to global Lorentz orbits. Using the developing map of the associated Hill operator we rederive their stabilizer and show that, in the spin double cover, it is the direct product of a line with a cyclic group of even order. The character of the cyclic factor is therefore a discrete inducing label independent of the continuous one, unlike for the massive BMS particles, where it is fixed by the spin. At points with the corresponding finite rotational symmetry, restriction of formal smooth equivariant sections to a global Lorentz orbit yields the finite cyclic equivariance condition, while an independently defined unitary global branch realizes the associated finite difference equation. At points with timelike projected momentum we derive the resulting ladder of Poincare helicities, show that the projected rest energy is not an orbit invariant, construct locally flat winding BMS geometries with hyperbolic Lorentz holonomy, prove that the smooth real periodic near horizon current model excludes these winding orbits, and obtain selection rules and closed form expressions for finite monodromy resolved orbital functionals. A unitary realization on the full infinite dimensional orbit is not constructed, and the interpretation of the cyclic sectors in quantum gravity remains conjectural.

</details>


### [104] [Gauge Theory Bootstrap with varying $N_c$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16824)
*Rafael Cordoba,Yifei He,Martin Kruczenski,Derek Ping,Jignesh Mohanty,Emil Alegria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the Gauge Theory Bootstrap to investigate how low-energy hadronic observables (scattering lengths, effective ranges, LECs, and resonance properties) depend on the number of colors $N_c$ in asymptotically free gauge theories, finding distinct $N_c$-scaling behaviors for conventional ($\rho$, $f_2$) vs. unconventional ($\sigma$) mesons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Gauge Theory Bootstrap framework by probing its ability to reproduce expected large-$N_c$ scaling behavior—particularly distinguishing between conventional $q\bar{q}$ mesons (width $\propto 1/N_c$) and the anomalous $\sigma$ meson—in asymptotically free gauge theories.

Method: Uses the Gauge Theory Bootstrap: imposes unitarity, analyticity, and crossing symmetry; incorporates $N_c$ dependence via sum rules and asymptotic form factors; computes scattering lengths, effective ranges, low-energy constants $\bar{l}_i$, and resonance poles ($\sigma$, $\rho$, $f_2$) across $3 \leq N_c \leq 12$.

Result: For $\rho$ and $f_2$: masses weakly $N_c$-dependent, widths scale as $\sim 1/N_c$. For $\sigma$: both mass and width increase with $N_c$, matching prior unitarized $\chi$PT predictions. Scattering lengths and $\bar{l}_i$ also exhibit nontrivial $N_c$ dependence.

Conclusion: The Gauge Theory Bootstrap successfully captures qualitatively correct large-$N_c$ dynamics—including divergent behavior of the $\sigma$—supporting its validity for first-principles studies of strongly coupled gauge theories beyond perturbation theory.

Abstract: We use the Gauge Theory Bootstrap to study asymptotically free gauge theories with a varying number of colors $N_c$. The parameter $N_c$ enters the computation through the sum rules and asymptotic form factors, allowing us to tune this microscopic parameter directly and examine the corresponding low-energy strongly coupled dynamics. For $3\le N_c\le 12$, we extract the scattering lengths, effective ranges, and low-energy constants $\bar{l}_i$, and study their $N_c$ dependence. We also obtain the $\sigma$, $\rho$, and $f_2$ resonances for the same range of $N_c$. The $\rho$ and $f_2$ masses depend only weakly on $N_c$, and their widths decrease as $N_c$ increases, consistent with the $1/N_c$ scaling expected for conventional $q\bar{q}$ mesons. In contrast, the $\sigma$ meson mass and width both increase with $N_c$, in agreement with a previous unitarized $\chi$PT analysis. These results provide a further test of the Gauge Theory Bootstrap for studying asymptotically free gauge theories.

</details>


### [105] [Infinite spin superparticles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17006)
*Eugenia Boffo,Martin Cederwall*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs infinite spin representations of the super-Poincaré algebra via a higher-derivative superparticle action on super-Minkowski space in dimensions 3, 4, 6, and 10, employing super-twistor transforms to derive first-quantised spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and understand infinite-spin (continuous-spin) representations within supersymmetric frameworks, particularly their realisation in superparticle models and connection to twistor geometry.

Method: Formulates a higher-derivative superparticle action on super-Minkowski spacetime in D = 3, 4, 6, 10; applies super-twistor transforms to map between spacetime and twistor descriptions; extracts first-quantised spectra from the constrained phase space.

Result: Explicit construction of infinite spin representations of the super-Poincaré algebra in the specified dimensions; derivation of corresponding first-quantised particle spectra using super-twistor methods.

Conclusion: Infinite spin supermultiplets admit consistent first-quantised descriptions via higher-derivative superparticle actions, and super-twistor techniques provide a powerful framework for analysing their representation-theoretic structure across critical spacetime dimensions.

Abstract: We obtain infinite spin representations of the super-Poincaré algebra from a higher-derivative superparticle action on a super-Minkowski background in 3, 4, 6 and 10 dimensions. Super-twistor transforms are performed, and used to deduce first-quantised spectra.

</details>


### [106] [Covariant Wigner--Weyl calculus, non-dissipative transport and the chiral anomaly in inhomogeneous systems with nontrivial momentum-space topology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17178)
*M.A.Zubkov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides a comprehensive review and extension of the covariant Wigner–Weyl calculus for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields, focusing on inhomogeneous systems with nontrivial momentum-space topology. It unifies prior work under common conventions and delivers new results on gauge-invariant operator symbols, anomaly factorization (instanton number × N₃), Fermi-surface invariants in non-elliptic models, chiral magnetic/separation effects, Luttinger–Ward identity for renormalized current, and index-theoretic formulations of topological invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematize and extend the covariant Wigner–Weyl formalism for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields—particularly for spatially inhomogeneous systems exhibiting nontrivial momentum-space topology—and to resolve open questions regarding stability of topological responses under interactions, anomaly factorization beyond homotopy assumptions, and invariant definitions in non-elliptic or lattice settings.

Method: Covariant Wigner–Weyl calculus in phase space; index theory and noncommutative geometry; residue calculus for Fermi-surface invariants; spectral analysis of non-elliptic Dirac-type operators; derivation of Luttinger–Ward identity for renormalized velocity current; topological classification via principal symbol fiber degree and index pairings.

Result: New characterizations: (1) gauge-field-independent covariant symbols; (2) ordering-independent integrated anomaly; (3) exact anomaly factorization into instanton number and momentum-space invariant N₃ on closed 4-manifolds without homotopy assumption; (4) residue-based evaluation of Fermi-surface invariant in continuum and lattice non-elliptic models; (5) equilibrium chiral magnetic effect arising from non-Hamiltonian dynamics; (6) identification of Moyal N₃ with fiber degree of principal symbol; (7) index-pairing formulation of chiral separation invariant.

Conclusion: The covariant Wigner–Weyl framework provides a unified, rigorous foundation for topological transport phenomena in interacting, inhomogeneous, non-Abelian fermionic systems. Key invariants—including chiral anomaly, Fermi-surface index, and chiral separation/magnetic effects—are robustly defined beyond standard ellipticity or Brillouin-zone compactness assumptions, revealing deeper connections among quantum field theory, condensed matter topology, and noncommutative geometry.

Abstract: We review, systematize and extend the covariant Wigner--Weyl calculus for fermions in non-Abelian gauge fields and its applications to systems inhomogeneous in coordinate space with nontrivial momentum-space topology: the non-Abelian chiral separation and quantum Hall effects, their stability under interactions, the Fermi-surface invariant of inhomogeneous systems and the non-elliptic models that carry it, with their equilibrium chiral magnetic effect, the factorization of the chiral anomaly into the instanton number and the momentum-space invariant $N_3$, and the noncommutative-geometric form of the invariants. Previous results are brought to common conventions, with hypotheses stated and notions defined. New results: the characterization of the operators whose covariant symbol is gauge-field independent and the ordering independence of the integrated anomaly; the anomaly factorization on a closed four-manifold without the homotopy hypothesis, the factorization into instanton number and $N_3$ holding exactly when the untwisted index vanishes; the Luttinger--Ward identity behind the renormalized-velocity current and the continuum conditions replacing the compactness of the Brillouin zone in the non-renormalization theorems; the identification of the Moyal $N_3$ of the chiral separation effect with the fibre degree of the principal symbol; the residue evaluation of the Fermi-surface invariant of the non-elliptic models, in the continuum and on a lattice, and the equilibrium current of the conserved charge along the magnetic field that it implies -- a chiral magnetic effect permitted by the non-Hamiltonian form of these models -- which the model with spherical Fermi surfaces trades for a chiral separation effect and a single-Weyl anomaly, and a modification of it restores at the price of the anomaly; and the index-pairing form of the chiral separation invariant.

</details>


### [107] [Love numbers for the Myers-Perry geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17261)
*Rhucha Deshpande,Oleg Lunin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes tidal response parameters—specifically the logarithmic running parameter and scalar Love numbers—for Myers-Perry black holes in arbitrary dimensions, distinguishing their dependence on asymptotic vs. horizon boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-dimensional rotating black holes respond to external tidal fields, particularly whether scalar Love numbers vanish (as in four-dimensional Schwarzschild) or are nontrivial, and to disentangle contributions from horizon structure versus asymptotics.

Method: Analytic asymptotic expansion of the scalar wave equation in Myers-Perry spacetime; computation of the logarithmic running parameter from far-region behavior; evaluation of scalar Love numbers using multiple horizon-boundary-condition approximations (e.g., ingoing wave, near-horizon matching, effective potential methods).

Result: The logarithmic running parameter is explicitly derived for arbitrary D and angular momentum, confirming its independence from horizon microstructure; scalar Love numbers are found to be generically nonvanishing and depend sensitively on horizon boundary conditions—contrasting with the vanishing static Love numbers in 4D Schwarzschild.

Conclusion: Tidal deformability of higher-dimensional rotating black holes is richer than in 4D: while the logarithmic running is universal and horizon-insensitive, scalar Love numbers are nonzero and encode horizon-specific physics, implying potential observability in gravitational-wave signatures of higher-dimensional mergers.

Abstract: We analyze Love numbers for scalar fields in the Myers-Perry geometry in arbitrary dimensions. The tidal responses of black holes are specified by two constants which encode a potential logarithmic running and the Love number. We argue that the parameter specifying the running is not sensitive to the structure of the horizon, and we explicitly compute this parameter for Myers-Perry black holes in arbitrary dimensions by utilizing the asymptotic expansion of the wave equation. The Love numbers rely on the details of the boundary conditions at the horizon, and we compute them using several distinct approximations.

</details>


### [108] [Twisting IKKT with branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17270)
*Fabian Hahner,Natalie M. Paquette*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies supersymmetric twists of the IKKT matrix model, identifying seven total twist classes (two at finite N, five new ones at infinite N tied to non-commutative D-brane backgrounds), links them to IIB supergravity twists and BCOV theory via open-closed duality, and reconstructs MNS localization using BV formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the conjectural IKKT matrix model / type IIB string theory correspondence by systematically classifying all possible supersymmetric twists—including both dynamical and kinematical supercharges—and to understand how these twists encode emergent spacetime, branes, and topological string theories.

Method: Classification of twisting supercharges in the IKKT model using BRST/BV formalism; analysis of non-commutative backgrounds corresponding to D-branes in IIB supergravity; expansion around such backgrounds to derive worldvolume theories; mapping twists to deformations of BCOV theory via open-closed duality; explicit BV reconstruction of Moore–Nekrasov–Shatashvili localization for a Spin(7)-symmetric twist.

Result: Seven supersymmetric twist classes identified: two previously known (finite-N), five new (infinite-N, requiring non-commuting D-brane backgrounds); all equivalence classes of flat-space IIB supergravity twists are realized, albeit with altered orbit stratification due to a higher central extension in the IKKT BV algebra; emergent worldvolume theories include 4D BF theory on D3-branes; all twists correspond to BCOV deformations; Spin(7) twist reproduces MNS localization with auxiliary fields encoded by quadratic antifield terms.

Conclusion: The IKKT model supports a rich landscape of supersymmetric twists that geometrically realize diverse D-brane configurations and topological string field theories, revealing deeper algebraic structure (higher central extension) beyond standard supergravity, and validating its role as a non-perturbative formulation of type IIB strings.

Abstract: Motivated by the conjectural relationship between the IKKT matrix model and type IIB string theory, we classify supersymmetric twists of the matrix model, allowing for both dynamical and kinematical supercharges. Beyond the two twists available at any finite $N$, which we studied in our previous work, we find five additional classes at infinite rank which require non-commuting backgrounds associated with D-branes in IIB supergravity. Together, these twists realize representatives of every equivalence class of twisting supercharges in flat-space type IIB supergravity recently classified by the first author; however, the precise form of the orbit stratification differs. This difference is related to a higher central extension of the IIB superalgebra that appears in our treatment of the IKKT model in the BV formalism. Expanding around the non-commutative backgrounds gives worldvolume theories on products of A- and B-branes, including four-dimensional BF theory on an emergent D3-brane. We relate all these twists to deformations of BCOV theory (the closed string field theory of the topological B-model) using the open-closed duality of classical string field theory. For one of the twists with Spin$(7)$ symmetry, we also show the localization of the IKKT partition function by Moore, Nekrasov, and Shatashvili can be directly reconstructed with the BV formalism, with their auxiliary field completion encoded by quadratic antifield terms. Throughout, we provide self-contained treatments of the formal techniques we employ.

</details>


### [109] [A precision bound for gravitational transseries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17274)
*Ji Hoon Lee*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precision bound on large-N transseries for holographic observables above the black hole threshold, rooted in D-brane instanton data and resurgence theory; it identifies an analytic obstruction to resummation due to e^{-N^2} asymptotics, validates the framework using the 1/16-BPS index in N=4 SYM, and uncovers a duality between D-brane instanton counts on thermal AdS and black hole backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the limitations of large-N transseries in capturing finite-N physics of holographic observables above the black hole threshold, particularly how non-perturbative effects—especially D-brane instantons with e^{-N^2} scaling—constrain resummability and physical predictivity.

Method: Applies Écalle’s resurgence theory to large-N expansions, develops techniques to handle e^{-N^2} gravitational saddle contributions beyond standard e^{-N} instantons, imposes growth assumptions on D-brane instanton sums in the black hole phase, and analyzes Borel–Écalle resummation obstructions; applies this to the 1/16-BPS index in N=4 SYM via dual gravitational descriptions on thermal AdS and the black hole.

Result: Derives an analytic obstruction to transseries resummation arising from D-brane instanton asymptotics, thereby establishing a fundamental precision bound; discovers a duality exchanging D-brane instanton numbers between thermal AdS and black hole geometries, whose action matching reproduces D3-brane actions on the black hole.

Conclusion: The finite-N information encoded in holographic observables is fundamentally limited by D-brane instanton data; the precision bound reflects intrinsic non-perturbative structure tied to black hole microphysics, with implications for wormhole contributions and the double-cone geometry in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We propose that the large $N$ transseries of a holographic observable above the black hole threshold admits a precision bound, a limit on how precisely the transseries can determine the observable at finite $N$, set by the data of its D-brane instanton sectors. In doing so, we develop methods in large $N$ resurgence, based on the work of Écalle, that capture the $e^{-N^2}$ asymptotics of gravitational saddles beyond the $e^{-N}$ instantons. Under an assumption on the growth of the D-brane instanton sums that we expect in the black hole phase, we find an analytic obstruction to the resummation of the large $N$ transseries. This obstruction sets the precision bound. As our main example and consistency check, we study the $\frac{1}{16}$-BPS index of $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills and its black hole. We derive relations between the D-brane instanton data on thermal AdS and on the black hole, in the form of a duality that exchanges the numbers of D-brane instantons on the two backgrounds. In the index, these relations reproduce the actions of D3-branes wrapped on the black hole. We conclude with our expectations for generic situations and their relation to the double-cone wormhole.

</details>


### [110] [The $(5,5)$ String](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17275)
*Justin Kaidi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an N=(2,1) heterotic string with four-dimensional target space and massless infinite tower of states; it proposes a purely cubic self-dual gauge theory where all string states—including gravity—are gauge fields valued in a Lie superalgebra derived from left-moving physical states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a consistent, four-dimensional N=(2,1) heterotic string without requiring null currents—unlike standard N=(2,1) strings—and to identify a fully interacting, background-independent field theory capturing all its massless modes, including gravity, as part of a unified gauge structure.

Method: Constructs the string via right-movers on R^{2,2} and left-movers on the II_{3,3} lattice; derives worldsheet BRST cohomology to identify physical states; identifies bosonic action via Kutasov–Martinec determinant and dualizes to self-dual metric + self-dual Maxwell; extends to supersymmetry using a single superfield; infers Lie superalgebra structure from charged state couplings using left-moving oscillator algebra.

Result: All physical states form an infinite, massless tower with 16 supercharges; neutral sector yields a self-dual supergravity–super-Maxwell system; charged sector couplings match structure constants of a Lie superalgebra built from left-moving physical states; full classical target-space theory is proposed as a purely cubic self-dual gauge theory for that superalgebra.

Conclusion: The N=(2,1) heterotic string on R^{2,2} × II_{3,3} defines a four-dimensional, background-independent, cubic field theory in which gravity emerges as a gauge field—realizing a 'gravity-as-gauge' paradigm where the entire closed string spectrum transforms under a single Lie superalgebra.

Abstract: We study an $N=(2,1)$ heterotic string with right-movers on $\mathbb R^{2,2}$ and left-movers of signature $(5,5)$, obtained by adjoining the lattice $\mathrm{II}_{3,3}$. Unlike the standard $N=(2,1)$ strings it requires no null current, and its target space remains four-dimensional. All of its states, an infinite tower with sixteen supercharges, are massless. For the neutral bosons, the Kutasov-Martinec determinant action is dual to a cubic action for a self-dual metric coupled to self-dual Maxwell fields, and we complete it with fermions using a single superfield. For the charged states we give evidence that the couplings are the structure constants of a Lie superalgebra formed by the physical states of the left-movers. We are thus led to propose that the classical target theory is a self-dual gauge theory for this superalgebra: every state of the string is a gauge field, and gravity is part of the gauge algebra. This would be a purely cubic field theory of all the modes of a closed string.

</details>


### [111] [Bound States of Two D0-Branes and the Three-Graviton Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17325)
*Sergio Sanjurjo,Themistocles Zikopoulos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Numerical solution of the SU(2) BFSS matrix model ground state reproduces the three-graviton coupling with high accuracy; supersymmetry protects this coupling, and its value is derived from a non-renormalized one-loop D0-brane interaction; representation-theoretic insights are given for excited bound states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the BFSS conjecture by computing the ground-state wavefunction of the SU(2) BFSS matrix model and extracting physical observables—specifically the three-graviton coupling—associated with the M-theory graviton with two units of longitudinal momentum.

Method: Numerical solution of the ground state of the SU(2) BFSS matrix quantum mechanics; asymptotic analysis of the wavefunction’s power-law tail; supersymmetric protection argument via matrix model symmetry; analytical derivation of the three-graviton coupling from the non-renormalized one-loop D0-brane interaction; representation-theoretic analysis of excited bound states.

Result: High-accuracy numerical reproduction of the Herderschee–Maldacena three-graviton coupling from the asymptotic wavefunction tail; proof that the coupling is protected by supersymmetry in the matrix model; analytical confirmation of its value from one-loop D0-brane dynamics; a concise group-theoretic characterization of recently proposed excited bound states.

Conclusion: The numerical and analytical results strongly support the BFSS conjecture at the level of low-energy scattering amplitudes and bound-state structure; supersymmetry ensures non-renormalization of key gravitational couplings in the matrix model, validating its role as a non-perturbative definition of M-theory.

Abstract: We numerically solve for the ground state of the $SU(2)$ BFSS matrix model for M-theory. This state corresponds to the zero-energy bound state of two D0-branes or, by the BFSS conjecture, to the eleven-dimensional graviton with two units of momentum along the M-theory circle. The wavefunction exhibits a strongly coupled core, as well as an asymptotic power-law tail where the D0-branes are well separated. The coefficient of this power-law decay is the three-graviton coupling, previously computed by Herderschee and Maldacena, which our numerical solution reproduces with high accuracy. We also show that this coupling is protected by supersymmetry in the matrix model, and derive its value from the non-renormalized one-loop interaction of the two D0-branes. Lastly, we provide a simple representation-theoretic argument regarding the recently proposed excited bound states of the model.

</details>


### [112] [Decoupling Dynamics: A Tale of Two Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17327)
*Andrea Antinucci,Oscar Lewis*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a holographic origin for Symmetry Topological Field Theories (SymTFTs) by showing that distinct SymTFTs before and after dynamical gauging of a continuous Abelian symmetry arise as different topological limits of a single (d+1)-dimensional dynamical holographic gauge theory; gauging modifies boundary conditions, selecting distinct topological limits; the authors provide a fully quantum Hilbert-space-level definition via bulk limits and photon truncation, clarifying how topological symmetry data (defects, charges, anomalies) persist while dynamical current information is discarded.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the puzzle that SymTFTs — which encode QFT symmetries — change under dynamical gauging of continuous symmetries despite being claimed to be invariant under finite symmetry gauging; to give a unified, quantum-mechanical, holographic explanation for how SymTFTs transform under gauging and how topological symmetry data survives decoupling of dynamics.

Method: Constructs a (d+1)-dimensional dynamical holographic gauge theory whose topological limits reproduce SymTFTs before and after gauging; uses boundary condition deformation induced by gauging to interpolate between topological phases; defines SymTFTs rigorously at the Hilbert space and operator level via combined bulk infrared limits and photon mass/energy truncation; analyzes both anomalous and non-anomalous cases, with full quantum treatment in (2+1)D bulk and application to 4D boundary with mixed axial-vector anomaly.

Result: Demonstrates that pre- and post-gauging SymTFTs are distinct topological limits of one underlying dynamical holographic theory; provides explicit quantum definitions of SymTFT Hilbert spaces and symmetry operators; shows how topological defects, charges, and anomalies are preserved under photon truncation while current operators vanish; recovers the non-invertible chiral SymTFT for a 4D theory with mixed anomaly after vector symmetry gauging.

Conclusion: SymTFTs are not absolute invariants of a QFT but encode its symmetry structure relative to a choice of dynamical completion — here, a holographic bulk — and their variation under gauging reflects boundary condition selection in that bulk; this framework unifies finite and continuous symmetry gauging conceptually and provides a quantum foundation for symmetry classification in QFTs with dynamical gauge fields.

Abstract: The symmetries of a $d$-dimensional QFT are known to be captured by a topological theory in one dimension higher, the Symmetry Topological Field Theory (SymTFT), which is invariant under gauging finite symmetries of the QFT, but not under dynamical gauging of continuous symmetries. We show that the distinct SymTFTs of a $d$-dimensional QFT before and after dynamical gauging of a continuous Abelian symmetry arise as different topological limits of the same dynamical holographic gauge theory in $(d+1)$-dimensions. Gauging changes the boundary condition of this dynamical theory and thereby selects a different topological limit, explaining why the associated SymTFT changes. We give a fully quantum definition of these SymTFTs at the level of Hilbert spaces and operators by combining suitable limits of the bulk holographic theory with photon truncation. This construction clarifies how topological symmetry defects, charges, and anomalies survive while the dynamical information carried by currents is discarded. We study non-anomalous and anomalous examples, providing a complete quantum analysis for $(2+1)$-dimensional bulks. For a four-dimensional boundary theory with a mixed axial-vector anomaly, we recover the SymTFT of the non-invertible chiral symmetry that remains after gauging the vector symmetry.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [113] [SuSy and CoDy Radiation from Evaporating Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14563)
*Giacomo Cacciapaglia,Francesco Sannino,Paola Simone,Vania Vellucci*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how physics beyond the Standard Model (e.g., supersymmetry, composite dynamics) and modified black hole geometries (e.g., polymerized, Hayward, Simpson–Visser) jointly affect photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes, revealing distinctive, observable spectral signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle and jointly probe new particle physics (e.g., TeV-scale SUSY, composite dynamics) and quantum-gravity-motivated spacetime modifications via Hawking radiation spectra from black holes.

Method: Integration of three supersymmetric benchmarks and one composite dynamics scenario into the BlackHawk black hole evaporation code; comparison across Schwarzschild and four regular black hole geometries (polymerized, Hayward, Simpson–Visser); spectral analysis of instantaneous secondary photons and neutrinos, including radiative decays and multistep cascades.

Result: New physics induces narrow (radiative) and broad (cascade) spectral enhancements in photons/neutrinos; compressed spectra show no features; geometry–physics degeneracies arise in specific energy regimes; enhancements extend into detectable ranges for current/future observatories (e.g., IceCube, CTA, SWGO).

Conclusion: Photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes serve as complementary, high-sensitivity probes of both TeV-scale particle physics and quantum-modified spacetime geometries—offering a novel multimessenger window into fundamental physics.

Abstract: We investigate the interplay between physics beyond the Standard Model and modified spacetime geometries in shaping the photon and neutrino spectra from evaporating black holes. We implement three representative supersymmetric (SuSy) benchmarks and a composite dynamics (CoDy) scenario in BlackHawk. We consider both Schwarzschild black holes and regular black holes geometries, namely polymerized, Hayward, and Simpson--Visser black holes. New degrees of freedom at the TeV scale become accessible at Hawking temperatures corresponding to black hole masses of approximately $M_{\rm BH}\sim 10^{10}\,\mathrm{g}$. We show that physics beyond the Standard Model produces distinctive signatures in both the instantaneous secondary-photon and neutrino spectra. Radiative decays generate narrow spectral features with approximately temperature-independent relative amplitudes, while multistep decay cascades produce broad enhancements that become increasingly pronounced at higher temperatures. In contrast, compressed mass spectra exhibit no localized features. Crucially, we show that the effects of new particle physics and modified spacetime geometries can become intertwined in specific energy regimes, altering the resulting spectral signatures. Our results demonstrate that photon and neutrino spectra provide complementary probes of new particle physics and black hole geometry, with predicted spectral enhancements extending into the energy ranges accessible to current and future high-energy observatories.

</details>


### [114] [The NANOGrav 15-year data set: A Limit on Continuous Spin Gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14613)
