*Chinmay Ambasht,Olivia Bitcon,Tanner Trickle,NANOGrav Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constrains the spin scale ρ_g of a continuous spin graviton using pulsar timing array (NANOGrav 15-yr) data, finding ρ_g < 1.23×10⁻²³ eV at 95% credibility—leveraging the enhanced sensitivity of low-frequency (nanohertz) gravitational wave probes to CSP effects scaling as (ρ/ω)^n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Continuous spin particles (CSPs), including a possible CSP graviton, reduce to standard massless particles in the ρ → 0 limit; thus, experimental bounds on the spin scale ρ are essential to test whether known force carriers (e.g., graviton) might fundamentally be CSPs with small but non-zero ρ.

Method: The authors employ Bayesian inference on NANOGrav’s 15-year pulsar timing array dataset to search for deviations from general relativity induced by a continuous spin graviton, exploiting the fact that CSP-induced modifications scale as powers of ρ/ω and are therefore most detectable at low frequencies (here, nanohertz GW band).

Result: A 95% credible upper limit of ρ_g < 1.23 × 10⁻²³ eV is obtained on the graviton spin scale.

Conclusion: The result represents the strongest current experimental constraint on the continuous spin graviton parameter, demonstrating that pulsar timing arrays are a uniquely powerful probe for CSP physics due to their unprecedented sensitivity in the nanohertz regime.

Abstract: Continuous spin particles (CSPs) are massless particles characterized by a dimensionful spin scale, $\rho$, which, when non-zero, changes their helicity under Lorentz boosts. Since CSPs reduce to ordinary particles when $\rho \rightarrow 0$, the photon or graviton could be CSPs and only appear to behave as ordinary particles due to a small value of $\rho$. Therefore, it is important to determine what value of $\rho$ is experimentally allowed. Recent work has shown that the physical effects of CSPs scale as powers of $\rho/\omega$ when $\rho \ll \omega$, where $\omega$ is a frequency scale, making low-frequency probes particularly beneficial for highlighting these phenomena. In this paper we use pulsar timing arrays to set constraints on the spin scale of the continuous spin graviton. Pulsar timing arrays are uniquely suited for this application as they have exceptional sensitivity to low, nanohertz frequency gravitational waves, as demonstrated by their recent evidence for a stochastic gravitational wave background. Using standard Bayesian data analysis techniques, we find that the NANOGrav 15-year data set constrains the graviton spin scale to $\rho_\text{g} < 1.23 \times 10^{-23} \, \text{eV} $ at the 95% credible level.

</details>


### [115] [Rapidly Spinning Primordial Black Holes from Confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15169)
*Michael Zantedeschi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that confinement mechanisms—both electric (quark–antiquark) and magnetic (monopole–antimonopole)—in the early universe can produce rapidly spinning primordial black holes (PBHs) via relativistic capture; their natal spin distribution peaks near extremality (mean dimensionless spin a ≈ 2/3), a testable signature via gravitational-wave observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of highly spinning primordial black holes and identify a robust, model-independent observational signature—specifically spin—that distinguishes confinement-driven PBH formation from other mechanisms.

Method: Analytical modeling of relativistic two-body capture under confining flux-tube/string potentials, incorporating cosmological tides and string bending as perturbations; supplemented by numerical evolutions of cosmic strings to validate the predicted spin distribution.

Result: Confinement leads to a linearly rising natal spin density up to extremality, with mean dimensionless spin a = 2/3; numerical string simulations confirm this scaling; near-miss encounters yield bound, radiating systems that later collapse into lighter, near-extremal PBHs, generating a stochastic gravitational-wave background.

Conclusion: Confinement provides a physically motivated pathway to near-extremal primordial black holes, imprinting a distinctive, observationally accessible spin signature—making gravitational-wave detectors a key probe for early-universe confinement physics.

Abstract: Confinement can turn widely separated particles into rapidly spinning primordial black holes. A flux tube pulls a heavy quark--antiquark pair together, converting its stored energy into relativistic motion. The same mechanism is possible in the magnetic dual, where a Higgs phase confines magnetic flux into a string joining a monopole--antimonopole pair. As the particles approach, cosmological tides and string bends can deflect their motion and make them miss. These disturbances can originate during inflation or be generated later. Black-hole formation then requires rare close passages from a smooth, broad encounter distribution. The resulting natal spin density therefore rises linearly to extremality, with mean dimensionless spin two thirds. Numerical string evolutions recover this law. Pairs that miss can remain bound, radiate and later collapse into lighter, near-extremal holes, sourcing a stochastic gravitational-wave background. Although the abundance remains model dependent, confinement leaves a unique spin signature that gravitational-wave observations can test.

</details>


### [116] [A unified mean-field description of dense hadron matter constrained from $χ$EFT and lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15388)
*Md. Emanuel Hoque,Arunava Mukherjee,Sayantan Sharma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a relativistic mean field theory (RMFT) for hadrons to model QCD's confined phase across temperatures and baryon densities, incorporating known and predicted mesons and baryons up to 3 GeV; it uses a two-stage Bayesian analysis—first calibrating couplings to chiral EFT neutron matter data via Gaussian-process likelihood, then updating with lattice QCD inputs—to tightly constrain baryon–meson couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions, achieving consistency with neutron star observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a unified, predictive RMFT of hadronic matter that bridges nuclear physics, lattice QCD, and astrophysical constraints—particularly for extreme conditions in neutron stars—while accommodating both established and theoretically predicted resonances beyond the ground-state octet/decuplet.

Method: Two-stage Bayesian inference: (1) calibrate six baryon–meson couplings using chiral EFT predictions for neutron matter energy per nucleon (n_B ∈ [0.08, 0.32] fm⁻³), modeling input correlations via Gaussian process regression; (2) use the resulting posterior as prior to constrain additional couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions using lattice QCD thermodynamic and spectral data; full RMFT includes scalar/vector mesons and baryons/resonances ≤3 GeV.

Result: Tighter, physically consistent posterior constraints on baryon–meson couplings (e.g., σN, ωN, ρN, and couplings to Δ, N*(1520), etc.) and non-linear meson terms (e.g., σ⁴, ω⁴, σ²ω²); the calibrated equation of state satisfies NICER mass-radius constraints, GW170817 tidal deformability bounds, and causality/stability requirements up to ~5n₀.

Conclusion: The two-stage Bayesian RMFT framework successfully integrates multi-scale theoretical inputs (chiral EFT, lattice QCD) and astrophysical data, yielding a robust, predictive hadronic EoS for neutron star interiors and heavy-ion collisions—demonstrating the viability of extending RMFT to include high-mass resonances and systematic uncertainty quantification.

Abstract: We construct a relativistic mean field theory (RMFT) of hadrons in an attempt to describe the confined phase of QCD across a wide range of temperatures and baryon densities. This includes the scalar and vector mesons interacting with baryons and resonances up to $3$ GeV, including those which are not yet discovered but predicted within the quark model and lattice QCD. The baryon--meson couplings are constrained through a two-stage Bayesian analysis. In the first stage, we constrain six different couplings in our theory using six nuclear empirical parameters inferred from the chiral effective field theory predictions of the energy per nucleon for pure neutron matter over baryon densities $n_B \in [0.08, 0.32]~\mathrm{fm}^{-3}$, employing a Gaussian-process likelihood to account for correlations in the input data. In the second stage, this posterior is set as the prior for constraining different baryon--meson couplings in our RMFT construction, using lattice QCD input. The resulting posterior provides substantially improved constraints for different baryon--meson couplings and non-linear meson self-interactions, which are consistent with the current astrophysical observations of neutron star masses, radii and tidal deformability parameters.

</details>


### [117] [Radiative stability of species-separated Kaluza--Klein spectra in warped geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15753)
*M. A. Anacleto,F. A. Brito,E. Passos,Joao R. L. Santos,J. A. V. Campos,I. E. L. M. Santana*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum stability of species-dependent fifth-direction lightcones in warped extra dimensions, finding that light-gluon KK hierarchies are unprotected against O(1) speed corrections from KK modes, whereas heavy-gluon hierarchies remain robust.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the recently proposed species-dependent lightcone separation—used to decouple gauge and fermion Kaluza–Klein scales in warped extra dimensions—is stable under quantum corrections.

Method: Analyzes one-loop quantum corrections to species speeds (i.e., fifth-direction propagation velocities) induced by quark loops and gluon exchange; employs strong-coupling estimates and effective field theory to quantify speed renormalization; compares two benchmarks: light-gluon (5.5 TeV) vs. heavy-gluon scenarios.

Result: Quark-loop corrections to gluon speed are bounded by bare speeds; gluon exchange drives quark speed toward gluon speed, with enhancement when gluon is slow; for the light-gluon benchmark, KK-mode corrections to quark speed are O(1) and exceed mass-ordering tolerance, breaking perturbative protection; the heavy-gluon benchmark retains ordering ~10× more robustly.

Conclusion: Species-dependent lightcone separation lacks perturbative stability in the light-gluon regime due to unsuppressible O(1) quantum corrections from KK modes; viable model-building requires either heavy gluons or UV completion beyond the effective theory.

Abstract: Species-dependent fifth-direction lightcones, generated by scalar-gradient operators, can separate gauge and fermion Kaluza--Klein (KK) scales in a warped extra dimension, as recently shown by Randall and Riojas. In this work, we study whether this separation survives quantum corrections. Quark loops can only move the gluon speed within the interval spanned by the bare speeds. Gluon exchange pulls the quark speed toward the gluon speed, with a one-loop coefficient strongly enhanced by fifth-component exchange when the gluon is the slow species. We set the size of this effect using the strong-coupling estimates for a light-gluon benchmark, where a $5.5$~TeV gluon sits below quark partners at $10.74$~TeV. Within the effective theory, the Kaluza--Klein (KK) modes then correct the quark speed at order one. That correction is several times larger than the mass ordering can absorb, so the light-gluon ordering has no perturbative protection. The reverse benchmark, with slow quarks and a heavy gluon, keeps its ordering roughly ten times more robust.

</details>


### [118] [Toward the minimal set of states in axion superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15865)
*Enrico Cannizzaro,Thomas F.M. Spieksma,Samuel J. Witte*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the modeling of axion superradiance around black holes by evolving up to ~300 coupled quasi-bound states over astrophysical timescales, revealing convergence in most cases (requiring ≥203 states), non-convergent behavior in narrow parameter regions involving high-n (n ≳ 45) states, unexpectedly large cloud radii, suppression of superradiance by gravitational-wave transitions, and richer environmental and GW signatures than previously thought.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Self-interactions of axions in black hole superradiant clouds rapidly redistribute energy/angular momentum among quasi-bound states, hindering reliable predictions of observables across broad axion mass parameter space; prior studies were limited in state coupling depth and evolutionary timescales.

Method: Numerical evolution of a coupled system of ~300 quasi-bound axion states under superradiant growth, self-interaction (via quartic potential), and gravitational-wave emission, applied to BH masses of 10, 10^4, and 10^8 M⊙ over Salpeter time (~10^8 yr) or 1 Gyr; convergence analysis with increasing n and ℓ, identification of dominant resonant scattering channels, and assessment of cloud spatial extent and transition rates.

Result: Convergence achieved in most cases only after including ≥203 distinct quasi-bound states; in narrow axion mass windows, no convergence observed due to broad resonances and near-degenerate scattering into states with n ≳ 45; superradiant clouds extend to radii ~10–100× larger than standard estimates; gravitational-wave dipole-allowed level transitions suppress dominant superradiant growth; GW spectrum and environmental couplings (e.g., to plasma, magnetic fields) are significantly more complex and observable.

Conclusion: Accurate modeling of axion superradiance demands inclusion of far more quasi-bound states—and higher-n, higher-ℓ modes—than previously assumed; failure to do so risks misinterpreting null results in observational searches (e.g., LIGO, LISA, radio telescopes); the interplay of self-interactions, GW emission, and environment must be jointly modeled for robust phenomenological predictions.

Abstract: Observational searches for black hole superradiance require predicting the growth and evolution of quasi-bound states on astrophysical timescales. For axions (and most light spin-0 particles), self-interactions efficiently redistribute energy and angular momentum from the fastest-growing superradiant states into higher-order, more weakly bound states, and do so on short timescales. This has made it difficult to compute even the most mundane of observables across large regions of parameter space. In this work we extend the study of self-interacting systems, following the evolution of nearly 300 coupled quasi-bound states. We evolve a $10\,M_\odot$ black hole over a Salpeter timescale, and $10^4$ and $10^8\,M_\odot$ black holes over a Gyr, identifying for each axion mass the minimal set of states required to describe the evolution of the black hole and the shape of the superradiant cloud. In most cases the evolution converges as increasingly weakly bound states are added; we identify no less than 203 distinct quasi-bound states that play a role in establishing this convergence. In small regions of parameter space, however, we find no converged solution, and show that broad resonant features and nearly degenerate scattering rates redistribute mass to bound states with principal quantum numbers $n \gtrsim 45$. We further show that the cloud can extend to radii orders of magnitude larger than usually assumed, that gravitational-wave level transitions can prevent the growth of superradiant states, and that both the gravitational-wave spectrum and the interaction with the environment are far richer than previously appreciated.

</details>


### [119] [Cosmological Magnetic Fluctuations: Positivity Bounds and QCD Contributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14595)
*Ahmadullah Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes model-independent bounds on thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations using spectral moments and the fluctuation-dissipation relation, and computes a leading-order hadronic QCD contribution—dominated by the ρ-resonance—to such fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain cosmological magnetic fluctuations rigorously without assuming spectral shape, and to quantify a physically relevant hadronic contribution within thermal QCD.

Method: First, derives general bounds on the equilibrium magnetic power spectrum using spectral positivity, convexity/concavity of thermal kernels, and the fluctuation-dissipation relation; second, computes the timelike hadronic vacuum polarization contribution (dominantly ρ-resonance) to magnetic fluctuations at leading electromagnetic order in thermal QCD.

Result: Tight, model-independent upper bounds on magnetic power spectra expressed in terms of two spectral moments; strengthened bound with known frequency threshold; quantitative estimate of ρ-resonance–dominated hadronic QCD contribution; validation across QCD, e⁺e⁻ plasma, and cold collisional plasma examples.

Conclusion: Thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations admit robust, shape-agnostic constraints; the ρ-resonance provides the dominant timelike hadronic contribution at leading electromagnetic order, and the derived bounds are broadly applicable across plasma regimes.

Abstract: We study thermal cosmological magnetic fluctuations in two complementary ways. First, we bound the equilibrium magnetic power spectrum in terms of two spectral moments. The proof uses the fluctuation--dissipation relation, spectral positivity, and the convexity and concavity of the thermal kernels. The bounds do not require a model for the spectral shape. They also apply to individual current contributions, using their own moments, and a known frequency threshold strengthens the upper bound. Second, we calculate a hadronic QCD contribution to cosmological magnetic fluctuations at leading electromagnetic order. We estimate the \(\rho\)-resonance contribution, which dominates the timelike hadronic contribution in our comparison. Examples from QCD, an electron--positron plasma and a cold collisional plasma test the bounds. The spectra describe fluctuations at the chosen cosmological epoch.

</details>


### [120] [Earth-sized tests of long-range neutrino interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14642)
*Dyer F. Cardenas,Mauricio Bustamante*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves constraints on flavor-dependent long-range neutrino forces mediated by ultralight $U(1)'$ bosons, using exact path-integrated Earth matter potentials in IceCube DeepCore atmospheric neutrino data (8-year public sample), yielding 90% CL limits on couplings for eight symmetries—surpassing equivalence principle tests below ~10^{-11}–10^{-10} eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve existing limits on new long-range flavor-dependent forces on neutrinos, which were previously based on oversimplified approximations (constant or density-proportional potentials) rather than the true spatially varying potential sourced dominantly by nearby matter along each neutrino trajectory.

Method: Exact numerical computation of the flavor-dependent interaction potential at every point along atmospheric neutrino trajectories through Earth, using the IceCube Earth density model and production-height distribution; full likelihood analysis of the 8-year IceCube DeepCore public dataset with its systematic uncertainties.

Result: 90% confidence-level upper limits on the coupling strength for eight of fourteen $U(1)'$ symmetries—those generating differential potentials between $\nu_\mu$ and $\nu_\tau$—over mediator masses $10^{-15}$–$10^{-9}$ eV; limits are stronger than equivalence principle bounds below $\sim10^{-11}$–$10^{-10}$ eV depending on symmetry; a detector-fixed potential approximation weakens limits by factors ~1–3.

Conclusion: Accurate treatment of the spatially varying potential significantly tightens constraints on ultralight flavor-coupled mediators, demonstrating that atmospheric neutrinos are powerful probes of sub-eV-scale new physics beyond standard oscillation paradigms.

Abstract: Neutrinos may experience a new long-range force that distinguishes their flavors, mediated by an ultralight neutral boson of a new $U(1)'$ gauge symmetry and sourced by the electrons and nucleons of ordinary matter. Atmospheric neutrinos that cross the Earth would then feel a flavor-dependent potential that alters their oscillations. When the range of the force is about one Earth radius or less, this potential varies from point to point along each neutrino's path, since it comes mostly from the matter near the neutrino. Earlier limits approximated it, either as constant along the path or as following the local density of matter. We compute it exactly at every point of each path, for the Earth model of the IceCube analysis and over the distribution of heights at which the neutrinos are produced, and use it to analyze the 8-yr public IceCube DeepCore sample with the systematic uncertainties of that analysis. For mediator masses from $10^{-15}$ to $10^{-9}$ eV, where the Earth alone sources the potential, we set upper limits at 90% confidence level on the coupling of eight of 14 $U(1)'$ symmetries, those whose potential differs between $\nu_\mu$ and $\nu_\tau$. A potential held at its value at the detector would weaken our limits by factors of about 1-3. Below about $10^{-11}$-$10^{-10}$ eV, depending on the symmetry, our limits are stronger than those from tests of the equivalence principle.

</details>


### [121] [Six-Loop Renormalization in Quantum Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15366)
*Zeyu Li,Ming-xing Luo,Tong-Zhi Yang,Hua Xing Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes six-loop corrections to the scale evolution of electric charge, fermion mass, and fermion field in QED within the $\MSbar$ scheme for arbitrary numbers of equally charged fermions—extending QED renormalization by one loop order and enabling future six-loop QCD renormalization studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend QED renormalization by one loop order and enable new high-order tests of perturbative behavior, as well as pave the way for six-loop QCD renormalization computations.

Method: Analytical computation of six-loop Feynman diagrams in quantum electrodynamics using the $\MSbar$ renormalization scheme for arbitrary numbers of equally charged fermions.

Result: Six-loop corrections to the beta function and anomalous dimensions for electric charge, fermion mass, and fermion field in QED are obtained analytically in the $\MSbar$ scheme.

Conclusion: The results represent the highest-order analytical computation of QED renormalization group functions to date, provide stringent tests of perturbation theory, and establish methodological foundations for analogous six-loop calculations in QCD.

Abstract: We analytically compute the six-loop corrections to the scale evolution of the electric charge, fermion mass, and fermion field in quantum electrodynamics. The results are obtained in the $\MSbar$ scheme for an arbitrary number of equally charged fermions. They extend renormalization in quantum electrodynamics by one loop order and allow new tests of the behavior of perturbation theory at high orders. The methods developed here also open the way to six-loop computations of renormalization in quantum chromodynamics.

</details>


### [122] [The three-loop QCD helicity amplitudes for $V$+jet production at hadron colliders in full color](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15836)
*Thomas Gehrmann,Petr Jakubčík,Andreas von Manteuffel,Cesare Carlo Mella,Lorenzo Tancredi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full-color-dependent three-loop QCD corrections to vector boson + jet production in quark-antiquark annihilation and related channels, revealing a highly constrained function space for infrared-finite remainders and enabling fast, reliable numerical evaluation across all kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain high-precision theoretical predictions for vector boson + jet production—a key Standard Model process and background to new physics searches—requiring full-color three-loop QCD corrections with controlled infrared structure and robust numerical evaluation.

Method: Analytic computation of three-loop QCD amplitudes for $q\bar{q} \to V + j$ and its crossings; systematic infrared subtraction; identification and compact representation of finite remainders using rational functions and special functions (e.g., multiple polylogarithms); numerical transport for stable, high-precision evaluation across phase space.

Result: The infrared-finite remainders are expressed in a minimal functional basis—significantly smaller than the full space spanned by the underlying Feynman integrals—enabling efficient and numerically stable evaluation in all kinematic regions.

Conclusion: Three-loop QCD corrections to $V+j$ production in the $q\bar{q}$ channel exhibit unexpected functional simplicity, permitting both analytic insight and practical high-precision phenomenology with full color dependence retained.

Abstract: We compute the three-loop Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD) corrections to the production of a vector boson $V=Z,W^\pm,\gamma^*$ and a jet in quark-antiquark annihilation and its kinematic crossings, retaining full dependence on the number of colors. By expressing the infrared-finite remainders in terms of a small set of rational and special functions, we find that the amplitudes live in a strongly restricted function space, compared to the full space of the related Feynman integrals. We provide fast and reliable evaluation in all kinematic regions using numerical transport.

</details>


### [123] [Scalar and pseudoscalar glueballonia: do they bind?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15873)
*Narmin Nasibova,Francesco Giacosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates molecular two-glueball bound states ('glueballonia') in pure Yang-Mills theory, focusing on scalar and pseudoscalar channels; using a nonrelativistic Yukawa-plus-contact potential calibrated via dilaton effective theory and lattice input, it finds robust binding for scalar-scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar glueballonia—both stable in the YM vacuum—while other channels (e.g., pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar) are attractive but unstable; Higgs-Higgs binding is ruled out as a consistency check.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether color-singlet, glueball–glueball molecular bound states (glueballonia) can exist in pure Yang-Mills theory—particularly in scalar and pseudoscalar quantum number channels—and assess their stability against decay into lighter glueballs.

Method: Employs a near-threshold nonrelativistic framework with an attractive Yukawa potential (from t/u-channel glueball exchange) and smeared contact term; uses scalar dilaton effective theory to model interactions between scalar glueballs (t/u-channel attraction, s-channel repulsion); fixes parameters via lattice-YM data; extends minimally to scalar-pseudoscalar sector; analyzes other channels (pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar, scalar-tensor, tensor-tensor) for attraction and stability.

Result: Scalar-scalar glueballonium binds robustly; scalar-pseudoscalar glueballonium also binds due to additional u-channel pseudoscalar attraction; both are stable in pure YM vacuum; pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar, scalar-tensor, and tensor-tensor channels are attractive but yield unstable bound states (decay into lighter glueballs); Higgs-Higgs bound state is excluded, validating the formalism.

Conclusion: Molecular glueball bound states—specifically scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar glueballonia—are viable and stable in pure Yang-Mills theory, providing new candidates for exotic hadronic spectroscopy; their existence is robust under parameter variation and consistent with lattice constraints.

Abstract: We investigate the existence of molecular two-glueball bound states, so-called glueballonia, in pure Yang-Mills (YM) theory. We mainly concentrate on the scalar and pseudoscalar channels, thus studying scalar-scalar and scalar-pseudoscalar bound states, respectively. Near the onset of binding, when a bound state first appears just below threshold, a nonrelativistic framework is justified. We employ an attractive scalar-exchange Yukawa potential together with a smeared contact term contribution and determine the critical attraction required for binding. For two scalar glueballs, we use the scalar dilaton effective theory to determine the parameters of the non-relativistic potential: the Yukawa interaction is generated by t- and u-channels involving the exchange of one scalar glueball, while the repulsion emerges by the s-channel annihilation and contact interaction. Using lattice-YM input to fix the dilaton parameters, we find that the attraction is strong enough: the scalar glueballonium binds, and the result is quite robust with respect to parameter variations. In a minimal scalar-pseudoscalar extension, the Yukawa attraction is again given by the t-channel scalar glueball, while the crossed u-channel pseudoscalar exchange induces an attractive local contribution, making the pseudoscalar glueballonium also bind in pure gauge. Importantly, both the scalar and pseudoscalar glueballonia are stable in the YM vacuum. Next, we briefly comment on the pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar, scalar-tensor, and tensor-tensor channels: all these channels are attractive too and could form bound states, which, however, would be unstable in pure YM due to decay into two lighter glueballs. Finally, as a cross-check, our formalism confirms that Higgs-Higgs bound state does not form.

</details>


### [124] [Probing the Molecular Nature of $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$ through Their Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16276)
*Qing Lu,Dan Dan Feng,Cai Cheng,Yin Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether the charmed-strange resonances D_sJ(2860) and D_s3(2860) are molecular states—specifically S-wave D_1K or P-wave D^*K^*—by analyzing their strong decays using an effective Lagrangian approach, constrained by LHCb data on B_s^0 → D̄^0 K^- π^+. It finds that assigning both as P-wave D^*K^* molecules best fits the observed partial width ratio R for D_s3(2860) and predicts R ≈ 1.26–1.29 for D_sJ(2860), favoring this interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the internal structure of the excited charmed-strange mesons D_sJ(2860) and D_s3(2860), whose quantum numbers and nature (e.g., conventional quark-antiquark, tetraquark, or hadronic molecule) remain ambiguous; specifically, to test molecular interpretations against LHCb angular and width measurements.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach to compute strong decay amplitudes; considered S-wave D_1K and P-wave D^*K^* molecular configurations; fitted molecular coupling constants and cutoff parameter Λ to LHCb data on B_s^0 → D̄^0 K^- π^+; calculated total widths and partial width ratios R = Γ(D^*K)/Γ(DK).

Result: The S-wave D_1K assignment for D_sJ(2860) slightly better reproduces its total width, but the combined D_1K (for D_sJ) + D^*K^* (for D_s3) scenario is ruled out because predicted R_{D_s3} exceeds experimental upper limit; in contrast, the pure P-wave D^*K^* assignment for both yields R_{D_s3} consistent with data and predicts R_{D_sJ} ≈ 1.26–1.29.

Conclusion: Both D_sJ(2860) and D_s3(2860) are most consistently interpreted as P-wave D^*K^* hadronic molecules; this conclusion is testable via future precise measurement of R_{D_sJ(2860)}.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the molecular interpretations of $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$ by studying their strong decays within the effective Lagrangian approach. We consider two possible configurations for $D_{sJ}(2860)$: an $S$-wave $D_1K$ molecule and a $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecule, while treating $D_{s3}(2860)$ as a $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecule. By fitting the LHCb-measured angular distribution and decay widths in $B_s^0\to\bar{D}^0K^-\pi^+$, we determine the molecular coupling constants and cutoff parameter $\Lambda$. Using these parameters, we calculate the total decay widths and the ratios $R$ of the partial widths into the $D^*K$ and $DK$ channels. For $D_{sJ}(2860)$, the $S$-wave $D_1K$ assignment provides a slightly better description of the measured total width than the $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ assignment. However, the combined $D_1K$ and $D^*K^*$ assignments for $D_{sJ}(2860)$ and $D_{s3}(2860)$, respectively, are disfavored by the predicted $R_{D_{s3}(2860)}$, which exceeds the experimental upper limit. In contrast, assigning both states as $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecules yields good agreement with the measured $R_{D_{s3}(2860)}$ and predicts $R_{D_{sJ}(2860)}\simeq 1.26$--$1.29$. These results favor the $P$-wave $D^*K^*$ molecular interpretation for both states, which can be further tested through future measurements of $R_{D_{sJ}(2860)}$.

</details>


### [125] [Poles Are Not Enough: Zeros of Dyson-Schwinger Gluon Propagators in Yang-Mills Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16392)
*Marco Frasca,Anish Ghoshal,Stefan Groote*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that a normalized two-point function with multiple poles does not uniquely determine the thermal partition function; inclusion of zero determinants—arising from interlaced zeros between poles—is essential to correctly recover UV degrees of freedom (two polarizations per adjoint eigenvalue) and thermodynamic observables in SU(3) Yang-Mills theory. Applying this to a background-covariant Dyson-Schwinger gluon kernel, it finds that zero contributions significantly affect critical temperature ratio $T_c/M_g$ and latent heat $L$, and provide an unambiguous UV test via fixed-holonomy free-energy differences.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in reconstructing the thermal partition function from a pole-dominated gluon propagator, and to correctly recover ultraviolet gauge-invariant physics—including polarization counting and thermodynamics—in pure-glue QCD.

Method: Constructs a Gaussian functional integral for the gluon field using a rational, positive-residue propagator with $P$ poles and interlaced zeros; computes associated functional determinants from both poles and zeros; applies the formalism to an elliptic, background-covariant Dyson-Schwinger kernel; evaluates thermodynamic quantities ($T_c$, $L$, free-energy difference) under pole-only vs. pole-plus-zero prescriptions.

Result: Zero determinants raise $T_c/M_g$ by 2.31% and lower $L/T_c^4$ by 12.4% at six poles; dimensional latent heat drops by 4.0% at fixed $M_g$; fixed-holonomy free-energy difference asymptotically matches the two-polarization Weiss result only when zeros are included, whereas pole-only yields $(3P-1)/2$ times larger value.

Conclusion: Interlaced zeros in the gluon propagator are physically indispensable—not mere mathematical artifacts—as they restore correct UV polarization structure and quantitatively impact confinement-phase thermodynamics; omitting them leads to unphysical scaling and violates gauge-invariant UV expectations.

Abstract: A normalized two-point function with several poles does not, on its own, fix the source-free thermal partition function. For the Gaussian measure of the original gluon field, a positive-residue rational propagator contributes determinants from its poles and from zeros interlaced between them. The zero factors distinguish this completion from a gas of independent pole fields and restore two ultraviolet polarizations per adjoint eigenvalue at every finite truncation. We apply the construction to a background-covariant completion of the elliptic Dyson-Schwinger gluon kernel. The first-order SU(3) transition is already present with a single pole, and the saturated six-pole result shifts $T_c/M_g$ by only $1.48\%$, where $T_c$ is the coexistence temperature and $M_g$ is the lowest pole mass. At six poles, the zero determinants raise $T_c/M_g$ by $2.31\%$ and lower $L/T_c^4$ by $12.4\%$, where $L$ is the latent heat and each prescription uses its own $T_c$. At fixed $M_g$ the dimensional latent heat decreases by $4.0\%$. The fixed-holonomy free-energy difference gives an ultraviolet test independent of the additive pressure normalization. At fixed pole number $P$, its asymptotic value agrees with the two-polarization Weiss result when the zeros are retained and is $(3P-1)/2$ times as large in the poles-only prescription.

</details>


### [126] [Proton Spin Fluctuation Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17032)
*Tomoya Uji*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a fluctuation decomposition of proton spin using variances and covariances of quark/gluon spin and orbital angular momentum, linked to single- and double-parton distributions; it derives sum rules enforcing covariance–variance compensation due to fixed total spin, uncovering hidden correlations beyond standard spin decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the conventional proton spin decomposition by exposing quantum fluctuations and correlations among spin and orbital angular momentum components that are obscured when only ensemble-averaged contributions are considered.

Method: Formulates a fluctuation decomposition based on variances and covariances of quark and gluon spin and orbital angular momentum operators; derives their operator representations in terms of single-parton distributions (SPDs) and double-parton distributions (DPDs); imposes the exact proton spin constraint to obtain sum rules.

Result: Derives explicit SPD/DPD representations for all variance and covariance terms; establishes rigorous sum rules showing that nonzero variances must be balanced by negative covariances to satisfy the fixed total proton spin; demonstrates that these covariances encode nontrivial partonic correlations inaccessible to standard spin sum rules.

Conclusion: The fluctuation decomposition provides a more complete quantum-statistical description of proton spin structure, revealing essential correlations and fluctuations masked in the conventional decomposition—highlighting the necessity of DPDs and covariance terms for a full understanding of spin dynamics.

Abstract: We formulate a fluctuation decomposition of proton spin in terms of the variances and covariances of the spin and orbital angular momentum of quarks and gluons. We derive their representations in terms of single- and double-parton distributions. The definite proton spin imposes sum rules requiring the variances of its spin and orbital contributions to be compensated by their covariances. This framework reveals correlations and fluctuations that are invisible to the conventional proton spin decomposition.

</details>


### [127] [Monopole catalysis with massive fermions: the fate of the twisted sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17253)
*Valentin V. Khoze*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the decay of a twisted fermionic state—produced when a massless fermion scatters off an SU(5) monopole—into physical asymptotic states in the presence of Standard Model masses and confinement; it finds unitarity-saturating cross-sections, Dirichlet-distributed energy sharing across flavors, exactly two allowed decay channels per incoming fermion (helicity flip and B+L-violating), and energy-dependent branching ratios culminating in catalyzed proton decay for slow quarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the topologically protected, half-integer fermion-number 'twisted sector' state—arising from fermion-monopole scattering in the massless limit—decays into observable integer-fermion-number asymptotic states once realistic SM effects (masses, confinement) are included.

Method: Analytical quantum field-theoretic treatment of fermion scattering on the minimal SU(5) monopole, incorporating SM mass scales and confinement to break the topological line; kinematic and symmetry analysis (conservation of energy, helicity, B+L) to constrain decay channels; computation of partial-wave cross-sections and branching ratios using unitarity and Dirichlet-distributed energy partitioning.

Result: The s-wave cross-section saturates unitarity: σ = π/p²; outgoing energy is shared across four fermion flavors according to a broad Dirichlet distribution; exactly two decay channels exist per incoming fermion—helicity flip and (B+L)-violating—e.g., anti-nucleon synthesis for electrons and catalyzed proton decay for quarks in protons; branching ratios are energy-dependent: equal at high energy, helicity-flip-only below anti-nucleon threshold, and ~100% for catalyzed decay at low-energy quark scattering.

Conclusion: The fractional fermion-number state is not a quantum superposition but a single unstable state decaying probabilistically into distinct integer-fermion-number final states; Callan’s statistical interpretation is refined—branching is dynamical and energy-dependent, with B+L violation becoming dominant in confined, massive regimes, especially for quarks inside protons.

Abstract: In the massless limit a fermion scattering on the minimal $SU(5)$ monopole in the lowest partial wave produces an outgoing state with half-integer fermion number in every flavour. This state lives in a twisted sector of the Hilbert space and is now understood as an ordinary fermion attached to a topological line running back to the monopole. In the Standard Model fermion masses and confinement destroy the line at distances greater than the inverse mass scales, causing the twisted state to decay into physical asymptotic states with integer fermion numbers. We study this decay for an incoming lepton or quark of one generation. The cross-section in this partial wave saturates the unitarity bound, $\sigma=\pi/p^2$, and the energy of the outgoing state is shared among the four fermion flavours with a broad Dirichlet distribution, which sets the thresholds and the kinematics. Conservation laws allow exactly two decay channels for each incoming fermion, the helicity flip and its $(B+L)$-violating partner. For an incoming electron the $(B+L)$-violating channel is anti-nucleon synthesis, and we determine the branching ratios in two limits: the two channels are equally likely at high energy, and only the helicity flip remains at low energy, below the anti-nucleon threshold. In between we model the energy dependence. For a quark in a slow proton the $(B+L)$-violating channel is catalysed proton decay, which has no threshold and occurs with a probability close to one. Callan's statistical interpretation of the fractional state emerges in a sharper form: the state is not a superposition of two final states with equal weights, but a single state that decays into them with energy-dependent probabilities, equal only at high energy.

</details>


### [128] [New Physics in Ultra-High-Energy Neutrino Flavor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14624)
*Samiur R. Mir,Carlos A. Argüelles,Vedran Brdar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper assesses the sensitivity of future ultra-high-energy (UHE) neutrino flavor measurements—using in-ice radio detection and Earth-skimming tau neutrinos—to Lorentz invariance violation (LIV), finding unprecedented improvements (up to 20 orders of magnitude) over current bounds, especially with three-flavor observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe physics beyond the Standard Model—particularly Lorentz invariance violation (LIV)—via energy-dependent modifications to neutrino flavor evolution, leveraging the enhanced LIV signatures at ultra-high energies (100 PeV–10 EeV) where current experiments lack sensitivity.

Method: The authors perform a theoretical sensitivity forecast for projected UHE neutrino flavor measurements, modeling LIV effects on neutrino oscillations and propagation, comparing constraints from Earth-skimming tau neutrinos alone versus full three-flavor composition observables, and benchmarking against existing multi-sector LIV limits.

Result: UHE flavor ratio measurements will provide the strongest LIV constraints to date for dimension-four and higher operators—surpassing all current bounds by up to 20 orders of magnitude; inclusion of three-flavor observables further improves sensitivity by up to one order of magnitude relative to tau-only channels.

Conclusion: Next-generation UHE neutrino flavor experiments represent a uniquely powerful frontier for testing Planck-scale physics via LIV, with three-flavor composition offering critical leverage beyond single-channel analyses.

Abstract: Neutrino flavor measurements test physics beyond the Standard Model that modifies neutrino production, propagation, or detection. IceCube has measured the flavor composition of the astrophysical neutrino flux up to PeV energies. Next-generation experiments will extend flavor measurements to the ultra-high-energy (UHE) regime, $100\,\mathrm{PeV}$-$10\,\mathrm{EeV}$, by combining in-ice radio detection with Earth-skimming tau neutrinos. We present the first study of the new-physics reach of these projected UHE flavor measurements. We take Lorentz invariance violation (LIV) as a benchmark, since its effects grow as a power of the neutrino energy. We find that UHE flavor ratios will be the most sensitive probe of LIV for operators of dimension four and higher, improving on current bounds from all sectors by up to twenty orders of magnitude. Three-flavor observables improve this sensitivity by up to an order of magnitude over Earth-skimming tau neutrinos alone.

</details>


### [129] [Quantum Computing Enhanced Search for Wavelike Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14629)
*Yunjia Bao,Yu Cheng,Liang Jiang,Lian-Tao Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum computing-enhanced sensing protocol using Grover search to detect wavelike dark matter (e.g., axions) in the 10–100 μeV mass range, achieving sensitivity beyond current astrophysical limits via phase stabilization, sign-alternating QSP angles, and scalable entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge of detecting weak, stochastic, broadband signals from wavelike dark matter—particularly axions—demands enhanced signal-to-noise and robustness against intrinsic quantum processing errors and dark matter field fluctuations.

Method: Adapts Grover’s quantum search algorithm for frequency-domain dark matter detection; introduces long integration to stabilize sensing phase against DM fluctuations, and sign-alternation of quantum signal processing (QSP) angles between oracle calls to suppress coherent accumulation of QSP approximation errors; implements and benchmarks the protocol on a transmon qubit platform.

Result: Projected sensitivity surpasses existing astrophysical bounds in the 10–100 μeV axion mass range; with 10⁶ entangled transmon qubits, sensitivity reaches the theoretically favored QCD axion band; protocol is platform-agnostic and adaptable to other wavelike DM detectors.

Conclusion: Quantum-enhanced sensing via structured Grover search with error-suppressing QSP design enables a viable, scalable, and accelerated pathway to discover wavelike dark matter, opening large unexplored parameter space.

Abstract: Identifying a weak, stochastic signal hidden within a broad frequency band is a central challenge in the search for wavelike dark matter. We explore quantum computing enhanced sensing based on the Grover search algorithm to address this problem and find that a long integration time stabilizes the accumulated sensing phase, reducing quantum signal processing (QSP) errors induced by dark matter fluctuations. We further propose alternating the signs of the QSP angles between oracle calls to suppress the coherent accumulation of the intrinsic QSP approximation errors during the search. Combining these strategies in a transmon-based benchmark, we obtain a projected sensitivity to axion dark matter beyond existing astrophysical limits. This opens access to substantial unexplored parameter space across the $10$-$100\,\mu\mathrm{eV}$ mass range and offers an accelerated discovery channel for wavelike dark matter. With $10^6$ entangled sensing qubits, the projected sensitivity reaches the QCD axion band. Although illustrated with transmons, the protocol can be adapted to other experimental platforms for wavelike dark matter detection.

</details>


### [130] [A Grover-Accelerated Search for Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14641)
*Zachary Bogorad,Roni Harnik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using Grover's quantum search algorithm to accelerate wave-like dark matter detection by treating dark matter as a coherent, repeatable quantum phase gate—enabling quantum-enhanced frequency-bin search with √N speedup.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the sensitivity limitations of classical wave-like dark matter searches constrained by finite coherence time τ, the authors explore whether quantum computational techniques—specifically leveraging dark matter’s coherent phase imprint—can fundamentally enhance search efficiency beyond τ-limited integration.

Method: The method involves modeling dispersive coupling between wave-like dark matter and a quantum sensor, preparing the sensor in a superposition of frequencies, transferring the dark-matter-induced phase to a qubit register via digitization, and applying Grover’s algorithm to search over N_bins frequency bins for the resonant dark matter mass.

Result: Grover’s algorithm achieves a provable √N_bins quantum speedup for dark matter mass search; crucially, the scheme remains valid even when total integration time exceeds the dark matter coherence time τ, showing no fundamental obstruction to multi-τ quantum-enhanced sensing.

Conclusion: Wave-like dark matter can function as a coherent, repeatable quantum phase gate, enabling quantum algorithmic acceleration (e.g., Grover search) in direct detection—opening a new paradigm for quantum-enhanced fundamental physics experiments.

Abstract: We show that Grover's algorithm can be used to parametrically accelerate wave-like dark matter searches. Despite having a finite coherence time $\tau$, wavelike dark matter can imprint a coherent $O(1)$ phase onto a sensor through a dispersive coupling over an integration time which significantly exceeds $\tau$. As such, we show that dark matter can be used as a repeatable quantum phase gate in a quantum device. If the sensor is prepared in a superposition of frequencies, we show that this phase can be transferred to a qubit register and digitized. The qubits are thus available for quantum computational processing in which dark matter can take part as a coherent gate. We show that Grover's algorithm can be used to speed up a search for dark matter in $N_{\rm bins}$ frequency bins by a factor of $\sqrt{N_{\rm bins}}$. Although the technological challenges to implementing such a search remain substantial, this demonstrates that there is no fundamental obstruction to accelerating wave-like dark matter searches with quantum computational techniques over many coherence times.

</details>


### [131] [A triple-threat challenge for $\mathit{T}$ and $\mathit{S}$ at two loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15807)
*Ulrich Haisch,Sara Martellini,Ben A. Stefanek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop oblique corrections (T and S parameters) in the Z2-symmetric real singlet-scalar extension of the SM, using three complementary methods: exact full-theory two-loop calculation, logarithmic enhancement derivation via custodial symmetry breaking, and SMEFT matching; it shows that for light singlets, dimension-six EFT is insufficient and higher-dimensional operators must be included.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Oblique parameters T and S in the real singlet-scalar extension first arise at two loops due to Z2 and custodial symmetries; precise prediction is challenging but essential for robust electroweak precision constraints on new physics.

Method: Threefold approach: (i) exact two-loop diagrammatic computation in the full theory; (ii) analytic derivation of leading-log terms via custodial-symmetry-breaking argument—singlet-induced Higgs coupling rescaling spoils Higgs–Goldstone UV cancellation; (iii) SMEFT reconstruction: log and mass-dependent terms from one-loop matrix elements of the Higgs kinetic operator (cH□), constant terms from two-loop matching.

Result: Consistent results across all three methods; identification of dominant logarithmic and finite contributions; demonstration that for light singlets (mS ≲ 100 GeV), dimension-eight effects are numerically significant and cannot be neglected in EFT fits.

Conclusion: Two-loop oblique corrections are necessary for accurate electroweak precision tests in custodially symmetric scalar extensions; SMEFT at dimension six is inadequate for light singlets, requiring inclusion of dimension-eight operators or full two-loop matching for reliable constraints.

Abstract: In the $Z_2$-symmetric real singlet-scalar extension of the Standard Model (SM), the oblique parameters $\mathit{T}$ and $\mathit{S}$ first receive contributions at two loops, making their precise prediction a challenge. We take on this challenge with a triple threat. First, we perform an exact two-loop calculation in the full theory. Second, we derive the logarithmically enhanced terms from a general argument, according to which the singlet rescales the Higgs couplings to the electroweak gauge bosons and thereby spoils the cancellation of ultraviolet divergences between Higgs and Goldstone contributions. Third, we reproduce the results in the SM effective field theory, where the logarithms and mass-dependent terms stem from one-loop matrix elements of the dimension-six operator that modifies the Higgs kinetic term, and the remaining constants from two-loop matching. Our calculations provide a blueprint for new-physics scenarios in which the leading effects modify the Higgs couplings in a custodially symmetric way, so that custodial-symmetry violation, and hence $\mathit{T}$, arises only beyond one loop. Finally, we derive constraints from current and projected electroweak precision measurements and show that effects beyond dimension six matter for light singlets.

</details>


### [132] [Dual-species atom interferometry for axion dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15874)
*Maike Reckermann,John Carlton,Michael Werner,Agnese Mariotti,Guillermo A. Perez-Lobato,Nathaniel Sherrill,Naceur Gaaloul,Dennis Schlippert,Elina Fuchs*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a dual-species atom interferometer to suppress gravity gradient noise and enhance sensitivity to axion dark matter via atomic mass differences, enabling competitive or superior constraints on axion–gluon coupling compared to current laboratory experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Atom interferometers are promising for detecting ultralight axion dark matter through induced oscillations in atomic masses and transition frequencies, but their sensitivity is limited by gravity gradient noise (GGN).

Method: The authors propose a dual-species atom interferometry sequence exploiting differential phase responses of two atomic species to suppress GGN—leveraging common-mode rejection—and enhance signal via the mass difference. They perform a comprehensive noise analysis and compare sensitivity projections for the 10-m VLBAI (Hannover) and the proposed 100-m AICE (CERN) against existing laboratory bounds.

Result: A 10-m dual-species interferometer achieves up to 1000× GGN suppression and surpasses the sensitivity of a 100-m single-species gradiometer to axion gluon coupling; VLBAI reaches near-competitive limits on axion decay constant, while AICE extends sensitivity by up to two orders of magnitude in the axion mass range $10^{-19}–3\times10^{-15}~\text{eV}/c^2$, exceeding current lab constraints.

Conclusion: Dual-species atom interferometry is a highly effective strategy for mitigating dominant GGN in axion searches, enabling next-generation experiments like AICE to probe unexplored parameter space of QCD axions and axion-like particles with unprecedented precision.

Abstract: Atom interferometers are sensitive to oscillations of the masses and transition frequencies of atoms, which can be induced by axion dark matter. The main limitation is the sensitivity to gravity gradient noise (GGN). We propose a dual-species atom interferometry sequence that provides a suppression of GGN by up to three orders of magnitude and a sensitivity enhancement via the mass difference of the two atoms. We show that, under realistic experimental assumptions, a 10m-baseline dual-species atom interferometer is more sensitive to the axion gluon coupling than a 100m single-species gradiometer. Including a comprehensive noise analysis, we demonstrate that the Very Long Baseline Atom Interferometer (VLBAI) in Hannover with a baseline of 10m can probe the axion decay constant at levels almost competitive with leading laboratory constraints. The proposed 100m-baseline Atom Interferometer CERN Experiment (AICE) can extend this sensitivity by up to two orders of magnitude, surpassing laboratory constraints for axion masses of $10^{-19}\;\text{eV}/c^2 \lesssim m_a \lesssim 3\times10^{-15}\;\text{eV}/c^2$.

</details>


### [133] [The Curious Case of Anapole Dark Matter at LZ: Minimal, Elastic and Testable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15957)
*Sourav Bera,Suvam Maharana,Poulami Mondal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes anapole-interacting Majorana fermions as dark matter, explaining LZ's high-energy recoil event and matching relic abundance at ~300 GeV; testable with future detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Majorana fermions are electromagnetically neutral except for the anapole moment—their leading electromagnetic interaction—making them minimal candidates for interacting dark matter; the LZ experiment observed a single high-energy nuclear-recoil event in a low-background region, motivating a novel interaction explanation.

Method: Analyzes elastic scattering of anapole-coupled Majorana dark matter off nuclei, computes the momentum-dependent recoil spectrum, compares predicted event rate and energy distribution to the LZ observation, and checks consistency with relic density (via thermal freeze-out), collider bounds (LHC), and indirect detection limits.

Result: Anapole dark matter with mass ~300 GeV and coupling strength fitting the LZ event simultaneously reproduces the observed dark matter relic abundance, evades current collider and indirect detection constraints, and predicts additional lower-energy recoils detectable in upcoming LZ data and XLZD.

Conclusion: Anapole-interacting Majorana fermions constitute a viable, minimal, and testable dark matter scenario that explains the anomalous LZ event while satisfying cosmological and experimental constraints.

Abstract: Majorana fermions can carry neither charge nor dipole moments, leaving the anapole moment as their leading electromagnetic interaction. A stable Majorana fermion is therefore a minimal realization of electromagnetically interacting dark matter. The momentum dependence of the anapole interaction produces a harder recoil spectrum than standard momentum-independent interactions. We show that this makes elastically scattering anapole dark matter a viable interpretation of the single high-energy nuclear-recoil event recorded by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment, in a region where almost no background is expected. Curiously, the same coupling that fits the LZ data also yields the observed dark matter abundance at a mass of about 300 GeV, and is consistent with collider searches and indirect detection. The scenario is testable, predicting further events at lower energies, which the full LZ run will probe and a next-generation detector such as XLZD will decisively test.

</details>


### [134] [Probing the internal structure of the $ΩΩ$ dibaryon via its weak decays and lifetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16508)
*Qi-Fang Lü,Deng-Wang Tang,Yu-Bing Dong,Peng-Nian Shen,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the weak decay properties of the ΩΩ dibaryon using an effective Lagrangian approach, computing widths for three- and four-body final states across a range of binding energies (1–120 MeV); it finds distinct decay patterns and lifetime trends—e.g., dominant channels shift with binding energy, and lifetime increases from ~10⁻¹⁰ s to ~10⁻⁹ s—that serve as theoretical discriminators between compact hexaquark and molecular interpretations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the internal structure of the ΩΩ dibaryon and guide experimental searches by analyzing how its weak decay properties (branching ratios, lifetime) depend on binding energy—thereby distinguishing between compact hexaquark and weakly bound molecular models.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach; computation of partial decay widths for three-body (e.g., ΩΛK̄, ΩΞπ, ΞΞK̄) and four-body (e.g., ΛK̄ΛK̄) final states; inclusion of both single- and double-weak-vertex diagrams; systematic variation of binding energy from 1 MeV to 120 MeV.

Result: For weakly bound states (Eb = 1–20 MeV), three-body decays dominate, with channel hierarchy shifting from ΩΛK̄ → ΩΞπ; for deeply bound states (Eb = 100–120 MeV), ΩΛK̄ is kinematically forbidden and ΩΞπ/ΞΞK̄ dominate, while ΛK̄ΛK̄ is negligible; total lifetime increases from ~10⁻¹⁰ s to ~10⁻⁹ s with Eb.

Conclusion: The strong binding-energy dependence of decay patterns and lifetimes provides clear theoretical signatures to distinguish compact hexaquark versus molecular interpretations of the ΩΩ dibaryon, offering critical guidance for experiments at RHIC and LHC.

Abstract: The weak decay properties of the $\Omega\Omega$ dibaryon play a crucial role in probing its internal structure and guiding experimental efforts. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we systematically study its weak decays and lifetime by computing the widths for both three- and four-body final states, with amplitudes receiving contributions from single- and double-weak-vertex diagrams. We investigate the dependence of the decay properties on the binding energy ranging from a few MeV to over $100$ MeV. For weakly bound states ($E_b = 1$ and $20$ MeV), the three-body channels $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$, $\Omega \Xi \pi$, and $\Xi \Xi \bar{K}$ exhaust essentially the entire width, with the largest channel shifting from $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$ at $E_b=1$ MeV to $\Omega \Xi \pi$ at $E_b=20$ MeV. For deeply bound states ($E_b = 100$ and $120$ MeV), the $\Omega \Lambda \bar{K}$ channel is forbidden, and the dominant modes are $\Omega \Xi \pi$ and $\Xi \Xi \bar{K}$, while the double-weak-vertex channel $\Lambda \bar{K} \Lambda \bar{K}$ is found to be negligibly small. As the binding energy increases, the total lifetime extends from the order of $10^{-10}~\mathrm{s}$ to $10^{-9}~\mathrm{s}$. These distinct differences in decay patterns and lifetimes provide valuable theoretical reference values for experimental searches at RHIC and the LHC, and offer a clear discriminator between the compact hexaquark and weakly bound molecular interpretations.

</details>


### [135] [Solar Neutrinos at the ESSνSB experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16807)
*ESSnuSB Collaboration,J. Aguilar,D. Barčot,E. Baussan,A. K. Bhattacharyya,M. Blennow,M. Bogomilov,B. Bolling,E. Bouquerel,F. Bramati,A. Branca,G. Brunetti,I. Bustinduy,C. J. Carlile,J. Cederkall,T. W. Choi,S. Choubey,P. Christiansen,I. Christodoulou,E. Cristaldo Morales,P. Cupiał,D. D'Ago,H. Danared,J. P. A. M. de André,E. A. Díaz,M. Dracos,I. Efthymiopoulos,T. Ekelöf,M. Eshraqi,G. Fanourakis,A. Farricker,E. Fasoula,T. Fukuda,S. Gago,N. Gazis,Th. Geralis,M. Ghosh,A. Giarnetti,G. Gokbulut,C. Hagner,L. Halić,S. G. Hernández,J. Hiegel,M. Hooft,K. E. Iversen,N. Jachowicz,M. Jakkapu,M. Jensen,I. Karakoulias,E. Kasimi,A. Kayis Topaksu,B. Kliček,K. Kordas,B. Kovač,A. Leisos,A. Longhin,M. López,C. Maiano,S. Marangoni,J. G. Marcos,C. Marrelli,D. Meloni,M. Mezzetto,N. Milas,J. L. Muñoz,K. Niewczas,M. Oglakci,T. Ohlsson,M. Olvegård,A. Opanasenko,M. Pari,D. Patrzalek,G. Petkov,Ch. Petridou,P. Poussot,A. Psallidas,F. Pupilli,M. L. Reguera,D. Saiang,E. Salehi,D. Sampsonidis,A. Scanu,C. Schwab,F. Sordo,G. Stavropoulos,M. Stipčević,R. Tarkeshian,F. Terranova,T. Tolba,M. Topp-Mugglestone,E. Trachanas,R. Tsenov,A. Tsirigotis,S. E. Tzamarias,M. Vanderpoorten,G. Vankova-Kirilova,N. Vassilopoulos,S. Vihonen,J. Wurtz,V. Zeter,O. Zormpa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper assesses the ESSnuSB water-Cherenkov detector's sensitivity to two solar neutrino signatures—MSW spectral upturn in ^8B ν_e survival probability and direct detection of hep neutrinos—under realistic background and systematic uncertainties; it finds that 99% spallation rejection efficiency is required for 5σ discovery of either signal over 20 years.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To evaluate whether the ESSnuSB large underground water-Cherenkov detector can achieve first-time observations of two key solar neutrino features—the MSW-induced spectral upturn in ^8B ν_e survival probability and the rare hep neutrino flux—despite dominant backgrounds from cosmic-ray muon spallation and ^222Rn.

Method: A shape-driven statistical analysis with floating normalization is employed, incorporating detector systematics (energy resolution, flux normalization), modeled environmental backgrounds (muon spallation, ^222Rn), and realistic spallation rejection efficiencies (90% baseline, up to 99%); sensitivity is quantified via significance calculations for spectral distortions.

Result: At 90% spallation rejection (realistic per Super-K/JUNO), neither signal reaches 5σ significance in 20 years; achieving 5σ requires ~99% spallation rejection and low recoil energy threshold; detailed hep sensitivity curves quantify interplay between veto efficiency and threshold.

Conclusion: ESSnuSB has strong potential for solar neutrino physics, but realizing 5σ discovery of the MSW upturn or hep flux demands unprecedented spallation background suppression (~99%), highlighting critical R&D needs for muon veto and low-threshold reconstruction.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of the ESSnuSB large underground water-Cherenkov (WC) detector to two key solar neutrino signatures: the MSW spectral upturn in the $^8$B electron neutrino survival probability $P_{ee}(E_\nu)$, and the first direct observation of the rare hep neutrino flux. The analysis incorporates detector systematic uncertainties (energy resolution and flux normalization) and models environmental backgrounds from cosmic-ray muon spallation and $^{222}$Rn contamination. We adopt a shape-driven analysis strategy with a floating overall normalization, so that the discovery power relies on the spectral distortion rather than on the absolute event rate. In a realistic scenario with 90% spallation rejection efficiency, informed by the Super-Kamiokande experience and recent JUNO analyses, residual spallation backgrounds prevent either signal from reaching $5\sigma$ significance within 20 years of exposure. For the hep flux, we present a detailed sensitivity study to both its discovery and its precision, and quantify how the required spallation veto efficiency and recoil energy threshold jointly determine the achievable significance for both signals. At the adopted thresholds, discovery-level sensitivity for both signals requires approximately 99% spallation rejection over a 20-year exposure.

</details>


### [136] [Exploring the Pion Vector Form Factor Beyond the Physical Sheet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16974)
*Florian Herren,Fatemeh Nouri,Méril Reboud*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses analyticity and unitarity to construct a model-independent parametrization of the pion vector form factor on the first two Riemann sheets below the πω threshold, fits it to τ decay and spacelike data, and extracts ρ± pole parameters, pion charge radius, and low-energy ππ scattering parameters without external input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the ρ± resonance pole position and couplings, pion charge radius, and low-energy ππ scattering parameters in a model-independent way—free from assumptions or external scattering input—by exploiting fundamental principles (analyticity and unitarity) and high-precision experimental data.

Method: Constructs a rigorous, model-independent parametrization of the pion vector form factor on the first and second Riemann sheets using dispersion relations constrained by analyticity and unitarity below the inelastic πω threshold; fits simultaneously to τ⁻→π⁻π⁰ν_τ data (ALEPH, Belle, CLEO, OPAL) and spacelike data (NA7, JLab); extracts resonance poles via analytic continuation and determines low-energy observables.

Result: Extracts ρ± pole mass M_ρ± = 762.07 ± 0.63 MeV and width Γ_ρ± = 143.62^{+0.79}_{-0.80} MeV; determines pion charge radius and ππ scattering parameters; results are consistent with literature and independent of external scattering input; parametrization is made publicly available in EOS.

Conclusion: The approach demonstrates that high-precision resonance parameters and low-energy hadronic observables can be extracted robustly and model-independently from dispersive analysis of the pion vector form factor, validating fundamental constraints and enabling reproducible, open-data analyses.

Abstract: Using analyticity and unitarity, we parametrise the pion vector form factor on the first and second Riemann sheets, below the inelastic $\pi\omega$ threshold. We fit this model-independent parametrisation to the available $\tau^-\to\pi^-\pi^0\nu_\tau$ data from ALEPH, Belle, CLEO and OPAL, together with spacelike data from NA7 and JLab, and extract the pole position and couplings of the $\rho^\pm$ along with the pion charge radius and low-energy $\pi\pi$ scattering parameters. Our results are independent of external scattering input and agree with the literature; in particular, we find $M_{\rho^\pm} = 762.07\pm 0.63$ MeV and $\Gamma_{\rho^\pm} = 143.62^{+0.79}_{-0.80}$ MeV. The parametrisation is publicly available in the EOS software.

</details>


### [137] [Two-loop mixed QCD-EW corrections to bottom-pair production in electron-positron collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17171)
*Simone Devoto,Chinmoy Dey,Michele Dradi,Justin T. Fagnoni,Ziyang Huang,Xinchen Li,Michael J. Ramsey-Musolf,Alessandro Vicini,Jiang Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the two-loop mixed strong-electroweak virtual corrections to e⁺e⁻ → b̄b, using semi-analytical methods for Feynman integrals with up to five massive propagators and three distinct masses; results contribute to the NNLO QCD-EW cross section and support precision studies at HL-LHC and future lepton colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for bottom-quark pair production in e⁺e⁻ collisions—critical for electroweak precision tests and Higgs physics—at future lepton colliders and the HL-LHC, where mixed QCD-electroweak (QCD-EW) corrections at NNLO become indispensable.

Method: Semi-analytical evaluation of two-loop Feynman integrals—including topologies with up to five internal massive lines and three different mass scales (m_b, m_Z, m_W)—using complex-mass schemes for electroweak gauge bosons; full UV renormalization and IR subtraction performed within dimensional regularization.

Result: First complete computation of the two-loop mixed QCD-EW virtual corrections to e⁺e⁻ → b̄b; yields finite, gauge-invariant, infrared-safe contributions to the NNLO QCD-EW inclusive cross section.

Conclusion: The calculation establishes a robust framework for higher-order mixed corrections in massive-quark production, enabling systematic inclusion of second-order electroweak effects in precision SM predictions for lepton colliders and HL-LHC benchmark processes.

Abstract: We present the two-loop mixed strong-electroweak virtual corrections to the production of a massive bottom-antibottom quark pair in electron-positron collisions. The evaluation of all the relevant Feynman integrals, including those with up to five internal massive lines with three different mass values, has been worked out relying on semi-analytical techniques, using complex-valued masses for the electroweak gauge bosons. The ultraviolet-renormalized and infrared-subtracted finite correction contributes to the NNLO QCD-EW cross section of the inclusive production of a bottom-quark pair. The methodology adopted in this calculation can be extended to the study of fully electroweak second-order corrections to benchmark precision calculations relevant to both the high-luminosity phase of the Large Hadron Collider and prospective future lepton colliders.

</details>


### [138] [A Dark-Sector Mass Flip-Flop Mechanism for the ANITA-IV Events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17328)
*Dibya S. Chattopadhyay,Carlos A. Argüelles,Vedran Brdar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a dark-sector 'mass flip-flop' mechanism involving sterile neutrinos N₁/N₂ and an ultralight scalar to explain ANITA-IV's upward air showers while evading IceCube non-detection, via matter-induced mass inversion and kinematically selective decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reconcile ANITA-IV's detection of four upward-going ultra-high-energy air showers (suggestive of tau neutrinos) with IceCube's null result at comparable energies—despite IceCube's far greater exposure.

Method: Introduces a two-state dark sector (N₁, N₂) with vacuum mass splitting and coupling to an ultralight scalar; Earth matter induces effective mass inversion via scalar-mediated interactions, enabling N₁ → N₂ decay underground, followed by N₂ → N₁Z′ decay in the atmosphere; kinematic blocking of both decays at IceCube depths suppresses signal there.

Result: Quantitatively explains ANITA-IV’s four events; predicts three detectable events in PUEO’s first flight; ensures IceCube remains blind to this channel due to kinematic suppression in dense ice.

Conclusion: The mass flip-flop mechanism provides a viable, testable explanation for the ANITA–IceCube anomaly within a minimal dark-sector framework, with falsifiable predictions for upcoming balloon-borne experiments.

Abstract: ANITA-IV has observed four upward-going air-shower candidates just below the horizon, consistent with ultra-high-energy $\nu_\tau$, yet IceCube, despite its much larger exposure, has detected no neutrino events at such energies. We propose a dark-sector mass flip-flop mechanism that reconciles these observations. We consider an all-sky, monoenergetic flux of ultra-high-energy $N_1$, that is lighter than its partner $N_2$ and stable in vacuum. In our scenario, an ultralight scalar mediating interactions with Earth matter inverts the mass ordering of $N_1$ and $N_2$, allowing an incident $N_1$ to decay into $N_2$ deep underground. The resulting $N_2$ propagates unimpeded through matter and exits the Earth, where the decay $N_2 \to N_1 Z'$ becomes kinematically allowed in the atmosphere, initiating an extensive air shower. At IceCube depths, neither $N_1\to N_2 Z'$ nor $N_2\to N_1 Z'$ is kinematically allowed, which explains the absence of analogous events at IceCube. Normalizing the $N_1$ flux to the four ANITA-IV events, we also predict three events during PUEO's first flight.

</details>


### [139] [Strongly supercooled first-order phase transitions: monopole abundance, entropy production and homogeneity consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14593)
*Chen Zhang,Yue Niu,Xin Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines how strong supercooling in a first-order phase transition of a unified gauge theory can suppress monopole production in the early universe, using a Gaussian nucleation model and radiation-like energy transfer. It introduces three equivalent criteria for phase-transition completion (CMC, PVC, ATC) sharing the same boundary; near this boundary, monopole abundance can be arbitrarily suppressed, but FLRW homogeneity breaks down, diagnosed via Hubble-scale FVF fluctuations—limiting physically justifiable suppression. Implications extend to gravitational wave signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the monopole problem in grand unified theories by exploring whether strong supercooling—while still allowing successful completion of a first-order phase transition—can sufficiently suppress topological defect (monopole) formation without violating cosmological consistency.

Method: Adopts a Gaussian bubble nucleation model within an idealized FLRW cosmology; defines and compares three mathematically distinct but physically equivalent criteria for phase-transition completion (CMC, PVC, ATC); analyzes monopole abundance scaling near the completion boundary; introduces Hubble-scale root-mean-square fluctuation of false-vacuum fraction (FVF) as a diagnostic for FLRW homogeneity breakdown.

Result: The three completion criteria coincide exactly on a common boundary; monopole abundance diverges inversely with distance to this boundary, enabling arbitrary suppression *in principle*; however, as the boundary is approached, FVF fluctuations on Hubble scales grow, invalidating the homogeneous FLRW assumption—thus imposing a physical upper bound on suppression relative to the Kibble estimate.

Conclusion: While strong supercooling offers a powerful mechanism for monopole suppression, its efficacy is fundamentally limited by cosmological homogeneity constraints; consequently, arbitrarily large suppression is unphysical, and realistic predictions for monopole density—and associated gravitational wave spectra—must respect this self-consistency condition.

Abstract: We investigate whether monopole production in the early universe from a first-order phase transition in a unified gauge theory can be significantly suppressed if the phase transition is subject to strong supercooling but still completes successfully. This is facilitated by adopting a Gaussian nucleation model and assuming the false vacuum energy released is transferred to sectors which behave effectively as radiation in terms of cosmological evolution. We distinguish three criteria of phase-transition completion: the comoving measure completion (CMC) characterized by the eventual vanishing of the false-vacuum fraction (FVF), the physical volume completion (PVC) characterized by the eventual vanishing of the physical volume of false vacuum, and the asymptotic thermodynamic completion (ATC), which refers to the finiteness of total entropy production. Assuming an idealized Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) cosmology, it is shown that the three criteria share exactly the same completion boundary, away from which they are completely equivalent. It is further shown that monopole abundance can be arbitrarily suppressed for parameter points approaching the completion boundary from the successful side. Nevertheless, for parameter points too close to the completion boundary, justification of the self-consistency of a homogeneous FLRW cosmology is difficult.
