We propose the use of the Hubble-scale FVF root-mean-square fluctuation to diagnose the homogeneity consistency issue, and find that arbitrarily strong suppression of monopole abundance relative to a reasonable Kibble estimate is not justifiable in terms of homogeneity consistency. These findings also have implications for gravitational wave production in strongly supercooled phase transitions.

</details>


### [140] [$N_{\mathrm{eff}}$, Radiative Dirac Neutrinos, and Flavor Physics from Leptoquarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14616)
*Patricio Escalona,Guillermo Gambini,Vítor Pereira,C. A. de S. Pires,Farinaldo S. Queiroz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a two-scalar-leptoquark + three-RHN model that simultaneously explains B→Kνν excess (Belle II), generates radiative Dirac neutrino masses, satisfies μ→eγ and ΔN_eff constraints, and shows cosmological N_eff bounds can exceed collider limits on leptoquark mass (~12–14 TeV).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify explanations of flavor anomalies (especially B⁺→K⁺νν̄), rare meson decays, and Dirac neutrino mass generation within a consistent leptoquark framework.

Method: Constructs a scalar leptoquark model with two mixed leptoquarks (S₁ and R₂) and three right-handed neutrinos; computes one-loop radiative Dirac neutrino masses, analyzes contributions to B⁺→K⁺νν̄, μ→eγ, and ΔN_eff; confronts results with Belle II data, MEG bound, and Planck/BBN cosmological constraints.

Result: The model accommodates the Belle II B⁺→K⁺νν̄ central value while satisfying μ→eγ and ΔN_eff < 0.3; leptoquark masses below ~12–14 TeV are required for sufficient B-meson decay enhancement; cosmological N_eff bounds can be more stringent than current LHC direct search limits.

Conclusion: A minimal two-leptoquark + three-RHN framework successfully reconciles the B→Kνν anomaly with Dirac neutrino mass generation and key low-energy/cosmological constraints; future precision in N_eff and B→Kνν will critically test this scenario.

Abstract: Leptoquarks provide a common framework for addressing flavor anomalies, rare meson decays, and neutrino-mass generation. We study a model with two mixed scalar leptoquarks and three right-handed neutrinos that generates radiative Dirac neutrino masses and contributes to $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$, charged-lepton flavor violation, and the effective number of relativistic species. We show that the model can accommodate the Belle~II measurement of $B^+\to K^+\nu\bar\nu$ while satisfying the $\mu\to e\gamma$ bound and the cosmological constraint on $\Delta N_{\mathrm{eff}}$. When both leptoquark masses exceed approximately $12$--$14\,\mathrm{TeV}$, the new contribution to the $B$-meson decay becomes too small, and the prediction approaches the SM value, which lies below the Belle~II measurement. Interestingly, the lower bounds on the leptoquark mass scale derived from $N_{\mathrm{eff}}$ can be stronger than those from direct collider searches.

</details>


### [141] [Lepton flavor violation at mass dimension eight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14669)
*Julian Heeck,Mikheil Sokhashvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies UV completions where lepton flavor violation arises dominantly at dimension-8 (not dimension-6), due to accidental suppression of lower-dimensional operators—expanding the phenomenologically viable space of LFV operators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether dimension-8 lepton-flavor-violating (LFV) operators—traditionally considered subleading—can dominate over dimension-6 ones in certain UV completions, thereby broadening the scope of experimentally testable LFV signals.

Method: Systematic derivation of all tree-level UV completions for LFV operators up to mass dimension 8 using heavy scalars and fermions; identification of models where d=6 contributions are loop-suppressed or vanish due to coupling structure.

Result: Several UV completions exclusively generate LFV at d=8 at tree level, with d=6 contributions suppressed by loop factors or absent due to coupling misalignment—establishing 'accidentally protected' d=8 scenarios.

Conclusion: Dimension-8 LFV operators can be leading rather than subleading in specific UV frameworks, significantly enlarging the set of testable LFV phenomena beyond conventional d=6 expectations.

Abstract: Operators that violate lepton flavor first arise at mass dimension $d=5$ in the Standard Model Effective Field Theory, while charged lepton flavor violation first appears at $d=6$, inducing the typical exotic particle decays currently probed by experiments. Operators at $d=8$ carry the same quantum numbers as those at $d=6$ and are thus seemingly doomed to be subleading corrections. After (re)deriving the complete set of tree-level UV completions for lepton-flavor-violating $d\leq 8$ operators via heavy scalars and fermions, we show that some UV completions exclusively generate $d=8$ flavor violation at tree level, with $d=6$ contributions suppressed by loop factors and, in some cases, unrelated couplings. Such accidentally protected $d=8$ scenarios enlarge the set of testable lepton-flavor-violating operators.

</details>


### [142] [Symmetry adapted tensor networks and quantum classical geometric learning for hadronic tomography: a proton construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14711)
*Dustin Keller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a calibrated, symmetry-resolved tensor-network model of the proton’s GTMD $F_{1,1}$ (valence-$u$, zero skewness), integrating experimental, lattice, and phenomenological inputs via a unified light-front state; it reveals strong constraints on forward transverse response but significant freedom in the nonforward $(x, \Delta_\perp)$-dependence—uncaptured by current data—and demonstrates scalability to neutrons, deuterons, and higher-spin nuclei using shared quantum-classical tensor subunits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build a first-principles, experimentally and lattice-calibrated model of the proton generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distribution $F_{1,1}$ that respects QCD symmetries (color singlet, exchange statistics, $J_z$, $I_z$) while accommodating heterogeneous input sources with their native operator definitions and scales.

Method: Constructs a light-front wavefunction encoded as a symmetry-resolved tree tensor network, incorporating soft-subtracted GTMD definition, one-loop nonsinglet matching, NLO collinear evolution, and nonperturbative joint closure; integrates ART25 transverse projections, HERA inclusive DIS, lattice matrix elements, elastic form factors, and static properties; employs classical and quantum geometric nodes for transverse response; verifies quantum circuits as feature maps and quantifies their capacity/cost.

Result: Forward inputs constrain transverse response by ~81% at $x=0.4$, yet the full $(x, \Delta_\perp)$-dependence remains underdetermined—e.g., a symmetry-allowed deformation shifts $F_{1,1}(x=0.4, \\Delta_\\perp \\sim 0.5\\,\\text{GeV})$ from 0.588 to 0.378 GeV$^{-2}$ without violating any current constraint; dense momentum-space surfaces and phase-space maps characterize this freedom; tensor architecture is shown extensible to neutron, deuteron ($J=1$), and spin-$3/2$ nuclei.

Conclusion: A symmetry- and scale-aware tensor-network representation enables rigorous, multi-source calibration of GTMDs and exposes critical gaps in nonforward kinematic coverage; the framework provides a scalable, compositional foundation for nucleon and nuclear GTMD modeling, where increasing symmetry constraints make structural (tensor-based) encoding practically essential.

Abstract: We construct a calibrated model of the proton generalized transverse-momentum-dependent distribution (GTMD) $F_{1,1}$ in the real, staple-even valence-$u$ sector at zero skewness. Experimental measurements, lattice matrix elements, and phenomenological distributions enter through a single shared light-front state, organized as a symmetry-resolved tree tensor network in which color-singlet structure, exchange statistics, and the additive $J_z$ and $I_z$ charges are built in. Classical and quantum geometric nodes represent the residual transverse response. The GTMD is defined with soft subtraction, one-loop nonsinglet matching, NLO collinear evolution, and an explicit nonperturbative joint closure, so that each input is used with its own operator convention and scale. Calibrating against correlated ART25 transverse projections, HERA inclusive data, and lattice, elastic, and static information gives a simple picture. The forward inputs narrow the attainable transverse response by about $81\%$ at $x=0.4$. The simultaneous dependence on quark momentum and momentum transfer, however, is untouched by any of them: a deformation that leaves every present input invariant moves the nonforward value from $0.588$ to $0.378\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-2}$. Dense momentum-space surfaces and finite-transfer-window phase-space maps show the structure this freedom controls. The tensor-network organization is chosen with composition in mind. The same shared subunits, operator hierarchy, and qualification protocol extend to the neutron, the spin-1 deuteron, and spin-$3/2$ nuclei, where the number of symmetry and exchange constraints grows quickly and a structural representation becomes the practical route. The quantum circuits are verified feature maps and state encodings; their capacity and cost are measured alongside the classical blocks.

</details>


### [143] [Three-gluon vertex planar degeneracy: a perturbative explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14896)
*Daniel O. R. Azevedo,Nahuel Barrios,Marcela Peláez,Nicolás Wschebor*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explains the planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex in Yang-Mills theory via perturbative analysis using the Curci-Ferrari model in Landau gauge, showing that tree-level dominance and flat one-loop corrections account for the degeneracy, with symmetric approximation errors ≤3%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a perturbative explanation for the observed planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex — previously seen in lattice and Dyson-Schwinger studies — within a controlled, infrared-safe framework.

Method: Uses the Curci-Ferrari model (a mass-extended Faddeev-Popov Lagrangian) in Landau gauge with an infrared-safe renormalization scheme; performs a full one-loop calculation of the three-gluon vertex for arbitrary kinematics in d = 3 and d = 4.

Result: i) Tree-level form factor dominates due to suppression of perturbative corrections; ii) One-loop corrections are remarkably flat across phase space; iii) Symmetric approximation error peaks at ~3% in both dimensions, largest near soft-gluon limits due to tamed UV-collinear divergences.

Conclusion: Planar degeneracy is a natural perturbative phenomenon, not solely non-perturbative; the CF model successfully captures key infrared QCD dynamics analytically, bridging perturbative calculations with non-perturbative evidence.

Abstract: We provide a perturbative explanation for the planar degeneracy of the three-gluon vertex in Yang-Mills theory, using the Curci-Ferrari (CF) model in the Landau gauge. By employing an infrared safe renormalization scheme whose coupling remains regular at all scales, we perform a one-loop calculation for arbitrary kinematics in both three and four dimensions. Our results demonstrate that the observed planar degeneracy can be understood naturally due to perturbative reasons: i) the tree-level form factor dominates the vertex structure simply because perturbative corrections are suppressed; ii) the one loop corrections to this form factor remain remarkably flat across the allowed phase space. We quantify the deviations from the symmetric configuration, finding that the mean error of the symmetric approximation, in the case of the tree-level form factor, peaks at approximately 3% both in three and four dimensions, with the most significant departures occurring near soft-gluon configurations due to tamed ultraviolet collinear divergences. This work explains previous lattice and Dyson-Schwinger studies while reinforcing the capacity of the mass-extended Faddeev-Popov Lagrangian to capture complex infrared QCD dynamics through perturbative analytical techniques.

</details>


### [144] [Suppressed Direct Detection of Dark Matter from a Non-Invertible Selection Rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15030)
*Riasat Sheikh,Koji Tsumura*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a non-invertible selection rule to explain suppression of dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section, yielding a suppressed yet predictable signal for direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct detection experiments have found no viable signal, suggesting strong suppression of the dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section; the paper seeks a theoretical origin for this suppression.

Method: Introduces a non-invertible selection rule as a symmetry-like constraint governing dark matter–nucleon interactions, and performs numerical analysis to quantify its effect on the scattering cross-section.

Result: Demonstrates that the proposed mechanism yields a suppressed but quantitatively predictable dark matter–nucleon scattering cross-section.

Conclusion: A non-invertible selection rule provides a viable theoretical explanation for the observed suppression in direct detection experiments, offering a new framework beyond conventional symmetries.

Abstract: Although the existence of dark matter is well established, its particle nature is still unknown. Direct detection experiments have been struggling to find any viable signal, implying that the dark matter--nucleon scattering cross-section must be suppressed. Here we propose a mechanism to explain the origin of this suppression by considering a non-invertible selection rule. Our numerical analysis shows that a suppressed yet predictable dark matter--nucleon scattering cross section is possible.

</details>


### [145] [Novel method for measuring the isovector partner of X(3872)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15158)
*Qiu-Lin Tan,Mao-Jun Yan,Chun-Sheng An,Cheng-Rong Deng,Xiao-Hai Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the triangle singularity (TS) mechanism in B⁰ → πK D̄⁰D*⁰ decays to enhance detection sensitivity for the predicted isovector partner Wc1 of X(3872), leveraging coupled-channel D D̄* dynamics and TS-induced structures in invariant mass distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The isovector partner Wc1 of X(3872) is predicted but expected to produce only a weak signal near the charged D D̄* threshold, making experimental identification difficult; thus, a mechanism to amplify its signature is needed.

Method: The authors employ the triangle singularity mechanism in B⁰ → πK D̄⁰D*⁰ decays, specifically modeling the Ds1(2536) D̄* loop with realistic coupled-channel D D̄* scattering amplitudes derived from the Lippmann-Schwinger equation.

Result: The interplay between triangle singularity and coupled-channel dynamics generates distinctive kinematic structures in K D̄⁰D*⁰ invariant mass distributions; notably, the charged-channel component shows a pronounced feature sensitive to the Wc1 pole location.

Conclusion: The triangle singularity mechanism significantly enhances sensitivity to the elusive Wc1 state, providing a viable and complementary strategy for its experimental search at Belle II and LHCb.

Abstract: The predicted isovector partner of the $X(3872)$, denoted as $W_{c1}$, is expected to produce only a relatively weak structure near the charged $D \bar{D}^*$ threshold, making its experimental identification challenging. In this work, we explore the triangle singularity (TS) mechanism as a means of enhancing the sensitivity to the $W_{c1}$ in the decays $B^0 \to \pi K \bar{D}^0 D^{*0}$. We investigate the $D_{s1}(2536) \bar{D}^*$ triangle rescattering mechanism, incorporating the coupled-channel $D \bar{D}^*$ scattering amplitudes obtained from the Lippmann-Schwinger equation. The interplay between the TS and the coupled-channel dynamics produces characteristic structures in the $K \bar{D}^0 D^{*0}$ invariant mass distributions associated with the $X(3872)$ and its isovector partner $W_{c1}$. In particular, the charged-channel contribution exhibits a distinct structure sensitive to the nearby $W_{c1}$ pole. Our results demonstrate that the TS mechanism provides a promising way to probe the otherwise weak manifestation of the $W_{c1}$, offering a complementary experimental strategy for searching for this predicted state at Belle II and LHCb.

</details>


### [146] [Enhancing Graviton--Photon Conversion through Galactic Magnetic Turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15218)
*Hongliang Tian,Lei Wu,Yang Zhang,Bin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that Galactic magnetic turbulence can enable coherent graviton-photon conversion by compensating plasma-induced phase mismatch, leading to a linearly distance-dependent conversion probability governed by the magnetic power spectrum; this yields significantly improved (up to 100×) strain limits from photon background observations in the inertial range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome plasma dispersion suppression of coherent graviton-photon conversion over astrophysical distances, which has limited previous bounds on gravitational-wave strain.

Method: Analytical modeling of graviton-photon conversion in magnetized plasma, incorporating turbulent magnetic field statistics and phase-matching via magnetic mode compensation; ensemble-averaging over turbulent realizations in the long-path limit.

Result: Conversion probability grows linearly with distance under phase matching; resulting strain limits from cosmic photon backgrounds exceed prior Galactic conversion bounds by up to two orders of magnitude within the inertial-range phase-matching window.

Conclusion: Galactic magnetic turbulence enables observable coherent graviton-photon conversion, transforming photon background data into a competitive probe of ultralight gravitational waves in a previously inaccessible parameter space.

Abstract: Plasma dispersion suppresses coherent graviton-photon conversion over astrophysical distances. We show that Galactic magnetic turbulence enables phase matching by compensating the plasma-induced mismatch, allowing matched magnetic modes to contribute coherently. In the long path limit, the ensemble-averaged conversion probability grows linearly with propagation distance in the phase matched regime and is governed by the projected magnetic power spectrum. Applied to photon background observations, this mechanism yields characteristic strain limits that are stronger than previous Galactic conversion bounds in the inertial range phase matching window, with improvements of up to two orders of magnitude.

</details>


### [147] [Tau polarization, new physics and CP violation in $ν_τ$-nucleus hyperon production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15252)
*E. Hernández,J. Nieves,J. E. Sobczyk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies tau neutrino charged-current interactions on nuclei to probe new physics, especially CP violation, via tau lepton polarization encoded in pion angular distributions from tau decays; it models nuclear effects using the impulse approximation and a general spectral function, finds that a CP-odd azimuthal pion asymmetry survives nuclear effects and remains sensitive to NP, and estimates percent-level or smaller asymmetries for representative new-physics scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Charged-current tau neutrino interactions are the least constrained SM flavor sector; tau polarization provides sensitivity to new physics, including CP-violating couplings. Hyperon and charmed-baryon production accesses u→s and d→c transitions, but measurements occur on nuclei, necessitating proper treatment of nuclear effects.

Method: The authors analyze sequential reactions ν_τ A Z → τ⁻(→π⁻ν_τ) Λ_c⁺ X and ν̄_τ A Z → τ⁺(→π⁺ν̄_τ) Λ X within the impulse approximation. They derive pion energy and angular distributions—including an azimuthal asymmetry relative to the neutrino–baryon plane—for arbitrary nuclear hole spectral functions, and compute results using the local Fermi gas model for ¹⁶O.

Result: Nuclear effects lower reaction thresholds but preserve pion distributions and tau polarization information close to the free-nucleon case. A CP-odd azimuthal pion asymmetry persists and vanishes in the SM, making it a robust probe of CP violation independent of hadronic form factors. Predicted asymmetries for representative NP scenarios are at the percent level or below, with stronger form-factor dependence in the Λ channel.

Conclusion: Tau neutrino–nucleus scattering with subsequent tau decay offers a viable, nuclear-effect-resilient channel to search for CP-violating new physics via the azimuthal pion asymmetry—a clean, form-factor-independent observable—especially with future high-statistics tau neutrino experiments.

Abstract: Charged-current interactions of the $\tau$ neutrino are the least constrained sector of the Standard Model (SM) flavour structure, and the polarisation of the produced $\tau$ lepton probes new-physics (NP) couplings, including CP-violating ones. Hyperon and charmed-baryon production gives access to the $u\to s$ and $d\to c$ transitions, but any measurement would be made on nuclei, which calls for a proper treatment of nuclear effects. We therefore study the sequential reactions $\nu_\tau\,{}^A Z\to\tau^-(\to\pi^-\nu_\tau)\,\Lambda_c^+ X$ and $\bar\nu_\tau\,{}^A Z\to\tau^+ (\to\pi^+\bar\nu_\tau)\,\Lambda X$ within the impulse approximation. The energy and angular distributions of the pion, which encode the $\tau$ polarization, are derived for an arbitrary nuclear hole spectral function. As for free nucleons, an azimuthal pion asymmetry with respect to the plane spanned by the neutrino and the outgoing baryon is shown to remain. This is a CP-odd observable that vanishes in the SM, so that a nonzero value would signal CP violation irrespective of the hadronic form factors. Local Fermi gas results for ${}^{16}$O show that nuclear effects lower the reaction thresholds but leave the pion distributions, and hence the polarization information, close to their free-nucleon form. For representative NP scenarios the predicted asymmetries are at the percent level or below, with a magnitude that, in the $\Lambda$ channel, depends strongly on the transition form factors.

</details>


### [148] [CP Violation in Beauty Baryon Decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15273)
*Jian-Peng Wang,Qin Qin,Stephane Monteil,Fu-Sheng Yu,Yan-Xi Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review summarizes the 2025 discovery of CP violation in the baryon sector—a landmark achievement in flavor physics—and synthesizes theoretical frameworks (e.g., perturbative QCD, re-scattering) and experimental techniques (rate asymmetry measurements), while proposing multi-dimensional observables to guide future precision tests and new physics searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consolidate the theoretical and experimental progress culminating in the first observation of CP violation in baryons, and to chart a path forward for enhanced sensitivity to physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Comprehensive literature synthesis integrating dynamical theoretical approaches—including perturbative QCD and re-scattering models—with experimental methodologies centered on rate asymmetry analyses in baryon decays.

Result: A unified narrative of the historical and technical trajectory leading to the 2025 baryonic CP violation discovery, plus a conceptual framework for constructing multi-dimensional observables to improve precision and new physics reach.

Conclusion: The baryonic CP violation discovery validates long-standing theoretical expectations and opens a new frontier; multi-dimensional observables are identified as critical tools for next-generation flavor experiments.

Abstract: The 2025 discovery of CP violation in the baryon sector marks a tectonic shift in flavor physics, bridging a sixty-year gap since the initial discovery of CP asymmetry in mesons. This review provides a comprehensive synthesis of the theoretical and experimental journey to this milestone. We integrate the development of dynamical calculation frameworks, such as perturbative QCD and the re-scattering mechanism, with the experimental breakthrough achieved through rate asymmetry measurements. Furthermore, we establish a roadmap for the future of the field by detailing the construction of multi-dimensional observables, which are instrumental in advancing the next era of precision physics and refining the search for new physics beyond the standard model.

</details>


### [149] [On the reduction of negative-weight events in MC@NLO with Machine Learning techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15541)
*L. Beccatini,V. Fossa,S. Frixione,A. Valassi,M. Zaro*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores using Machine Learning (Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows) to reduce negative weights in ${\mathbb S}$-event MC@NLO short-distance cross sections, achieving reductions in most cases with modest computational overhead; it serves as a realistic proof-of-concept, identifying failure modes where reduction is insignificant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To mitigate the numerical inefficiency caused by large fractions of negative event weights in MC@NLO simulations, which degrade statistical precision and increase computational cost in high-energy physics event generation.

Method: Applies Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows to reweight or resample phase-space points in ${\mathbb S}$-event MC@NLO short-distance cross sections; evaluates across diverse processes in $e^+e^-$ and $pp$ collisions within a fully realistic, assumption-free simulation framework.

Result: Significant reduction of negative weight fractions in most tested processes; identifies root causes (e.g., kinematic/dynamical complexity) where reduction fails; modest computational overhead observed; no public-code implementation yet.

Conclusion: ML-based redefinition of short-distance cross sections is a viable, realistic strategy to suppress negative weights in MC@NLO, warranting future integration into production tools and further methodological refinement.

Abstract: We demonstrate that Machine Learning techniques can be successfully employed to re-define ${\mathbb S}$-event MC@NLO short-distance cross sections in a manner which in most cases leads to a reduction of their fractions of negative weights, with a modest increase of computational costs. We work with both Decision Trees and Normalizing Flows, and consider several processes, in both $e^+e^-$ and $pp$ collisions, that vastly differ in their kinematical and dynamical complexity. In those situations where the ML-induced reduction of negative weights is not significant we are able to identify the primary reason(s) for this behaviour, thus paving the way for it to be addressed in future studies. This work is still to be considered as a proof of concept, given that the proposed methodology is not implemented in a public code; yet, it is based upon a fully realistic simulation environment, in which no simplifying assumptions are made.

</details>


### [150] [A Non-Relativistic Model of Heavy Quarkonium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15628)
*Christian Farina*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A non-relativistic, spin-dependent phenomenological model for heavy quarkonium is developed using a hydrogen-like Schrödinger equation with spin interactions; solved via matrix mechanics, variational optimization, and first-order spin–orbit perturbation, yielding good agreement with experimental spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an accessible, pedagogically suitable quantum-mechanical framework for modeling heavy quarkonium spectra while incorporating essential spin-dependent effects.

Method: Constructs a spin-dependent Hamiltonian including hyperfine spin–spin and spin–orbit terms; solves the corresponding Schrödinger equation via matrix mechanics (Hamiltonian diagonalization), energy optimization via variational methods, and first-order time-independent perturbation theory for spin–orbit coupling.

Result: The computed energy spectrum shows good agreement with experimental data for conventional heavy quarkonium states (e.g., charmonium and bottomonium).

Conclusion: The combined use of matrix mechanics, variational optimization, and perturbation theory yields an accurate and instructive non-relativistic model for heavy quarkonium, appropriate for advanced undergraduate or graduate quantum mechanics instruction.

Abstract: We develop a non-relativistic, spin-dependent, phenomenological model of heavy quarkonium. The model is based on a hydrogen-like Schrödinger equation derived from a spin-dependent Hamiltonian that includes a hyperfine spin--spin interaction term. The resulting system is solved using matrix mechanics by diagonalizing the Hamiltonian to obtain the energy spectrum. The energies are further optimized using variational methods. Spin--orbit corrections are included at first order in perturbation theory. The resulting spectrum shows good agreement with experimental data for conventional heavy quarkonium states. The framework combines matrix mechanics, variational methods, and perturbation theory, and is therefore suitable for students familiar with quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [151] [Deeply inelastic scattering as an inverse problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15665)
*Hsiang-nan Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel analyticity-based formalism for inclusive QCD processes, using dispersion relations on a complex baryon mass plane to directly compute proton structure functions $F_2$ and $F_L$ as an inverse problem—bypassing conventional factorization—with only the proton mass as the nonperturbative input; successfully reproduces data at intermediate $x_B$ and wide $Q^2$, and extends to pion structure via pion mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome conceptual and practical limitations of conventional QCD factorization by leveraging analyticity and dispersion relations for a first-principles, nonperturbative treatment of inclusive scattering in the intermediate-$x_B$ regime where factorization is fragile or ill-defined.

Method: Constructs dispersion relations for double deeply virtual Compton scattering on the complex baryon mass plane; inputs perturbative heavy-baryon amplitudes; solves for $F_2$ and $F_L$ via inverse problem formulation; incorporates scaling behavior in $Q^2$ and quark counting rules near $x_B \to 1$ as prior constraints; treats PDF extraction as a secondary inverse problem using derived factorization formulas.

Result: Predictions for $F_2$ and $F_L$ agree with experimental data at $x_B = 0.18$–0.65 and $Q^2 = 10^2$–$10^4\,\text{GeV}^2$ using only the proton mass as nonperturbative parameter; PDFs extracted as byproduct; same formalism with pion mass reproduces $F_2^\pi$ data.

Conclusion: Analyticity-based dispersion formalism provides a viable, factorization-free alternative for computing inclusive structure functions in QCD, with predictive power grounded in a single physical mass scale and consistency with both proton and pion data.

Abstract: A novel formalism for high-energy inclusive QCD processes based on analyticity of a scattering amplitude is proposed, which is conceptually distinct from the conventional factorization theorem. We illustrate how to analyze proton deeply inelastic scattering by means of the dispersion relations for double deeply virtual Compton scattering defined on a complex baryon mass plane. Inputting the perturbative results for the heavy baryon scattering amplitudes, we solve for the proton structure functions $F_2$ and $F_L$ in the nonperturbative low-mass region directly from the dispersion relations, i.e., as an inverse problem. The prior information, including the scaling of the structure functions in photon virtuality $Q^2$ and the quark counting rules for the threshold region with the Bjorken variable $x_B\to 1$, is incorporated. The only nonperturbative parameter at leading-power precision in $1/Q$ is the proton mass, with which we demonstrate the agreement between our predictions and the data for $F_2$ and $F_L$ at intermediate $x_B=0.18$-0.65 in a wide range of $Q^2=10^2$-$10^4$ GeV$^2$. Parton distribution functions can be extracted from the obtained structure functions as a byproduct of the analysis by treating the associated factorization formulas as another inverse problem. The same formalism with the pion mass as the nonperturbative parameter explains the data for the pion structure function $F_2^\pi$.

</details>


### [152] [Accelerating exact solutions of relativistic hydrodynamics and Levy-shaped freeze-out sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15727)
*Máté Csanád,Ágoston Benyó,Márton Nagy,Bence Szentkuti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper addresses the 'Lévy hydro puzzle'—the coexistence of Lévy-stable (non-Gaussian) pion source distributions and hydrodynamic $R^2 \propto 1/m_T$ scaling in heavy-ion collisions—and shows that perfect-fluid hydrodynamics can accommodate Lévy-like sources only if power-law tails are either inherited from pressure (requiring $1/r$ proper acceleration) or introduced via comoving 'dressing' of temperature/density; it introduces two new relativistic self-similar flows ($\lambda$-PF and dressed Gubser) realizing distinct routes to Lévy-like behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the apparent contradiction between experimentally observed Lévy-stable (power-law tailed) pion source distributions and standard hydrodynamic expectations (Gaussian sources with $R^2 \propto 1/m_T$ scaling), i.e., the 'Lévy hydro puzzle'.

Method: Analytical relativistic hydrodynamics: derivation of constraints on source tail behavior from freeze-out hypersurface pressure bounds; construction and analysis of new self-similar potential flows ($\lambda$-PF) and dressed Gubser flow; classification of conditions (ultra-relativistic Hubble-type vs. slow isothermal with $1/r$ acceleration) under which Lévy-stable or Lévy-like source tails emerge.

Result: Demonstrates that Lévy-like source tails cannot be dynamically generated by perfect-fluid hydrodynamics alone; they require either (i) a power-law pressure tail inherited from $1/r$ proper acceleration, or (ii) explicit 'dressing' of thermodynamic fields; $\lambda$-PF with Hubble dressing yields true Lévy-stable tails in a parameter range, while isothermally dressed Gubser flow yields Lévy-like (but non-Lévy-stable) tails compatible with observed $m_T$ scaling.

Conclusion: The Lévy hydro puzzle is resolved: Lévy-like source shapes and hydrodynamic $m_T$ scaling are compatible within perfect-fluid hydrodynamics, but the power-law tail is not generated by hydrodynamics—it is either imposed via initial/boundary conditions (e.g., $1/r$ acceleration → power-law pressure) or introduced through comoving dressing of temperature/density.

Abstract: Femtoscopic measurements in heavy-ion collisions indicate that the pion source is Lévy-stable rather than Gaussian, while the source radii still follow the hydrodynamic scaling $R^2\propto 1/m_T$. We call this coexistence the Lévy hydro puzzle. For a perfect fluid with $\varepsilon=\kappa p$ and $p=nT$, flow and pressure form a closed system, and density and temperature can be ``dressed'' by an arbitrary comoving function, generalising a freedom known from Hubble-type solutions to arbitrary flows. We show that, at nonzero pair momentum, the source is bounded by the pressure on the freeze-out hypersurface, so its tail can never be heavier than the pressure tail. This yields a criterion for power-law source tails with two routes: either the outer flow is ultra-relativistic and the temperature grows at least linearly (Hubble-type solutions), or the flow is slow, the temperature uniform, and the proper acceleration falls off as $1/r$. We present a new family of relativistic, accelerating, self-similar potential flows ($\lambda$-PF) and a new dressing of Gubser's flow. The acceleration of $\lambda$-PF generates no power-law tail, but with a Hubble-type dressing its source tail is Lévy-stable in a range of parameters, generalising the Hubble-type Lévy source to accelerating cores. The isothermally dressed Gubser flow realises the $1/r$ route: its tail exponent lies outside the Lévy-stable range, but over the measured range it gives Lévy-like sources with $1/R^2$ linear in $m_T$. Thus Lévy-like sources and the hydrodynamic $m_T$ scaling are compatible in perfect-fluid hydrodynamics, but hydrodynamics does not create the power-law tail: it is either inherited from a power-law pressure, requiring a $1/r$ acceleration, or put in through the dressing. We outline extensions towards non-spherical geometries and elliptic flow.

</details>


### [153] [The Dark Side of the Periodic Table: Theory and Signatures of Nuclear Atomic Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15852)
*Pouya Asadi,Zackaria Chacko,David Curtin,Micah Mellors,Ethan T. Neil*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a Mirror Twin Higgs model where stable twin neutrons form the bulk of dark matter, and a subdominant fraction forms nuclear atomic dark matter (naDM); it solves the matter coincidence problem via a Z₂ symmetry, predicts two-stage dark acoustic oscillations, and motivates directional direct detection and exotic naDM astrophysics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the matter coincidence problem (Ω_DM ≈ 5Ω_b) by correlating visible and dark sector mass scales and number densities through a Z₂ mirror symmetry; to explore testable cosmological and phenomenological signatures of twin-sector baryogenesis and nucleosynthesis.

Method: Analyzes twin nucleon freeze-out and twin Big Bang nucleosynthesis (BBN) to compute naDM abundance and elemental composition; computes dark acoustic oscillations (DAO) including neutron-proton scattering effects; evaluates Higgs portal interactions for direct detection; constructs a supersymmetric twin Froggatt-Nielsen model with spontaneous Z₂ breaking in the flavon sector.

Result: Twin-BBN produces elements up to twin carbon; percent-level twin neutron-proton mass splitting enables appreciable naDM; strong twin n–p scattering induces a distinctive two-stage DAO signature; Higgs portal couplings make the scenario viable for directional direct detection; naDM exhibits exotic astrophysical behavior.

Conclusion: The MTH framework with twin neutron DM and naDM provides a theoretically consistent, predictive, and observationally testable solution to the matter coincidence problem, linking early-universe cosmology, particle physics, and dark matter phenomenology.

Abstract: We study the theory and signatures of a Mirror Twin Higgs (MTH) scenario in which stable twin neutrons constitute most of the dark matter (DM), while a subdominant component of twin elements survives as nuclear atomic dark matter (naDM). This framework offers a solution to the $matter~coincidence~problem$ ($\Omega_{\rm DM}\simeq5\Omega_b$), as the twin $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry can correlate the visible and DM mass scales and their number densities. We analyze twin nucleon freeze-out and the onset of twin Big Bang nucleosynthesis (BBN) to estimate the total naDM abundance, and determine which elements in the dark periodic table could be populated. An appreciable but subdominant naDM component generally requires a fractional nucleon mass splitting near the percent level, with twin-BBN creating heavy elements up to twin carbon. Strong twin neutron-proton scattering allows nearly all of the dark matter, not only the atomic sub-component, to participate in $dark~acoustic~oscillations$ (DAO) at early times, giving rise to a characteristic two-stage DAO signature that may be detected with future cosmological probes. Interactions between the twin and visible sectors, including the irreducible Higgs portal, make this scenario a well-motivated target for future directional direct-detection searches, and the naDM sub-component is expected to exhibit highly exotic astrophysics. We outline a supersymmetric twin Froggatt-Nielsen realization in which spontaneous $\mathbb{Z}_2$ breaking in the flavon sector can generate the required fermion spectrum and Higgs potential.

</details>


### [154] [Beyond the Standard Model EFT: Stress Testing the Electroweak Phase Transition in the Real Singlet Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15856)
*Morgan E. Cassidy,Zhongtian Dong,Peisi Huang,Ian M. Lewis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses dimension-6 EFT to study how TeV-scale new physics affects electroweak phase transition and related observables in the real singlet scalar extension of the SM without Z₂ symmetry; it finds the Higgs trilinear coupling is sensitive to such new physics, while gravitational wave signals and resonant di-Higgs production rates remain robust.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The real singlet scalar model supports a strong first-order electroweak phase transition (EWPT) but lacks CP violation needed for baryogenesis, implying additional TeV-scale new physics; the paper aims to systematically assess how such physics—parameterized via EFT—affects EWPT and associated collider/gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Effective field theory up to dimension six with Λ = 1 TeV is employed; operators are added to the scalar potential of the real singlet model without Z₂ symmetry; correlations among strong first-order EWPT, Higgs trilinear coupling (κ₃), resonant di-Higgs production cross section, and gravitational wave spectrum (peak frequency and amplitude) are numerically analyzed.

Result: The allowed range of the Higgs trilinear coupling κ₃ is significantly narrowed and becomes sensitive to dimension-6 EFT coefficients; in contrast, predictions for the peak frequency and amplitude of the stochastic gravitational wave background, as well as the maximum resonant di-Higgs production rate (in absence of other mechanisms), are stable under weakly coupled TeV-scale new physics.

Conclusion: While the Higgs trilinear coupling serves as a sensitive probe of TeV-scale EFT corrections in the real singlet model, gravitational wave signals and resonant di-Higgs rates are robust observables—making them reliable discriminators of the underlying EWPT dynamics independent of unspecified UV completions.

Abstract: The real singlet scalar extension of the Standard Model is a much studied benchmark model for a strong first order electroweak phase transition (EWPT) and its role in electroweak baryogenesis. However, this model is not sufficient to generate the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, since it does not introduce any additional sources of CP violation. That is, additional new physics is still expected in the TeV range. In this paper we use effective field theory (EFT) methods to study the effects of new physics on the EWPT in the real singlet model with no $Z_2$. Effective operators up to dimension six with a cutoff scale of $\Lambda=1$ TeV are included in the scalar potential. We study the correlations between a strong first order EWPT, the Higgs trilinear coupling, resonant di-Higgs production, and gravitational wave signals in the EFT. We find that in the real singlet model with a strong first order EWPT the allowed region of the Higgs trilinear coupling is sensitive to TeV scale new physics, while the predictions of the gravitational wave signal and maximum resonant di-Higgs rate (with no additional production mechanisms) are robust against additional weakly coupled physics at or above the TeV scale.

</details>


### [155] [A Scattering Phase Space Primer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15861)
*Francesca Acanfora*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides the first comprehensive analytic treatment of 2→3 particle scattering phase space and differential cross sections with non-zero masses and non-constant matrix elements, using Gram determinants to derive exact closed-form expressions and physical boundaries—enabling accurate BSM phenomenology (e.g., VBF) without numerical approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the analytic intractability of 2→3 scattering with realistic kinematics (non-zero masses, energy-dependent matrix elements), which is essential for precision Beyond Standard Model searches such as Vector Boson Fusion.

Method: Uses Lorentz-invariant Gram determinants as the foundational formalism to analytically derive closed-form expressions for the 2→3 phase space volume and differential cross sections, while fully retaining particle mass dependence and general matrix element structure.

Result: Exact, closed-form analytic expressions for differential cross sections and complete, explicit characterization of the physical 2→3 phase space boundaries under arbitrary mass configurations and center-of-mass energies.

Conclusion: The 2→3 scattering problem with m ≠ 0 and |ℳ| ≠ 1 is analytically tractable using proper Gram-determinant-based kinematic methods; the results are universal, exact, and directly applicable to collider and dark matter phenomenology.

Abstract: The kinematic phase space is fundamental to all particle scattering processes across experiments - from lepton and hadron colliders to dark matter searches. While $2 \to 2$ scatterings are well understood, the simplest non-trivial case of three-body final states presents major analytic challenges that are typically circumvented by numerical methods. This paper provides the first comprehensive analytic treatment of $2 \to 3$ phase space and cross section distributions that uses a non-constant matrix element and keeps all particle masses non-zero. This is fundamental for an accurate description of crucial configurations for Beyond Standard Model searches, e.g. Vector Boson Fusion. Using Gram Determinants as our primary language for Lorentz-invariant kinematics, we derive exact, closed-form expressions for differential cross section distributions and explicitly construct the complete physical phase space boundaries. This paper serves as both a pedagogical introduction and a practical reference for the analytic computation of realistic $2 \to 3$ processes, showing how the $m\neq0, |{\cal M}|\neq 1$ problem is computationally tractable through proper kinematic methods. The results apply universally across all $2 \to 3$ scatterings regardless of center-of-mass energy, particle masses, or interaction type.

</details>


### [156] [Ensemble Learning with Unsupervised and Semi-Supervised Models for New Physics Searches at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15867)
*Alexandre Alves,Eduardo da Silva Almeida,Douglas Roberto de Matos Pimentel*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an ensemble-based unsupervised/semi-supervised strategy for anomaly detection in high-energy physics to improve robustness across diverse unknown new physics signals, trading marginal performance loss for significantly enhanced reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lack of undisputed experimental signals for beyond-Standard-Model physics makes model selection arbitrary; unsupervised ML methods suffer from signal-dependent performance due to inductive biases (No Free Lunch Theorem), risking missed discoveries.

Method: Constructs ensembles of unsupervised and semi-supervised anomaly detection classifiers; evaluates them across multiple distinct signal hypotheses to assess both average performance and robustness (i.e., consistent detection capability regardless of signal morphology).

Result: Ensemble models consistently deliver reliable detection across varied signal hypotheses, outperforming individual models in robustness—though often with slightly lower peak performance—thereby reducing the risk of overlooking genuinely unknown physics.

Conclusion: Ensemble learning provides a more model-independent, robust framework for unsupervised new physics searches at colliders, prioritizing discovery reliability over maximal sensitivity to any single signal type.

Abstract: New physics beyond the Standard Model is widely believed to exist motivated by key limitations of the current theory. However, in the absence of undisputed experimental signals, there is little guidance on what type of extended physics model should be built. To address the challenge of training machine learning classifiers to spot unknown phenomena, many algorithms have been applied to the task of either identifying anomalous events relative to background expectations or cluster collision events together in classes of similarity. However, as in the supervised case, the no free lunch theorem assures that the performance of unsupervised models depends on the specific signal hypothesis and their inductive biases, and the successful identification of a given signal by a particular algorithm does not guarantee comparable performance for other signals. In this study, we take a step toward a more model-independent unsupervised and semi-supervised search strategy by combining classifiers into ensembles. Our goal is addressing two complementary aspects of unsupervised ensemble learning - overall performance and robustness of the classification task from a thorough assessment of ensemble models. We show that, despite sacrificing some performance relative to the best individual model in the majority of cases, the ensembles provide robust and reliable classification predictions across several signal hypotheses, increasing confidence that genuinely unknown new physics will not pass unnoticed.

</details>


### [157] [Discovering new physics with semitauonic $b$-hadron decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15869)
*Markus Prim,Dean J. Robinson*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews theoretical and experimental aspects of lepton flavor universality violation (LFUV) anomalies in beauty hadron decays, assessing current interpretations and future challenges in confirming or excluding new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed anomalous lepton flavor universality violation (LFUV) in semileptonic B-meson decays suggests possible new physics beyond the Standard Model, necessitating rigorous, unbiased interpretation frameworks.

Method: The paper provides a critical review of theoretical foundations (e.g., effective field theory, operator bases), experimental methodologies (e.g., angular analyses, ratio observables like R(K), R(K*)), and systematic treatment of hadronic uncertainties and correlations.

Result: Current LFUV anomalies (e.g., in b → sℓ⁺ℓ⁻ transitions) remain statistically significant but inconclusive due to persistent tensions between datasets and unresolved hadronic effects; global fits favor certain NP scenarios (e.g., vector/leptoquark models), yet no definitive evidence is established.

Conclusion: Robust NP interpretation requires improved control over non-perturbative QCD effects, better experimental statistics from LHCb Run 3/Belle II, and coordinated theory–experiment efforts to break degeneracies among NP models and SM explanations.

Abstract: Anomalous lepton flavor universality violation (LFUV) observed in semileptonic decays of beauty hadrons may be the first signal of new physics (NP) beyond the Standard Model. We review the essential theoretical concepts and experimental considerations required for self-consistent and unbiased NP interpretations of these LFUV anomalies. We examine the status of present-day analyses, and assess the core challenges to the future precision studies that will be required to conclusively establish or reject an NP signal, as well as promising strategies to resolve them.

</details>


### [158] [Dark Matter Velocimetry with Quantum Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15878)
*Miguel Angel Soto Alcaraz,Doojin Kim,Kyoungchul Kong,Myeonghun Park*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using two-node quantum sensor networks to probe the Galactic dark matter halo's velocity distribution via spatial phase coherence, showing that Earth's rotation breaks degeneracies between isotropic (SHM) and radially anisotropic (Gaia-inspired SHM++) halo models, enabling tests of Milky Way assembly history.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between smooth, isotropic dark matter halo models (e.g., Standard Halo Model) and structured, radially anisotropic models (e.g., Gaia-inspired SHM++) using quantum sensor networks — thereby turning a dark matter detection into a probe of Galactic halo structure and formation history.

Method: Analyzes nonlocal exchange measurements in a minimal two-node quantum sensor network sensitive to the characteristic function of the dark matter velocity distribution; incorporates daily/annual modulation, Earth’s rotational scanning of baseline orientation, postselection, depolarization, and instrumental offset; performs likelihood-based model discrimination.

Result: Annual modulation alone cannot distinguish centered SHM from SHM++ due to identical phase waveforms when signal amplitude is unknown; Earth’s rotation breaks this degeneracy by exposing direction-dependent coherence, enabling discrimination; likelihood analysis demonstrates separation of anisotropic signal features from flat backgrounds using modulation and signal-odd controls.

Conclusion: Two-node quantum sensor networks—leveraging Earth’s rotation and temporal modulations—can discriminate local dark matter velocity distributions, transforming quantum sensing into a tool for astrophysical inference about the Milky Way’s halo structure and accretion history.

Abstract: Quantum sensor networks can use the spatial phase coherence of wavelike dark matter to probe not only particle properties but also the motion and structure of the Galactic halo. We explore this idea for the minimal two-node network, in which a nonlocal exchange measurement samples the characteristic function of the velocity distribution. We compare the Standard Halo Model (SHM) with a Gaia-inspired radially anisotropic component and their mixture (SHM++), taking into account daily and annual modulation, postselection, depolarizing events, and a flat instrumental offset. We show that annual modulation at one fixed local sidereal time has the same phase waveform for the centered SHM and Gaia-inspired SHM++ models, leaving them degenerate when the overall signal amplitude is unknown. Earth's rotation breaks this degeneracy by scanning the baseline through Galactic directions and exposing the direction-dependent coherence of an anisotropic population. Distinguishing these velocity distributions would test whether the local halo is smooth and nearly isotropic or contains a radially biased accreted component, turning a quantum-network detection into a probe of Milky Way structure. Our likelihood analysis shows how modulation and signal-odd controls separate this information from flat backgrounds and provides a path from model discrimination toward measurement of the local velocity distribution.

</details>


### [159] [Chains of Vector-like Leptons at Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15893)
*Tao Han,Tong Arthur Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vector-like leptons (VLLs) in chain flavor models, analyzing their production, decay, and discovery potential at current and future colliders (LHC, FCC-hh, e⁺e⁻/μ⁺μ⁻), linking VLL mass patterns to SM fermion mass hierarchies via hopping parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test chain flavor models that explain SM fermion mass hierarchy and mixing through vector-like lepton (VLL) multiplets, whose mass structure encodes the new physics scale and Yukawa couplings.

Method: Analyzes VLL production (pair via gauge interaction at hadron colliders; single via vector boson-lepton fusion) and decay; computes sensitivity limits for HL-LHC, FCC-hh, and lepton colliders using kinematic thresholds, cross sections, and partial width measurements.

Result: HL-LHC can probe VLL chain scales up to 800–900 GeV; FCC-hh exceeds 2.5 TeV; e⁺e⁻/μ⁺μ⁻ colliders enable clean pair production above threshold and enhanced single production via Z-lepton fusion down to lower masses, extending reach to collider kinematic limits.

Conclusion: VLL chains are testable across multiple collider environments; observation of flavor-correlated VLL multiplets and measurement of mass splittings would validate chain flavor models and constrain the origin of fermion masses.

Abstract: Vector-like leptons (VLLs) are predicted in the chain flavor models, which possess characteristic features to explain the mass hierarchy and mixing of the fermions in the Standard Model (SM). We investigate the production and decay of the VLL chains and quantify the discovery potential at the LHC, FCC-hh, and lepton colliders. The resonant mass sets the new physics scale responsible for the fermion mass generation. The observation of the VLL multiplets associated with each lepton flavor yields the chain structure. The mass difference among the VLL multiplets determines the hopping parameter that gives the power-law pattern for the observed SM fermion masses. The single VLL production via vector boson-lepton fusion directly measures the partial width, equivalently the new Yukawa coupling and the VLL mass hopping parameter. At hadron colliders, the leading production mechanism is pair production via their gauge interaction. We find that HL-LHC may have the sensitivity to reach the chain scale of $800-900\,\mathrm{GeV}$ and FCC-hh reaches $>2.5\,\mathrm{TeV}$. In $e^+e^-$ and $\mu^+\mu^-$ colliders, VLL pair production is a clear discovery channel once the threshold is reached. Single production via $Z$-lepton fusion becomes more accessible owing to a lower mass threshold and extends signal discovery to the collider kinematic limit.

</details>


### [160] [Probing gluon saturation with forward heavy-flavor correlations in proton-nucleus collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15897)
*Farid Salazar,Björn Schenke,Wenbin Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates heavy-meson pair azimuthal correlations in p+p and p+Pb collisions using the Color Glass Condensate framework, incorporating small-x evolution and Sudakov resummation; it successfully describes LHCb D-Dbar and b-hadron data and predicts both suppression and broadening of the away-side peak in p+Pb vs. p+p—key signatures of gluon saturation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe gluon saturation via heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations, leveraging heavy quarks' sensitivity to small-x gluons, mass-induced suppression of collinear radiation, and faithful momentum inheritance by heavy mesons—offering cleaner access to partonic kinematics than light hadrons.

Method: First full-azimuth calculation of heavy-meson (D-Dbar, b-hadron) pair correlations within the Color Glass Condensate effective theory, combining JIMWLK/BK small-x evolution initialized from HERA-constrained gluon distributions with Sudakov resummation near ΔΦ = π.

Result: Good agreement with LHCb data for D-Dbar and b-hadron correlations; prediction of ~15% away-side yield suppression and 10–25% peak broadening (Gaussian width) in p+Pb relative to p+p at ΔΦ ≈ π—distinctive saturation signatures absent in light-flavor correlations.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations are a robust, high-precision probe of gluon saturation, with predicted suppression and broadening providing complementary, experimentally accessible observables beyond light-hadron measurements.

Abstract: We investigate heavy-flavor azimuthal correlations as probes of gluon saturation in high-energy $p$+$p$ and $p$+Pb collisions within the Color Glass Condensate effective theory. Heavy flavor has several advantages: heavy-quark production is especially sensitive to the small-$x$ gluon distribution of the target, while the quark mass suppresses collinear radiation. Further, the heavy meson carries a large fraction of the momentum of its parent quark, so that the meson pair follows the partonic kinematics more closely than for light hadrons. We present the first calculation of heavy-meson pair correlations over the full azimuthal range that combines small-$x$ evolution from an initial condition constrained by HERA data with Sudakov resummation near the back-to-back limit. It provides a good description of the $D$-$\bar D$ and $b$-hadron pair correlations measured by LHCb. In contrast to the light-flavor case, we predict, in addition to a suppression of the away-side yield in $p$+Pb relative to $p$+$p$ collisions by about $15\%$, a broadening of the peak by approximately $10\%$ to $25\%$ for Gaussian fits near $\Delta\Phi=\pi$, which makes heavy-flavor correlations a promising observable in the search for gluon saturation.

</details>


### [161] [Polarized gluon TMD fragmentation into spin-1 quarkonium at NLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15918)
*Alessia Bongallino,Miguel G. Echevarria,Samuel F. Romera*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full set of gluon transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation functions (TMDFFs) into spin-1 quarkonium (e.g., J/ψ) at next-to-leading order (NLO) in QCD, including all gluon and quarkonium polarization states and relevant NRQCD color-octet channels; results validate known collinear limits and LO TMDFFs, and provide first-time NLO polarized TMDFFs with full k_T dependence for quarkonium polarization studies at EIC and e+e− colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide complete, perturbatively calculable gluon TMDFFs into polarized spin-1 quarkonia—essential for predicting and interpreting quarkonium polarization observables in high-energy collisions (e.g., at the Electron-Ion Collider and e+e− experiments)—where existing knowledge was limited to LO or unpolarized cases.

Method: Perturbative QCD calculation within the NRQCD factorization framework at NLO (O(α_s^2)); includes all contributing color-octet channels (^3S_1^[8], ^1S_0^[8], ^3P_J^[8]); systematically accounts for gluon and quarkonium spin/polarization degrees of freedom; uses dimensional regularization and MS-bar scheme; cross-checks against known collinear FFs and prior LO/NLO unpolarized TMDFF results.

Result: Complete set of gluon TMDFFs into 1^{--} quarkonium at NLO, including all eight leading-twist polarized and unpolarized TMDFFs with full transverse-momentum (k_T) dependence; agreement with collinear limits (up to δ(1−z) ambiguities) and LO TMDFFs; first derivation of polarized TMDFFs beyond LO; validation of unpolarized NLO TMDFFs against earlier work.

Conclusion: The NLO gluon TMDFFs into spin-1 quarkonium are now fully available for phenomenology; their polarization structure is governed by the spin-1 nature of the final state, making the formalism broadly applicable to other spin-1 hadrons; these results enable quantitative predictions for quarkonium spin alignment in jets and semi-inclusive processes.

Abstract: We compute the complete set of gluon transverse-momentum-dependent fragmentation functions (TMDFFs) into quarkonium through next-to-leading order in perturbative QCD. We account for all polarization configurations of both the fragmenting gluon and the produced $J^{\rm PC}=1^{--}$ quarkonium. Within NRQCD, we include all color-octet channels that contribute through $\mathcal O(\alpha_s^2)$ at the considered order in the heavy-quark relative velocity $v$, namely ${}^3S_1^{[8]}$, ${}^1S_0^{[8]}$ and ${}^3P_J^{[8]}$. Several structures of the calculation follow only from the spin-1 nature of the final-state hadron, so our framework applies more generally to the fragmentation of a gluon into any spin-1 hadron. Our results agree with all known collinear fragmentation functions, up to differences in $\delta(1-z)$ terms that we discuss, and with the TMDFFs at LO, and we cross-check the unpolarized TMDFFs at NLO against previous calculations. Most of the polarized TMDFFs, and their full transverse-momentum dependence, are presented here for the first time. They provide the perturbative input for studies of quarkonium polarization in jets, at the EIC and in $e^+e^-$ annihilation.

</details>


### [162] [Matching 2HDM to HEFT in triple Higgs boson production from WBF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15979)
*L. Barreiro,D. Domenech,D. Garcia-Tejada,M. Herrero,S. Salazar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper matches HEFT coefficients to 2HDM parameters by equating $WW \to hhh$ scattering amplitudes in the heavy Higgs limit, deriving expressions for $a,b,c,\kappa_3,\kappa_4$ in terms of $c_{\beta-\alpha}, \tan\beta, m_{12}$, and highlights non-decoupling effects—especially under misalignment ($c_{\beta-\alpha} \neq 0$)—that produce a large deviation from SM cross section predictions, serving as indirect probes of heavy 2HDM states at future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically capture non-decoupling contributions from heavy 2HDM scalars ($H, A, H^\pm$) to triple Higgs production via $WW \to hhh$, using HEFT as an effective description valid when heavy states are kinematically inaccessible but still influence low-energy observables through loop or symmetry-breaking effects.

Method: Amplitude matching: the $WW \to hhh$ scattering amplitudes in the heavy-mass limit ($m_H, m_A, m_{H^\pm} \gg m_h$) are computed in both 2HDM and HEFT frameworks, and their leading-order terms are equated to solve for HEFT Wilson coefficients $a, b, c, \kappa_3, \kappa_4$. The matching is performed analytically, retaining only non-decoupling (mass-independent or logarithmically enhanced) terms.

Result: Explicit analytic expressions for HEFT coefficients $a, b, c, \kappa_3, \kappa_4$ in terms of 2HDM parameters $c_{\beta-\alpha}, \tan\beta, m_{12}$ are derived; these encode the dominant non-decoupling effects. Numerical evaluation shows that $\sigma(WW \to hhh)$ in the matched HEFT deviates significantly from the SM prediction when $c_{\beta-\alpha} \neq 0$, with enhancements up to orders of magnitude depending on misalignment and $\tan\beta$.

Conclusion: The HEFT–2HDM matching for $WW \to hhh$ provides a robust, parameterized framework to probe otherwise inaccessible heavy 2HDM states via triple Higgs production. The persistent non-decoupling behavior—especially under misalignment—makes $\sigma(WW \to hhh)$ a sensitive indirect observable for BSM scalar sectors at high-energy colliders.

Abstract: In this work we compare the Higgs Effective Field Theory (HEFT) predictions with the Two Higgs Doublet Model (2HDM) predictions for triple Higgs production via $WW \rightarrow hhh$ scattering, under the hypothesis of very heavy Higgs bosons $H$, $A$, and $H^\pm$, and assuming the lightest one $h$ has the observed mass in the experiments, $m_h \simeq 125$ GeV. By requiring the matching of the two corresponding scattering amplitudes for this $WW \rightarrow hhh$ process in the limit of very heavy mass for these bosons, we find the solutions for the leading order HEFT coefficients. Concretely, we determine the coefficients $a$, $b$, $c$, $\kappa_3$ and $\kappa_4$ defining the effective interactions $WWh$, $WWhh$, $WWhhh$, $hhh$ and $hhhh$, respectively, in terms of the 2HDM input parameters, $c_{\beta-\alpha}$, $\tan \beta$ and $m_{12}$. We will also compare these solutions with the corresponding solutions found for the case of double Higgs production. The solutions presented here for the matched coefficients summarize the non-decoupling effects from the heavy modes, $H$, $A$ and $H^\pm$ in the scattering process $WW \rightarrow hhh$ and provide the most relevant contributions to the cross section $\sigma(WW \rightarrow hhh)$ that do not decrease as inverse powers of the heavy masses. In the last part, we will also discuss the numerical predictions of the cross section $\sigma(WW \rightarrow hhh)$ in terms of the HEFT-matched coefficients. The focus of this discussion is set on the big gap between the HEFT-matched prediction and the SM prediction due to the non-decoupling behaviour occurring whenever misalignment is assumed in the 2HDM, i.e. for $c_{\beta-\alpha} \neq 0$. This big gap could serve as potential signals of the 2HDM heavy modes, $H$, $A$ and $H^\pm$, at future high energy colliders, even if they are too heavy and cannot be produced at these colliders.

</details>


### [163] [KNO functions for dipole-dipole scattering from large Pomeron loops: log-normal tails and a test in exactly solvable models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16081)
*Michael Roa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reexamines multiplicity distributions derived from resummed Pomeron loops in QCD using BK evolution and AGK cutting rules, showing that KNO-scaling functions have log-normal (not Gaussian) tails expressible via the Lambert W function; it demonstrates—via exactly solvable 0D models—that while amplitude resummation reproduces exact S-matrix well, the associated AGK-derived multiplicity distributions fail at high energy due to a decoupling between rare (amplitude-determining) and typical (multiplicity-determining) configurations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To critically assess the validity of multiplicity distributions obtained by combining BK evolution, Pomeron loop resummation, and AGK cutting rules—particularly their claimed KNO scaling and entropy relation—by probing analytic structure, tail behavior, and exact model benchmarks.

Method: Analytic asymptotic analysis of the BK and dipole-dipole KNO functions using Gaussian approximation of the loop series; closed-form derivation via Lambert W function; comparison with log-normal mixture interpretations; validation against two exactly solvable zero-transverse-dimension models (unitary toy model), including direct computation of exact S-matrix and multiplicity distributions.

Result: KNO functions exhibit log-normal tails: lnΨ_BK ∼ −½ ln²ξ, lnΨ_dd ∼ −¼ ln²ξ; closed forms obtained via Lambert W; KNO scaling requires discarding power-like coefficient factors; alternative contour representations yield sign-changing functions; in 0D models, resummed amplitude matches exact S-matrix within 2.5–10%, but AGK-derived multiplicity fails beyond dilute regime—no KNO scaling, mean multiplicity grows as power of rapidity, rest-frame entropy ∼ ½ ln⟨n⟩.

Conclusion: The agreement between resummed amplitude and exact S-matrix does not imply reliability of AGK-derived multiplicity distributions; multiplicity is governed by typical cascade configurations, whereas amplitude is controlled by rare ones—hence the two are decoupled at high energy, invalidating the standard AGK+loop-resummation approach for multiplicity predictions.

Abstract: The sum of large Pomeron loops in QCD, based on the $t$-channel unitarity and on the solution of the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation in the saturation region, has been combined with the AGK (Abramovsky, Gribov and Kancheli) cutting rules to obtain multiplicity distributions with KNO (Koba, Nielsen and Olesen) scaling and an entropy equal to the logarithm of the gluon structure function. We reexamine these distributions for the BK amplitude and for dipole-dipole scattering. The KNO functions that follow from the Gaussian representation of the asymptotic series have log-normal and not Gaussian tails, $\ln\Psi_{\rm BK}\simeq-\frac{1}{2}\ln^2\xi$ and $\ln\Psi_{\rm dd}\simeq-\frac{1}{4}\ln^2\xi$, and we give them in closed form in terms of the Lambert function. They are log-normal mixtures of the distributions of one and two BFKL cascades, and the summation is not unique. The KNO scaling requires that power-like factors in the coefficients of the series be discarded, and the alternative contour representation leads to functions that change sign. We then test the approach in two exactly solvable models in zero transverse dimensions. In the unitary toy model we confirm that the resummed series reproduces the exact $S$-matrix to within 2.5-10%, while the $S$-matrix changes by many orders of magnitude. Nevertheless, the multiplicity distribution obtained from the same series with the AGK rules agrees with the exact one only as long as both cascades are dilute. At higher energies the exact mean grows as a power of rapidity, the KNO scaling is absent, and the entropy in the rest frame of the target grows as one half of the logarithm of the mean multiplicity. The amplitude is determined by rare configurations of the cascades and the multiplicity by the typical ones, so that the agreement of the amplitudes does not validate the multiplicity distributions.

</details>


### [164] [Connecting Electroweak Fine-Tuning, Tens-of-GeV Dark Matter, and Exotic Higgs Decays in the GNMSSM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16191)
*Zhaoxia Heng,Haijing Zhou,Fei Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a natural parameter region in the GNMSSM where electroweak fine-tuning is mild (Δ ≤ 40), singlino dark matter (~10–20 GeV) coexists with observable exotic Higgs decays h → asas (BR ≈ 7%–25%), and both scenarios (h₁ or h₂ SM-like) satisfy LZ 2024 constraints; LHC already excludes part of the low-mass as window, but HL-LHC retains discovery potential—especially in the h₂ case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile electroweak naturalness, viable singlino dark matter (~tens of GeV), and experimentally accessible exotic Higgs decays within the GNMSSM, avoiding excessive fine-tuning while satisfying current direct detection (LZ 2024) and collider constraints.

Method: Parameter space scan of the GNMSSM enforcing fine-tuning Δ ≤ 40, singlino LSP mass ~10–20 GeV, and the mass hierarchy m_S̃ < m_B̃ < m_H̃ with μ_tot ∈ [200, 500] GeV; computation of Br(h → asas), simulation of h → asas → bb̄τ⁺τ⁻ signatures at LHC, and comparison with LZ 2024 limits and projected HL-LHC sensitivity.

Result: A natural region is found where Br(h → asas) = 7%–25% for 10 GeV ≤ m_as ≤ 20 GeV; two SM-Higgs candidates survive LZ: h₁-case (12% excluded by current LHC for 12–15.5 GeV m_as) and h₂-case (10% excluded, with significant remaining reach at HL-LHC).

Conclusion: The GNMSSM can simultaneously realize natural electroweak symmetry breaking, viable singlino dark matter, and observable exotic Higgs decays; the h₂-case offers a particularly promising and less constrained target for HL-LHC searches.

Abstract: In the general next-to-minimal supersymmetric standard model (GNMSSM), we connect electroweak fine-tuning, a singlino dark matter candidate with mass around tens of GeV, and Exotic Higgs Decays, identifying a natural region characterized by the mass hierarchy $m_{\tilde{S}}<m_{\tilde{B}}<m_{\tilde{H}}$ and $\mu_{\rm tot}\in [200, 500]$ GeV. For fine-tuning measure $\Delta \leq 40$, the branching ratio $\mathrm{Br}(h\to a_sa_s)$ falls in the range 7\%$-$25\%, with the pseudoscalar mass $m_{a_s}$ lying mostly between 10 and 20 GeV. This region splits into two viable scenarios consistent with LZ 2024 direct detection: one where the lightest CP-even Higgs $h_1$ is SM-like ($h_1$ case), and another where the second-lightest $h_2$ is SM-like ($h_2$ case). LHC searches for $h\to a_sa_s \to b\bar{b}\tau^+\tau^-$ exclude 12\% (10\%) of samples with 12 GeV $\leq m_{a_s} \leq$ 15.5 GeV in the $h_1$ ($h_2$) case, while a substantial fraction remains accessible at the HL-LHC in the $h_2$ case. Our analysis unifies naturalness, dark matter, and observable exotic Higgs decays, yielding concrete targets for future experiments.

</details>


### [165] [High Energy Asymptotics of Quark-Gluon Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16298)
*Jian Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives infrared evolution equations for twist-3 quark-gluon correlation functions (Qiu-Sterman and Boer-Mulders) at double logarithmic accuracy, revealing shared small-x intercepts with twist-2 counterparts but with an extra 1/ln(1/x) suppression from coherent gluon color structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the high-energy (small-x) behavior of quantum interference between partonic configurations in the proton—specifically flavor-nonsinglet quark-gluon correlations relevant to transverse spin asymmetries.

Method: Recursive factorization at double logarithmic accuracy, incorporating coherent gluon insertion; derivation of infrared evolution equations for twist-3 matrix elements.

Result: Twist-3 functions (Qiu-Sterman and chiral-odd Boer-Mulders) inherit the leading small-x intercepts of their twist-2 analogues but are suppressed by an additional 1/ln(1/x) factor due to the color structure of the coherent gluon.

Conclusion: The derived evolution provides a theoretical basis for extrapolating quark-gluon correlations to small x, informing measurements of transverse spin phenomena at the Electron Ion Collider.

Abstract: The internal structure of the proton is encoded not only in single parton densities, but also in the quantum interference between distinct partonic configurations. We study the high energy behavior of this interference by focusing on flavor-nonsinglet quark-gluon correlations relevant to transverse spin phenomena. By establishing recursive factorization at double logarithmic accuracy in the presence of a coherent gluon insertion, we derive infrared evolution equations for the Qiu-Sterman function and its chiral odd Boer-Mulders counterpart. We find that these twist-3 functions share the leading small $x$ intercepts of their respective twist-2 counterparts, but acquire an additional $1/\ln(1/x)$ suppression due to the color structure of the coherent gluon insertion. These results may guide small $x$ extrapolations of quark-gluon correlations at the Electron Ion Collider.

</details>


### [166] [Disentangling the Effects of Phase-Specific Transport Properties on Elliptic Flow Centrality Dependence in the AMPT Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16394)
*Yao Zhang,Xipei Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the AMPT model to disentangle how partonic and hadronic transport properties—specifically shear viscosity (η/s), phase transition temperature (T_tr), and hadronic cross sections—affect charged hadron elliptic flow (v₂) centrality dependence in Au+Au collisions at 200 GeV. It finds that hadronic rescattering increasingly dominates v₂ toward peripheral collisions, and increasing hadronic cross sections by 4× improves agreement with STAR data, suggesting larger effective hadronic cross sections may explain v₂ flattening in peripheral/small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the relative contributions of partonic and hadronic phases to anisotropic flow, particularly the centrality-dependent flattening of v₂ observed in experiments, by systematically varying phase-specific transport parameters in a controlled theoretical framework.

Method: Employed the AMPT (A Multi-Phase Transport) model with independent tuning of shear viscosity over entropy density (η/s) in the partonic phase, phase transition temperature (T_tr), and hadronic scattering cross sections; performed centrality-dependent v₂ calculations and compared to STAR experimental data.
