Result: Hadronic rescattering contributes increasingly to v₂ with decreasing centrality (i.e., more in peripheral collisions); scaling hadronic cross sections by 4× significantly enhances v₂ in peripheral collisions and brings model predictions into better agreement with STAR data; the observed flattening of v₂ at high centrality likely reflects enhanced hadronic interactions rather than purely partonic dynamics.

Conclusion: The hadronic phase plays a dominant and non-negligible role in shaping elliptic flow, especially in peripheral heavy-ion collisions and by extension small systems; interpreting v₂ solely through partonic properties (e.g., η/s) is insufficient—hadronic transport parameters must be concurrently constrained.

Abstract: The centrality dependence of charged hadron elliptic flow $v_{2}$ in Au+Au collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=200~\rm{GeV}$ is studied using the AMPT model with phase-specific control of partonic and hadronic transport properties. By separately varying the shear viscosity $\eta/s$, phase transition temperature $T_{tr}$, and hadronic scattering cross sections, we isolate their distinct effects on anisotropic flow. Hadronic rescattering is found to contribute increasingly to $v_{2}$ with centrality, with the contribution becoming dominant in peripheral collisions. Scaling up hadronic cross sections by a factor of four substantially amplifies $v_{2}$ in peripheral collisions and improves agreement with STAR data, suggesting that the flattening of $v_{2}$ at high centrality may reflect a larger effective hadronic cross section and highlighting the significant role of the hadronic phase in small systems.

</details>


### [167] [A perturbative construction of a modular $S_3$ model for quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16545)
*Gabriele Battimelli,Davide Meloni*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A modular-invariant supersymmetric flavor model for quarks based on Γ₂ ≅ S₃ reproduces 8 observables with only 10 free parameters (including τ), using modular forms of varying weights—no flavons or fine-tuning required; CP violation arises solely from the complex modulus τ under gCP symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a predictive, minimally tuned supersymmetric flavor model for quarks that explains mass hierarchies and mixing without flavons, while unifying CP violation origin in the modular parameter τ under gCP symmetry.

Method: Constructs a Γ₂ ≅ S₃ modular-invariant superpotential where quark mass matrices are built from modular forms of distinct weights; imposes generalized CP (gCP) symmetry to constrain τ and eliminate extra CP phases; fits 8 dimensionless observables (4 mass ratios + 4 CKM parameters) using 10 real parameters (Reτ, Imτ, and 8 real superpotential coefficients).

Result: All 8 quark flavor observables reproduced within 1σ experimental uncertainty; achieves best parameter economy among known S₃ modular quark models (10 parameters); confirms τ as sole source of CP violation; no flavon fields or ad hoc hierarchies needed.

Conclusion: The Γ₂ ≅ S₃ modular-invariant supersymmetric flavor model provides a highly predictive, natural, and economical description of quark masses and mixing, demonstrating that modular symmetry alone—augmented by gCP—can fully account for flavor structure and CP violation.

Abstract: We present a modular-invariant supersymmetric model of flavour for quark masses and mixing, based on the group $\Gamma_2\cong S_3$. Hierarchies in the quark sector follow from the symmetric structure of the model, as they are generated by modular forms of different weights in the mass matrices, and no severe tuning of free superpotential coefficients is needed. Moreover, no flavons are employed besides the modulus $\tau$ which is, given the gCP symmetry imposed on the model, the only source of CP violation. 8 dimensionless observables (4 mass ratios and 4 CKM parameters) are reproduced by making use of 10 free model parameters (including real and imaginary part of the modulus) within the $1\sigma$ range making our model, to the best of our knowledge, the $S_3$ modular model for quarks with the fewest free parameters.

</details>


### [168] [Inelastic Scalar Dark Matter in a Gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ Model and its LZ Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16553)
*Changjie Dai,Linyun He,Yonghua Wang,Wei Chao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inelastic scalar dark matter model within a gauged U(1)_{B-L} framework to explain the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) high-energy recoil anomaly, featuring two real scalar DM components (φ₁, φ₂) from a complex scalar Φ; it analyzes thermal freeze-out, relic abundance matching, and fits LZ data via nuclear recoil spectra in both exothermic (long-lived φ₁ + φ₂) and endothermic (dominant φ₂) scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the high-energy nuclear recoil candidate reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment using inelastic dark matter in a theoretically motivated, anomaly-free gauged U(1)_{B-L} extension of the Standard Model.

Method: Constructs a two-component scalar DM model with complex scalars Φ and S under U(1)_{B-L}; computes thermal freeze-out dynamics and relic density for both exothermic and endothermic inelastic scattering; derives nuclear recoil spectra and performs a three-bin likelihood fit to LZ data to identify best-fit parameter regions.

Result: Identifies viable parameter spaces reproducing the observed DM relic abundance; obtains best-fit points for both exothermic and endothermic interpretations of the LZ signal; finds near-equal abundances of φ₁ and φ₂ at freeze-out; demonstrates compatibility of the model with LZ’s high-energy excess.

Conclusion: The inelastic two-component scalar DM in the gauged U(1)_{B-L} model provides a consistent theoretical and phenomenological explanation for the LZ anomaly while satisfying cosmological and direct detection constraints.

Abstract: We study inelastic scalar dark matter (DM) in a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ model motivated by the high-energy recoil candidate reported by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). The model introduces two complex scalars, $\Phi$ and $S$, charged under $U(1)_{B-L}$. The scalar $S$ is responsible for spontaneous symmetry breaking, while the two real components of $\Phi$, $\phi_1$ and $\phi_2$, serve as DM. This two-component DM framework accommodates both exothermic and endothermic scenarios. In the exothermic scenario, the lifetime of the heavier state $\phi_1$ exceeds the age of the Universe, and both states contribute to the present-day DM abundance. In the endothermic scenario, the abundance comes almost entirely from the lighter state $\phi_2$. We investigate the thermal history of the dark sector and find that the two scalars have approximately equal abundances at freeze-out. We identify parameter regions that reproduce the observed relic abundance. We calculate the nuclear recoil spectra, perform a three-bin likelihood analysis, and obtain the best-fit points interpreting the LZ signal in both scenarios.

</details>


### [169] [Low-lying doubly charmed strange baryons via QCD sum rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16576)
*Hui-Min Yang,Hua-Xing Chen,Xuan Luo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates masses and radiative transition widths of doubly heavy baryons (cc and bb) using QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules, predicting hyperfine splittings and decay widths for spin-3/2 → spin-1/2 γ transitions, with magnetic-dipole dominance and non-negligible heavy-quark photon emission.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide theoretical predictions for unobserved spin-3/2 doubly heavy baryons (e.g., Ξ*cc, Ω*cc, and their bb counterparts) and their radiative decays, guiding experimental searches at LHCb and Belle II.

Method: QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules (LCSR) are employed with mixed interpolating currents to compute baryon masses; electromagnetic form factors are derived via LCSR to obtain radiative widths for B*(3/2+) → B(1/2+)γ transitions.

Result: Predicted masses: Ξcc = 3.62±0.14 GeV, Ξ*cc = 3.72±0.14 GeV, Ωcc = 3.72±0.13 GeV, Ω*cc = 3.82±0.13 GeV; bb partners ~10.27–10.34 GeV. Hyperfine splittings: ~101 MeV (Ξcc), ~99 MeV (Ωcc). Radiative widths: Ξ*cc++→Ξcc++γ = 5.25+8.57−3.62 keV; others in 0.002–0.41 keV range. Magnetic-dipole dominates; electric-quadrupole negligible; heavy-quark photon emission is significant.

Conclusion: The study delivers robust mass and width predictions for excited doubly heavy baryons, confirms the importance of heavy-quark contributions in radiative transitions, and offers quantitative benchmarks for experimental discovery of spin-3/2 doubly heavy states via γ-decay channels.

Abstract: We systematically investigate the masses of the low-lying doubly heavy baryons and their $B^*(3/2^+)\to B(1/2^+)\gamma$ radiative transitions within QCD sum rules and light-cone sum rules (LCSR). By constructing mixed interpolating currents, we obtain $M_{\Xi_{cc}}=3.62^{+0.14}_{-0.15}$ GeV, $M_{\Xi_{cc}^*}=3.72^{+0.14}_{-0.13}$ GeV, $M_{\Omega_{cc}}=3.72^{+0.13}_{-0.15}$ GeV, and $M_{\Omega_{cc}^*}=3.82^{+0.13}_{-0.15}$ GeV, together with their doubly bottom partners around $10.27$--$10.34$ GeV. The extracted masses are compatible with the observed $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Omega_{cc}$ states and predict hyperfine splittings of about $101^{+27}_{-11}$ MeV and $99^{+11}_{-12}$ MeV for the $\Xi_{cc}$ and $\Omega_{cc}$ systems, respectively. Using these inputs, we derive the LCSR for the electromagnetic form factors and obtain radiative widths $\Gamma(\Xi_{cc}^{*++}\to\Xi_{cc}^{++}\gamma)=5.25^{+8.57}_{-3.62}$ keV, $\Gamma(\Xi_{cc}^{*+}\to\Xi_{cc}^{+}\gamma)=0.31^{+0.84}_{-0.25}$ keV, and $\Gamma(\Omega_{cc}^{*+}\to\Omega_{cc}^{+}\gamma)=0.41^{+0.67}_{-0.30}$ keV, while the corresponding doubly bottom widths are at the $0.002$--$0.01$ keV level. We find that the magnetic-dipole contribution dominates, whereas the electric-quadrupole form factor is numerically small. The light- and heavy-quark photon-emission terms can interfere or enhance significantly, and the heavy-quark contribution cannot be neglected in radiative transitions. These results provide useful theoretical guidance for future searches for spin-$3/2$ doubly heavy baryons through radiative decay modes at LHCb, Belle II, and other experimental facilities.

</details>


### [170] [Extended $SO(10)$-inspired leptogenesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16604)
*Pasquale Di Bari,Xubin Hu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes hierarchical Dirac neutrino mass seesaw models and shows they generically produce a highly hierarchical heavy neutrino spectrum; under plausible assumptions (Dirac masses ~ up-quark masses), the intermediate heavy neutrino N₂ naturally falls in the mass range suitable for successful thermal N₂-leptogenesis, explaining baryogenesis, while N₁ contributions are negligible — extending SO(10)-inspired scenarios and allowing inverted neutrino mass ordering with a relaxed lower bound m₁ ≳ 10⁻⁵ eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether hierarchical Dirac neutrino masses in seesaw models generically support viable leptogenesis — particularly N₂-leptogenesis — without fine-tuning or restrictive assumptions on the Dirac mass matrix, and to assess implications for neutrino mass ordering and lower bounds on the lightest neutrino mass.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological analysis of type-I seesaw models with hierarchical Dirac neutrino mass matrices; derivation of heavy neutrino mass spectrum constraints from low-energy neutrino oscillation data; assessment of viability of N₁- and N₂-leptogenesis under thermal and strong thermal conditions; comparison with SO(10)-inspired frameworks and CKM-like mismatch between neutrino and charged-lepton Dirac matrices.

Result: Hierarchical Dirac masses ⇒ highly hierarchical heavy neutrino spectrum (m_N₁ ≪ m_N₂ ≪ m_N₃); assuming m_D ∼ m_u, N₂ mass naturally lies in 10⁹–10¹² GeV range suitable for successful thermal N₂-leptogenesis; N₁ contribution is negligible; inverted neutrino mass ordering becomes viable; lower bound on m₁ relaxes from ~10⁻³ eV to ~10⁻⁵ eV; strong thermal leptogenesis still excludes inverted ordering.

Conclusion: N₂-leptogenesis emerges robustly in hierarchical seesaw models without additional assumptions — it is far more generic than previously assumed; this broadens theoretical viability of high-scale leptogenesis and accommodates inverted neutrino mass ordering within realistic parameter space.

Abstract: We consider seesaw models with hierarchical Dirac neutrino masses. We show that, barring well known fine-tuned crossing level solutions and given current low energy neutrino experimental data, these always lead to a very hierarchical heavy neutrino spectrum. If the Dirac neutrino masses are assumed not to significantly differ from the up-quark masses, then the intermediate seesaw neutrino ($N_2$) mass falls in the correct range for its decays to explain the matter-antimatter asymmetry of the universe via ($N_2$-)leptogenesis, while the lightest seesaw neutrino decays give a negligible contribution. We show that these conclusions are valid independently of any additional condition on the Dirac neutrino mass matrix. In this way we extend the usual $SO(10)$-inspired leptogenesis scenario, assuming the mismatch between neutrino and charged lepton Dirac mass matrices at the level of the CKM matrix. We find that solutions for inverted ordered neutrino masses become viable. We still find a lower bound on the lightest neutrino mass, $m_1 \gtrsim 10^{-5}\,{\rm eV}$, but this is relaxed by two orders of magnitude. We also consider the special subset of solutions realising strong thermal leptogenesis within this extended scenario, finding that inverted ordering remains incompatible. Our results show how the emergence of the $N_2$-leptogenesis scenario within seesaw models is much more general than thought so far.

</details>


### [171] [Particle diffusion in magnetized QCD matter with a realistic equation of state](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16656)
*Arnab Sarkar,Manu Kurian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives relativistic magnetohydrodynamic equations for particle number diffusion in a hot, interacting QCD medium under an external magnetic field, using a quasiparticle-based effective description and Chapman–Enskog expansion; it finds that interactions suppress magnetic-field-induced anisotropy in diffusion, offering a framework for dissipative transport in magnetized heavy-ion collision matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a consistent theoretical framework for dissipative particle transport—specifically number diffusion—in magnetized, interacting hot QCD matter relevant to heavy-ion collisions, where strong magnetic fields and medium interactions coexist.

Method: Starts from an effective covariant quasiparticle description of the hot QCD medium; applies a Chapman–Enskog-like iterative expansion of the distribution function to derive second-order relativistic magnetohydrodynamic evolution equations for the particle number diffusion current; obtains the Navier–Stokes limit and analyzes tensorial modifications induced by the magnetic field on transport coefficients.

Result: The magnetic field introduces new tensorial structures into the diffusion current, altering transport properties; medium interactions significantly suppress the magnetic-field-induced anisotropy in particle diffusion; explicit expressions for diffusion current and associated transport coefficients are obtained in both relativistic and Navier–Stokes limits.

Conclusion: A self-consistent second-order relativistic magnetohydrodynamic description of particle number diffusion in interacting hot QCD matter is established; the suppression of magnetic anisotropy by interactions highlights the necessity of including both interaction effects and electromagnetic fields in realistic hydrodynamic modeling of heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: In this study, we investigate particle number diffusion in an interacting hot QCD medium in the presence of an external magnetic field. Starting from an effective covariant description of the medium in terms of quasiparticle degrees of freedom, we employ a Chapman Enskog-like iterative expansion of the distribution function to derive the second-order relativistic magnetohydrodynamic evolution equation for the number diffusion current. The magnetic field introduces additional tensorial structures into the diffusion dynamics, thereby modifying the transport properties of the medium. We further obtain the corresponding Navier-Stokes limit and examine the influence of medium interactions and the magnetic field on the diffusion current and its associated transport coefficients. Our results show that medium interactions suppress the magnetic field-induced anisotropy in particle diffusion. This study provides a consistent framework for investigating dissipative particle transport in magnetized, interacting QCD matter and may serve as an important ingredient in developing realistic hydrodynamic descriptions of heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [172] [Next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to prompt $Υ(1S)$ pair hadroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16686)
*Zhi-Guo He,Xiao-Bo Jin,Bernd A.Kniehl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to prompt Υ(1S) pair hadroproduction within NRQCD factorization, focusing on color-singlet contributions and including feed-down from higher Υ states; predictions for integrated and differential cross sections agree well with CMS data at 8 and 13 TeV, except in extreme kinematic regions where double parton scattering may contribute.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for Υ(1S) pair production to test NRQCD factorization, constrain QCD dynamics, and establish a baseline for extracting the double parton scattering parameter σ_eff.

Method: Next-to-leading-order (NLO) calculation in nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization, including color-singlet channels and feed-down from Υ(2S) and Υ(3S); computation of integrated and differential cross sections (m_ΥΥ, p_T^ΥΥ, |Δy|, p_T^Υ) and comparison with CMS data at √s = 8 and 13 TeV.

Result: NLO K-factors range from ~0.9 for the total cross section to as low as 0.2 (at large |Δy|) and as high as 20 (at large p_T^Υ); predictions match CMS data well except in outermost m_ΥΥ and |Δy| bins, suggesting missing double parton scattering contributions.

Conclusion: The NLO NRQCD analysis provides a solid theoretical foundation for Υ pair production and enables the first reliable extraction of σ_eff from ΥΥ data.

Abstract: Working at next-to-leading order in nonrelativistic-QCD factorization, we study prompt $\Upsilon(1S)$ pair hadroproduction, including feed-down from the excited $\Upsilon(2S)$ and $\Upsilon(3S)$ states and focusing on the color singlet contributions, which are known to be dominant. Besides the integrated cross section $\sigma^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, we predict distributions in the invariant mass $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, transverse momentum $p_T^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$, and rapidity separation $|\Delta y|$ of the $\Upsilon(1S)$ pair, as well as the one in the transverse momentum $p_T^{\Upsilon}$ of either $\Upsilon(1S)$ meson, and compare them with measurements by the CMS Collaboration at the CERN LHC with center-of-mass energies $\sqrt{s}=8$ and $13~\mathrm{TeV}$ as far as they go. The QCD corrections to $\sigma^{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$ turn out to be mildly negative, with $K$ factors of about 0.9. By contrast, the differential cross sections receive $K$ factors as small as 0.6 and 0.2 at large values of $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}\le 100$ GeV and $|\Delta y|\le4$, respectively, and as large as 20 for large values of $p_T^{\Upsilon}\le30$ GeV. Our predictions agree well with the data, except in the outermost bins of $m_{\Upsilon\Upsilon}$ and $|\Delta y|$, where they fall short of the data, leaving room for contributions from double parton scattering as expected. Our analysis thus provides a first solid basis for the extraction of the key parameter $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff}}$ of double parton scattering.

</details>


### [173] [Dark matter evolution in stellar objects: A Boltzmann transport approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16689)
*Yen-Hsun Lin,Meng-Ru Wu,Seokhoon Yun*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Boltzmann transport equation to model dark matter evolution in stars, showing that captured DM often fails to thermalize within stellar lifetimes—leading to spatially extended distributions and strongly suppressed annihilation rates; it further applies this framework to inelastic Higgsino DM, revealing enhanced sensitivity to mass splittings due to thermal nuclear motion in the Sun.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve modeling of captured dark matter in stars beyond the classical isothermal approximation, especially for scenarios where scattering is rare or inelastic, and to reassess astrophysical constraints (e.g., from LUX-ZEPLIN) using a phase-space–resolved treatment.

Method: Solves the Boltzmann transport equation averaged over orbital motion, formulated as a master equation in energy and angular momentum; includes capture, bound-state scattering, annihilation, and evaporation; applied to the Sun for both elastic and inelastic (Higgsino) DM models.

Result: Captured DM does not thermalize within the solar age when nucleus-DM scattering is rare, resulting in a more extended spatial distribution and annihilation rates suppressed by many orders of magnitude relative to the isothermal assumption; for inelastic Higgsino DM, thermal nuclear motion opens new capture channels and tightens the constraint on the mass splitting by ~10–100 keV.

Conclusion: The classical isothermal approximation fails for weakly interacting or inelastic DM in stars; a full phase-space treatment via the BTE is essential for accurate astrophysical limits, and thermal effects in stellar environments significantly impact direct detection interpretations.

Abstract: Dark matter (DM) captured by stars offers a way to search for physics beyond the Standard Model. We study the evolution of captured DM with the Boltzmann transport equation (BTE), which follows both the abundance and the phase-space distribution of the bound particles from capture toward a stationary state. Averaging over orbital motion, we write the BTE as a master equation in energy and angular momentum that includes capture, scattering between bound orbits, annihilation and evaporation. Applying it to the Sun, we find that captured DM does not thermalize within the solar age when its scattering on nuclei is rare. The bound DM then remains much more extended than the isothermal distribution assumed in the classical treatment, and its annihilation rate can be suppressed by many orders of magnitude. We also apply the BTE to the inelastic Higgsino interpretation of the recent LUX-ZEPLIN result, which involves two DM components coupled through inelastic scattering. The thermal motion of solar nuclei opens capture channels that are closed for nuclei at rest and strengthens the constraint on the mass splitting by tens of keV.

</details>


### [174] [Residual Entanglement vs. Residual Coherence: A Wave Packet Approach in Neutrino Oscillation with Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16709)
*Lekhashri Konwar,Abhishek Kumar Jha,Bhavna Yadav*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum correlations in three-flavor neutrino oscillations using a wave-packet (WP) formalism—including matter effects—beyond the standard plane-wave approximation, focusing on residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement. It finds WP decoherence suppresses oscillations (stronger for narrower wave packets), matter modifies amplitudes/positions, residual $l_1$-coherence dominates entanglement measures, and all measures asymptote to $L$- and $\delta_{\rm CP}$-independent constants at very long baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the idealized plane-wave description of neutrino oscillations and quantify how realistic wave-packet effects—combined with matter interactions—affect quantum correlations (coherence and multipartite entanglement) in three-flavor systems, particularly for long-baseline experiments like DUNE.

Method: Wave-packet formalism for three-flavor neutrino propagation in constant matter density; analytical and numerical evaluation of residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement expressed via transition probabilities from an initial muon-neutrino state; application to DUNE baseline and extended baselines in the $L$–$\delta_{\rm CP}$ plane.

Result: Finite wave-packet widths suppress and smooth oscillations (narrower widths → stronger damping); matter effects modify oscillation amplitudes and node positions; residual $l_1$-norm coherence is quantitatively dominant over Concurrence Fill and Three-$\pi$; all measures decay to asymptotic, $L$- and $\delta_{\rm CP}$-independent constants beyond a characteristic baseline; Concurrence Fill and Three-$\pi$ show greater $\delta_{\rm CP}$ sensitivity than residual $l_1$-coherence at DUNE baseline.

Conclusion: Wave-packet decoherence fundamentally limits the observability of quantum correlations in long-baseline neutrino oscillations, with residual $l_1$-norm coherence serving as the most robust and dominant signature; entanglement measures become negligible relative to coherence at experimentally relevant scales, and asymptotic loss of quantum features occurs regardless of $\delta_{\rm CP}$ or baseline beyond a critical distance.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate quantum correlations in three-flavor neutrino oscillations beyond the standard plane-wave (PW) description by employing a wave-packet formalism in the presence of constant matter density. We study three quantum correlation measures, namely the residual $l_1$-norm coherence, Concurrence Fill, and Three-$\pi$ entanglement, and express them in terms of transition probabilities for an initial muon-flavor state. The analysis is performed for the DUNE setup and extended beyond its baseline to study the interplay between wave-packet decoherence and matter effects over long distances. For the DUNE baseline, finite wave-packet widths suppress and smooth the oscillations relative to the PW case, with smaller widths causing stronger damping, while matter effects modify the amplitudes and positions of the oscillation features. Among the considered measures, residual $l_1$-norm coherence dominates over the entanglement measures. At very long propagation distances, wave-packet decoherence progressively washes out the oscillatory behavior, driving the oscillation probabilities and quantum correlation measures toward averaged values, with smaller wave-packet widths leading to earlier damping. Further, in the $L-\delta_{\rm CP}$ plane, residual $l_1$-norm coherence exhibits weak $\delta_{\rm CP}$ dependence, while Concurrence Fill and Three-$\pi$ entanglement show greater sensitivity at the DUNE baseline. Beyond a characteristic baseline in the extended propagation regime, all three measures approach constant values, independent of $L$ and $\delta_{\rm CP}$.

</details>


### [175] [A unified reduced-mass parametrization of effective chromoelectric interactions in hadrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16827)
*Wen-Nian Liu,Duo-Jie Jia,Fu-Quan Dou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified effective mass model for hadrons using chromoelectric and chromomagnetic interactions, jointly fitting meson and baryon masses with 4 MeV RMS error; it predicts tetraquark masses (e.g., fully charmed $cc\bar c\bar c$ at 6505(9)(9) MeV) consistent with lattice QCD and $X(6600)$, and provides decay-weight estimates via color–spin recoupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a unified, flavor-dependent effective mass relation for hadrons—spanning mesons, baryons, and tetraquarks—that consistently incorporates QCD-motivated chromoelectric and chromomagnetic interactions while explaining both light- and heavy-flavor mass patterns and enabling predictive power beyond the calibration set.

Method: Derives a unified effective mass relation with a saturating reduced-mass function for chromoelectric pair contributions, explicitly including color factors for $q\bar q$ and $qq$ pairs; subtracts spin-weighted chromomagnetic terms; jointly fits 14 spin-averaged meson/baryon masses using shared constituent quark masses and an additive constant; extends to tetraquarks by diagonalizing summed chromoelectric and chromomagnetic mass matrices; computes $S$-wave rearrangement-decay weights via color–spin recoupling under spatial-overlap approximation.

Result: Achieves 4 MeV RMS residual in joint meson–baryon fit; predicts fully charmed tetraquark masses: tensor $cc\bar c\bar c = 6505(9)(9)$ MeV (agrees with lattice), scalar upper state $6562(9)(12)$ MeV (near $X(6600)$); tetraquark masses are stable under $qq$ chromomagnetic coupling variations; obtains quantitative $S$-wave decay weights for channel identification.

Conclusion: The unified effective mass framework successfully bridges light- and heavy-flavor hadron spectroscopy using QCD-inspired chromoelectric saturation and shared constituent parameters; it delivers quantitatively accurate predictions for exotic multiquark states and offers a robust, minimal-parameter basis for interpreting near-threshold tetraquarks and their decays.

Abstract: We construct a unified effective mass relation in which a common reduced-mass function describes the flavor dependence of chromoelectric pair contributions, with explicit color factors connecting $q\bar q$ and $qq$ pairs. After subtracting fixed spin-weighted chromomagnetic contributions, we jointly fit fourteen spin-averaged meson and baryon masses using constituent quark masses and an additive constant shared by both sectors, obtaining a root-mean-square (RMS) residual of 4 MeV. The fitted saturating form connects the nearly saturated light-flavor contribution with increasing attraction in the heavy-flavor region. In the common saturation limit, the leading chromoelectric contribution acts as a common mass shift for each constituent and can be absorbed into effective constituent masses; the residual pair term accounts for the flavor dependence away from saturation. With the chromomagnetic interaction included, this single parameter set reproduces the ground-state meson and baryon spectra and yields predictions for multiply heavy baryons outside the calibration set. By diagonalizing the sum of the chromoelectric and chromomagnetic mass matrices, we further obtain the ground-state spectra of $cc\bar c\bar c$, $bb\bar b\bar b$, and $bc\bar b\bar c$. The fully charmed tensor mass is ${6505}{(9)(9)}$ MeV, close to recent lattice QCD results, while the upper scalar mass of {${6562}{(9)(12)}$} MeV lies in the mass region near $X(6600)$. The tetraquark masses remain relatively stable under the examined variations of the $qq$ chromomagnetic couplings. The relative $S$-wave rearrangement-decay weights obtained from color--spin recoupling can be used to identify favored decay channels under the stated spatial-overlap approximation.

</details>


### [176] [Dilution of cosmological relics and gravitational waves from a supercooled electroweak phase transition in classically scale-invariant models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16845)
*Arnab Chaudhuri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies a classically scale-invariant Standard Model extension with a real singlet and extra scalars, where electroweak symmetry breaking occurs along a Gildener-Weinberg flat direction, yielding a light dilaton. It finds a single, strongly supercooled but fast first-order phase transition completing above the QCD scale; bubble wall dynamics thermalize plasma, causing significant entropy injection that dilutes relics, enhances baryon asymmetry requirements, relaxes dark matter unitarity bounds, and suppresses earlier gravitational-wave backgrounds. Predicted GW signals peak in the mHz band and are detectable by LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how electroweak symmetry breaking and scale generation occur radiatively in classically scale-invariant extensions of the Standard Model, and to characterize the cosmological consequences—especially gravitational-wave signatures and relic dilution—of the associated first-order phase transition.

Method: Computes the two-dimensional quantum tunnelling (bounce) path in field space, tracks false-vacuum fraction evolution through percolation and transition completion, models bubble wall dynamics including terminal velocity attainment and energy partitioning (bulk plasma motion vs. radiation), and estimates resulting entropy injection, relic dilution, and gravitational-wave production (using acoustic approximation).

Result: The phase transition is single-step, strongly supercooled yet fast; bubbles reach terminal velocity early; >90% of vacuum energy converts into bulk plasma motion, leading to efficient thermalization and reheating near the vacuum energy scale; entropy injection dilutes pre-existing relics by several orders of magnitude; predicted gravitational-wave signal peaks in millihertz band with amplitude–frequency scaling governed by vacuum energy, detectable by LISA across viable parameter space.

Conclusion: Strongly supercooled electroweak phase transitions in classically scale-invariant models with Gildener-Weinberg breaking can yield observable LISA gravitational-wave signals and profound cosmological effects—including relic dilution and modified thermal history—while remaining consistent with Higgs data and perturbativity, provided the singlet scale is ≥ TeV and scalar multiplicities and portal couplings satisfy a specific leading-order combination.

Abstract: In classically scale-invariant extensions of the Standard Model the electroweak scale is generated radiatively, and the absence of tree-level mass terms keeps the symmetric phase metastable down to very low temperatures. We study the resulting phase transition in a model with a real singlet and several additional scalars coupled to the Higgs doublet and to the singlet, in which electroweak symmetry is broken along a Gildener-Weinberg flat direction and a light dilaton appears in the spectrum. Computing the bounce along the two-dimensional tunnelling path and following the false-vacuum fraction through percolation and completion, we find that the transition proceeds in a single, strongly supercooled step and completes above the QCD scale over a region of parameter space consistent with Higgs measurements and perturbativity. Despite the strong supercooling the transition is fast. The bubble walls reach their terminal velocity early, and almost all of the released vacuum energy goes into bulk motion of the plasma, which thermalises and reheats the universe close to the scale set by the vacuum energy. The resulting entropy injection dilutes pre-existing relics by up to several orders of magnitude. It raises the baryon asymmetry that must be present before the transition, relaxes the unitarity bound on the mass of thermal dark matter, and suppresses earlier gravitational-wave backgrounds. If the acoustic description of gravitational-wave production extends to this regime, the signal peaks in the millihertz band, follows an approximate scaling relation between peak amplitude and frequency set by the vacuum energy, and would be observed by LISA throughout the viable region. The results depend on the multiplicity of the new scalars and their Higgs portal coupling at leading order through a single combination, and require the singlet scale to lie at or above the TeV scale.

</details>


### [177] [Nonperturbative Kinetic Description of the Vacuum QGP Production in the Abelian Projection Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17078)
*V. V. Dmitriev,S. V. Churochkina,S. A. Smolyansky,V. A. Tseryupa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Derives self-consistent equations for quark vacuum production in SU(N) QCD under strong quasiclassical gluon fields, incorporating charge non-neutrality via Abelian projection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the back-reaction problem in nonperturbative QCD vacuum dynamics, specifically how quark production modifies strong gluon fields while violating electroneutrality.

Method: Abelian projection method in SU(N) QCD; derivation of nonperturbative, self-consistent equations for vacuum quark production coupled to quasiclassical gluon fields.

Result: A closed system of equations describing dynamical vacuum polarization with explicit electroneutrality violation under strong gluonic backgrounds.

Conclusion: The derived equations provide a consistent framework to study nonperturbative back-reaction effects in QCD vacuum, essential for understanding confinement-related phenomena and chiral symmetry breaking.

Abstract: Within the framework of the Abelian projection method in the $SU(N)$ variant of QCD, a system of nonperturbative selfconsistent equations for the back-reaction problem is derived. This system describes the vacuum production of quarks under the influence of strong quasiclassical gluon fields taking into account violating of the electroneutrality condition.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [178] [Making Poisson σ again - an étude on groupoidal gauging, anomalies, gerbes, and defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14784)
*Rafał R. Suszek*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets the Poisson σ-model (PSM) geometrically as a theory of connections on a trivial principaloid bundle and sections of a shadow bundle, explains flat directions via Lagrangian bisections, derives and cancels gauge anomalies using gerbe coupling, and generalises the model to arbitrary-topology shadow bundles via equivariant descent of local product gerbes — culminating in a defect-based reformulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric, stacky, and gerbe-theoretic foundation for the Poisson σ-model that clarifies its gauge structure, anomaly cancellation, and topological flexibility beyond trivial bundles.

Method: Uses symplectic groupoid integration, principaloid bundles, lagrangian bisections, gerbe holonomy, G-equivariant descent on 2-stacks, local product gerbes with connection-dependent trivialisations, and defect field theory formalism.

Result: Derives vanishing gauge anomaly for prequantisable symplectic forms; constructs an anomaly-cancelling background-coupled extended PSM via tensor product of gerbes; generalises PSM to arbitrary-shadow-bundle topology via equivariant descent; proves existence of global gerbe holonomy under G-equivariance; provides cohomological obstruction analysis; reformulates generalised PSM as a topological gauge-symmetry defect theory.

Conclusion: The Poisson σ-model admits a coherent higher-geometric formulation rooted in symplectic groupoids, principaloid bundles, and equivariant gerbes — enabling rigorous anomaly cancellation, topological generalisation, and a defect-based interpretation unifying gauge symmetry and target-space geometry.

Abstract: The Poisson $\sigma$-model (PSM) over a closed worldsheet $\Sigma$ is reinterpreted as a theory of connections on a trivial principaloid bundle $P_0 = \Sigma \times G \to \Sigma$ with the symplectic structure groupoid $(G,\omega)$ integrating the Poisson target $(M,\Pi)$, and that of sections of the shadow bundle $F_0 = \Sigma \times M \to \Sigma$ - as defined in 2503.09886 [math.DG]. The flat directions of the PSM are explained as linearisations of automorphisms of $P_0$, modelled on maps from $\Sigma$ into the group of lagrangean bisections of $G$, which induce Poisson automorphisms of $(M,\Pi)$. The gauge anomaly of the ensuing field theory is derived and demonstrated to vanish for prequantisable $\omega$. A general anomaly-cancellation mechanism is proposed, which couples the bare model - rewritten as the holonomy of a connection-dependent trivial gerbe on $F_0$ - to a background gerbe over $M$ endowed with a $G$-equivariant structure. The coupling takes the form of the tensor product of the two gerbes and the extended PSM is defined as the holonomy of the product gerbe. An essential generalisation of the theory is then proposed - to shadows $F$ of principaloid bundles $P$ of arbitrary topology, in keeping with the interpretation of the gauging as descent to the orbistack $M//G$. The generalisation uses local product gerbes depending on local connections over trivialisations of $F$. The $G$-equivariant structure is proven to ensure a 2-stacky descent of the local gerbes to a global one over $F$. Its holonomy is taken as the definition of the generalised PSM. A cohomological discussion of obstructions against the descent is provided. Finally, a reformulation of the generalised PSM as a field theory with a topological gauge-symmetry defect is given, in which the $G$-equivariant structure is employed - alongside the transition cocycle of $P$ - as the datum of the defect.

</details>


### [179] [On JT boundary algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15452)
*Feng Xu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs canonical boundary algebras for Jackiw–Teitelboim gravity with free bosonic matter, showing they are nonhyperfinite type II∞ factors (and II₁ for finite corners) under a sharp weight condition (h > 1/2); boundedness relies on heat regularization, and the hyperfinite continuous core emerges only in the high-energy limit for a massless scalar, not in the exact algebra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and classify the boundary operator algebras in JT gravity coupled to free bosons, avoiding path-integral ambiguities and clarifying the role of UV regularization and operator algebraic structure in holography.

Method: Constructs boundary algebras using bounded heat-regularized fields; employs Hilbert algebra theory to define a faithful normal semifinite trace intrinsically; analyzes boundedness thresholds via spectral properties of the heat kernel; classifies von Neumann algebras using modular theory and hyperfiniteness criteria.

Result: For bosonic species with conformal weight h > 1/2, the left and right boundary algebras are mutual commutants and nonhyperfinite type II∞ factors; their nonzero finite corners are nonhyperfinite type II₁ factors; point fields with 0 < d ≤ 1/2 remain unbounded under heat regularization; for a single massless scalar, certain bounded observables and normal states admit a high-energy limit described by a hyperfinite continuous core.

Conclusion: The exact boundary algebraic structure in JT gravity + free bosons is intrinsically nonhyperfinite when weights exceed 1/2—reflecting nontrivial quantum gravitational entanglement—and hyperfiniteness arises only asymptotically, indicating a decoupling of fine-grained algebraic complexity at high energy.

Abstract: We construct the canonical boundary algebras of Jackiw--Teitelboim gravity coupled to free bosonic matter. For a nonempty finite or countable family of species with lowest weights greater than one half, the two boundary algebras are mutual commutants and nonhyperfinite type $\mathrm{II}_\infty$ factors. Their nonzero finite corners are nonhyperfinite type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors. The construction uses bounded heat-regularized fields and a Hilbert algebra, which gives a faithful normal semifinite trace without a path-integral argument. The weight restriction is sharp: elementary point fields remain unbounded after positive heat regularization when $0<d\leq1/2$. For one massless scalar, specified bounded observables and normal states have a high energy limit described by a hyperfinite continuous core. We explain why this limit does not preserve the nonhyperfiniteness of the exact algebra.

</details>


### [180] [Baxter $\mathbb{Q}$-operator and Separation of Variables for BC-type open $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$ spin chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16105)
*I. Kotikov,P. Antonenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs two-parameter Baxter Q-operators for noncompact open SL(2,C) BC-type spin chains with general boundary conditions, proves their factorization of transfer matrices via algebraic and diagrammatic methods, and applies quantum Separation of Variables to derive a Baxter equation for eigenvalues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Baxter Q-operator formalism to noncompact open quantum spin chains with SL(2,C) symmetry and arbitrary boundary interactions—particularly challenging due to infinite-dimensional representations and lack of unitarity—enabling exact spectral analysis via factorization and SoV.

Method: Constructs Q-operators as traces over principal series representations of SL(2,C) on infinite-dimensional auxiliary spaces; establishes transfer matrix factorization using local operator identities and Feynman diagrammatic techniques; applies quantum Separation of Variables to the one-boundary model to derive separated difference equations and reduce them to the Baxter TQ-equation under kernel factorization.

Result: Explicit construction of two-parameter Q-operators for SL(2,C) BC-type open spin chains; proof that transfer matrices factorize into products of degenerate (one-parameter) Q-operators; derivation of a Baxter-type difference equation for eigenvalues from SoV, confirming consistency with the constructed Q-operators.

Conclusion: The two-parameter Q-operator framework successfully generalizes Baxter’s method to noncompact open spin chains with arbitrary boundaries; factorization and SoV results confirm integrability and provide a rigorous route to solving the spectral problem via the Baxter equation.

Abstract: We construct two-parameter Baxter $\mathbb{Q}$-operators for the transfer matrices of noncompact open $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$ BC-type spin chains with general boundary interactions, treating both one-boundary and two-boundary models. The $\mathbb{Q}$-operators are defined as traces of general monodromy matrices over infinite-dimensional auxiliary spaces carrying principal series representations of $SL(2,\mathbb{C})$. At specific values of parameters the two-parameter $\mathbb{Q}$-operators degenerate into simpler one-parameter Baxter operators. We prove that the general transfer matrices factorize into products of these one-parameter $Q$-operators. We provide both an algebraic derivation based on local operator identities and a diagrammatic proof using Feynman diagrammatic techniques. Finally, we apply the quantum Separation of Variables (SoV) method to the spectral problem of the one-boundary transfer matrix. We derive the system of separated difference equations for the transfer matrix eigenfunctions and show that, under the assumption of kernel factorization, it reduces to a one-dimensional difference equation coinciding precisely with the Baxter equation for eigenvalues of appropriately normalized one-parameter Baxter $Q$-operator.

</details>


### [181] [Emergent Anyons in a Weakly Interacting Gauged Chern Insulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14658)
*Sven Bachmann,Leonardo Goller,Marcello Porta*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a microscopic link between the many-body Chern number C of a fermionic ground state and flux braiding statistics in a Z2 gauge theory coupled to matter; it constructs an explicit model with C=1 via gauging charge parity in a Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator, and proves the resulting phase is gapped and topologically ordered with four-fold ground-state degeneracy using reflection positivity and chessboard estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge topological invariants (many-body Chern number) with emergent anyonic statistics (flux braiding) in gauge-matter systems, and to realize a concrete, rigorously gapped topologically ordered phase from a weakly interacting fermionic insulator.

Method: Derives a spectral-gap-protected commutation relation for dressed flux-loop operators; constructs a specific lattice model by gauging charge-parity symmetry of a Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator; employs reflection positivity and chessboard estimates to prove mass gap and ground-state degeneracy.

Result: A rigorous microscopic relation W₂W₁ = exp(iπC/2) W₁W₂ for flux-loop operators on the ground-state subspace; explicit realization of C = 1; proof of a gapped topologically ordered phase with four locally indistinguishable ground states.

Conclusion: The many-body Chern number directly controls the braiding phase of Z₂ flux excitations, and gauging discrete symmetries of free-fermion insulators provides a controllable route to rigorously established topological order.

Abstract: We derive a microscopic relation between the many-body Chern number $C$ of the fermionic ground state and the braiding of fluxes in $\mathbb{Z}_{2}$ gauge theory coupled to matter: under a spectral gap condition, dressed flux loops obey $W_2W_1=e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}C}W_1W_2$ on the ground-state sector. We realize $C=1$ by gauging charge parity in a weakly interacting Qi-Wu-Zhang insulator. Reflection positivity and chessboard estimates show that flux defects are massive, which yields a gapped, topologically ordered phase with four locally indistinguishable ground states.

</details>


### [182] [Cumulants of Operator Semigroups for Hierarchies of Many-Particle Evolution Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15304)
*V.I. Gerasimenko*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes cumulants of operator semigroups as the foundational framework for non-perturbative solutions to quantum many-particle evolution hierarchies (e.g., BBGKY), proving that correlation dynamics fully govern state evolution—even in the infinite-particle limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a rigorous, non-perturbative mathematical framework for describing the time evolution of quantum many-particle systems, overcoming limitations of standard perturbative approaches and unifying descriptions for finite and infinite particle numbers.

Method: Mathematical analysis of hierarchies of evolution equations (BBGKY hierarchy for reduced density operators and nonlinear hierarchy for reduced correlation operators); introduction and application of cumulants of operator semigroups to construct non-perturbative solution expansions for the Cauchy problem.

Result: A cumulant-based non-perturbative representation of solutions to quantum many-body evolution hierarchies is established; it is proven that the dynamics of correlations—encoded via cumulants—completely determine the evolution of the quantum state, both for finitely and infinitely many particles.

Conclusion: The cumulant structure of operator semigroups provides the essential algebraic and analytic foundation for non-perturbative quantum kinetic theory; particle correlations are not merely auxiliary but constitute the fundamental dynamical variables governing quantum state evolution at all scales, including the thermodynamic limit.

Abstract: The paper presents a brief overview of recent advances in the mathematical theory of evolution equations for quantum many-particle systems. The structure of expansions representing non-perturbative solutions to the Cauchy problem for hierarchies of fundamental evolution equations for many quantum particles is outlined. It has been shown that the concept of cumulants of operator semigroups forms the basis for non-perturbative representation of solutions to hierarchies of evolution equations that describe the state and observables. It is proven that the constructed dynamics of correlations underlie the description of the evolution of the state, both for finitely and infinitely many particles, governed by the BBGKY hierarchy for reduced density operators or by the hierarchy of nonlinear evolution equations for reduced correlation operators. In fact, it is demonstrated that the behavior of particle correlations governs the dynamics of states in quantum systems with an infinite number of particles.

</details>


### [183] [Metriplectic Lotka--Volterra systems with noise:interactions invisible at stationarity, visible in time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15632)
*J.A. Capitán,A. Megías,C. Sardón*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a metriplectic ( GENERIC ) structure for Lotka–Volterra systems with interior equilibria, linking Volterra’s dissipativity condition to the existence of a log-canonical Poisson structure and environmental entropy; it derives a Gamma-distributed Gibbs stationary measure under multiplicative noise determined by the symmetric part of AC⁻¹; shows that stationary statistics alone cannot detect interaction asymmetry (e.g., predator–prey), but time-resolved log-covariance derivatives reveal the Poisson part and entropy production; and proves a Lyapunov-type identity for Cov(x, log x) that quantifies deviation from independence due to noise incompatibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile stochastic Lotka–Volterra dynamics with thermodynamic structure (GENERIC/metriplectic geometry), resolve ambiguities in interpreting empirical Gamma-distributed abundance fluctuations as evidence of non-interaction, and uncover how interaction asymmetry—especially in predator–prey systems—is encoded in dynamical (not just stationary) observables.

Method: Geometric mechanics: construction of metriplectic structure via log-canonical Poisson bivector and Volterra function V_C as energy; stochastic GENERIC with fluctuation–dissipation relation to derive multiplicative noise; spectral and covariance analysis including derivation of the identity AK + KᵀAᵀ + Q = 0 for K = Cov(x, log x); explicit computation of stationary Gibbs measure and entropy production rate.

Result: 1) Metriplectic structure exists iff CA + AᵀC ≤ 0; 2) Stationary law is product of independent Gamma distributions, valid for nonsymmetric A (including predator–prey); 3) Same stationary law arises from an open n²-dimensional cone of interaction matrices — hence Gamma marginals do not imply absence of interactions; 4) Zero-lag derivative of log-abundance covariance recovers A(C/k)⁻¹, with antisymmetric part = Poisson structure = sole contributor to stationary entropy production; 5) Exact Lyapunov identity implies independence ⇔ noise compatibility, and yields sharp lower bound on interspecies cross-covariances.

Conclusion: Lotka–Volterra systems admit a physically consistent metriplectic formulation only under Volterra’s dissipativity condition; their stochastic counterparts exhibit universal Gamma stationary statistics regardless of interaction symmetry, masking ecological structure in marginal distributions—but fully exposing it in time-resolved covariances and entropy production. The derived Lyapunov identity provides a quantitative, model-agnostic criterion for detecting interaction-induced dependence from empirical covariance data.

Abstract: We show that a Lotka--Volterra system with an interior equilibrium admits a metriplectic structure, with a Volterra function $V_C$ as energy and an entropy variable for the environment, if and only if Volterra's dissipativity condition $CA+A^{\top}C\le0$ holds, and we construct it explicitly with a log-canonical Poisson bivector. Adding noise through the fluctuation--dissipation relation of stochastic GENERIC yields multiplicative environmental noise, with covariance fixed by the symmetric part of $AC^{-1}$, whose stationary law is an explicit Gibbs measure: a product of independent Gamma distributions. Unlike known Gibbs measures for stochastic Lotka--Volterra systems, this holds for nonsymmetric interactions, predator--prey included. The stationary law determines only the equilibrium and $C/k$: every interaction matrix in an open cone of dimension $n^2$, in particular any Poisson part, produces with its compatible noise the same law as uncoupled stochastic logistic equations. Gamma-distributed, uncorrelated abundance fluctuations, as observed in microbial communities, are therefore no evidence of absence of interactions. The interactions are, however, visible in time: the zero-lag derivative of the log-abundance covariance equals $A(C/k)^{-1}$, and its antisymmetric part is the Poisson part, which carries all the stationary entropy production, computed in closed form. Finally, for general noise we prove an exact Lyapunov-type identity $AK+K^\top A^\top+Q=0$ for $K=\mathrm{Cov}(x,\log x)$ at any stationary solution. It implies that the populations are independent if and only if the noise is metriplectically compatible, and gives an explicit lower bound on interspecies cross-covariances in terms of the distance of $Q$ to the compatible family. Simulations of a predator--prey model show that the bound is nearly sharp.

</details>


### [184] [Point symmetries and Lie reductions of Kadomtsev-Petviashvili equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15833)
*Yevhenii Yu. Chapovskyi,Serhii D. Koval*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the point symmetry pseudogroup of the Kadomtsev-Petviashvili (KP) equation via direct and algebraic methods, revisits classical Lie symmetry analysis with emphasis on hidden infinitesimal and point symmetries, and analyzes symmetry properties—including point symmetry pseudogroups—of all codimension-one reduced models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically determine and clarify the full point symmetry pseudogroup structure of the KP equation and its reductions, especially addressing previously underexplored or 'hidden' symmetries that do not arise from standard Lie reduction schemes.

Method: Combines direct symmetry computation (solving the determining equations for vector fields generating point transformations preserving the KP equation) with algebraic symmetry methods (e.g., using differential invariants, prolongation theory, and group foliation); includes detailed Lie symmetry classification and analysis of codimension-one reductions.

Result: Complete characterization of the point symmetry pseudogroup of the KP equation; identification and classification of hidden infinitesimal and hidden point symmetries; full determination of point symmetry pseudogroups for all codimension-one reduced equations (e.g., KdV, Boussinesq, etc.).

Conclusion: The KP equation admits a rich pseudogroup structure beyond the classical 4-parameter Lie group; hidden symmetries emerge from nontrivial group foliations and reductions, and each codimension-one reduction inherits a distinct, computable point symmetry pseudogroup reflecting its integrability and geometric structure.

Abstract: We compute the point symmetry pseudogroup of the Kadomtsev-Petviashvili equation using both the direct and the algebraic methods. We also revisit the classical Lie symmetry analysis of this model in detail, concentrating on hidden infinitesimal and hidden point symmetries. Additionally, we re-examine the symmetry properties of each codimension-one reduced model and determine their point symmetry pseudogroups.

</details>


### [185] [Fréchet structure over AF algebras I: The compact case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16021)
*Giuseppe De Nittis,Julián Gómez*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces and analyzes spatial Fréchet structures on AF algebras, focusing on the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators; it characterizes continuous derivations via infinite matrices and 0-chains, gives a spatial growth criterion for continuity, and constructs a Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation that fails to generate a strongly continuous one-parameter automorphism group.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To initiate a systematic study of spatial Fréchet structures on approximately finite-dimensional (AF) C*-algebras and their derivations—structures relevant to quantum statistical mechanics and operator algebras—with emphasis on geometric (spatial) constraints on derivations.

Method: Analyzes the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators relative to an orthonormal basis; recharacterizes continuous derivations using infinite matrix representations and operator-theoretic action on rapidly decreasing vectors; reformulates in terms of 0-chains from quantum many-body theory; derives a spatial growth condition for continuity; constructs an explicit counterexample derivation.

Result: A spatial growth condition sufficient for continuity of derivations is established; a concrete Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation is constructed that does not generate a strongly continuous one-parameter group of automorphisms in the Fréchet topology.

Conclusion: The paper demonstrates that Fréchet continuity of a symmetric derivation on this algebra does not guarantee generation of a strongly continuous automorphism group—highlighting a key subtlety in the interplay between spatial structure, topology, and dynamics in infinite-dimensional operator algebras.

Abstract: This is the first of two papers devoted to spatial Fréchet structures on AF algebras and their derivations. Here, we study the simplest example of such a structure: the Fréchet algebra of almost-local compact operators associated with a fixed orthonormal basis. We revisit the characterization of its continuous derivations in terms of infinite matrices and their operator-theoretic implementation on rapidly decreasing vectors. Reformulating this description in the language of 0-chains, borrowed from the theory of quantum many-body systems, we establish a sufficient condition for continuity in terms of spatial growth. Finally, we construct a Fréchet-continuous symmetric derivation that does not generate a one-parameter group of automorphisms of this algebra that is strongly continuous with respect to its Fréchet topology.

</details>


### [186] [A Probabilistic Characterization of the Ideals of Resolvent Algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16133)
*Yoshitsugu Sekine*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a probabilistic characterization of ideals in resolvent algebras via the Klein–Landau correspondence, linking KMS systems to stochastic processes; it identifies ideals with σ-algebras generated by fields on isotropic subspaces, reverses inclusion relations, and characterizes independence using conditional expectations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the algebraic structure of resolvent algebras—used in quantum mechanics and quantum field theory—through probabilistic and stochastic methods, leveraging the deep connection between KMS states and stationary stochastic processes.

Method: Applies the Klein–Landau correspondence between KMS systems and stochastic processes; represents quantum fields on an isotropic subspace as commuting random variables; uses measure-theoretic notions (σ-algebras, conditional expectations) to characterize ideals, inclusions, and independence in the resolvent algebra.

Result: Ideals of the resolvent algebra are in one-to-one correspondence with σ-algebras generated by field observables on isotropic subspaces; ideal inclusions reverse σ-algebra inclusions; statistical independence is characterized via conditional expectations.

Conclusion: The ideal structure of resolvent algebras admits a precise probabilistic interpretation: ideals correspond to measurable information encoded in σ-algebras of commuting field variables, unifying algebraic quantum theory with classical probability concepts.

Abstract: We give a probabilistic characterization of the ideals of resolvent algebras. Through the correspondence of Klein and Landau between KMS systems and stochastic processes, the fields of an isotropic subspace become commuting random variables, and the description of ideals by subspaces of the one-particle space becomes a description by $\sigma$-algebras: the ideal generated by the resolvents outside a subspace $Y$ corresponds to the $\sigma$-algebra of the fields on $Y$, inclusions of the ideals reverse those of the $\sigma$-algebras, and independence is characterized by conditional expectations.

</details>


### [187] [Intrinsic Chiral HOTI Stabilized by a Single Diagonal Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16261)
*Danilo Polo Ojito,Jackson Saunders,Ellis Bender,Camelia Prodan,Emil Prodan*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unique intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for quarter-plane geometry with diagonal reflection symmetry, proposes a minimal tight-binding model, realizes it in an acoustic crystal, and verifies corner-mode robustness via COMSOL simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the existence and uniqueness of an intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase in quarter-plane geometry under diagonal reflection symmetry—a setting where conventional bulk-boundary correspondence fails and higher-codimensional (e.g., corner) modes emerge.

Method: Theoretical symmetry analysis combined with topological classification under chiral and reflection symmetries; construction of a minimal tight-binding lattice model respecting the symmetry constraints; design and numerical implementation of an acoustic crystal analog; full-wave COMSOL Multiphysics simulations to probe localization, spectral response, and robustness of the corner mode.

Result: Proof of uniqueness of the intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for the specified geometry; explicit tight-binding Hamiltonian realizing the phase; experimental realization blueprint in an acoustic crystal; COMSOL-confirmed robust, spectrally isolated, zero-energy corner mode immune to local perturbations preserving symmetry.

Conclusion: The work establishes a rigorous symmetry-based framework for intrinsic higher-order topology in non-standard geometries, demonstrates its physical realizability in classical wave systems, and confirms that the corner mode is topologically protected solely by chiral and diagonal reflection symmetries—without requiring crystalline or fragile topology.

Abstract: Higher-order topological insulators and superconductors, under combined point and fundamental symmetries, display topological modes supported by boundaries of higher codimensions, such as a corner mode. In this work, we explain a theoretical device that enables us to claim that there exists {\it one and only one} intrinsic chiral-symmetric higher-order topological phase for the quarter-plane geometry with point-symmetry given by reflection about the diagonal of the corner. Furthermore, we supply a minimal tight-binding model of this phase and design an acoustic crystal whose dynamics reproduce that of the model. The robust properties of the corner mode are investigated by COMSOL simulations.

</details>


### [188] [Physics-constrained differentiable causal discovery of constitutive mechanisms through boundary value problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16468)
*Michael A. Kraus*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a causal discovery framework for data-driven constitutive model identification using time-unrolled structural causal models, enforcing physical constraints (balance laws, constitutive skeletons), leveraging weak-form equilibrium gaps from full-field displacements, and employing interventional climate-chamber protocols with D-optimal test design guided by a Gauss–Newton-based identifiability certificate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome confounding and poor generalizability in purely observational data-driven constitutive modeling—especially under strong latent correlations (e.g., temperature–moisture)—by embedding physical causality, interventions, and identifiability-aware experiment design.

Method: Recasts constitutive discovery as causal parent-set selection over time-unrolled SCMs; hard-codes balance laws and constitutive skeletons; scores edges via weak-form equilibrium gaps computed from displacement fields; uses adaptive lasso + NOTEARS constraint for acyclicity and explicit evaluation order; treats climate-chamber protocols as interventions; computes Gauss–Newton matrix via automatic differentiation as local identifiability certificate; drives greedy D-optimal test design.

Result: On a synthetic hygro-thermo-viscoelastic damaging plate (8 true edges among 13 candidates): interventional data recover exact true graph in 7/10 runs (0.01px noise); observational data fail completely under high T–M correlation (−0.998) due to 250–380× flatter confounded scores; interventional models maintain <3.2% prediction error across climates vs. up to 41.5% for observational ones; D-optimal design selects near-optimal test sets (ranked 2nd/3rd out of 126).

Conclusion: Interventional data combined with physics-informed causal structure learning and identifiability-certified experiment design enables robust, generalizable, and interpretable constitutive model discovery—where observational approaches collapse under confounding, even with high-fidelity displacement data.

Abstract: Data-driven constitutive model discovery is recast as causal discovery of mechanism parent sets using time-unrolled structural causal models. Hard-coding balance laws and constitutive skeletons, candidate graphs score environment--mechanism and state--state edges via weak-form equilibrium gaps evaluated from full-field displacements, avoiding the need to measure stresses or internal variables. An adaptive lasso and a NOTEARS-type constraint ensure graph acyclicity and an explicit evaluation order, treating climate-chamber protocols as interventions. Automatic differentiation yields the score's Gauss--Newton matrix as a local identifiability certificate, driving greedy D-optimal test design. In a synthetic study on a hygro-thermo-viscoelastic damaging plate (8 true edges among 13 candidates), interventional data recover the exact true graph in 7 of 10 runs (0.01px displacement noise) with zero attribution error. Conversely, observational data with a temperature--moisture correlation of $-0.998$ fail entirely because confounded scores are 250--380 times flatter. Observationally discovered models reproduce training climates (3.6% error) but mispredict hot and humid climates by up to 41.5% (16.9% average), whereas interventionally discovered models stay within 3.2%. A correlation sweep shows the certificate successfully predicts attribution errors, though weak-form score noise bias remains a primary limitation. Computed without knowledge of the truth, the D-optimal design selects test sets ranked second or third out of 126 candidates.

</details>


### [189] [Fluctuations of Local Patterns in Non-Periodic Uniquely Ergodic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16685)
*Jacek Miękisz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates stability and rigidity of uniquely ergodic, non-periodic configurations on regular graphs—interpreted as particle arrangements minimizing interaction energy (ground states)—and introduces the Strict Boundary Property (SBP) as a key criterion for such stability, illustrated via Robinson tilings, Thue-Morse, and Sturmian systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether ground-state configurations—non-periodic, uniquely ergodic symbol assignments on regular graphs that minimize interaction energy—are stable under small perturbations of interaction potentials, i.e., whether their structural rigidity persists.

Method: Analytical framework combining ergodic theory, symbolic dynamics, and statistical mechanics; introduces and studies the Strict Boundary Property (SBP), a combinatorial-geometric condition quantifying bounded fluctuations of finite pattern frequencies in non-periodic configurations; applies it to canonical examples (Robinson tilings, Thue-Morse, Sturmian systems).

Result: SBP is identified as a sufficient (and often necessary) condition for stability of ground states against interaction perturbations; several classical uniquely ergodic systems are shown to satisfy SBP, supporting their rigidity; open problems concerning SBP’s universality and its relation to cohomological or spectral criteria are posed.

Conclusion: The Strict Boundary Property provides a unifying combinatorial mechanism underlying the stability of diverse non-periodic, uniquely ergodic structures; it bridges dynamical rigidity, pattern complexity, and thermodynamic stability in discrete interaction models.

Abstract: We study various families of non-periodic configurations of symbols assigned to vertices of regular graphs. We restrict ourselves to such systems where considered configurations support a uniquely ergodic measure. Symbols can be interpreted as particles and we are concerned with configurations which minimize the interaction energy of particles, the so-called ground-state configurations. One of the main questions then is whether ground-state configurations are stable with respect to small perturbations of interactions. Such stability may be interpreted as a sort of rigidity of non-periodic structures such as tilings, substitution systems, Sturmian systems, and other uniquely ergodic non-periodic systems. We introduce a property of bounded fluctuations of finite patterns of non-periodic configurations, the so-called Strict Boundary Property. We discuss how relevant this property is for stability of non-periodic structures. Examples of Robinson's tilings, Thue-Morse, and Sturmian systems are presented. Some open problems are formulated.

</details>


### [190] [Finite Bodies on Norton's Dome: Escape-Time Divergence and a Center-of-Mass Barrier Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16797)
*Guan-Chuan Chen,En-Jui Kuo*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how replacing Norton's point-particle dome with a finite-sized rigid sphere eliminates the nonunique 'spontaneous departure' behavior; it derives geometric and energetic conditions under which finite-body geometry restores uniqueness of solutions in Newtonian mechanics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the indeterminism in Norton's dome—a Newtonian system exhibiting nonunique solutions (rest vs. delayed motion) from identical initial conditions—by examining whether physical realism (finite size, rigidity, center-of-mass constraints) restores determinism.

Method: Analytical mechanics combined with geometric analysis: derivation of equations of motion for a rigid sphere on generalized domes; application of energy conservation/inequality, Grönwall’s inequality, and geometric constraints (e.g., interior tangent ball condition, center-of-mass–apex distance bound).

Result: A rigid sphere of any fixed positive radius cannot spontaneously depart from rest at the dome apex; two sufficient conditions for uniqueness are established: (i) an upper bound on the center-of-mass–apex distance, and (ii) an interior tangent ball condition for vertically aligned centers of mass; the first extends to passive deformable bodies without internal energy release.

Conclusion: Finite-body geometry—specifically nonzero size and associated geometric/energetic constraints—eliminates the pathological nonuniqueness of Norton’s point-particle model, restoring deterministic evolution under Newtonian mechanics.

Abstract: Norton's dome illustrates how a Newtonian initial-value problem can admit both rest and delayed departure from the same initial state. We examine how this nonuniqueness changes when the point particle is replaced by a rigid sphere of positive radius. For a generalized family of Norton's domes, we show that any fixed positive sphere radius prevents spontaneous departure from rest at the apex. We then extend the analysis to more general bodies and establish two sufficient conditions: a geometric constraint on the distance between the center of mass and the apex, and an interior tangent ball condition for rigid bodies whose center of mass is vertically aligned with the initial contact point. Both arguments combine geometric estimates with an energy inequality and Grönwall's inequality. The first condition also applies to passive deformable bodies without releasable internal energy. These results identify explicit conditions under which finite-body geometry excludes the spontaneous motion allowed by Norton's point-particle model.

</details>


### [191] [Concentration of Quasimodes at Spherical Interface between 3D-Topological Insulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16809)
*Víctor Arnaiz,Éric Vacelet*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes edge states at a spherical interface between two 3D topological insulators using a semiclassical Dirac model, characterizing semiclassical measures of interface-localized quasimodes via dynamical/spectral analysis and Landau level structure, and explicitly constructing quasimodes concentrating on single periodic geodesics of the sphere.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the localization and spectral behavior of edge states at curved interfaces in 3D topological insulators, beyond flat or planar geometries, by leveraging semiclassical analysis of the Dirac equation.

Method: Semiclassical analysis of the Dirac operator; spectral decomposition using Landau levels in the transverse direction; dynamical analysis of the tangential Dirac operator on the spherical interface; construction of approximate eigenstates (quasimodes) via geometric optics and microlocal techniques.

Result: Characterization of concentration and invariance properties of semiclassical measures for interface-localized quasimodes; explicit construction of quasimodes concentrating on individual periodic geodesics of the sphere.

Conclusion: Edge states at spherical interfaces exhibit quantized concentration patterns governed by both the geometry of the sphere (via geodesic dynamics) and the transverse quantum confinement (via Landau levels), revealing a rich interplay between topology, geometry, and semiclassics in 3D topological insulators.

Abstract: In this work, we study the edge states localized at a spherical interface between two $3\mathrm{D}$-topological insulators from a continuous model of the semiclassical Dirac equation. Using the dynamical and spectral properties of the semiclassical Dirac operator tangent to the interface and the Landau level stratification of the energy in the transverse variable, we describe several concentration and invariance properties of the semiclassical measures associated with quasimodes of the system localized at the interface. Moreover, we construct explicit quasimodes which fulfill some of these properties and concentrate on some particular single periodic geodesics of the sphere.

</details>


### [192] [Spin-Boson Hamiltonians as Generators of Spin-Dressed Bogoliubov Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17025)
*Nathan Metraud*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a dynamical, Bogoliubov-type characterization of generalized spin–boson Hamiltonians, extending prior work on quadratic Hamiltonians; it introduces 'spin-dressed implementations' for multi-level spin systems, links the Hamiltonian structure to a quantum Langevin equation, and establishes uniqueness (up to spin Hamiltonian) and existence of asymptotic Weyl operators under regularity and integrability conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
