Motivation: To generalize the dynamical Bogoliubov framework—previously established for quadratic (bosonic) Hamiltonians—to spin–boson systems, especially beyond single-spin-level cases where standard Bogoliubov groups fail; to unify renormalized and ultraviolet-singular models under a coherent dynamical definition.

Method: Develops 'spin-dressed implementations': unitary evolutions where the bosonic field evolves freely up to bounded, spin-dependent, time-vanishing displacements; characterizes generators via coupling maps and one-particle Hamiltonian Υ; uses functional analysis on Fock space, domain conditions (e.g., form factors in D(Υ⁻¹ᐟ²)), and asymptotic operator theory (Weyl operators).

Result: Generalized spin–boson Hamiltonians are precisely spin-dressed implementations at the finest regularity scale with form factors in D(Υ⁻¹ᐟ²), unique up to the spin Hamiltonian; at coarser scales, renormalized two-level models with UV-singular form factors qualify; integrability of the coupling map yields asymptotic Weyl operators on a dense test-function subspace.

Conclusion: A consistent dynamical definition—via spin-dressed Bogoliubov-type flows—unifies diverse spin–boson models, clarifies the role of regularity and coupling structure, and recovers key quantum stochastic features (e.g., quantum Langevin equation, asymptotic Weyl operators) directly from the generator’s properties.

Abstract: Following Bach, Lieb and Solovej, quadratic Hamiltonians can be defined as generators of unitary groups implementing one-parameter groups of Bogoliubov transformations. We develop an analogous dynamical definition of generalized spin--boson Hamiltonians. For a single spin level, we review results of Guichardet and Dereziński: the van Hove Hamiltonians, including the renormalized ones, are exactly the generators of affine Bogoliubov dynamics that do not mix creation and annihilation operators. For several spin levels, there is no analogue of this group, and we introduce spin-dressed implementations, that is to say the free flow of the fields is implemented up to bounded operator-valued displacements, which vanish at a rate measured by a so-called regularity scale and have a spin observable, the coupling map, as derivative at time zero. The one-particle Hamiltonian $\Upsilon$ and the coupling map are determined by the Hamiltonian, and the field obeys a quantum Langevin equation. Spin-dressed implementations in Fock space, at the finest scale and for form factors in $D(\Upsilon^{-1/2})$, are exactly the generalized spin--boson Hamiltonians, unique up to the spin Hamiltonian. At the roughest scale, the renormalized spin--boson Hamiltonian of a two-level system with ultraviolet-singular form factor and arbitrary tunnelling is a spin-dressed implementation, and the definition alone yields asymptotic Weyl operators on the test functions along which the coupling map is integrable, a dense set under standard assumptions.

</details>


### [193] [Generalized ladder operators and construction of families of superintegrable systems with Painlevé VI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17117)
*Ian Marquette,Anthony J. L. Parr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs generalized ladder operators for Schrödinger operators with quadratic spectral dependence, applies them to a Painlevé VI angular Hamiltonian, and derives higher-order integrals and deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras for 2D superintegrable systems—including new TTW- and PW-like models with Painlevé VI potentials. It classifies spectral structures, distinguishes formal vs. physical representations, and identifies rational Darboux-deformed Pöschl–Teller and state-adding exceptional sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of superintegrable systems by incorporating Painlevé transcendents—specifically Painlevé VI—into angular components of quantum Hamiltonians, and to systematically generate higher-order symmetries and polynomial algebras beyond classical (e.g., Lie-algebraic) frameworks.

Method: Constructs generalized ladder operators satisfying a nonlinear commutation relation with a square-root number operator N = √M; combines angular shifts (from Painlevé VI Hamiltonian) with radial shift operators (oscillator, Coulomb, free Bessel); derives higher-order integrals and deformed Racah algebras; analyzes special solutions via Gromak–Laine–Shimomura classification; computes Casimir and energy spectra using deformed-oscillator realizations; distinguishes self-adjoint domains and spectral classes.

Result: New families of 2D superintegrable Hamiltonians with Painlevé VI angular potentials and arbitrarily high-order integrals; explicit deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras for TTW- and PW-like models; classification of spectral sectors (regular rational, state-adding, singular barrier); explicit Casimir for oscillator extension; identification of exceptional-Jacobi isospectral sectors and isolated angular levels.

Conclusion: The framework unifies and generalizes known superintegrable models with third- and fourth-order integrals, reveals deep algebraic structure tied to Painlevé VI, and provides a systematic route to construct and classify quantum systems with transcendental potentials and nonlinear symmetry algebras.

Abstract: We study quantum Hamiltonians whose separated angular component is expressed through a generic Painlevé VI transcendent. We construct generalized ladder operators for one-dimensional Schrödinger operators with a quadratic spectral variable. For the Painlevé VI angular Hamiltonian these operators satisfy $[N,J_\pm]=\pm2kJ_\pm$, where $N=\sqrt M$, and close through an eighth-degree norm polynomial. Combining the angular shifts with the radial shift operators of the oscillator, Coulomb and free Bessel systems yields higher-order integrals and deformed Racah-type polynomial algebras for the two-dimensional superintegrable models. This construction provides new families of Tremblay-Turbiner-Winternitz (TTW)- and Post-Winternitz (PW)-like Hamiltonians with integrals of arbitrarily high order and Painlevé VI in the potential. This framework also recovers models from the classification of third- and fourth-order integrals as particular cases. For a representative oscillator extension we compute the Casimir explicitly and recover the finite-dimensional energy spectrum from a deformed-oscillator realization. We then analyze the special-solution sector using the explicit families collected by Gromak, Laine and Shimomura. The resulting potentials are organized into a small number of spectral classes, and the distinction between formal zeros of the ladder norm and physical self-adjoint representations is made explicit. The regular nonclassical rational examples are rational Darboux deformations of the trigonometric Pöschl--Teller system and lead to strictly isospectral exceptional-Jacobi sectors or to state-adding representations with an isolated angular level. A singular branch produces an interior inverse-square barrier and a split-domain problem. For one regular exceptional sector and two state-adding sectors we display the polynomial algebras.

</details>


### [194] [Existence of a fluid phase for the two-dimensional one-component plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17174)
*Jeanne Boursier,Thomas Leblé*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves exponential decay of correlations and uniqueness of the Gibbs state for the 2D one-component plasma at inverse temperature β ≤ 2, establishing part of the predicted fluid phase; it constructs an infinite-volume limit generalizing the Ginibre process (Gin_β) and verifies the Debye-Hückel approximation for the truncated two-point function at the Debye length scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish the existence and properties of the fluid phase in the two-dimensional one-component plasma—a fundamental model in statistical mechanics and plasma physics—particularly verifying long-standing physical predictions such as exponential correlation decay, Gibbs state uniqueness, and validity of the Debye-Hückel approximation.

Method: Rigorous probabilistic and statistical mechanical analysis: construction and control of infinite-volume Gibbs states via point process theory, fluctuation bounds, cluster expansion or renormalization group techniques (implied by 'fluctuations controlled'), and asymptotic analysis of correlation functions at the Debye length scale.

Result: Exponential decay of correlations and uniqueness of the Gibbs state for β ≤ 2; existence of an infinite-volume limit generalizing the Ginibre process (Gin_β); first-order agreement of the truncated two-point function with the Debye-Hückel approximation at the Debye length scale.

Conclusion: The fluid phase of the 2D one-component plasma is rigorously confirmed for β ≤ 2, bridging statistical mechanics theory with physical approximations and extending known integrable structures (e.g., Ginibre) to a broader temperature regime.

Abstract: The two-dimensional one-component plasma is a system of point charges that repel each other logarithmically, in a uniform neutralizing background. When the inverse temperature $\beta$ is at most $2$, we prove that its correlations decay exponentially, and that its Gibbs state is unique among point processes whose fluctuations are controlled. This constitutes part of the fluid phase predicted by physicists. As a consequence, we obtain the existence of an infinite-volume limit, extending the Ginibre point process, which we call $\mathrm{Gin}_\beta$. We show that its truncated two-point function, at the scale of the Debye length, is given to first order in $\beta$ by the Debye-Hückel approximation.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [195] [Monopole crystal and effective theory for a monopole-enhanced 3D U(1) gauge theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15922)
*Alessandro Mariani*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies a modified U(1) lattice gauge theory with monopole enhancement, revealing an antiferromagnetic Néel-ordered monopole ground state at strong enhancement; it derives an effective sine-Gordon model, predicts first-order and Ising-class phase transitions analytically, and verifies key predictions numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To deepen understanding of monopole dynamics in U(1) lattice gauge theory beyond the dilute-monopole approximation, especially when monopole enhancement drives dense, ordered configurations.

Method: Combined analytical techniques—including Polyakov-type effective action derivation, renormalization-group reasoning, and symmetry-based classification of phase transitions—with numerical lattice simulations to map the phase diagram.

Result: Discovery of a Néel-ordered monopole ground state at large monopole enhancement; derivation of an effective sine-Gordon model capturing confinement physics; analytical prediction of a first-order transition (monopole charge dominance change) and an Ising-universality transition (gauge coupling variation); numerical confirmation of these transitions and qualitative agreement with effective theory.

Conclusion: Monopole enhancement fundamentally alters the vacuum structure of U(1) lattice gauge theory, inducing long-range magnetic order and new confinement mechanisms, while retaining universal features describable by low-energy effective models—bridging topological excitations, spontaneous symmetry breaking, and critical phenomena.

Abstract: Recently, a version of U(1) lattice gauge theory on the cubic lattice with a monopole enhancement term was considered in the literature. In this work, we further our understanding of this theory with a mixture of analytical and numerical methods. We show that for sufficiently large monopole enhancement, the monopoles are no longer dilute and they arrange themselves into an antiferromagnetic, Néel-ordered ground state. Nonetheless, a variant of Polyakov's argument for the dilute excitations over the dense ground state leads to an effective sine-Gordon model which is qualitatively similar to that of the standard theory. We also predict analytically the presence of first-order transitions as the monopole chemical potential is varied at fixed gauge coupling, corresponding to a change in the dominant monopole charge. Similarly, we predict a transition in the Ising universality class as the gauge coupling is varied. We then use numerical methods to study the phase diagram of the theory and check some of our predictions.

</details>


### [196] [Critical behavior of the bosonized Schwinger model with uniform matrix product states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16092)
*Takuya Okuda*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the critical mass of the continuum Schwinger model at θ=π using a bosonized Hamiltonian lattice formulation and VUMPS-based infinite MPS simulations, achieving high-precision agreement with staggered-fermion results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the critical mass (m/g)_c of the Schwinger model at θ=π — a nontrivial benchmark for non-perturbative quantum field theory methods — using a dual bosonic formulation that avoids fermionic sign problems and enables exact charge-conjugation symmetry preservation.

Method: Hamiltonian lattice discretization of the bosonized (non-compact scalar) Schwinger model; variational uniform matrix product state (VUMPS) algorithm to compute the infinite-chain ground state; oscillator basis optimized via free-limit one-site reduced density matrix diagonalization; Symanzik effective theory–constrained continuum extrapolation of lattice data.

Result: Critical mass (m/g)_c = 0.333 555(5), obtained with controlled truncation and systematic error estimation via Symanzik improvement; consistent with recent staggered-fermion Monte Carlo results at comparable precision (~1.5×10⁻⁶ relative uncertainty).

Conclusion: The bosonized Hamiltonian MPO-MPS approach with symmetry-preserving VUMPS and Symanzik-guided extrapolation provides a robust, high-precision method for critical parameter determination in 1+1D gauge theories, validating the dual description and establishing a benchmark for tensor-network studies of topological θ-vacua.

Abstract: Quantum electrodynamics in two spacetime dimensions, also known as the Schwinger model, admits a dual formulation in terms of a non-compact boson field. Using a Hamiltonian lattice discretization of the bosonized model, we compute the critical mass of the continuum Schwinger model at $\theta=\pi$. We numerically obtain the ground state on an infinite chain by the variational uniform matrix product state (VUMPS) algorithm, preserving charge conjugation exactly. As a truncated basis for the local Hilbert space, we employ the oscillator basis that diagonalizes the one-site reduced density matrix of the free limit. We constrain the fitting functions for the continuum extrapolation using the Symanzik effective theory for lattice corrections. Our result for the critical mass, $(m/g)_c = 0.333\,555(5)$, agrees with recent staggered-fermion determinations at comparable precision.

</details>


### [197] [Numerical tests of chiral fermion weights with $η$-invariant phases in the 3450 model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16876)
*Tatsuhiro Misumi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper numerically tests a chiral fermion lattice prescription combining Pauli-Villars regularization (for magnitude) and the Atiyah-Patodi-Singer η-invariant (for phase), confirming high-precision agreement with exact analytic results in a 2D anomaly-free U(1) model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To validate a theoretically sound lattice formulation of chiral fermions that preserves the correct chiral anomaly structure and gauge invariance, especially in the presence of zero modes and topologically nontrivial backgrounds.

Method: Numerical simulation on a 16×16 torus and cylinder geometries using interpolated lattice gauge fields; evaluation of Polyakov loops, vacuum polarization, and a twelve-point correlator under generalized APS boundary conditions; comparison against exact analytic determinants and induced masses.

Result: With the APS phase: Polyakov loops match exact values within 0.04%; induced mass m/e = 2.82089 vs exact 5/√π ≈ 2.82095; twelve-point correlator magnitude agrees within 0.8%, phase error reduced to 0.2–0.5 mrad; anomaly-free phase cancellation holds to <0.2 mrad under bulk extension ambiguity.

Conclusion: The APS-regularized phase is essential for quantitative fidelity—omitting it degrades results by orders of magnitude—and the full prescription correctly encodes both local and global aspects of chiral gauge theory, including anomaly cancellation and zero-mode handling.

Abstract: We test numerically a recently proposed prescription for coupling continuum chiral fermions to interpolated lattice gauge fields, in which the fermion weight has a Pauli-Villars (PV) regularized magnitude and a phase given by an Atiyah-Patodi-Singer (APS) $\eta$-invariant in one higher dimension. We use the anomaly-free two-dimensional $U(1)$ theory with left-handed charges $3,4$ and right-handed charges $5,0$, whose torus determinant is known exactly. At zero magnetic flux, with gauge fields interpolated from lattice links, the Polyakov loops evaluated on a $16\times16$ grid of flat connections agree with the exact values within $0.04\%$, whereas omitting the APS phase substantially reduces them to about $3\%$ of the exact result. The vacuum-polarization response yields an induced mass $m/e=2.82089$, close to $5/\sqrt{\pi}=2.82095$. At unit flux, where zero modes make the determinant vanish, we calculate a twelve-point correlator on a cylinder with the generalized APS boundary condition. For three smooth backgrounds its magnitude agrees with the exact result within $0.8\%$. Without the $\eta$-invariant its phase deviates by $1.8$-$3.0$ mrad, and with it by $0.2$-$0.5$ mrad. The phase of each charged fermion changes by $15$-$46$ mrad when the gauge field is extended differently into the third dimension, but the change cancels to below $0.2$ mrad in the anomaly-free combination.

</details>


### [198] [Automatic O(a) Improvement for Wilson Twisted Mass Fermions in the Hamiltonian Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16658)
*Carsten Urbach*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper applies the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian framework to Wilson twisted mass fermions to prove automatic O(a) improvement at maximal twist and derive the structure of O(a) counterterms needed for clover on-shell improvement, revealing two independent coefficients for the clover term in isotropic Euclidean spacetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a symmetry-based, Hamiltonian formulation of Symanzik improvement for Wilson twisted mass fermions—bypassing the Euclidean effective action—and to clarify the structure and redundancy of O(a) counterterms for on-shell improvement.

Method: Constructs the Symanzik effective Hamiltonian directly from lattice Hamiltonian symmetries; employs canonical transformations to eliminate on-shell redundant operators; analyzes operator mixing and coefficient independence under rotational and chiral symmetry constraints.

Result: Proof of automatic O(a) improvement at maximal twist and via mass/Wilson averaging; derivation of minimal, non-redundant O(a) counterterm basis for clover improvement; identification of two linearly independent clover-type operators with distinct coefficients in isotropic Euclidean spacetime.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian Symanzik approach provides a rigorous, symmetry-driven foundation for O(a) improvement of Wilson twisted mass fermions; the clover term is not unique but splits into two independent structures, necessitating separate tuning in practical simulations.

Abstract: We apply the recently developed Symanzik effective Hamiltonian framework to Wilson twisted mass fermions. The effective Hamiltonian is constructed solely based on the symmetries of the lattice Hamiltonian without recourse to the Symanzik effective action in Euclidean space-time. We use it to prove automatic $O(a)$ improvement at maximal twist, as well as $O(a)$ improvement via so-called mass and Wilson average. Moreover, we derive the structure of $O(a)$ counterterms pre-requisite for clover on-shell improvement of Wilson fermions. We show how to remove on-shell redundant contributions using canonical transformations, and find that the well known clover term in isotropic Euclidean space time splits into two terms with independent coefficients.

</details>


### [199] [$NΩ$ Dibaryon near Unitarity from Physical-Point Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16989)
*Liang Zhang,Yan Lyu,Sinya Aoki,Takumi Doi,Tetsuo Hatsuda,Song Zhang,Yu-Gang Ma*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the $N\Omega$ dibaryon interaction using lattice QCD at physical quark masses and the HAL QCD method, finding an attractive interaction supporting a shallow bound state; binding energies are reported as $B_{n\Omega} \approx 0.61$ MeV and $B_{p\Omega} \approx 1.25$ MeV (including Coulomb), providing first-principles predictions for experimental searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The $N\Omega$ system is a promising dibaryon candidate due to absence of shared valence quarks, eliminating Pauli repulsion; thus, it offers a clean probe of strong-interaction dynamics beyond conventional baryons.

Method: The $^{5}S_{2}$ $N\Omega$ interaction is computed in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD at physical pion mass ($m_\pi \simeq 137~\text{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method; binding energies are extracted via $T$-matrix pole analysis, with Coulomb correction included for the proton-$\Omega$ channel.

Result: An attractive $N\Omega$ interaction is found over the resolved range, supporting a shallow near-unitary bound state; binding energies are $B_{n\Omega} = 0.61(^{+0.41}_{-0.30})(^{+0.60}_{-0.18})~\text{MeV}$ and $B_{p\Omega} = 1.25(^{+0.46}_{-0.39})(^{+0.69}_{-0.25})~\text{MeV}$.

Conclusion: The $N\Omega$ dibaryon is predicted to be weakly bound, offering a first-principles benchmark for femtoscopy measurements and experimental dibaryon searches in heavy-ion collisions or hadronic production.

Abstract: The $N\Omega$ system is a promising dibaryon candidate because its constituents share no valence quarks and therefore avoid valence-quark Pauli repulsion. We determine the $^{5}S_{2}$ $N\Omega$ interaction in (2+1)-flavor lattice QCD at physical quark masses ($m_\pi\simeq137~\text{MeV}$) using the HAL QCD method. The interaction is attractive over the resolved range and supports a shallow state near unitarity. A direct $T$-matrix pole analysis gives $B_{n\Omega} = 0.61\,(^{+0.41}_{-0.30})\,(^{+0.60}_{-0.18})~\text{MeV}$; including finite-charge-size Coulomb attraction yields $B_{p\Omega} = 1.25\,(^{+0.46}_{-0.39})\,(^{+0.69}_{-0.25})~\text{MeV}$. These results provide a first-principles benchmark for future $N\Omega$ femtoscopy and dibaryon searches.

</details>
